\displaystyle=\alpha_{t+1}v^{\star}+\sum_{k=1}^{t}\beta_{t}^{k}\phi_{k}+\underset{\eqqcolon\,\xi_{t}}{\underbrace{\sum_{k=1}^{t-1}z_{k}\left[\langle W_{k}z_{k},\eta_{t}(x_{t})\rangle-\langle\eta_{t}^{\prime}(x_{t})\rangle\beta_{t-1}^{k}-\beta_{t}^{k}z_{k}^{\top}W_{k}z_{k}\right]-\sum_{k=1}^{t}\beta_{t}^{k}\zeta_{k}}}.

(54)

where the second line invokes (
52
), the fourth line makes use of the fact that

W_{t}\eta_{t}(x_{t})=W_{t}\big(I-U_{t-1}U_{t-1}^{\top}\big)\eta_{t}(x_{t})=W_{t}(\beta_{t}^{t}z_{t}),

and the last line in (
54
) follows from (
47
).
By construction,
\zeta_{k}\in U_{k}
, and hence the expression of
\xi_{t}
in (
54
) immediately reveals that
\xi_{t}\in U_{t}
.
∎

Before moving on, we further take a moment to derive an alternative expression of
\xi_{t}
.
Let us first make the following observation
arising from the definition (
46
) and the decomposition (
44
):

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{t}\beta_{t}^{k}\zeta_{k}=\sum_{k=1}^{t}\beta_{t}^{k}\left[\bigg(\frac{\sqrt{2}}{2}-1\bigg)z_{k}z_{k}^{\top}W_{k}z_{k}+\sum_{i=1}^{k-1}g_{i}^{k}z_{i}\right]=\sum_{k=1}^{t}z_{k}\left[\beta_{t}^{k}\bigg(\frac{\sqrt{2}}{2}-1\bigg)z_{k}^{\top}W_{k}z_{k}+\sum_{i=k+1}^{t}\beta_{t}^{i}g_{k}^{i}\right],

where the last line holds since

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{t}\beta_{t}^{k}\sum_{i=1}^{k-1}g_{i}^{k}z_{i}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{t-1}z_{i}\sum_{k=i+1}^{t}\beta_{t}^{k}g_{i}^{k}=\sum_{k=1}^{t-1}z_{k}\sum_{i=k+1}^{t}\beta_{t}^{i}g_{k}^{i}=\sum_{k=1}^{t}z_{k}\sum_{i=k+1}^{t}\beta_{t}^{i}g_{k}^{i}.

Additionally, apply the decomposition (
44
) and the (
46
) once again to reach

\displaystyle\big\langle\zeta_{k},\eta_{t}(x_{t})\big\rangle=\left\langle\Big(\frac{\sqrt{2}}{2}-1\Big)z_{k}z_{k}^{\top}W_{k}z_{k}+\sum_{i=1}^{k-1}g_{i}^{k}z_{i},\sum_{k=1}^{t}\beta_{t}^{k}z_{k}\right\rangle=\bigg(\frac{\sqrt{2}}{2}-1\bigg)\beta_{t}^{k}z_{k}^{\top}W_{k}z_{k}+\sum_{i=1}^{k-1}\beta_{t}^{i}g_{i}^{k}

for any
k\leq t
.
Substituting the above two equalities into (
54
), we arrive at

\displaystyle\xi_{t}

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=1}^{t-1}z_{k}\left[\langle W_{k}z_{k},\eta_{t}(x_{t})\rangle-\langle\eta_{t}^{\prime}(x_{t})\rangle\beta_{t-1}^{k}-\beta_{t}^{k}z_{k}^{\top}W_{k}z_{k}\right]-\sum_{k=1}^{t}\beta_{t}^{k}\zeta_{k}

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=1}^{t-1}z_{k}\left[\langle\phi_{k},\eta_{t}(x_{t})\rangle-\langle\zeta_{k},\eta_{t}(x_{t})\rangle-\langle\eta_{t}^{\prime}(x_{t})\rangle\beta_{t-1}^{k}-\beta_{t}^{k}z_{k}^{\top}W_{k}z_{k}\right]-\sum_{k=1}^{t}\beta_{t}^{k}\zeta_{k}

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=1}^{t-1}z_{k}\Bigg[\Big\langle\phi_{k},\eta_{t}\Big(\alpha_{t}v^{\star}+\sum_{k=1}^{t-1}\beta_{t-1}^{k}\phi_{k}+\xi_{t-1}\Big)\Big\rangle-\langle\eta_{t}^{\prime}(x_{t})\rangle\beta_{t-1}^{k}-\sum_{i=1}^{k-1}\beta_{t}^{i}g_{i}^{k}-\sum_{i=k+1}^{t}\beta_{t}^{i}g_{k}^{i}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad-\big(\sqrt{2}-1\big)\beta_{t}^{k}z_{k}^{\top}W_{k}z_{k}\bigg]-\bigg(\frac{\sqrt{2}}{2}-1\bigg)\beta_{t}^{t}z_{t}z_{t}^{\top}W_{t}z_{t},

(55)

where the last line invokes the decomposition (
51
).

Step 4: bounding the residual term
\|\xi_{t}\|_{2}
.

Everything then boils down to controlling
\|\xi_{t}\|_{2}
.
Let us define a vector
\mu_{t}=[\mu_{t}^{k}]_{1\leq k\leq t}\in\mathbb{R}^{t}
with coordinates

\mu_{t}^{k}\coloneqq\frac{\xi_{t}^{\top}z_{k}}{\|\xi_{t}\|_{2}},\qquad 1\leq k\leq t.

Given that
\{z_{k}\}_{k\leq t}
forms an orthonormal basis and that
\xi_{t}\in U_{t}
, one can easily see that

\|\mu_{t}\|_{2}=1\qquad\text{and}\qquad\xi_{t}=\|\xi_{t}\|_{2}\sum_{k=1}^{t}\mu_{t}^{k}z_{k}.

Hence, we can deduce that

\displaystyle\|\xi_{t}\|_{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{\langle\xi_{t},\,\xi_{t}\rangle}{\|\xi_{t}\|_{2}}=\frac{\langle\|\xi_{t}\|_{2}\sum_{k=1}^{t}\mu_{t}^{k}z_{k},\,\xi_{t}\rangle}{\|\xi_{t}\|_{2}}=\sum_{k=1}^{t}\mu_{t}^{k}\langle z_{k},\,\xi_{t}\rangle

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(\text{i})}}{{=}}\sum_{k=1}^{t-1}\mu_{t}^{k}\Bigg[\Big\langle\phi_{k},\eta_{t}\Big(\alpha_{t}v^{\star}+\sum_{k=1}^{t-1}\beta_{t-1}^{k}\phi_{k}+\xi_{t-1}\Big)\Big\rangle-\langle\eta_{t}^{\prime}(x_{t})\rangle\beta_{t-1}^{k}-\sum_{i=1}^{k-1}\beta_{t}^{i}g_{i}^{k}-\sum_{i=k+1}^{t}\beta_{t}^{i}g_{k}^{i}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad-\big(\sqrt{2}-1\big)\beta_{t}^{k}z_{k}^{\top}W_{k}z_{k}\bigg]-\bigg(\frac{\sqrt{2}}{2}-1\bigg)\beta_{t}^{t}\mu_{t}^{t}z_{t}^{\top}W_{t}z_{t}

\displaystyle=\bigg\langle\sum_{k=1}^{t-1}\mu_{t}^{k}\phi_{k},\delta_{t}\bigg\rangle-\langle\delta_{t}^{\prime}\rangle\sum_{k=1}^{t-1}\mu_{t}^{k}\beta_{t-1}^{k}-\bigg(\frac{\sqrt{2}}{2}-1\bigg)\beta_{t}^{t}\mu_{t}^{t}z_{t}^{\top}W_{t}z_{t}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad-\sum_{k=1}^{t-1}\mu_{t}^{k}\left[-\big\langle\phi_{k},\eta_{t}(v_{t})\big\rangle+\big\langle\eta_{t}^{\prime}(v_{t})\big\rangle\beta_{t-1}^{k}+(\sqrt{2}-1)\beta_{t}^{k}z_{k}^{\top}W_{k}z_{k}+\sum_{i=1}^{k-1}\beta_{t}^{i}g_{i}^{k}+\sum_{i=k+1}^{t}\beta_{t}^{i}g_{k}^{i}\right]

\displaystyle=\Big\langle\sum_{k=1}^{t-1}\mu_{t}^{k}\phi_{k},\delta_{t}\Big\rangle-\langle\delta_{t}^{\prime}\rangle\sum_{k=1}^{t-1}\mu_{t}^{k}\beta_{t-1}^{k}-\bigg(\frac{\sqrt{2}}{2}-1\bigg)\beta_{t}^{t}\mu_{t}^{t}z_{t}^{\top}W_{t}z_{t}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\Delta_{t}-\sum_{k=1}^{t-1}\mu_{t}^{k}\left[(\sqrt{2}-1)\beta_{t}^{k}z_{k}^{\top}W_{k}z_{k}+\sum_{i=1}^{k-1}\beta_{t}^{i}g_{i}^{k}+\sum_{i=k+1}^{t}\beta_{t}^{i}g_{k}^{i}\right],

(56)

where
(\text{i})
invokes expression (
55
) and the fact that
\{z_{k}\}_{k\leq t}
are orthogonal to each other,
and the last two lines rely on the definitions in (
11
) as follows:

\displaystyle v_{t}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\alpha_{t}v^{\star}+\sum_{k=1}^{t-1}\beta_{t-1}^{k}\phi_{k},

\displaystyle\Delta_{t}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\sum_{k=1}^{t-1}\mu_{t}^{k}\Big[\big\langle\phi_{k},\eta_{t}(v_{t})\big\rangle-\big\langle\eta_{t}^{\prime}(v_{t})\big\rangle\beta_{t-1}^{k}\Big],

\displaystyle\delta_{t}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\eta_{t}\Big(\alpha_{t}v^{\star}+\sum_{k=1}^{t-1}\beta_{t-1}^{k}\phi_{k}+\xi_{t-1}\Big)-\eta_{t}(v_{t}),

\displaystyle\delta_{t}^{\prime}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\eta_{t}^{\prime}\Big(\alpha_{t}v^{\star}+\sum_{k=1}^{t-1}\beta_{t-1}^{k}\phi_{k}+\xi_{t-1}\Big)-\eta_{t}^{\prime}(v_{t}).

To establish Theorem
1
, it then suffices to control the last term on the right-hand side of (
56
).
This is accomplished in the following lemma, whose proof is deferred to Section
B.2
.

Lemma 3
.

With probability at least
1-O(n^{-11})
, for any
t\leq n
we have

\displaystyle\bigg|\sum_{k=1}^{t-1}\mu_{t}^{k}\Big((\sqrt{2}-1)\beta_{t}^{k}z_{k}^{\top}W_{k}z_{k}+\sum_{i=1}^{k-1}\beta_{t}^{i}g_{i}^{k}+\sum_{i=k+1}^{t}\beta_{t}^{i}g_{k}^{i}\Big)\bigg|

\displaystyle\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{t\log n}{n}}\|\beta_{t}\|_{2}.

Taking this lemma collectively with equality (
56
) and the trivial bound
|z_{t}^{\top}W_{t}z_{t}|\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{\log n}{n}}
leads to

\displaystyle\|\xi_{t}\|_{2}=\Big\langle\sum_{k=1}^{t-1}\mu_{t}^{k}\phi_{k},\delta_{t}\Big\rangle-\langle\delta_{t}^{\prime}\rangle\sum_{k=1}^{t-1}\mu_{t}^{k}\beta_{t-1}^{k}+\Delta_{t}+O\bigg(\sqrt{\frac{t\log n}{n}}\|\beta_{t}\|_{2}\bigg),

(57)

thus completing the proof of Theorem
1
.

4.2
Proof of Theorem
2

Before embarking on the proof, we remind the reader of several results that have already proven for
\alpha_{t}
and
\beta_{t}
.
Recall that in the proof of Theorem
1
, we decompose the AMP iterate
x_{t+1}
as follows

\displaystyle x_{t+1}=\alpha_{t+1}v^{\star}+\sum_{k=1}^{t}\beta_{t}^{k}\phi_{k}+\xi_{t},\qquad 1\leq t\leq n,

where
\xi_{t}\in U_{t}
(some linear subspace of dimension
t
) represents some residual term, and

\displaystyle\alpha_{t+1}

\displaystyle=\lambda v^{\star\top}\eta_{t}(x_{t})=\lambda v^{\star\top}\eta_{t}\Big(\alpha_{t}v^{\star}+\sum_{k=1}^{t-1}\beta_{t-1}^{k}\phi_{k}+\xi_{t-1}\Big),

(58a)

\displaystyle\|\beta_{t}\|_{2}

\displaystyle=\|\eta_{t}(x_{t})\|_{2}=\Big\|\eta_{t}\Big(\alpha_{t}v^{\star}+\sum_{k=1}^{t-1}\beta_{t-1}^{k}\phi_{k}+\xi_{t-1}\Big)\Big\|_{2}.

(58b)

We have also shown in Theorem
1
that with probability at least
1-O(n^{-11})
, the residual term satisfies

\displaystyle\|\xi_{t}\|_{2}=\Big\langle\sum_{k=1}^{t-1}\mu^{k}_{t}\phi_{k},\delta_{t}\Big\rangle-\langle\delta_{t}^{\prime}\rangle\sum_{k=1}^{t-1}\mu^{k}_{t}\beta_{t-1}^{k}+\Delta_{t}+O\Big(\sqrt{\frac{t\log n}{n}}\|\beta_{t}\|_{2}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\Big\langle\sum_{k=1}^{t-1}\mu^{k}_{t}\phi_{k},\delta_{t}\Big\rangle-\langle\delta_{t}^{\prime}\rangle\sum_{k=1}^{t-1}\mu^{k}_{t}\beta_{t-1}^{k}+A_{t}+O\Big(\sqrt{\frac{t\log n}{n}}\|\beta_{t}\|_{2}\Big),

(59)

where the last step invokes property (
14a
) in Assumption
2
as well as the definition (
11d
) of
\Delta_{t}
.

Step 1: bounding
\Delta_{\alpha,t}
and
\Delta_{\beta,t}
in terms of
\delta_{t}
and
\delta_{t}^{\prime}
.

We begin by controlling the size of the term
\Delta_{\alpha,t}
. In view of its definition in (
15a
),
we have

\displaystyle\Delta_{\alpha,t}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\frac{\alpha_{t+1}}{\lambda}-v^{\star\top}\int\eta_{t}\Big(\alpha_{t}v^{\star}+\frac{\|\beta_{t-1}\|_{2}}{\sqrt{n}}x\Big)\varphi_{n}(\mathrm{d}x),

\displaystyle=v^{\star\top}\delta_{t}+v^{\star\top}\eta_{t}\Big(\alpha_{t}v^{\star}+\sum_{k=1}^{t-1}\beta_{t-1}^{k}\phi_{k}\Big)-v^{\star\top}\int\eta_{t}\Big(\alpha_{t}v^{\star}+\frac{\|\beta_{t-1}\|_{2}}{\sqrt{n}}x\Big)\varphi_{n}(\mathrm{d}x),

where the second line follows from (
58a
) and the definition (
11b
) of
\delta_{t}
.
As a direct consequence of the assumption (
14b
), we obtain

\displaystyle|\Delta_{\alpha,t}|

\displaystyle\leq\left|\langle v^{\star},\,\delta_{t}\rangle\right|+B_{t}.

(60)

We then move on to the term
\Delta_{\beta,t}
.
Recognizing that

\|\beta_{t}\|_{2}^{2}=\|\eta_{t}(x_{t})\|_{2}^{2}=\Big\|\eta_{t}\Big(\alpha_{t}v^{\star}+\sum_{k=1}^{t-1}\beta_{t-1}^{k}\phi_{k}\Big)+\delta_{t}\Big\|_{2}^{2},

we can combine it with the definition (
15b
) to obtain

\displaystyle\Delta_{\beta,t}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\|\beta_{t}\|_{2}^{2}-\int\Big\|\eta_{t}\Big(\alpha_{t}v^{\star}+\frac{\|\beta_{t-1}\|_{2}}{\sqrt{n}}x\Big)\Big\|_{2}^{2}\varphi_{n}(\mathrm{d}x)

\displaystyle=\Big\langle 2\eta_{t}\Big(\alpha_{t}v^{\star}+\sum_{k=1}^{t-1}\beta_{t-1}^{k}\phi_{k}\Big),\delta_{t}\Big\rangle+\|\delta_{t}\|_{2}^{2}+\Big\|\eta_{t}\Big(\alpha_{t}v^{\star}+\sum_{k=1}^{t-1}\beta_{t-1}^{k}\phi_{k}\Big)\Big\|_{2}^{2}-\int\Big\|\eta_{t}\Big(\alpha_{t}v^{\star}+\frac{\|\beta_{t-1}\|_{2}}{\sqrt{n}}x\Big)\Big\|_{2}^{2}\varphi_{n}(\mathrm{d}x).

By virtue of the assumption (
14c
), we obtain

\displaystyle|\Delta_{\beta,t}|

\displaystyle\leq\Big|\Big\langle 2\eta_{t}\Big(\alpha_{t}v^{\star}+\sum_{k=1}^{t-1}\beta_{t-1}^{k}\phi_{k}\Big),\delta_{t}\Big\rangle\Big|+\|\delta_{t}\|_{2}^{2}+C_{t}.

(61)

Step 2: bounding
\delta_{t}
and
\delta_{t}^{\prime}
.

To further control the right-hand side of (
60
) and (
61
),
we proceed by bounding terms associated with
\delta_{t}
.
Given that
\eta_{t}(\cdot)
is assumed to be continuous, one can derive

\displaystyle\delta_{t}

\displaystyle=\eta_{t}\Big(\alpha_{t}v^{\star}+\sum_{k=1}^{t-1}\beta_{t-1}^{k}\phi_{k}+\xi_{t-1}\Big)-\eta_{t}\Big(\alpha_{t}v^{\star}+\sum_{k=1}^{t-1}\beta_{t-1}^{k}\phi_{k}\Big)

\displaystyle={\displaystyle\int}_{0}^{1}\bigg\{\eta_{t}^{\prime}\Big(\alpha_{t}v^{\star}+\sum_{k=1}^{t-1}\beta_{t-1}^{k}\phi_{k}+\tau\xi_{t-1}\Big)\circ\xi_{t-1}\bigg\}\mathrm{d}\tau

\displaystyle=\eta_{t}^{\prime}\Big(\alpha_{t}v^{\star}+\sum_{k=1}^{t-1}\beta_{t-1}^{k}\phi_{k}\Big)\circ\xi_{t-1}+{\displaystyle\int}_{0}^{1}\bigg\{\bigg[\eta_{t}^{\prime}\Big(\alpha_{t}v^{\star}+\sum_{k=1}^{t-1}\beta_{t-1}^{k}\phi_{k}+\tau\xi_{t-1}\Big)-\eta_{t}^{\prime}\Big(\alpha_{t}v^{\star}+\sum_{k=1}^{t-1}\beta_{t-1}^{k}\phi_{k}\Big)\bigg]\circ\xi_{t-1}\bigg\}\mathrm{d}\tau,

(62)

where the second line invokes the fundamental theorem of calculus.
Note that
\eta_{t}^{\prime}(\cdot)
has a finite number of discontinuous points.
Recalling that
|\eta_{t}^{\prime}(w)|\leq\rho
and
|\eta_{t}^{\prime\prime}(w)|\leq\rho_{1}
for any continuous point
w\in\real
(see Assumption
1
),
we have

\displaystyle\left|\eta_{t}^{\prime}\Big(\alpha_{t}v^{\star}+\sum_{k=1}^{t-1}\beta_{t-1}^{k}\phi_{k}+\tau\xi_{t-1}\Big)-\eta_{t}^{\prime}\Big(\alpha_{t}v^{\star}+\sum_{k=1}^{t-1}\beta_{t-1}^{k}\phi_{k}\Big)\right|

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\left|{\displaystyle\int}_{0}^{1}\bigg\{\eta_{t}^{\prime\prime}\Big(\alpha_{t}v^{\star}+\sum_{k=1}^{t-1}\beta_{t-1}^{k}\phi_{k}+\tau_{1}\tau\xi_{t-1}\Big)\circ\big(\tau\xi_{t-1}\big)\bigg\}\mathrm{d}\tau_{1}\right|+2\rho\Gamma

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\rho_{1}\big|\xi_{t-1}\big|+2\rho\Gamma,

(63)

where
\Gamma=[\Gamma_{j}]_{1\leq j\leq n}\in\real^{n}
is a term reflecting the influence of discontinuous points.
More precisely,
\Gamma_{j}
denotes the number of discontinuities of
\eta_{t}^{\prime}(\cdot)
encountered between

\big[\alpha_{t}v^{\star}_{j}+\sum_{k=1}^{t-1}\beta_{t-1}^{k}\phi_{k,j},\alpha_{t}v^{\star}_{j}+\sum_{k=1}^{t-1}\beta_{t-1}^{k}\phi_{k,j}+\xi_{t-1,j}\big]
.
Note that if a point
m
is contained in an interval
[a,b]
,
then one must have
a+\tau(b-a)=m
for some
\tau\in[0,1]
,
which requires that
|b-a|\geq|\tau(b-a)|=|a-m|
. This basic fact allows us to take

\displaystyle\Gamma_{j}=\sum_{m\in\mathcal{M}_{\mathsf{dc}}}\ind\bigg\{\big|\xi_{t-1,j}\big|\geq\Big|\alpha_{t}v_{j}^{\star}+\sum_{k=1}^{t-1}\beta_{t-1}^{k}\phi_{k,j}-m\Big|\bigg\}\eqqcolon\sum_{m\in\mathcal{M}_{\mathsf{dc}}}\Gamma_{j}(m).

(64)

Substitution into (
62
) yields

\displaystyle\Big|\delta_{t}

\displaystyle-\eta_{t}^{\prime}\Big(\alpha_{t}v^{\star}+\sum_{k=1}^{t-1}\beta_{t-1}^{k}\phi_{k}\Big)\circ\xi_{t-1}\Big|\leq\rho_{1}\big|\xi_{t-1}\big|^{2}+2\rho\Gamma\circ\big|\xi_{t-1}\big|.

(65)

Similarly, we can repeat the same argument (particularly (
63
) and (
65
)) to bound
\delta_{t}^{\prime}
as follows:

\displaystyle\bigg|\delta_{t}^{\prime}-\eta_{t}^{\prime\prime}\Big(\alpha_{t}v^{\star}+\sum_{k=1}^{t-1}\beta_{t-1}^{k}\phi_{k}\Big)\circ\xi_{t-1}\bigg|

\displaystyle=\bigg|\eta_{t}^{\prime}\Big(\alpha_{t}v^{\star}+\sum_{k=1}^{t-1}\beta_{t-1}^{k}\phi_{k}+\xi_{t-1}\Big)-\eta_{t}^{\prime}\Big(\alpha_{t}v^{\star}+\sum_{k=1}^{t-1}\beta_{t-1}^{k}\phi_{k}\Big)-\eta_{t}^{\prime\prime}\Big(\alpha_{t}v^{\star}+\sum_{k=1}^{t-1}\beta_{t-1}^{k}\phi_{k}\Big)\circ\xi_{t-1}\bigg|

\displaystyle\leq\bigg|{\displaystyle\int}_{0}^{1}\bigg\{\eta_{t}^{\prime\prime}\Big(\alpha_{t}v^{\star}+\sum_{k=1}^{t-1}\beta_{t-1}^{k}\phi_{k}+\tau\xi_{t-1}\Big)\circ\xi_{t-1}\bigg\}\mathrm{d}\tau-\eta_{t}^{\prime\prime}\Big(\alpha_{t}v^{\star}+\sum_{k=1}^{t-1}\beta_{t-1}^{k}\phi_{k}\Big)\circ\xi_{t-1}\bigg|+2\rho\Gamma

\displaystyle\leq\rho_{2}\big|\xi_{t-1}\big|^{2}+2\rho\Gamma+2\rho_{1}\Gamma\circ\big|\xi_{t-1}\big|.

(66)

With the above bounds on
\delta_{t}
and
\delta_{t}^{\prime}
in place,
we are ready to establish the advertised results (
16a
), (
16b
) and (
16
),
which we will look at one by one in the sequel.

Step 3: establishing inequality (
16
).

With these relations in place, let us start with controlling quantity
\|\xi_{t}\|_{2}
.
In view of expression (
59
), it requires us to bound
\langle\mu_{t}^{k}\phi_{k},\delta_{t}\rangle-\langle\delta_{t}^{\prime}\rangle\sum_{k=1}^{t-1}\mu_{t}^{k}\beta_{t-1}^{k}
.
Taking the bounds (
65
) and (
66
) collectively with (
59
),
and recalling the definition (
11a
) of
v_{t}
, we arrive at

\displaystyle\|\xi_{t}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\Big\langle\sum_{k=1}^{t-1}\mu_{t}^{k}\phi_{k},\delta_{t}\Big\rangle-\langle\delta_{t}^{\prime}\rangle\sum_{k=1}^{t-1}\mu_{t}^{k}\beta_{t-1}^{k}+A_{t}+O\Big(\sqrt{\frac{t\log n}{n}}\|\beta_{t}\|_{2}\Big)

\displaystyle=\bigg\langle\sum_{k=1}^{t-1}\mu_{t}^{k}\phi_{k},\,\eta_{t}^{\prime}(v_{t})\circ\xi_{t-1}\bigg\rangle-\bigg\langle\eta_{t}^{\prime\prime}(v_{t})\circ\xi_{t-1}\bigg\rangle\sum_{k=1}^{t-1}\mu_{t}^{k}\beta_{t-1}^{k}

\displaystyle\qquad+\rho_{1}\bigg\langle\bigg|\sum_{k=1}^{t-1}\mu_{t}^{k}\phi_{k}\bigg|,\big|\xi_{t-1}\big|^{2}\bigg\rangle+\rho_{2}\Big\langle\big|\xi_{t-1}\big|^{2}\Big\rangle\bigg|\sum_{k=1}^{t-1}\mu_{t}^{k}\beta_{t-1}^{k}\bigg|

\displaystyle\qquad+2\rho\bigg\langle\bigg|\sum_{k=1}^{t-1}\mu_{t}^{k}\phi_{k}\bigg|,\,\Gamma\circ\big|\xi_{t-1}\big|\bigg\rangle+\Big\{2\rho\langle\Gamma\rangle+2\rho_{1}\big\langle\Gamma\circ\big|\xi_{t-1}\big|\big\rangle\Big\}\bigg|\sum_{k=1}^{t-1}\mu_{t}^{k}\beta_{t-1}^{k}\bigg|

\displaystyle\qquad+A_{t}+O\Big(\sqrt{\frac{t\log n}{n}}\|\beta_{t}\|_{2}\Big).

(67)

This leaves us with several terms to control, which is the content of the lemma below; the proof is deferred to Section
B.3
.

Lemma 4
.

Consider any
t\leq n
. Given
\kappa_{t}
defined in (
2.2
), it holds that

\displaystyle\bigg\langle\sum_{k=1}^{t-1}\mu_{t}^{k}\phi_{k},\eta_{t}^{\prime}(v_{t})\circ\xi_{t-1}\bigg\rangle-\Big\langle\eta_{t}^{\prime\prime}(v_{t})\circ\xi_{t-1}\Big\rangle\sum_{k=1}^{t-1}\mu_{t}^{k}\beta_{t-1}^{k}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\kappa_{t}^{2}+D_{t}}\,\|\xi_{t-1}\|_{2}

(68a)

\displaystyle\rho_{1}\bigg\langle\bigg|\sum_{k=1}^{t-1}\mu_{t}^{k}\phi_{k}\bigg|,\big|\xi_{t-1}\big|^{2}\Big\rangle+\rho_{2}\Big\langle\big|\xi_{t-1}\big|^{2}\Big\rangle\bigg|\sum_{k=1}^{t-1}\mu_{t}^{k}\beta_{t-1}^{k}\bigg|

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\rho_{1}\frac{\sqrt{t}+\sqrt{\log n}}{\sqrt{n}}+\frac{\rho_{2}\|\beta_{t-1}\|_{2}}{n}\right)\|\xi_{t-1}\|_{2}^{2}

(68b)

hold with probability at least
1-O(n^{-11})
.
In addition, one has

\displaystyle 2\rho\bigg\langle\bigg|\sum_{k=1}^{t-1}\mu_{t}^{k}\phi_{k}\bigg|,\,\Gamma\circ\big|\xi_{t-1}\big|\bigg\rangle+\Big\{2\rho\langle\Gamma\rangle+2\rho_{1}\big\langle\Gamma\circ\big|\xi_{t-1}\big|\big\rangle\Big\}\bigg|\sum_{k=1}^{t-1}\mu_{t}^{k}\beta_{t-1}^{k}\bigg|

\displaystyle\qquad\lesssim\rho\sqrt{\frac{(E_{t}+t)\log n}{n}}\big\|\xi_{t-1}\big\|_{2}+\frac{(\rho+\rho_{1}\big\|\xi_{t-1}\big\|_{\infty})E_{t}\big\|\beta_{t-1}\big\|_{2}}{n}.

(68c)

Combining Lemma
4
with (
67
) immediately completes the proof of inequality (
16
).

Step 4: establishing inequalities (
16a
) and (
16b
).

Finally, we return to establish the advertised bounds on
|\Delta_{\alpha,t}|
and
|\Delta_{\beta,t}|
.
Towards this, we are in need of the following lemma, whose proof is provided in Section
B.4
.

Lemma 5
.

The following inequalities hold true:

\displaystyle|\langle v^{\star},\delta_{t}\rangle|

\displaystyle\lesssim\rho\|\xi_{t-1}\|_{2},

(69a)

\displaystyle\Big|\Big\langle\eta_{t}\big(\alpha_{t}v^{\star}+\sum_{k=1}^{t-1}\beta_{t-1}^{k}\phi_{k}\big),\delta_{t}\Big\rangle\Big|

\displaystyle\lesssim F_{t}\|\xi_{t-1}\|_{2}+\rho_{1}G_{t}\|\xi_{t-1}\|_{2}^{2}+\rho\sqrt{E_{t}}G_{t}\|\xi_{t-1}\|_{2},

(69b)

\displaystyle\|\delta_{t}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\rho^{2}\|\xi_{t-1}\|_{2}^{2}.

(69c)

Substituting the results in Lemma
5
into inequalities (
60
) and (
61
) immediately
establishes (
16a
) and (
16b
).
We have thus completed the proof of Theorem
2
.

5
Discussion

In this paper,
we have proposed a general recipe towards analyzing the finite-sample performance of the AMP algorithm when applied to spiked Wigner models.
Our analysis framework makes explicit a crucial decomposition of each AMP iterate (as a superposition of a signal term and a Gaussian-type stochastic component),
with a residual term that can be tracked recursively without exploding rapidly.
Further, this analysis framework can be seamlessly integrated with spectral initialization.
The power of our analysis strategy has been demonstrated via two concrete applications:
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
synchronization and sparse PCA.
In both cases, explicit non-asymptotic behaviors of AMP have been derived up to a polynomial number of iterations, thereby revealing new insights about the finite-sample convergence properties of AMP.
After finishing this paper,
we have posted a companion paper
Li et al., 2023
that — built upon and extending the analysis framework herein — characterizes the finite-sample dynamics of random initialized AMP for the problem of
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
synchronization. It is proved there that AMP is capable of escaping random initialization and entering a local refinement phase within at most
O(\log n)
iterations. In other words, an informative initialization is not crucial at all for the effectiveness of AMP.

Our work leaves open a variety of questions; we conclude the paper by highlighting a few of them.

•

Firstly, while we have illustrated the effectiveness of our master theorems with two examples of different flavor,
there is no shortage of other signal structures that are of practical interest.
For instance, one might wonder how AMP behaves non-asymptotically when the signal
v^{\star}
is known to satisfy certain shape constraints (e.g., having non-negative entries, residing in a monotone or convex cone
(
Bandeira et al., 2019
;
Wei et al., 2019
)
). In some of these cases, the natural denoising functions might not be separable, therefore while the decomposition in Theorem
1
still holds true, controlling those residual terms is significantly more complicated.

•

Secondly, our analysis is tailored to the spiked Wigner model where the noise takes the form of an independent Gaussian matrix.
It remains unclear whether our non-asymptotic characterizations can be generalized to accommodate non-Gaussian noise matrices
(
Bayati et al., 2015
;
Chen and Lam, 2021
;
Dudeja et al., 2022
)
.
Developing universality results in a non-asymptotic manner is an important yet highly challenging task worthy of future investigation.

•

Additionally, we have observed in our empirical simulations that: in many examples, AMP continues to work well even when
t
further increases (to a point that goes far beyond
n
).
This suggests that the dependence on
t
in our statistical bounds might fall short of tightness in general.
How to tighten the statistical performance guarantees for large
t
remains an interesting open question, which is left for future studies.

•

Finally, moving beyond spiked models,
we expect that our non-asymptotic framework can be generalized to accommodate other important settings such as sparse linear regression and generalized linear models (GLMs).
In fact, the update rule of AMP for regression and GLMs can often be viewed as AMP applied to
asymmetric
matrix models;
more specifically, given an asymmetric design matrix
X
, AMP for GLMs maintains two sequences of updates as follows:

\displaystyle s_{t}

\displaystyle=XF_{t}(\beta_{t})-\left\langle F_{t}^{\prime}\right\rangle G_{t-1}(s_{t-1}),

\displaystyle\beta_{t+1}

\displaystyle=X^{\top}G_{t}(s_{t})-\left\langle G_{t}^{\prime}\right\rangle F_{t}(\beta_{t}),

thus resembling the updating rule analyzed in the current paper.
To control these two sequences of updates, one can employ similar analysis ideas as the ones developed for Theorem
1
,
while in the meantime keeping track of two sets of orthogonal basis and two sequences of Gaussian random vectors.
Given that these two sequences rely heavily on each together, caution needs to be exercised when dealing with their accumulated errors.
Carrying out such analysis fully is fairly involved, and hence we will leave it for future investigation.

APPENDIX

Appendix A
Preliminaries: useful concentration results

This section gathers a few useful concentration results concerning functions of random vectors that will be applied multiple times throughout this paper.

A.1
List of concentration lemmas

The first result is concerned with Gaussian concentration for Lipschitz-continuous functions,
whose proof can be found in Section
A.2
.
Here and below, we remind the reader that
\mathbb{B}^{d}(r)
indicates the
d
-dimensional Euclidean ball with radius
r
centered at 0.

Lemma 6
.

Consider an
n
-dimensional Gaussian vector
X\sim\mathcal{N}(0,I_{n})
,
and a set of functions
f_{\theta}:\mathbb{R}^{n}\to\mathbb{R}
as parameterized by
\theta\in\Theta\subseteq\mathbb{B}^{d}(r)
.
Let
\mathcal{E}
be some convex set obeying
\mathbb{P}(X\in\mathcal{E})\geq 1-O(n^{-11})
.
Assume that for any fixed
\theta,\widetilde{\theta}\in\Theta
and any given
Z_{1},Z_{2}\in\mathcal{E}
, we have

\displaystyle|f_{\theta}(Z_{1})-f_{\theta}(Z_{2})|\leq\sigma\|Z_{1}-Z_{2}\|_{2}\qquad\text{and}\qquad\left\|f_{\theta}(Z)-f_{\widetilde{\theta}}(Z)\right\|_{2}\leq L\|\theta-\widetilde{\theta}\|_{2}.

(70)

In addition, suppose that for any fixed
\theta\in\Theta
, we have

\displaystyle\big|\mathbb{E}\left[f_{\theta}(\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{E}}(X))-f_{\theta}(X)\right]\big|\leq B,

(71)

where
\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{E}}(\cdot)
denotes the Euclidean projection onto the set
\mathcal{E}
.
Then for any
\epsilon<r
,

\displaystyle\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}\big|f_{\theta}(X)-\mathbb{E}\left[f_{\theta}(X)\right]\big|\lesssim\sigma\sqrt{d\log\left(\frac{nr}{\epsilon}\right)}+L\epsilon+B

(72)

holds with probability at least
1-O(n^{-11})
.

As an immediate consequence of Lemma
6
, we can take
\epsilon\asymp n^{-200}
to yield the following result:

Corollary 2
.

Under the assumptions of Lemma
6
, suppose the convex set
\mathcal{E}
obeys

\displaystyle\left\|f_{\theta}(Z)-f_{\widetilde{\theta}}(Z)\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim n^{100}\|\theta-\widetilde{\theta}\|_{2}\qquad\text{for all }Z\in\mathcal{E}\text{ and all }\theta,\widetilde{\theta}\in\Theta;

(73a)

\displaystyle\big|\mathbb{E}\left[f_{\theta}(\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{E}}(X))-f_{\theta}(X)\right]\big|

\displaystyle\lesssim n^{-100}.

(73b)

Then with probability at least
1-O(n^{-11})
one has

\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}\big|f_{\theta}(X)-\mathbb{E}\left[f_{\theta}(X)\right]\big|\lesssim\sigma\sqrt{d\log\left(nr\right)}+n^{-100}.

Next, we develop concentration results for a family of functions that include indicator functions.
Consider a set of independent random vectors
X_{1},\ldots,X_{m}\in\real^{n}
with
m\leq n
,
and for each
1\leq i\leq m
,
consider a collection of functions
f_{i,\theta},h_{i,\theta}:\mathbb{R}^{n}\to\mathbb{R}
indexed by
\theta\in\mathbb{B}^{d}(r)
.
The following concentration bound — whose proof is deferred to Section
A.3
— proves useful when establishing our main results.

Lemma 7
.

Suppose that for any given
\theta\in\Theta\subseteq\mathbb{B}^{d}(r)
,
the random variable
f_{i,\theta}(X_{i})\geq 0
is
\sigma_{i}
-subexponential.
Assume that there exist a set of events
\mathcal{E}_{i}
(
1\leq i\leq m\leq n)
obeying
\mathbb{P}(\bigcap_{i}\mathcal{E}_{i})>1-O(n^{-11})
such that:
for any
i\in[m]
and any
\theta,\widetilde{\theta}\in\Theta
,

\displaystyle\big\|f_{i,\theta}(Z_{i})-f_{i,\widetilde{\theta}}(Z_{i})\big\|_{2}+\big\|h_{i,\theta}(Z_{i})-h_{i,\widetilde{\theta}}(Z_{i})\big\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq L\big\|\theta-\widetilde{\theta}\big\|_{2}\qquad\text{for all }Z_{i}\text{ with }\ind_{\mathcal{E}_{i}}(Z_{i})=1,

(74a)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[f_{i,\theta}(X_{i})\ind\left(\mathcal{E}_{i}^{\mathrm{c}}\right)\big]

\displaystyle\leq B.

(74b)

Also, for any
i\in[m]
and any
\theta\in\Theta
, define

\varrho_{i,\theta}\coloneqq\mathbb{E}\big[f_{i,\theta}(X_{i})\big]+L\epsilon+\sigma_{i}\log n,\qquad 1\leq i\leq m.

(75)

Then for any
0<\epsilon<r
, with probability at least
1-O(n^{-11})
one has

\displaystyle\left|\sum_{i=1}^{m}\Big(f_{i,\theta}(X_{i})\ind\big(h_{i,\theta}(X_{i})>\tau\big)-\mathbb{E}\Big[f_{i,\theta}(X_{i})\ind\big(h_{i,\theta}(X_{i})>\tau\big)\Big]\Big)\right|

\displaystyle\qquad\lesssim\sqrt{\sum_{i=1}^{m}\varrho_{i,\theta}^{2}\Big(\mathbb{P}\left(h_{i,\theta}(X_{i})>\tau\right)+\frac{1}{n}\Big)d\log\frac{rn}{\epsilon}}+\Big(\max_{1\leq i\leq m}\varrho_{i,\theta}\Big)d\log\frac{rn}{\epsilon}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad+mL\epsilon+mB+\sum_{i=1}^{m}\varrho_{i,\theta}\mathbb{P}\Big(\tau-(3L+1)\epsilon\leq h_{i,\theta}(X_{i})\leq\tau+(3L+1)\epsilon\Big)

(76)

simultaneously for all
\theta\in\Theta
and all
\tau\in[-r,r]
.

Similar to Corollary
2
,
we can take
\epsilon=n^{-200}
to derive the following immediate consequence.

Corollary 3
.

Under the assumptions of Lemma
7
, suppose that

\displaystyle\big\|f_{i,\theta}(Z_{i})-f_{i,\widetilde{\theta}}(Z_{i})\big\|_{2}+\big\|h_{i,\theta}(Z_{i})-h_{i,\widetilde{\theta}}(Z_{i})\big\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq n^{100}\big\|\theta-\widetilde{\theta}\big\|_{2}\qquad\text{for all }Z_{i}\text{ with }\ind_{\mathcal{E}_{i}}(Z_{i})=1

(77a)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[f_{i,\theta}(X_{i})\ind\left(\mathcal{E}_{i}^{\mathrm{c}}\right)\big]

\displaystyle\leq n^{-100}

(77b)

for any
i\in[m]
and any
\theta,\widetilde{\theta}\in\Theta
. Also, suppose that

\mathbb{P}\Big(\tau-400n^{-100}\leq h_{i,\theta}(X_{i})\leq\tau+400n^{-100}\Big)\lesssim m^{-1},\qquad 1\leq i\leq m

for any
\theta\in\Theta
and any
\tau\in[-r,r]
.
If we redefine

\varrho_{i,\theta}\coloneqq\mathbb{E}\big[f_{i,\theta}(X_{i})\big]+\sigma_{i}\log n,\qquad 1\leq i\leq m,

(78)

then with probability at least
1-O(n^{-11})
one has

\displaystyle\left|\sum_{i=1}^{m}\Big(f_{i,\theta}(X_{i})\ind\big(h_{i,\theta}(X_{i})>\tau\big)-\mathbb{E}\Big[f_{i,\theta}(X_{i})\ind\big(h_{i,\theta}(X_{i})>\tau\big)\Big]\Big)\right|

\displaystyle\qquad\lesssim\sqrt{\sum_{i=1}^{m}\varrho_{i,\theta}^{2}\Big(\mathbb{P}\left(h_{i,\theta}(X_{i})>\tau\right)+\frac{1}{n}\Big)d\log(rn)}+\Big(\max_{1\leq i\leq m}\varrho_{i,\theta}\Big)d\log(rn)+\frac{n+d\log(rn)}{n^{100}}

simultaneously for all
\theta\in\Theta
and all
\tau\in[-r,r]
.

The third result is concerned with norms of (linear combinations of) independent Gaussian vectors;
the proof can be found in Section
A.4
.
Here and throughout, for every vector
x\in\real^{n}
, we adopt the convention and let
|x|_{(i)}
denote its
i
-th largest entry in magnitude.

Lemma 8
.

Consider a collection of independent Gaussian vectors
\{\phi_{k}\}_{1\leq k\leq n}
with
\phi_{k}\overset{\mathrm{i.i.d.}}{\sim}\mathcal{N}(0,\frac{1}{n}I_{n})
.
With probability at least
1-\delta
, it holds that

\displaystyle\Big|\max_{1\leq k\leq t-1}\|\phi_{k}\|_{2}-1\Big|

\displaystyle\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{\log\frac{n}{\delta}}{n}},

(79a)

\displaystyle\sup_{a=[a_{k}]_{1\leq k<t}\in\mathcal{S}^{t-2}}\bigg|\Big\|\sum_{k=1}^{t-1}a_{k}\phi_{k}\Big\|_{2}-1\bigg|

\displaystyle\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{t\log\frac{n}{\delta}}{n}},

(79b)

\displaystyle\sup_{a=[a_{k}]_{1\leq k<t}\in\mathcal{S}^{t-2}}\sum_{i=1}^{s}\Big|\sum_{k=1}^{t-1}a_{k}\phi_{k}\Big|_{(i)}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{(t+s)\log\frac{n}{\delta}}{n},\qquad\forall 1\leq s\leq n.

(79c)

Finally, we state a lemma that quantifies the 1-Wasserstein distance between a weighted combination of independent Gaussian vectors (with the weights being possibly dependent on the Gaussian vectors) and an i.i.d. Gaussian vector. The proof of this lemma can be found in Section
A.5
.

Lemma 9
.

Consider a set of i.i.d. random vectors
\phi_{k}\overset{\mathrm{i.i.d.}}{\sim}\mathcal{N}(0,\frac{1}{n}I_{n})
,
as well as any unit vector
\beta=[\beta_{i}]_{1\leq i\leq t}\in\mathcal{S}^{t-1}
that might be statistically dependent on
\{\phi_{k}\}
.
Then the 1-Wasserstein distance (cf. (
8
)) between the distribution of
\sum_{i=1}^{t}\beta_{k}\phi_{k}
— denoted by
\mu\big(\sum_{i=1}^{t}\beta_{k}\phi_{k}\big)
— and
\mathcal{N}\big(0,\frac{1}{n}I_{n}\big)
obeys

\displaystyle W_{1}\bigg(\mu\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{t}\beta_{k}\phi_{k}\Big),\mathcal{N}\Big(0,\frac{1}{n}I_{n}\Big)\bigg)\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{t\log n}{n}}.

(80)

A.2
Proof of Lemma
6

Let us define

\displaystyle g_{\theta}(X)\coloneqq f_{\theta}(\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{E}}(X)).

By Lipschitz property of
f_{\theta}
(cf. (
70
)), we can obtain the Lipschitz property for
g_{\theta}
as follows:

\displaystyle\left|g_{\theta}(X)-g_{\theta}(Y)\right|=\left|f_{\theta}(\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{E}}(X))-f_{\theta}(\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{E}}(Y))\right|\leq\sigma\|\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{E}}(X)-\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{E}}(Y)\|_{2}\leq\sigma\|X-Y\|_{2},

where the last step uses the non-expansiveness of Euclidean projection onto convex sets.
Gaussian isoperimetric inequalities (e.g.,
Massart, 2007
) then tells us that, for any fixed
\theta\in\Theta
,

\displaystyle\big|g_{\theta}(X)-\mathbb{E}\left[g_{\theta}(X)\right]\big|\leq\sigma\sqrt{2\log\frac{1}{\delta}}

(81)

holds with probability at least
1-\delta
.

Next, we need to establish uniform concentration over all
\theta\in\Theta
.
Towards this, let us construct an
\epsilon
-net
\mathcal{N}_{\epsilon}
for
\Theta
with smallest size such that:
for any
\theta\in\Theta
, there exists some
\widehat{\theta}\in\mathcal{N}_{\epsilon}
obeying
\|\theta-\widehat{\theta}\|_{2}\leq\epsilon
.
Given that
\Theta\subseteq\real^{d}
,
it is easily seen that the cardinality of the
\epsilon
-net can be chosen such that
|\mathcal{N}_{\epsilon}|\leq(\frac{2r}{\epsilon})^{d}

(
Vershynin, 2018
, Chapter 4.2)
.
Taking (
81
) with the union bound over the set
\mathcal{N}_{\epsilon}
reveals that: with probability at least
1-\delta
,

\displaystyle\sup_{\theta\in\mathcal{N}_{\epsilon}}\big|g_{\theta}(X)-\mathbb{E}\left[g_{\theta}(X)\right]\big|\leq\sigma\sqrt{2d\log\Big(\frac{2r}{\delta\epsilon}\Big)}.

(82)

With the above concentration result in place, we are ready prove the advertised inequality (
72
).
First, recalling that
\left|\mathbb{E}\left[g_{\theta}(X)-f_{\theta}(X)\right]\right|\leq B
and
f_{\theta}(X)=g_{\theta}(X)
with probability at least
1-O(n^{-11})
, one has

\displaystyle\big|f_{\theta}(X)-\mathbb{E}\left[f_{\theta}(X)\right]\big|

\displaystyle=\big|g_{\theta}(X)-\mathbb{E}\left[f_{\theta}(X)\right]\big|\leq\big|g_{\theta}(X)-\mathbb{E}\left[g_{\theta}(X)\right]\big|+\big|\mathbb{E}[f_{\theta}(X)]-\mathbb{E}[g_{\theta}(X)]\big|

\displaystyle\leq\big|g_{\theta}(X)-\mathbb{E}\left[g_{\theta}(X)\right]\big|+B

with probability at least
1-O(n^{-11})
.
In addition, our assumption (
70
) also indicates that

\displaystyle\big|g_{\theta_{1}}(X)-g_{\theta_{2}}(X)\big|=\big|f_{\theta_{1}}(\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{E}}(X))-f_{\theta_{2}}(\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{E}}(X))\big|\leq L\|\theta_{1}-\theta_{2}\|_{2},\qquad\text{and}

\displaystyle\big|\mathbb{E}[g_{\theta_{1}}(X)]-\mathbb{E}[g_{\theta_{2}}(X)]\big|

\displaystyle=\big|\mathbb{E}[f_{\theta_{1}}(\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{E}}(X))-f_{\theta_{2}}(\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{E}}(X))]\big|\leq\mathbb{E}\big[|f_{\theta_{1}}(\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{E}}(X))-f_{\theta_{2}}(\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{E}}(X))|\big]\leq L\|\theta_{1}-\theta_{2}\|_{2}.

Consequently, for every
\theta\in\Theta
, we obtain

\displaystyle\left|f_{\theta}(X)-\mathbb{E}\left[f_{\theta}(X)\right]\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left|g_{\widehat{\theta}}(X)-\mathbb{E}\left[g_{\widehat{\theta}}(X)\right]\right|+2L\epsilon+B

\displaystyle\lesssim\sigma\sqrt{d\log\left(\frac{nr}{\epsilon}\right)}+L\epsilon+B,

with probability exceeding
1-O(n^{-11})
,
where we invoke the Lipschitz property for
g_{\theta}
and
\mathbb{E}g_{\theta}
with respect to
\theta
,
and the last inequality follows from relation (
82
) with
\delta=n^{-11}
.
We have thus established Lemma
6
.

A.3
Proof of Lemma
7

Step 1: establishing concentration for any fixed
\theta
and
\tau
.

For notational simplicity, let us introduce

\displaystyle\mu_{i}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\mathbb{E}\big[f_{i,\theta}(X_{i})\ind(\mathcal{E}_{i})\big]

\displaystyle Z_{i}

\displaystyle\coloneqq f_{i,\theta}(X_{i})\ind\left(h_{i,\theta}(X_{i})>\tau\right)\ind(\mathcal{E}_{i})

for any
i\in[m]
, and any fixed
\theta\in\Theta
and
\tau\in[-r,r]
.
The goal of this step is to show the following Bernstein-type inequality: for any given
\theta\in\Theta
and
\tau\in[-r,r]
,

\displaystyle\Big|\sum_{i=1}^{m}Z_{i}-\mathbb{E}[Z_{i}]\Big|\lesssim\sqrt{\sum_{i=1}^{m}\left(\mu_{i}+\sigma_{i}\log n\right)^{2}\left(\mathbb{P}\big(h_{i,\theta}(X_{i})>\tau\big)+\frac{1}{n}\right)\log\frac{1}{\delta}}+\max_{i}\left(\mu_{i}+\sigma_{i}\log n\right)\log\frac{1}{\delta}

(83)

holds with probability at least
1-\delta
, where
\delta
can be any value in
(0,1)
.
The remainder of this step is devoted to establishing (
83
).

We find it useful to first single out several preliminary facts.
Recognizing that
f_{i,\theta}(X_{i})
is assumed to be non-negative, one has
\mu_{i}\leq\mathbb{E}\big[f_{i,\theta}(X_{i})\big]
.
Given that
f_{i,\theta}(X_{i})
is assumed to be
\sigma_{i}
-subexponential,
we see that
f_{i,\theta}(X_{i})\ind(\mathcal{E}_{i})
is also
\sigma_{i}
-subexponential,
which further implies that the centered version
f_{i,\theta}(X_{i})\ind(\mathcal{E}_{i})-\mu_{i}
is
O(\sigma_{i})
-subexponential
(see
Vershynin, 2018
);
this means that
there exists some universal constant
c_{5}\geq 1
such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Big(f_{i,\theta}(X_{i})\ind(\mathcal{E}_{i})\geq\mu_{i}+\tau\Big)\leq\mathbb{P}\left(\big|f_{i,\theta}(X_{i})\ind(\mathcal{E}_{i})-\mu_{i}\big|\geq\tau\right)\leq 2\exp\Big(-\frac{\tau}{c_{5}\sigma_{i}}\Big)

(84)

for any
\tau\geq 0
and any
i\in[m]
.
In what follows, we shall follow similar ideas for proving Bernstein’s inequality (e.g.,
Wainwright, 2019
).

For every integer
k\geq 1
,
let us first look at the
k
-moment of
Z_{i}-\mathbb{E}[Z_{i}]
.
Note that for any two non-negative numbers
a,b\geq 0
, one has
|(a-b)^{k}|\leq a^{k}+b^{k}
.
This fact taken together with Jensen’s inequality gives

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\big|Z_{i}-\mathbb{E}[Z_{i}]\big|^{k}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left[Z_{i}^{k}\right]+\big(\mathbb{E}[Z_{i}]\big)^{k}\leq 2\mathbb{E}\left[Z_{i}^{k}\right]

\displaystyle=2\mathbb{E}\left[\big(f_{i,\theta}(X_{i})\big)^{k}\ind\left(h_{i,\theta}(X_{i})>\tau\right)\ind(\mathcal{E}_{i})\right]

\displaystyle\leq 2\mathbb{E}\left[\big(f_{i,\theta}(X_{i})\big)^{k}\ind(\mathcal{E}_{i})\ind\left(h_{i,\theta}(X_{i})>\tau\right)\ind\big(f_{i,\theta}(X_{i})\leq\mu_{i}+c_{5}\sigma_{i}k\log n\big)\right]

\displaystyle\qquad+2\mathbb{E}\left[\big(f_{i,\theta}(X_{i})\big)^{k}\ind(\mathcal{E}_{i})\ind\big(f_{i,\theta}>\mu_{i}+c_{5}\sigma_{i}k\log n\big)\right]

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(\text{i})}}{{\leq}}2\left(\mu_{i}+c_{5}\sigma_{i}k\log n\right)^{k}\mathbb{E}\big[\ind\left(h_{i,\theta}(X_{i})>\tau\right)\big]+16(\mu_{i}+c_{5}\sigma_{i}k\log n)^{k}\exp(-k\log n)

\displaystyle\leq 16\left(\mu_{i}+c_{5}\sigma_{i}k\log n\right)^{k}\Big(\mathbb{P}\big(h_{i,\theta}(X_{i})>\tau\big)+\frac{1}{n}\Big).

(85)

To justify why (i) is valid, we note that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\big(f_{i,\theta}(X_{i})\big)^{k}\ind(\mathcal{E}_{i})\ind\big(f_{i,\theta}(X_{i})>\mu_{i}+c_{5}\sigma_{i}k\log n\big)\right]

\displaystyle\leq\int_{(\mu_{i}+c_{5}\sigma_{i}k\log n)^{k}}^{\infty}\mathbb{P}\left\{\big(f_{i,\theta}(X_{i})\ind(\mathcal{E}_{i})\big)^{k}\geq\tau\right\}\mathrm{d}\tau

\displaystyle=\int_{(\mu_{i}+c_{5}\sigma_{i}k\log n)^{k}}^{\infty}\mathbb{P}\left\{f_{i,\theta}(X_{i})\ind(\mathcal{E}_{i})\geq\tau^{1/k}\right\}\mathrm{d}\tau

\displaystyle=\int_{\mu_{i}+c_{5}\sigma_{i}k\log n}^{\infty}kx^{k-1}\mathbb{P}\Big\{f_{i,\theta}(X_{i})\ind(\mathcal{E}_{i})\geq x\Big\}\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle=\int_{c_{5}\sigma_{i}k\log n}^{\infty}k(x+\mu_{i})^{k-1}\mathbb{P}\Big\{f_{i,\theta}(X_{i})\ind(\mathcal{E}_{i})\geq x+\mu_{i}\Big\}\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\overset{(\mathrm{ii})}{\leq}2k\int_{c_{5}\sigma_{i}k\log n}^{\infty}(x+\mu_{i})^{k-1}\exp\Big(-\frac{x}{c_{5}\sigma_{i}}\Big)\mathrm{d}x,

(86)

where (ii) follows from inequality (
84
). Now the right-hand side of the above inequality can be further controlled as

(
86
)

\displaystyle=2c_{5}\sigma_{i}k\int_{k\log n}^{\infty}(c_{5}\sigma_{i}x+\mu_{i})^{k-1}\exp(-x)\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\overset{(\mathrm{iii})}{\leq}2c_{5}\sigma_{i}k\sum_{l=k\log n}^{\infty}(c_{5}\sigma_{i}l+\mu_{i})^{k-1}\exp(-l)

\displaystyle\overset{(\mathrm{iv})}{\leq}2c_{5}\sigma_{i}k\cdot(c_{5}\sigma_{i}k\log n+\mu_{i})^{k-1}\exp(-k\log n)\sum_{i=0}^{\infty}\Big(\frac{2}{e}\Big)^{i}

\displaystyle\leq 8(c_{5}\sigma_{i}k\log n+\mu_{i})^{k}\exp(-k\log n),

(87)

where (iii) is valid since the function
(ax+b)^{k}e^{-x}
with
a,b>0
is decreasing
in
x
for any
x>k
, and (iv) holds since for any
l\geq k\log n
,
one has

\frac{\big((l+1)c_{5}\sigma_{i}+\mu_{i}\big)^{k}\exp(-(l+1))}{\big(lc_{5}\sigma_{i}+\mu_{i}\big)^{k}\exp(-l)}\leq e^{-1}\Big(1+\frac{1}{l}\Big)^{k}\leq\frac{2}{e},

namely,
\big(lc_{5}\sigma_{i}+\mu_{i}\big)^{k}\exp(-l)
decreases geometrically in
l
with a contraction factor
2/e
.
This validates Step (i) in (
85
).

In view of (
85
), letting
\widetilde{Z}_{i}\coloneqq Z_{i}-\mathbb{E}[Z_{i}]
(so that
\mathbb{E}[\widetilde{Z}_{i}]=0
) and using the power series expansion, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\frac{\lambda^{k}\widetilde{Z}_{i}^{k}}{k!}\right]

\displaystyle=1+\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{k=2}^{\infty}\frac{\lambda^{k}\widetilde{Z}_{i}^{k}}{k!}\right]\leq\exp\left(\sum_{k=2}^{\infty}\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[\lambda^{k}\widetilde{Z}_{i}^{k}\right]}{k!}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\exp\left(\sum_{k=2}^{\infty}\frac{8\lambda^{k}\left(\mu_{i}+c_{5}\sigma_{i}k\log n\right)^{k}}{k!}\left(\mathbb{P}\big(h_{i,\theta}(X_{i})>\tau\big)+\frac{1}{n}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\leq\exp\left(16e^{2}c_{5}^{2}\lambda^{2}\left(\mu_{i}+\sigma_{i}\log n\right)^{2}\left(\mathbb{P}\big(h_{i,\theta}(X_{i})>\tau\big)+\frac{1}{n}\right)\right)

for any
\lambda>0
obeying
c_{5}\lambda(\mu_{i}+\sigma_{i}\log n)\leq(2e)^{-1}
,
where the first line applies the elementary inequality
1+x\leq\exp(x)
for any
x\in\real
,
and the last line holds since, by taking
z=c_{5}\lambda(\mu_{i}+\sigma_{i}\log n)\leq(2e)^{-1}
, one has

\sum_{k=2}^{\infty}\frac{\big[\lambda(\mu_{i}+c_{5}\sigma_{i}k\log n)\big]^{k}}{k!}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(\text{v})}}{{\leq}}\sum_{k=2}^{\infty}\frac{\big[\lambda c_{5}(\mu_{i}+\sigma_{i}\log n)k\big]^{k}}{\sqrt{2\pi k}\,k^{k}e^{-k}}\leq\sum_{k=2}^{\infty}(ez)^{k}\leq e^{2}z^{2}\sum_{i=0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{2^{i}}=2e^{2}z^{2},

where (v) follows from the fact
c_{5}\geq 1
and the well-known Stirling inequality
\sqrt{2\pi}k^{k+\frac{1}{2}}e^{-k}\leq k!
.
Given the above convergence of the power series, we conclude that for any
\lambda>0
obeying
c_{5}\lambda(\mu_{i}+\sigma_{i}\log n)\leq(2e)^{-1}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[\exp\big(\lambda\widetilde{Z}_{i}\big)\big]=\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\frac{\lambda^{k}\widetilde{Z}_{i}^{k}}{k!}\right]\leq\exp\left(16e^{2}c_{5}^{2}\lambda^{2}\left(\mu_{i}+\sigma_{i}\log n\right)^{2}\left(\mathbb{P}\big(h_{i,\theta}(X_{i})>\tau\big)+\frac{1}{n}\right)\right)\eqqcolon\exp(\lambda^{2}\nu_{0}^{2}).

(88)

To finish up, letting
L_{0}=\max_{1\leq i\leq n}2ec_{5}(\mu_{i}+\sigma_{i}\log n)
, we can apply Markov’s inequality to obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigg(\sum_{i=1}^{m}\widetilde{Z}_{i}>\tau\bigg)

\displaystyle\leq\min_{0<\lambda<\frac{1}{L_{0}}}\Bigg\{\exp(-\lambda\tau)\cdot\mathbb{E}\Big[\exp\Big(\lambda\sum_{i=1}^{m}\widetilde{Z}_{i}\Big)\Big]\Bigg\}\leq\min_{0<\lambda<\frac{1}{L_{0}}}\Big\{\exp(-\lambda\tau)\exp\big(\lambda^{2}\nu_{0}^{2}\big)\Big\}.

Repeating standard arguments for establishing Bernstein’s inequality (see, e.g.,
Vershynin, 2018
),
one can immediately conclude that

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{m}\widetilde{Z}_{i}

\displaystyle\lesssim\max\bigg\{\sqrt{\nu_{0}\log\frac{1}{\delta}}+L_{0}\log\frac{1}{\delta}\bigg\}

\displaystyle\asymp\sqrt{\sum_{i=1}^{m}\left(\mu_{i}+\sigma_{i}\log n\right)^{2}\left(\mathbb{P}\left(h_{i,\theta}(X_{i})>\tau\right)+\frac{1}{n}\right)\log\frac{1}{\delta}}+\max_{i}\big\{\mu_{i}+\sigma_{i}\log n\big\}\log\frac{1}{\delta}

with probability exceeding
1-\delta
.
Repeating the same argument reveals that the above inequality continues to hold if
\sum_{i=1}^{m}\widetilde{Z}_{i}
is replaced with

-\sum_{i=1}^{m}\widetilde{Z}_{i}
.
This in turn establishes (
83
) any fixed
\theta\in\Theta
and
\tau\in[-r,r]
.

Step 2: controlling the difference between the
\epsilon
-net and the remaining parameters.

To show uniform concentration over all
\theta
and
\tau
, we intend to invoke an
\epsilon
-net-based argument.
Towards this, let us
construct an
\epsilon
-net
\mathcal{N}_{\epsilon}^{\Theta}\subseteq\Theta\subseteq\mathbb{B}^{d}(r)
for the
d
-dimensional ball
\mathbb{B}^{d}(r)
of radius
r

— which can be chosen to have cardinality
|\mathcal{N}_{\epsilon}^{\Theta}|\leq(\frac{3r}{\epsilon})^{d}

(
Vershynin, 2018
, Chapter 4.2)
—
such that for any
\theta\in\Theta
, there exists some
\widehat{\theta}\in\mathcal{N}_{\epsilon}^{\Theta}
satisfying
\|\widehat{\theta}-\theta\|_{2}\leq\epsilon<r
.
In addition, we construct another
\epsilon
-net
\mathcal{N}_{\epsilon}^{[-r,r]}\subseteq[-r,r]

obeying
|\mathcal{N}_{\epsilon}^{[-r,r]}|\leq\frac{2r}{\epsilon}

for the interval
[-r,r]
,
such that for any
\tau\in[-r,r]
, there exists
\widetilde{\tau}\in\mathcal{N}_{\epsilon}^{[-r,r]}

obeying
|\tau-\widetilde{\tau}|\leq\epsilon
.

Let us now look at an arbitrary

\theta\in\Theta
and its nearest neighbor
\widehat{\theta}
in
\mathcal{N}_{\epsilon}^{\Theta}
(so that
\|\widehat{\theta}-\theta\|_{2}\leq\epsilon
).
In view of the Lipschitz property (
74
), we can deduce that

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{m}f_{i,\theta}(X_{i})\ind\big(h_{i,\theta}(X_{i})>\tau\big)\ind(\mathcal{E}_{i})

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{m}f_{i,\theta}(X_{i})\ind\left(h_{i,\widehat{\theta}}(X_{i})>\tau+h_{i,\widehat{\theta}}(X_{i})-h_{i,\theta}(X_{i})\right)\ind\left(\mathcal{E}_{i}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i=1}^{m}f_{i,\widehat{\theta}}(X_{i})\ind\left(h_{i,\widehat{\theta}}(X_{i})>\tau+h_{i,\widehat{\theta}}(X_{i})-h_{i,\theta}(X_{i})\right)\ind\left(\mathcal{E}_{i}\right)+O(mL\epsilon)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i=1}^{m}f_{i,\widehat{\theta}}(X_{i})\ind\left(h_{i,\widehat{\theta}}(X_{i})>\widehat{\tau}_{-}\right)\ind(\mathcal{E}_{i})+O(mL\epsilon)

(89)

for some point
\widehat{\tau}_{-}\in\mathcal{N}_{\epsilon}^{[-r,r]}
satisfying

\displaystyle\tau-(L+1)\epsilon\leq\widehat{\tau}_{-}\leq\tau+h_{i,\widehat{\theta}}(X_{i})-h_{i,\theta}(X_{i}).

Here, the second line in (
89
) applies the Lipschitz continuity of
f_{i,\theta}
w.r.t.
\theta
,
while the last line
relies on the Lipschitz condition that
|h_{i,\widehat{\theta}}(X_{i})-h_{i,\theta}(X_{i})|\leq L\epsilon
.
Similarly, we have the following lower bound:

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{m}f_{i,\theta}(X_{i})\ind\big(h_{i,\theta}(X_{i})>\tau\big)\ind\left(\mathcal{E}_{i}\right)\geq\sum_{i=1}^{m}f_{i,\widehat{\theta}}(X_{i})\ind\left(h_{i,\widehat{\theta}}(X_{i})>\widehat{\tau}_{+}\right)\ind(\mathcal{E}_{i})-O(mL\epsilon)

(90)

for some point
\widehat{\tau}_{+}\in\mathcal{N}_{\epsilon}^{[-r,r]}
obeying

\displaystyle\tau+h_{i,\widehat{\theta}}(X_{i})-h_{i,\theta}(X_{i})\leq\widehat{\tau}_{+}\leq\tau+(L+1)\epsilon.

Next, we turn attention to the mean term
\mathbb{E}\big[f_{i,\theta}(X_{i})\ind\left(h_{i,\theta}(X_{i})>\tau\right)\big].

Consider
\widehat{\tau}\in\mathcal{N}_{\epsilon}^{[-r,r]}
such that

\displaystyle|\widehat{\tau}-\tau|\leq(L+1)\epsilon.

Clearly, there might be more than one points in the
\epsilon
-net that are within distance

(L+1)\epsilon
to
\tau
, and we shall specify the choice of
\widehat{\tau}
momentarily.
Recall the assumption
\mathbb{E}\big[f_{i,\theta}(X_{i})\ind\left(\mathcal{E}_{i}^{\mathrm{c}}\right)\big]\leq B
(cf. (
74
))
and the non-negativity of
f_{i,\theta}
to arrive at

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[f_{i,\theta}(X_{i})\ind\left(h_{i,\theta}(X_{i})>\tau\right)\big]\leq\mathbb{E}\big[f_{i,\theta}(X_{i})\ind\left(h_{i,\theta}(X_{i})>\tau\right)\ind\left(\mathcal{E}_{i}\right)\big]+O(B)

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\mathbb{E}\left[f_{i,\theta}(X_{i})\ind\left(h_{i,\theta}(X_{i})>h_{i,\widehat{\theta}}(X_{i})-h_{i,\theta}(X_{i})+\tau\right)\ind\left(\mathcal{E}_{i}\right)\right]+O\left(B\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\mathbb{E}\left[f_{i,\widehat{\theta}}(X_{i})\ind\left(h_{i,\widehat{\theta}}(X_{i})>h_{i,\widehat{\theta}}(X_{i})-h_{i,\theta}(X_{i})+\tau\right)\ind\left(\mathcal{E}_{i}\right)\right]+O\left(L\epsilon+B\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\mathbb{E}\left[f_{i,\widehat{\theta}}(X_{i})\ind\left(h_{i,\widehat{\theta}}(X_{i})>\widehat{\tau}\right)\ind\left(\mathcal{E}_{i}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\qquad\quad+O\left(\mathbb{E}\left[f_{i,\widehat{\theta}}(X_{i})\ind\left(\tau-(L+1)\epsilon\leq h_{i,\widehat{\theta}}(X_{i})\leq\tau+(L+1)\epsilon)\right)\ind\left(\mathcal{E}_{i}\right)\right]+L\epsilon+B\right).

(91)

Here, the second inequality follows from the Lipschitz continuity of
f_{i,\theta}
w.r.t.
\theta
(cf. (
74
)), and
the last inequality holds since

\displaystyle\left\{Z_{i}:\ind\left(h_{i,\widehat{\theta}}(Z_{i})>h_{i,\widehat{\theta}}(Z_{i})-h_{i,\theta}(Z_{i})+\tau\right)\neq\ind\left(h_{i,\widehat{\theta}}(Z_{i})>\widehat{\tau}\right)\right\}

\displaystyle\subseteq\Big\{Z_{i}:\widehat{\tau}\leq h_{i,\widehat{\theta}}(Z_{i})\leq h_{i,\widehat{\theta}}(Z_{i})-h_{i,\theta}(Z_{i})+\tau\Big\}\cup\Big\{Z_{i}:h_{i,\widehat{\theta}}(Z_{i})-h_{i,\theta}(Z_{i})+\tau\leq h_{i,\widehat{\theta}}(Z_{i})\leq\widehat{\tau}\Big\}

\displaystyle\subseteq\Big\{Z_{i}:\tau-(L+1)\epsilon\leq h_{i,\widehat{\theta}}(Z_{i})\leq\tau+(L+1)\epsilon\Big\}.

(92)

where we invoke again
|h_{i,\widehat{\theta}}(Z_{i})-h_{i,\theta}(Z_{i})|\leq L\epsilon
and

|\widehat{\tau}-\tau|\leq(L+1)\epsilon
.
Let us augment the notation
\mu_{i}
to make explicit the dependency on
\theta_{i}
as follows

\mu_{i,\theta}\coloneqq\mathbb{E}\big[f_{i,\theta}(X_{i})\ind(\mathcal{E}_{i})\big].

(93)

Then an application of the bound (
87
) with
k=1
leads directly to

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[f_{i,\widehat{\theta}}(X_{i})\ind\left(\tau-(2L+1)\epsilon\leq h_{i,\widehat{\theta}}(X_{i})\leq\tau+(2L+1)\epsilon)\right)\ind\left(\mathcal{E}_{i}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\qquad=\mathbb{E}\left[f_{i,\widehat{\theta}}(X_{i})\ind\left(\tau-(2L+1)\epsilon\leq h_{i,\widehat{\theta}}(X_{i})\leq\tau+(2L+1)\epsilon)\right)\ind\left(\mathcal{E}_{i}\right)\ind\left(f_{i,\widehat{\theta}}(X_{i})\leq\mu_{i,\widehat{\theta}}+c_{5}\sigma_{i}\log n\right)\right]

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\mathbb{E}\left[f_{i,\widehat{\theta}}(X_{i})\ind\left(\tau-(2L+1)\epsilon\leq h_{i,\widehat{\theta}}(X_{i})\leq\tau+(2L+1)\epsilon)\right)\ind\left(\mathcal{E}_{i}\right)\ind\left(f_{i,\widehat{\theta}}(X_{i})>\mu_{i,\widehat{\theta}}+c_{5}\sigma_{i}\log n\right)\right]

\displaystyle\qquad\lesssim(\mu_{i,\widehat{\theta}}+\sigma_{i}\log n)\mathbb{P}\Big(\tau-(2L+1)\epsilon\leq h_{i,\widehat{\theta}}(X_{i})\leq\tau+(2L+1)\epsilon)\Big)+\frac{\mu_{i,\widehat{\theta}}+\sigma_{i}\log n}{n}.

Substituting it into (
91
) yields

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[f_{i,\theta}(X_{i})\ind\big(h_{i,\theta}(X_{i})>\tau\big)\Big]-\mathbb{E}\left[f_{i,\widehat{\theta}}(X_{i})\ind\big(h_{i,\widehat{\theta}}(X_{i})>\widehat{\tau}\big)\ind\left(\mathcal{E}_{i}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\qquad\lesssim(\mu_{i,\widehat{\theta}}+\sigma_{i}\log n)\mathbb{P}\Big(\tau-(2L+1)\epsilon\leq h_{i,\widehat{\theta}}(X_{i})\leq\tau+(2L+1)\epsilon\Big)+\frac{\mu_{i,\widehat{\theta}}+\sigma_{i}\log n}{n}+L\epsilon+B.

(94)

Clearly, repeating the above argument shows that (
94
) continues to hold if the left-hand side of (
94
) is replaced by

\mathbb{E}\big[f_{i,\widehat{\theta}}(X_{i})\ind\big(h_{i,\widehat{\theta}}(X_{i})>\widehat{\tau}\big)\ind\left(\mathcal{E}_{i}\right)\big]-\mathbb{E}\big[f_{i,\theta}(X_{i})\ind\big(h_{i,\theta}(X_{i})>\tau\big)\big]
. As a result, we conclude that

\displaystyle\Big|\mathbb{E}\Big[f_{i,\theta}(X_{i})\ind\big(h_{i,\theta}(X_{i})>\tau\big)\Big]-\mathbb{E}\left[f_{i,\widehat{\theta}}(X_{i})\ind\big(h_{i,\widehat{\theta}}(X_{i})>\widehat{\tau}\big)\ind\left(\mathcal{E}_{i}\right)\right]\Big|

\displaystyle\qquad\lesssim(\mu_{i,\widehat{\theta}}+\sigma_{i}\log n)\mathbb{P}\Big(\tau-(2L+1)\epsilon\leq h_{i,\widehat{\theta}}(X_{i})\leq\tau+(2L+1)\epsilon\Big)+\frac{\mu_{i,\widehat{\theta}}+\sigma_{i}\log n}{n}+L\epsilon+B.

(95)

Step 3: establishing uniform convergence.

We are now ready to establish the advertised concentration result (
76
).
Recall that the concentration result (
83
) holds for every fixed
(\widehat{\theta},\widehat{\tau})
pair.
By taking the union bound over all points in
\mathcal{N}_{\epsilon}^{\Theta}\times\mathcal{N}_{\epsilon}^{[-r,r]}

and setting
\delta=n^{-11}(\frac{\epsilon}{3r})^{d+1}
,
we can see that with probability at least
1-\delta(\frac{3r}{\epsilon})^{d+1}=1-O(n^{-11})
,

\displaystyle\bigg|\sum_{i=1}^{m}f_{i,\widehat{\theta}}(X_{i})\ind\left(h_{i,\widehat{\theta}}(X_{i})>\widehat{\tau}\right)\ind(\mathcal{E}_{i})-\mathbb{E}\Big[f_{i,\widehat{\theta}}(X_{i})\ind\left(h_{i,\widehat{\theta}}(X_{i})>\widehat{\tau}\right)\ind(\mathcal{E}_{i})\Big]\bigg|

\displaystyle\qquad\lesssim\sqrt{\sum_{i=1}^{m}\big(\mu_{i,\widehat{\theta}}+\sigma_{i}\log n\big)^{2}\left(\mathbb{P}\big(h_{i,\widehat{\theta}}(X_{i})>\widehat{\tau}\big)+\frac{1}{n}\right)d\log\frac{nr}{\epsilon}}+\max_{i}\big(\mu_{i,\widehat{\theta}}+\sigma_{i}\log n\big)d\log\frac{nr}{\epsilon}

(96)

holds simultaneously for all
(\widehat{\theta},\widehat{\tau})\in\mathcal{N}_{\epsilon}^{\Theta}\times\mathcal{N}_{\epsilon}^{[-r,r]}
.

Consider an arbitrary point
\theta\in\Theta
and
\tau\in[-r,r]
;
let
\widehat{\theta}
be its closest point in
\mathcal{N}_{\epsilon}^{\theta}
,
and take
\widehat{\tau}
to be either
\widehat{\tau}_{-}
or
\widehat{\tau}_{+}
.
Combining (
89
), (
90
) and (
95
)
and using the assumption
\mathbb{P}(\cap_{i}\mathcal{E}_{i})\geq 1-O(n^{-11})
lead to: with probability at least
1-O(n^{-11})
,

\displaystyle\left|\sum_{i=1}^{m}\Big(f_{i,\theta}(X_{i})\ind\left(h_{i,\theta}(X_{i})>\tau\right)-\mathbb{E}\big[f_{i,\theta}(X_{i})\ind\big(h_{i,\theta}(X_{i})>\tau\big)\big]\Big)\right|

\displaystyle\qquad=\left|\sum_{i=1}^{m}\Big(f_{i,\theta}(X_{i})\ind\left(h_{i,\theta}(X_{i})>\tau\right)\ind(\mathcal{E}_{i})-\mathbb{E}\big[f_{i,\theta}(X_{i})\ind\big(h_{i,\theta}(X_{i})>\tau\big)\big]\Big)\right|

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\left|\sum_{i=1}^{m}\left(f_{i,\widehat{\theta}}(X_{i})\ind\big(h_{i,\widehat{\theta}}(X_{i})>\widehat{\tau}\big)\ind(\mathcal{E}_{i})-\mathbb{E}\left[f_{i,\widehat{\theta}}(X_{i})\ind\big(h_{i,\widehat{\theta}}(X_{i})>\widehat{\tau}\big)\ind\left(\mathcal{E}_{i}\right)\right]\right)\right|

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+O\bigg(mL\epsilon+mB+\sum_{i}\big(\mu_{i,\widehat{\theta}}+\sigma_{i}\log n\big)\Big[\mathbb{P}\Big(\tau-(2L+1)\epsilon\leq h_{i,\widehat{\theta}}(X_{i})\leq\tau+(2L+1)\epsilon\Big)+\frac{1}{n}\Big]\bigg)

\displaystyle\qquad\lesssim\sqrt{\sum_{i=1}^{m}\big(\mu_{i,\widehat{\theta}}+\sigma_{i}\log n\big)^{2}\Big(\mathbb{P}\left(h_{i,\widehat{\theta}}(X_{i})>\widehat{\tau}\right)+\frac{1}{n}\Big)d\log\frac{rn}{\epsilon}}+\max_{i}\big(\mu_{i,\widehat{\theta}}+\sigma_{i}\log n\big)d\log\frac{rn}{\epsilon}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+mL\epsilon+mB+\sum_{i}\big(\mu_{i,\widehat{\theta}}+\sigma_{i}\log n\big)\underbrace{\mathbb{P}\Big(\tau-(2L+1)\epsilon\leq h_{i,\widehat{\theta}}(X_{i})\leq\tau+(2L+1)\epsilon\Big)}_{\eqqcolon\,\widehat{p}_{i}},

where the last inequality follows from (
A.3
).
Additionally, recognizing that
|h_{i,\widehat{\theta}}(X_{i})-h_{i,\theta}(X_{i})|\leq L\epsilon
(see (
74
)),
we see that

\widehat{p}_{i}\leq\mathbb{P}\Big(\tau-(3L+1)\epsilon\leq h_{i,\theta}(X_{i})\leq\tau+(3L+1)\epsilon\Big)\eqqcolon p_{i}.

Finally, we remind the readers of the set relation (
92
). Repeating the argument in (
91
), we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(h_{i,\widehat{\theta}}(X_{i})>\widehat{\tau}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\big(h_{i,\theta}(X_{i})>\tau\big)+\mathbb{P}\Big(\tau-(2L+1)\epsilon\leq h_{i,\theta}(X_{i})\leq\tau+(2L+1)\epsilon\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\big(h_{i,\theta}(X_{i})>\tau\big)+p_{i}.

We also make note of the following elementary relation

\displaystyle\sqrt{\sum_{i=1}^{m}\big(\mu_{i,\widehat{\theta}}+\sigma_{i}\log n\big)^{2}p_{i}d\log\frac{rn}{\epsilon}}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\left\{\max_{i}\big(\mu_{i,\widehat{\theta}}+\sigma_{i}\log n\big)d\log\frac{rn}{\epsilon}\right\}\sum_{i=1}^{m}\big(\mu_{i,\widehat{\theta}}+\sigma_{i}\log n\big)p_{i}}

\displaystyle\leq\max_{i}\big(\mu_{i,\widehat{\theta}}+\sigma_{i}\log n\big)d\log\frac{rn}{\epsilon}+\sum_{i}\big(\mu_{i,\widehat{\theta}}+\sigma_{i}\log n\big)p_{i}.

Putting the above pieces together then yields

\displaystyle\left|\sum_{i=1}^{m}\Big(f_{i,\theta}(X_{i})\ind\left(h_{i,\theta}(X_{i})>\tau\right)-\mathbb{E}\big[f_{i,\theta}(X_{i})\ind\big(h_{i,\theta}(X_{i})>\tau\big)\big]\Big)\right|

\displaystyle\qquad\lesssim\sqrt{\sum_{i=1}^{m}\left(\mu_{i,\widehat{\theta}}+\sigma_{i}\log n\right)^{2}\Big(\mathbb{P}\left(h_{i,\theta}(X_{i})>\tau\right)+p_{i}+\frac{1}{n}\Big)d\log\frac{rn}{\epsilon}}+\max_{i}\big(\mu_{i,\widehat{\theta}}+\sigma_{i}\log n\big)d\log\frac{rn}{\epsilon}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad+mL\epsilon+mB+\sum_{i}(\mu_{i,\widehat{\theta}}+\sigma_{i}\log n)p_{i}

\displaystyle\qquad\lesssim\sqrt{\sum_{i=1}^{m}\left(\mu_{i,\widehat{\theta}}+\sigma_{i}\log n\right)^{2}\Big(\mathbb{P}\left(h_{i,\theta}(X_{i})>\tau\right)+\frac{1}{n}\Big)d\log\frac{rn}{\epsilon}}+\max\big(\mu_{i,\widehat{\theta}}+\sigma_{i}\log n\big)d\log\frac{rn}{\epsilon}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad+mL\epsilon+mB+\sum_{i}(\mu_{i,\widehat{\theta}}+\sigma_{i}\log n)p_{i}.

(97)

Finally, recall that the Lipschitz continuity of
f_{i,\theta}
w.r.t.
\theta
gives

\mu_{i,\widehat{\theta}}=\mathbb{E}\big[f_{i,\widehat{\theta}}(X_{i})\ind(\mathcal{E}_{i})\big]\leq\mathbb{E}\big[f_{i,\theta}(X_{i})\ind(\mathcal{E}_{i})\big]+L\epsilon\leq\mathbb{E}\big[f_{i,\theta}(X_{i})\big]+L\epsilon.

Substitution into (
97
) thus completes the proof.

A.4
Proof of Lemma
8

For a set of random vectors
\{\phi_{k}\}_{k=1}^{t-1}
independently drawn from
\mathcal{N}(0,\frac{1}{n}I_{n})
,
standard concentration results for Wishart matrices (e.g.,
Wainwright, 2019
) together with the union bound tell us that

\displaystyle\left\|(\phi_{1},\ldots,\phi_{t-1})^{\top}(\phi_{1},\ldots,\phi_{t-1})-I_{t-1}\right\|\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{t\log\frac{n}{\delta}}{n}},\qquad\text{for any }1<t\leq n

(98)

with probability at least
1-\delta
.
Two immediate consequences of (
98
) are in order.

•

First, taking
t=2
in (
98
) reveals that with probability at least
1-\delta
,

\Big|\|\phi_{1}\|_{2}^{2}-1\Big|\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{\log\frac{n}{\delta}}{n}}\text{$\qquad$}\Longrightarrow\qquad\Big|\|\phi_{1}\|_{2}-1\Big|=\frac{\big|\|\phi_{1}\|_{2}^{2}-1\big|}{\big|\|\phi_{1}\|_{2}+1\big|}\leq\Big|\|\phi_{1}\|_{2}^{2}-1\Big|\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{\log\frac{n}{\delta}}{n}}.

Clearly, this inequality holds if
\phi_{1}
is replaced by any other
\phi_{k}
. Taking the union bound over all
1\leq k\leq n
establishes inequality (
79a
).

•

As another direct consequence of (
98
), we have, with probability at least
1-\delta
,

\displaystyle\sup_{a=[a_{k}]_{1\leq k<t}\in\mathcal{S}^{t-2}}\bigg|\Big\|\sum_{k=1}^{t-1}a_{k}\phi_{k}\Big\|_{2}^{2}-1\bigg|\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{t\log\frac{n}{\delta}}{n}},

which allows us to establish the claim (
79b
) as follows:

\displaystyle\sup_{a=[a_{k}]_{1\leq k<t}\in\mathcal{S}^{t-2}}\bigg|\Big\|\sum_{k=1}^{t-1}a_{k}\phi_{k}\Big\|_{2}-1\bigg|=\sup_{a=[a_{k}]_{1\leq k<t}\in\mathcal{S}^{t-2}}\frac{\Big|\big\|\sum_{k=1}^{t-1}a_{k}\phi_{k}\big\|_{2}^{2}-1\Big|}{\big\|\sum_{k=1}^{t-1}a_{k}\phi_{k}\big\|_{2}+1}\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{t\log\frac{n}{\delta}}{n}}.

(99)

Next, we turn attention to the claim (
79c
).
Here and throughout, for any vector
x\in\real^{n}
and any index set
S\subseteq[n]
,
we let
x_{S}
denote the subvector of
x
formed by the entries of
x
at indices from
S
.
Following the discretization argument
(
Wainwright, 2019
, Chapter 5)
, we can construct an
\epsilon
-net
\mathcal{N}_{\epsilon}
on
\mathcal{S}^{t-2}
— which can be chosen such that its cardinality does not exceed
(3/\epsilon)^{t}

(
Vershynin, 2018
, Eq. (4.10))
— such that for any
a\in\mathcal{S}^{t-2}
, one can find a point
\widetilde{a}\in\mathcal{N}_{\epsilon}
such that
\|a-\widetilde{a}\|_{2}\leq\epsilon<1
.

•

We first bound the supermum over
\mathcal{N}_{\epsilon}
.
Note that for any fixed
a\in\mathcal{N}_{\epsilon}\subseteq\mathcal{S}^{t-2}
and any subset
S\subseteq[n]
with
|S|=s
,
the vector
(\sum_{k=1}^{t-1}a_{k}\phi_{k})_{S}
is a Gaussian vector drawn from
\mathcal{N}(0,\frac{1}{n}I_{s})
.
Applying
Hsu et al., 2012
then implies that

\mathbb{P}\left\{\bigg\|\Big(\sum_{k=1}^{t-1}a_{k}\phi_{k}\Big)_{S}\bigg\|_{2}>\frac{2}{\sqrt{n}}(\sqrt{s}+\sqrt{\tau})\right\}\leq\mathbb{P}\left\{\bigg\|\Big(\sum_{k=1}^{t-1}a_{k}\phi_{k}\Big)_{S}\bigg\|_{2}^{2}>\frac{s}{n}+\frac{2\sqrt{s\tau}}{n}+\frac{2\tau}{n}\right\}\leq e^{-\tau}

for any
\tau>0
.
Setting
\tau=\log\big(\frac{1}{\delta}(\frac{3}{\epsilon})^{t}{n\choose s}\big)
and combining this inequality with the union bound over all
a\in\mathcal{N}_{\epsilon}
and all
S\subseteq[n]
with
|S|=s
lead to

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Bigg\{\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}a\in\mathcal{N}_{\epsilon}\\
S\subset[n],|S|=s\end{subarray}}\bigg\|\Big(\sum_{k=1}^{t-1}a_{k}\phi_{k}\Big)_{S}\bigg\|_{2}>\frac{2\sqrt{s}}{\sqrt{n}}+\frac{2}{\sqrt{n}}\sqrt{\log\left(\frac{1}{\delta}\Big(\frac{3}{\epsilon}\Big)^{t}{n\choose s}\right)}\Bigg\}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}a\in\mathcal{N}_{\epsilon}\\
S\subset[n],|S|=s\end{subarray}}\mathbb{P}\Bigg\{\bigg\|\Big(\sum_{k=1}^{t-1}a_{k}\phi_{k}\Big)_{S}\bigg\|_{2}>\frac{2\sqrt{s}}{\sqrt{n}}+\frac{2}{\sqrt{n}}\sqrt{\log\bigg(\frac{1}{\delta}\Big(\frac{3}{\epsilon}\Big)^{t}{n\choose s}\bigg)}\Bigg\}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\Big(\frac{3}{\epsilon}\Big)^{t}{n\choose s}\exp\left(-\log\bigg(\frac{1}{\delta}\Big(\frac{3}{\epsilon}\Big)^{t}{n\choose s}\bigg)\right)\leq\delta.

Taking
\epsilon=(\delta/n)^{10}
and using
{n\choose s}\leq n^{s}
imply that: with probability exceeding
1-\delta
,

\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}a\in\mathcal{N}_{\epsilon}\\
S\subset[n],|S|=s\end{subarray}}\bigg\|\Big(\sum_{k=1}^{t-1}a_{k}\phi_{k}\Big)_{S}\bigg\|_{2}\lesssim\frac{\sqrt{s\log\frac{n}{\delta}}}{\sqrt{n}}+\frac{\sqrt{t\log\frac{n}{\delta}}}{\sqrt{n}}

(100)

•

Next, consider an arbitrary vector
a\in\mathcal{S}^{t-2}
and let
\widetilde{a}\in\mathcal{N}_{\epsilon}
obey
\|a-\widetilde{a}\|_{2}\leq\epsilon=(\delta/n)^{10}
.
Then (
100
) together with the triangle inequality tells us that with probability exceeding
1-\delta
,

\displaystyle\bigg\|\Big(\sum_{k=1}^{t-1}a_{k}\phi_{k}\Big)_{S}\bigg\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\bigg\|\Big(\sum_{k=1}^{t-1}\widetilde{a}_{k}\phi_{k}\Big)_{S}\bigg\|_{2}+\bigg\|\Big(\sum_{k=1}^{t-1}(a_{k}-\widetilde{a}_{k})\phi_{k}\Big)_{S}\bigg\|_{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\sqrt{s\log\frac{n}{\delta}}}{\sqrt{n}}+\frac{\sqrt{t\log\frac{n}{\delta}}}{\sqrt{n}}+\|a-\widetilde{a}\|_{2}\Big\|\Big[\phi_{1},\cdots,\phi_{t-1}\Big]\Big\|

\displaystyle\asymp\frac{\sqrt{s\log\frac{n}{\delta}}}{\sqrt{n}}+\frac{\sqrt{t\log\frac{n}{\delta}}}{\sqrt{n}},

where the last line holds since
\|a-\widetilde{a}\|_{2}\leq(\delta/n)^{10}
and, with probability exceeding
1-\delta
,
\big\|\big[\phi_{1},\cdots,\phi_{t-1}\big]\big\|\leq\sqrt{t/\delta}

(
Vershynin, 2018
, Chapter 4.4)
. Given that
a
can be an arbitrary vector lying within
\mathcal{S}^{t-2}
, we have concluded the proof of the claim (
79c
).

A.5
Proof of Lemma
9

Recall the definition (
8
) of the Wasserstein metric between to probability measures.
In view of the celebrated Kantorovich-Rubinstein duality, the 1-Wasserstein distance admits the following dual representation:

\displaystyle W_{1}(\mu,\nu)=\sup\Big\{\mathbb{E}_{\mu}[f]-\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[f]:f\text{ is }1\text{-Lipschitz}\Big\},

(101)

which is the key to establishing this lemma.

Let us start by considering any given
1
-Lipschitz function
f
.
It is assumed without loss of generality that
f(0)=0
(as the expression (
101
) only involves the difference of
f
), which together with the 1-Lipschitz property gives

\displaystyle|f(x)|=|f(x)-f(0)|\leq\|x\|_{2}.

(102)

For any fixed unit vector
\widetilde{\beta}=[\widetilde{\beta}_{k}]_{1\leq k\leq t}\in\mathcal{S}^{t-1}
, the vector

\sum_{i=1}^{t}\widetilde{\beta}_{k}\phi_{k}
clearly follows a Gaussian distribution
\mathcal{N}(0,\frac{1}{n}I_{n})
.
Applying Gaussian isoperimetric inequalities (e.g.,
Massart, 2007
) yields

\displaystyle f\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{t}\widetilde{\beta}_{k}\phi_{k}\Big)-\mathop{\mathbb{E}}\limits_{g\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\frac{1}{n}I_{n})}\big[f(g)\big]\leq\sqrt{\frac{2\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{n}}

(103)

with probability at least
1-\delta
.
Next, let us construct an
\epsilon
-net
\mathcal{N}_{\epsilon}
of
\mathcal{S}^{t-1}
with cardinality not exceeding
(2/\epsilon)^{t}
,
such that for any
\widehat{\beta}\in\mathcal{S}^{t-1}
, one can find a point
\widetilde{\beta}\in\mathcal{N}_{\epsilon}
obeying
\|\widehat{\beta}-\widetilde{\beta}\|_{2}\leq\epsilon
.
Taking the above inequality with the union bound over
\mathcal{N}_{\epsilon}
then leads to

\displaystyle\sup_{\widetilde{\beta}\in\mathcal{N}_{\epsilon}}\bigg\{f\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{t}\widetilde{\beta}_{k}\phi_{k}\Big)-\mathop{\mathbb{E}}\limits_{g\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\frac{1}{n}I_{n})}\big[f(g)\big]\bigg\}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{t\log n}{n}}

with probability at least
1-O(n^{-11})
.
Armed with this result, for an arbitrary
\widehat{\beta}\in\mathcal{S}^{t-1}
one can show that

\displaystyle f\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{t}\widehat{\beta}_{k}\phi_{k}\Big)-\mathop{\mathbb{E}}\limits_{g\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\frac{1}{n}I_{n})}\big[f(g)\big]

\displaystyle\leq\Bigg\{f\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{t}\widetilde{\beta}_{k}\phi_{k}\Big)-\mathop{\mathbb{E}}\limits_{g\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\frac{1}{n}I_{n})}\big[f(g)\big]\Bigg\}+f\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{t}\widehat{\beta}_{k}\phi_{k}\Big)-f\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{t}\widetilde{\beta}_{k}\phi_{k}\Big)

\displaystyle\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{t\log n}{n}}+\bigg\|\sum_{i=1}^{t}\widehat{\beta}_{k}\phi_{k}-\sum_{i=1}^{t}\widetilde{\beta}_{k}\phi_{k}\bigg\|_{2}\asymp\sqrt{\frac{t\log n}{n}}+\bigg\|\sum_{i=1}^{t}\big(\widetilde{\beta}_{k}-\widehat{\beta}_{k}\big)\phi_{k}\bigg\|_{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{t\log n}{n}}+\big\|\widetilde{\beta}-\widehat{\beta}\big\|_{2}\big\|\left[\phi_{1},\cdots,\phi_{t}\right]\big\|\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{t\log n}{n}}+\epsilon\,\big\|\left[\phi_{1},\cdots,\phi_{t}\right]\big\|

\displaystyle\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{t\log n}{n}}+\frac{\sqrt{t}}{n^{5}}\asymp\sqrt{\frac{t\log n}{n}}

with probability at least
1-O(n^{-11})
,
where the second line results from the 1-Lipschitz property of
f
,
and the last line takes
\epsilon=1/n^{5}
and invokes standard random matrix theory
(
Vershynin, 2018
, Chapter 4.4)

that asserts

\mathbb{P}\Big\{\big\|\left[\phi_{1},\cdots,\phi_{t}\right]\big\|\leq C_{8}\sqrt{t}\Big\}\geq 1-O(n^{-11})

(104)

for some constant
C_{8}>0
.
Given that the above inequality holds simultaneously for all
\widehat{\beta}\in\mathcal{S}^{t-1}
,
we have

\displaystyle\sup_{\widehat{\beta}=[\widehat{\beta}_{k}]_{1\leq k\leq t}\in\mathcal{S}^{t-1}}\bigg\{f\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{t}\widehat{\beta}_{k}\phi_{k}\Big)-\mathop{\mathbb{E}}\limits_{g\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\frac{1}{n}I_{n})}\big[f(g)\big]\bigg\}

\displaystyle\leq C_{7}\sqrt{\frac{t\log n}{n}}

(105)

with probability exceeding
1-O(n^{-11})
, where
C_{7}>0
is some universal constant.

Next, we would like to use (
105
) to bound
\mathbb{E}\big[f\big(\sum_{i=1}^{t}\beta_{k}\phi_{k}\big)\big]
.
Let us define the following event:

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{1}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\left\{f\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{t}\beta_{k}\phi_{k}\Big)\leq\mathop{\mathbb{E}}\limits_{g\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\frac{1}{n}I_{n})}\big[f(g)\big]+C_{7}\sqrt{\frac{t\log n}{n}}\right\},

which clearly obeys

\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}_{1})\geq 1-O(n^{-11}).

One can then decompose

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigg[f\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{t}\beta_{k}\phi_{k}\Big)\bigg]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\bigg[f\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{t}\beta_{k}\phi_{k}\Big)\ind(\mathcal{E}_{1})\bigg]+\mathbb{E}\bigg[f\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{t}\beta_{k}\phi_{k}\Big)\ind(\mathcal{E}_{1}^{\mathrm{c}})\bigg]

(106)

The first term on the right-hand side of (
106
) can be controlled as follows:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigg[f\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{t}\beta_{k}\phi_{k}\Big)\ind(\mathcal{E}_{1})\bigg]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\bigg[\bigg\{\mathop{\mathbb{E}}\limits_{g\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\frac{1}{n}I_{n})}\big[f(g)\big]+C_{7}\sqrt{\frac{t\log n}{n}}\bigg\}\ind(\mathcal{E}_{1})\bigg]

\displaystyle\leq\mathop{\mathbb{E}}\limits_{g\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\frac{1}{n}I_{n})}\big[f(g)\big]+C_{7}\sqrt{\frac{t\log n}{n}}+\Bigg|\mathop{\mathbb{E}}\limits_{g\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\frac{1}{n}I_{n})}\big[f(g)\big]+C_{7}\sqrt{\frac{t\log n}{n}}\,\Bigg|\,\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}_{1}^{\mathrm{c}})

\displaystyle\leq\mathop{\mathbb{E}}\limits_{g\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\frac{1}{n}I_{n})}\big[f(g)\big]+O\left(\sqrt{\frac{t\log n}{n}}\right).

Here, the last line holds since
\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}_{1}^{\mathrm{c}})\leq O(n^{-11})
and

\bigg|\mathop{\mathbb{E}}\limits_{g\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\frac{1}{n}I_{n})}\big[f(g)\big]\bigg|\leq\mathop{\mathbb{E}}\limits_{g\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\frac{1}{n}I_{n})}\left[\|g\|_{2}\right]\leq 1+\mathop{\mathbb{E}}\limits_{g\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\frac{1}{n}I_{n})}\left[\|g\|_{2}^{2}\right]=2,

where the first inequality arises from (
102
).
When it comes to the second term on the right-hand side of (
106
),
we make the observation that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigg[f\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{t}\beta_{k}\phi_{k}\Big)\ind(\mathcal{E}_{1}^{\mathrm{c}})\bigg]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\bigg[\bigg\|\sum_{i=1}^{t}\beta_{k}\phi_{k}\bigg\|_{2}\ind(\mathcal{E}_{1}^{\mathrm{c}})\bigg]\leq\mathbb{E}\bigg[\|\beta\|_{2}\cdot\big\|\big[\phi_{1},\cdots,\phi_{t}\big]\big\|\ind(\mathcal{E}_{1}^{\mathrm{c}})\bigg]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\bigg[\big\|\big[\phi_{1},\cdots,\phi_{t}\big]\big\|\ind(\mathcal{E}_{1}^{\mathrm{c}})\bigg]\leq\mathbb{E}\Big[\big\|\big[\phi_{1},\cdots,\phi_{t}\big]\big\|_{\mathrm{F}}\ind(\mathcal{E}_{1}^{\mathrm{c}})\Big]

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\bigg[\big\|\big[\phi_{1},\cdots,\phi_{t}\big]\big\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\bigg]}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\big[\ind(\mathcal{E}_{1}^{\mathrm{c}})\big]}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{t}\cdot O(n^{-11})\leq O(n^{-10}),

where the first inequality comes from (
102
), the second line is valid since
\|\beta\|_{2}=1
, and the third line invokes the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality.
Substituting the above two inequalities into (
106
), we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigg[f\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{t}\beta_{k}\phi_{k}\Big)\bigg]

\displaystyle\leq\mathop{\mathbb{E}}\limits_{g\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\frac{1}{n}I_{n})}\big[f(g)\big]+O\left(\sqrt{\frac{t\log n}{n}}\right).

(107)

To finish up, combine (
107
) with (
101
) to arrive at

W_{1}\bigg(\mu\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{t}\beta_{k}\phi_{k}\Big),\mathcal{N}\Big(0,\frac{1}{n}I_{n}\Big)\bigg)=\sup\bigg\{\mathbb{E}\bigg[f\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{t}\beta_{k}\phi_{k}\Big)\bigg]-\mathbb{E}_{g\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\frac{1}{n}I_{n})}\big[f(g)\big]:f\text{ is }1\text{-Lipschitz}\bigg\}\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{t\log n}{n}}.

Appendix B
Proof of auxiliary lemmas for master theorems (Theorems
1
-
2
)

B.1
Proof of Lemma
2

Before embarking on the proof, let us introduce some notation and basic properties.
Recall that
\{z_{k}\}_{k\leq t}
are orthonormal (see Lemma
1
) and
U_{t-1}=[z_{1},\cdots,z_{t-1}]\in\real^{n\times(t-1)}
.
For any
1\leq k<n
,
we let
U_{k}^{\perp}\in\real^{n\times(n-k)}
represent the orthogonal complement of
U_{k}
(such that
U_{k}^{\top}U_{k}^{\perp}=0
and
U_{k}^{\perp\top}U_{k}^{\perp}=I_{n-k}
).
We also define the projection of
W_{k+1}
onto
U_{k}^{\perp}
as follows

\displaystyle\widetilde{W}_{k+1}

\displaystyle\coloneqq U_{k}^{\perp\top}W_{k+1}U_{k}^{\perp}

(108)

which together with the construction (
43c
) clearly satisfies

\displaystyle\widetilde{W}_{k+1}=U_{k}^{\perp\top}(I_{n}-z_{k}z_{k}^{\top})W_{k}(I_{n}-z_{k}z_{k}^{\top})U_{k}^{\perp}=U_{k}^{\perp\top}W_{k}U_{k}^{\perp}=\cdots=U_{k}^{\perp\top}WU_{k}^{\perp}\in\real^{(n-k)\times(n-k)}.

(109)

In view of the construction, we also have

\displaystyle W_{k+1}

\displaystyle=\left(I_{n}-z_{k}z_{k}^{\top}\right)W_{k}\left(I_{n}-z_{k}z_{k}^{\top}\right)=\cdots=\left(I_{n}-U_{k}U_{k}^{\top}\right)W\left(I_{n}-U_{k}U_{k}^{\top}\right)

\displaystyle=U_{k}^{\perp}U_{k}^{\perp\top}WU_{k}^{\perp}U_{k}^{\perp\top}=U_{k}^{\perp}\widetilde{W}_{k+1}U_{k}^{\perp\top}.

(110)

To establish Lemma
2
, the first step lies in proving the following claim.
In the sequel, let us prove this crucial claim first before moving on to the next step.

Claim 1
.

Consider any
2\leq k\leq n
. Conditional on
\{z_{i}\}_{i<k}
and
x_{1}
, the following hold:

•

\widetilde{W}_{k}
is a (rescaled) Wigner matrix in the sense that its entries
\big\{(\widetilde{W}_{k})_{ij}\mid i\geq j\big\}
are independent obeying

\displaystyle(\widetilde{W}_{k})_{ii}\sim\mathcal{N}\Big(0,\frac{2}{n}\Big)\qquad\text{and}\qquad(\widetilde{W}_{k})_{ij}=(\widetilde{W}_{k})_{ji}\sim\mathcal{N}\Big(0,\frac{1}{n}\Big)\quad\text{for any }i>j;

(111)

•

W_{k}
is conditionally independent of
\{W_{i}z_{i}\}_{i<k}
;

•

the randomness of
x_{k}
and
z_{k}
comes purely from that of
\{W_{i}z_{i}\}_{i<k}
and
x_{1}
, and hence
x_{k}
and
z_{k}
are conditionally independent of
W_{k}
.

Proof of Claim
1
.

The proof of this claim proceeds via an inductive argument.

The base case with
k=2
.

Consider first the case when
k=2
. In view of the definition (
43c
), we have

\displaystyle W_{2}

\displaystyle=\big(I-z_{1}z_{1}^{\top}\big)W\big(I-z_{1}z_{1}^{\top}\big)

where
z_{1}
is independent from
W
. Let
z_{1}^{\perp}=\real^{n\times(n-1)}
denote the orthogonal complement of
z_{1}
(so that
z_{1}^{\top}z_{1}^{\perp}=0
and
z_{1}^{\perp\top}z_{1}^{\perp}=I_{n-1}
), and define the projection of
W_{2}
onto
z_{1}^{\perp}
(see (
109
)) obeys:

\widetilde{W}_{2}=z_{1}^{\perp\top}W_{2}z_{1}^{\perp}=z_{1}^{\perp\top}Wz_{1}^{\perp}\overset{\mathrm{d}}{=}e_{1}^{\perp\top}We_{1}^{\perp},

(112)

where the last relation arises from the rotational invariance of the Wigner matrix (with
e_{1}
denoting the first standard basis vector).
Therefore, it is readily seen that: conditioned on
z_{1}
,

•

\widetilde{W}_{2}
is a (rescaled) Wigner matrix in
(n-1)×(n-1)
obeying (
111
);

•

\widetilde{W}_{2}
— and hence
W_{2}
— is statistically independent from
Wz_{1}
.

In addition, recalling the update rule (
3
), the definition (
43a
) of
z_{1}
and the assumption
\eta_{0}(x_{0})=0
, we have

\displaystyle x_{2}

\displaystyle=\lambda v^{\star}v^{\star\top}\eta_{1}(x_{1})+W\eta_{1}(x_{1})=\big(\lambda v^{\star\top}z_{1}\|\eta_{1}(x_{1})\|_{2}\big)\cdot v^{\star}+\|\eta_{1}(x_{1})\|_{2}\cdot Wz_{1},

where the last step relies on the definition (
43a
) of
z_{1}
.
Given that
z_{1}
is fully determined by
x_{1}
,
we see that the randomness of
x_{2}
— and hence that of
z_{2}
— comes entirely from
Wz_{1}
and
x_{1}
.
We have thus established the advertised claim for the case with
k=2.

The induction step.

Next, assuming that the claim holds for all step
i
with
i\leq k
, let us extend it to the
(k+1)
-th step.
To begin with, the inductive assumption tells us that: conditional on
\{z_{i}\}_{i<k}
and
x_{1}
,

(i)

W_{k}
is independent of
\{W_{i}z_{i}\}_{i<k}
;

(ii)

the randomness of
z_{k}
purely comes from
\{W_{i}z_{i}\}_{i<k}
, and hence
W_{k}
is also independent of
z_{k}
.

Taking these two conditions together reveals that:
if we condition on
\{z_{i}\}_{i\leq k}
and
x_{1}
(namely, we condition on an additional variable
z_{k}
compared to the above induction hypothesis),
then clearly
W_{k}
is still independent of
\{W_{i}z_{i}\}_{i<k}
.
Recalling that

W_{k+1}=\left(I_{n}-z_{k}z_{k}^{\top}\right)W_{k}\left(I_{n}-z_{k}z_{k}^{\top}\right),

(113)

we can readily conclude that: conditioned on
\{z_{i}\}_{i\leq k}
and
x_{1}
,

•

W_{k+1}
is also independent of
\{W_{i}z_{i}\}_{i<k}
,
given the conditional independence between
W_{k}
and
\{W_{i}z_{i}\}_{i<k}
and the fact that
z_{k}
is being conditioned now;

•

W_{k+1}
is independent of
\{W_{i}z_{i}\}_{i<k}
.

As a result, in order to show that
W_{k+1}
is conditionally independent from
\{W_{i}z_{i}\}_{i\leq k}
,
it suffices to justify that it is conditionally independent from
W_{k}z_{k}
, which we shall accomplish next.

Recall that
\widetilde{W}_{k}
is a rescaled Wigner matrix independent of
z_{k}
(see Property (ii) above) when conditioned on
\{z_{i}\}_{i<k}
and
x_{1}
.
Akin to the argument for the base case, the rotational invariance of the Wigner matrix together with expression (
113
) tells us that:
conditional on
\{z_{i}\}_{i\leq k}
and
x_{1}
,

•

\widetilde{W}_{k+1}
is a (rescaled) Wigner matrix in
(n-k)×(n-k)
obeying (
111
);

•

\widetilde{W}_{k+1}
— and hence
W_{k+1}
— is statistically independent from
W_{k}z_{k}
.

We can thus conclude that: conditional on
\{z_{i}\}_{i\leq k}
and
x_{1}
,
both
W_{k+1}
and
\widetilde{W}_{k+1}
are independent from
\big\{W_{i}z_{i}\big\}_{1\leq i\leq k}
.

In addition, given the AMP update rule (
3
), it is legitimate to write

\displaystyle x_{k+1}

\displaystyle=(\lambda v^{\star}v^{\star\top}+W)\eta_{k}(x_{k})-\big\langle\eta_{k}^{\prime}(x_{k})\big\rangle\cdot\eta_{k-1}(x_{k-1})

\displaystyle=\lambda v^{\star}v^{\star\top}\eta_{k}(x_{k})+\sum_{i=1}^{k}\beta_{k}^{i}W_{i}z_{i}+\sum_{i=1}^{k-1}z_{i}\Big[\langle W_{i}z_{i},\eta_{k}(x_{k})\rangle-\langle\eta_{k}^{\prime}(x_{k})\rangle\beta_{k-1}^{i}-\beta_{k}^{i}z_{i}^{\top}W_{i}z_{i}\Big],

where the last equality follows from expression (
53
).
Clearly,
x_{k+1}
is determined by
x_{k}
,
\big\{W_{i}z_{i}\big\}_{i\leq k}
, and
\{z_{i}\}_{i\leq k}
(given that
\beta_{k}^{i}
is also determined by
z_{i}
and
x_{k}
),
in addition to other deterministic objects.
Moreover, our induction hypothesis asserts that the randomness of
x_{k}
and
z_{k}
all comes from
\big\{W_{i}z_{i}\big\}_{i<k}
and
x_{1}
.
Consequently, these taken collectively imply that all randomness of
x_{k+1}
(and hence
z_{k+1}
) comes from
\{W_{i}z_{i}\}_{i\leq k}
and
x_{1}
.
We have thus established the claim for step
k+1.

To finish up, applying the inductive argument concludes the proof of Claim
1
.
∎

Armed with the results in Claim
1
, we can characterize the conditional distribution of
W_{k}z_{k}
.
Given that the
z_{i}
’s are orthonormal (cf. Lemma
1
), we can apply Claim
1
to show that:
conditional on
\{z_{i}\}_{1\leq i<k}
and
x_{1}
,

\displaystyle z_{i}^{\top}W_{k}z_{k}

\displaystyle=z_{i}^{\top}U_{k-1}^{\perp}U_{k-1}^{\perp\top}WU_{k-1}^{\perp}U_{k-1}^{\perp\top}z_{k}=0\qquad\qquad\text{for }i<k,

(114a)

\displaystyle z_{k}^{\top}W_{k}z_{k}

\displaystyle=z_{k}^{\top}U_{k-1}^{\perp}U_{k-1}^{\perp\top}WU_{k-1}^{\perp}U_{k-1}^{\perp\top}z_{k}=\left(U_{k-1}^{\perp\top}z_{k}\right)^{\top}\widetilde{W}_{k}\left(U_{k-1}^{\perp\top}z_{k}\right)\overset{\mathrm{d}}{=}e_{1}^{\top}\widetilde{W}_{k}e_{1}\sim\mathcal{N}\Big(0,\frac{2}{n}\Big),

(114b)

\displaystyle U_{k}^{\perp\top}W_{k}z_{k}

\displaystyle=U_{k}^{\perp\top}U_{k-1}^{\perp}U_{k-1}^{\perp\top}WU_{k-1}^{\perp}U_{k-1}^{\perp\top}z_{k}=\left(U_{k-1}^{\perp\top}U_{k}^{\perp}\right)^{\top}\widetilde{W}_{k}\left(U_{k-1}^{\perp\top}z_{k}\right)\sim\mathcal{N}\Big(0,\frac{1}{n}I_{n-k}\Big),

(114c)

where we have made use of the fact in Claim
1
that, conditional on
\{z_{i}\}_{1\leq i<k}
and
x_{1}
,

\widetilde{W}_{k}
is a rescaled Wigner matrix independent of
z_{k}
.
Therefore, if we generate i.i.d. Gaussian random variables
g_{i}^{k}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\frac{1}{n})
for all
i<k
,
then conditional on
\{z_{i}\}_{i\leq k}
and
x_{1}
, it follows that

\displaystyle\phi_{k}

\displaystyle\coloneqq W_{k}z_{k}+\Big(\frac{\sqrt{2}}{2}-1\Big)z_{k}^{\top}W_{k}z_{k}\cdot z_{k}+\sum_{i=1}^{k-1}g_{i}^{k}z_{i}

(115)

\displaystyle=\bigg(\sum_{i=1}^{k}z_{i}z_{i}^{\top}\bigg)W_{k}z_{k}+\big(U_{k}^{\perp}U_{k}^{\perp\top}\big)W_{k}z_{k}+\Big(\frac{\sqrt{2}}{2}-1\Big)z_{k}^{\top}W_{k}z_{k}\cdot z_{k}+\sum_{i=1}^{k-1}g_{i}^{k}z_{i}

\displaystyle=\frac{\sqrt{2}}{2}\left(z_{k}^{\top}W_{k}z_{k}\right)z_{k}+\sum_{i=1}^{k-1}g_{i}^{k}z_{i}+U_{k}^{\perp}\big(U_{k}^{\perp\top}W_{k}z_{k}\big)

\displaystyle\sim\mathcal{N}\Big(0,\frac{1}{n}I_{n}\Big).

(116)

Here, the penultimate line makes use of (
114a
) and a little algebra,
whereas the last line is valid since, along each basis direction (i.e.,
z_{1},\cdots,z_{k}
and each column of
U_{k}^{\perp}
), the projection of
\phi_{k}
is independent
\mathcal{N}(0,1/n)
.
In fact, (
116
) tells us that the conditional distribution of
\phi_{k}
is always
\mathcal{N}(0,\frac{1}{n}I_{n})
no matter what value the sequence
\{z_{i}\}_{i\leq k}
takes, thus indicating the (unconditional) distribution of
\phi_{k}
as follows:
