\displaystyle\phi_{k}\sim\mathcal{N}\Big(0,\frac{1}{n}I_{n}\Big).

(117)

Finally, we demonstrate that
\{\phi_{i}\}_{1\leq i\leq k}
are independent.
To this end, we first observe that
\phi_{k}
is independent of
\{z_{i}\}_{i<k}
and
x_{1}
,
which is an immediate consequence of the conditional distribution derivation (
116
).
Further, combining Claim
1
with the definition (
115
) of
\phi_{k}
(which depends only on
W_{k}z_{k}
and
\{g_{i}^{k}\}
conditional on
\{z_{i}\}_{1\leq i\leq k}
) reveals that:
conditional on
\{z_{i}\}_{i<k}
and
x_{1}
,
\phi_{k}
is statistically independent from
\phi_{k-1},\cdots,\phi_{1}
.
Letting us abuse the notation and use
f
to represent the pdf of the random vectors of interest, we obtain

\displaystyle f(\phi_{k},\phi_{k-1},\cdots,\phi_{1})={\displaystyle\int}f\big(\phi_{k},\phi_{k-1},\cdots,\phi_{1}\mid z_{k-1},\cdots,z_{1},x_{1}\big)\mu\left(\mathrm{d}z_{k-1},\cdots,\mathrm{d}z_{1},\mathrm{d}x_{1}\right)

\displaystyle={\displaystyle\int}f\big(\phi_{k}\mid z_{k-1},\cdots,z_{1},x_{1}\big)f\big(\phi_{k-1},\cdots,\phi_{1}\mid z_{k-1},\cdots,z_{1},x_{1}\big)\mu\left(\mathrm{d}z_{k-1},\cdots,\mathrm{d}z_{1},\mathrm{d}x_{1}\right)

\displaystyle=f(\phi_{k}){\displaystyle\int}f\big(\phi_{k-1},\cdots,\phi_{1}\mid z_{k-1},\cdots,z_{1},x_{1}\big)\mu\left(\mathrm{d}z_{k-1},\cdots,\mathrm{d}z_{1},\mathrm{d}x_{1}\right)

\displaystyle=f\big(\phi_{k}\big)f\big(\phi_{k-1},\cdots,\phi_{1}\big),

where the second line holds since, as shown above,
\phi_{k}
is independent of
\phi_{k-1},\cdots,\phi_{1}
when conditioned on
z_{1},\cdots,z_{k-1}
and
x_{1}
,
and the third line makes use of the statistical independence between
\phi_{k}
and
z_{k-1},\cdots,z_{1},x_{1}
.
Repeating the above derivation gives

\displaystyle f(\phi_{k},\phi_{k-1},\cdots,\phi_{1})

\displaystyle=f\big(\phi_{k}\big)f\big(\phi_{k-1},\cdots,\phi_{1}\big)=\cdots=f\big(\phi_{k}\big)f\big(\phi_{k-1}\big)\cdots f\big(\phi_{1}\big),

thereby justifying that
\{\phi_{i}\}_{1\leq i\leq k}
are statistically independent.

B.2
Proof of Lemma
3

To begin with, it is seen from property (
114
) that
z_{k}^{\top}W_{k}z_{k}
follows a normal distribution with variance
2/n
(given that this distribution is independent of
\{z_{i}\}_{1\leq i\leq k}
and
x_{1}
).
Standard Gaussian concentration inequalities
(
Vershynin, 2018
, Chapter 2.6)
together with the union bound tell us that

\max_{1\leq k\leq n}\big|z_{k}^{\top}W_{k}z_{k}\big|\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{\log n}{n}}

(118)

with probability at least
1-n^{-11}
. Consequently, we have

\displaystyle\Big|\sum_{k=1}^{t-1}\mu_{t}^{k}\beta_{t}^{k}z_{k}^{\top}W_{k}z_{k}\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\Big|\max_{1\leq k\leq n}z_{k}^{\top}W_{k}z_{k}\Big|\cdot\sum_{k=1}^{t-1}\big|\mu_{t}^{k}\beta_{t}^{k}\big|\leq\Big|\max_{1\leq k\leq n}z_{k}^{\top}W_{k}z_{k}\Big|\cdot\|\mu_{t}\|_{2}\|\beta_{t}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{\log n}{n}}\|\beta_{t}\|_{2},

(119)

with probability at least
1-n^{-11}
, where we remind the reader of the notation
\mu_{t}=[\mu_{t}^{k}]_{1\leq k\leq t}
and
\beta_{t}=[\beta_{t}^{k}]_{1\leq k\leq t}
and the fact that
\|\mu_{t}\|_{2}=1
.

Next, we turn to the following term

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{1}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\sum_{k=1}^{t-1}\mu_{t}^{k}\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{k-1}\beta_{t}^{i}g_{i}^{k}+\sum_{i=k+1}^{t}\beta_{t}^{i}g_{k}^{i}\Big)\eqqcolon\sum_{k=1}^{t-1}\mu_{t}^{k}\varrho_{k},

where we recall that each random variable
g_{i}^{k}
with
i\neq k
is independently generated from
\mathcal{N}(0,1/n)
,
which is also independent from
\beta_{t}
(but not
\mu_{t}
).
Conditional on
\beta_{t}
, one has

\displaystyle\mathsf{Var}\left(\varrho_{k}\mid\beta_{t}\right)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{k-1}\big(\beta_{t}^{i}\big)^{2}+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=k+1}^{t}\big(\beta_{t}^{i}\big)^{2}\leq\frac{\|\beta_{t}\|_{2}^{2}}{n},

which combined with Gaussian concentration inequalities
(
Vershynin, 2018
, Chapter 2.6)
and the union bound yields

\displaystyle\max_{1\leq k\leq n}|\varrho_{k}|\lesssim\frac{\|\beta_{t}\|_{2}\sqrt{\log n}}{\sqrt{n}}

(120)

with probability at least
1-O(n^{-11})
. As a result, the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality gives

\displaystyle|\mathcal{I}_{1}|

\displaystyle=\Big|\sum_{k=1}^{t-1}\mu_{t}^{k}\varrho_{k}\Big|\leq\|\mu_{t}\|_{2}\sqrt{\sum_{k=1}^{t-1}\varrho_{k}^{2}}\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{t\log n}{n}}\|\beta_{t}\|_{2}

(121)

with probability at least
1-O(n^{-11})
, where we have used (
120
) and the fact
\|\mu_{t}\|_{2}=1
.

Combining (
119
) and (
121
) immediately finishes the proof.

B.3
Proof of Lemma
4

Lemma
4
involves bounds concerning the continuous part of the function and that of the discontinuous part,
which we shall prove separately.

The continuous part: proof of inequalities (
68a
) and (
68b
).

First, some basic algebra leads to

\displaystyle\Big\langle\sum_{k=1}^{t-1}\mu_{t}^{k}\phi_{k},\eta_{t}^{\prime}(v_{t})\circ\xi_{t-1}\Big\rangle-\langle\eta_{t}^{\prime\prime}(v_{t})\circ\xi_{t-1}\rangle\sum_{k=1}^{t-1}\mu_{t}^{k}\beta_{t-1}^{k}

\displaystyle=\bigg\langle\sum_{k=1}^{t-1}\mu_{t}^{k}\phi_{k}\circ\eta_{t}^{\prime}(v_{t}),\xi_{t-1}\bigg\rangle-\bigg\langle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{k=1}^{t-1}\mu_{t}^{k}\beta_{t-1}^{k}\eta_{t}^{\prime\prime}(v_{t}),\xi_{t-1}\bigg\rangle

\displaystyle\leq\bigg\|\sum_{k=1}^{t-1}\mu_{t}^{k}\phi_{k}\circ\eta_{t}^{\prime}(v_{t})-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{k=1}^{t-1}\mu_{t}^{k}\beta_{t-1}^{k}\eta_{t}^{\prime\prime}(v_{t})\bigg\|_{2}\|\xi_{t-1}\|_{2}.

(122)

The condition (
14d
) imposed in Assumption
2
tells us that

\displaystyle\Big\|\sum_{k=1}^{t-1}\mu_{t}^{k}\phi_{k}\circ\eta_{t}^{\prime}-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{k=1}^{t-1}\mu_{t}^{k}\beta_{t-1}^{k}\eta_{t}^{\prime\prime}\Big\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\kappa_{t}^{2}+D_{t},

which taken collectively with inequality (
122
) concludes the proof of inequality (
68a
).

When it comes to the second claim (
68b
), we observe that for any
t\leq n
,

\displaystyle\rho_{1}\bigg\langle\bigg|\sum_{k=1}^{t-1}\mu_{t}^{k}\phi_{k}\bigg|,\big|\xi_{t-1}\big|^{2}\Big\rangle+\rho_{2}\Big\langle\big|\xi_{t-1}\big|^{2}\Big\rangle\bigg|\sum_{k=1}^{t-1}\mu_{t}^{k}\beta_{t-1}^{k}\bigg|

\displaystyle\leq\rho_{1}\bigg\{\max_{1\leq i\leq n}\Big|\sum_{k=1}^{t-1}\mu_{t}^{k}\phi_{k}\Big|_{i}\bigg\}\cdot\|\xi_{t-1}\|_{2}^{2}+\frac{\rho_{2}}{n}\|\xi_{t-1}\|_{2}^{2}\bigg|\sum_{k=1}^{t-1}\mu_{t}^{k}\beta_{t-1}^{k}\bigg|

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\rho_{1}\sqrt{\frac{t\log n}{n}}+\frac{\rho_{2}\|\beta_{t-1}\|_{2}}{n}\right)\|\xi_{t-1}\|_{2}^{2}.

Here, the last line makes use of two properties:
(i)
\big|\sum_{k=1}^{t-1}\mu_{t}^{k}\beta_{t-1}^{k}\big|\leq\|\mu_{t}^{k}\|_{2}\|\beta_{t-1}\|_{2}=\|\beta_{t-1}\|_{2}
(given that
\mu_{t}
is constructed as a unit vector);
(ii) the standard Gaussian concentration inequalities
(
Vershynin, 2018
, Chapter 4.4)
indicating that, with probability at least
1-O(n^{-11})
,

\max_{1\leq i\leq n}\Big|\sum_{k=1}^{t-1}\mu_{t}^{k}\phi_{k}\Big|_{i}\leq\max_{1\leq i\leq n}\|\mu_{t}\|_{2}\big\|[\phi_{1,i},\cdots,\phi_{t-1,i}]\big\|_{2}=\max_{1\leq i\leq n}\big\|[\phi_{1,i},\cdots,\phi_{t-1,i}]\big\|_{2}\lesssim\frac{\sqrt{t}+\sqrt{\log n}}{\sqrt{n}}.

This establishes inequality (
68b
).

The discontinuous part: proof of inequality (
68c
).

We first make the observation that: the quantity
\theta(m)
defined in expression (
14g
) obeys

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{n}\ind\Big(\Big|\alpha_{t}v^{\star}_{j}+\sum_{k=1}^{t-1}\beta_{t-1}^{k}\phi_{k,j}-m\Big|\leq|\xi_{t-1,j}|\Big)\leq\sum_{j=1}^{n}\ind\Big(\Big|\alpha_{t}v^{\star}_{j}+\sum_{k=1}^{t-1}\beta_{t-1}^{k}\phi_{k,j}-m\Big|\leq\theta(m)\Big),

(123)

which can be proved using the definition (
14g
) as follows.

Proof of (
123
).

By defining the set

\displaystyle\mathcal{J}\coloneqq\bigg\{j\in[n]:\Big|\alpha_{t}v_{j}^{\star}+\sum_{k=1}^{t-1}\beta_{t-1}^{k}\phi_{k,j}-m\Big|\leq|\xi_{t-1,j}|\bigg\},

we can easily see that

\displaystyle\sum_{j\in\mathcal{J}}\Big|\alpha_{t}v_{j}^{\star}+\sum_{k=1}^{t-1}\beta_{t-1}^{k}\phi_{k,j}-m_{i}\Big|^{2}\leq\sum_{j\in\mathcal{J}}|\xi_{t-1,j}|^{2}\leq\|\xi_{t-1}\|_{2}^{2}.

(124)

Additionally, if we define another set
\mathcal{J}^{\prime}
as follows

\displaystyle\mathcal{J}^{\prime}\coloneqq\bigg\{j\in[n]:\Big|\alpha_{t}v_{j}^{\star}+\sum_{k=1}^{t-1}\beta_{t-1}^{k}\phi_{k,j}-m\Big|\leq\theta(m)\bigg\},

(125)

then in view of definition of
\theta(m)
,
\mathcal{J}^{\prime}
is clearly the index set with the largest cardinality obeying

\sum_{j\in\mathcal{J}^{\prime}}\Big|\alpha_{t}v_{j}^{\star}+\sum_{k=1}^{t-1}\beta_{t-1}^{k}\phi_{k,j}-m\Big|^{2}\leq\|\xi_{t-1}\|_{2}^{2}.

Since
\mathcal{J}
also satisfies this relation (cf. (
124
)), we arrive at
\left|\mathcal{J}^{\prime}\right|\geq\left|\mathcal{J}\right|
, thus validating inequality (
123
).
∎

Next, for any
m\in\mathcal{M}_{\mathsf{dc}}
, define
\Gamma(m)\coloneqq\big[\Gamma_{j}(m)\big]_{1\leq j\leq n}\in\real^{n}
(see (
64
)).
Equipped with the above relation (
123
), one can show that

\displaystyle 2\rho\bigg\langle\bigg|\sum_{k=1}^{t-1}\mu_{t}^{k}\phi_{k}\bigg|,\,\Gamma\circ\big|\xi_{t-1}\big|\bigg\rangle

\displaystyle=2\rho\bigg\langle\bigg|\sum_{k=1}^{t-1}\mu_{t}^{k}\phi_{k}\bigg|\circ\Gamma,\,\big|\xi_{t-1}\big|\bigg\rangle=\sum_{m\in\mathcal{M}_{\mathsf{dc}}}2\rho\bigg\langle\bigg|\sum_{k=1}^{t-1}\mu_{t}^{k}\phi_{k}\bigg|\circ\Gamma(m),\,\big|\xi_{t-1}\big|\bigg\rangle

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{m\in\mathcal{M}_{\mathsf{dc}}}2\rho\Big\|\sum_{k=1}^{t-1}\mu_{t}^{k}\phi_{k}\circ\Gamma(m)\Big\|_{2}\cdot\big\|\xi_{t-1}\big\|_{2}.

(126)

To control the right-hand side of (
126
),
we first apply inequality (
79c
) in Lemma
8
with
s=t
to obtain

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{t}\Big|\sum_{k=1}^{t-1}\mu_{t}^{k}\phi_{k}\Big|_{(i)}^{2}\lesssim\frac{t\log{n}}{n}

with probability at least
1-O(n^{-11})
.
This relation in turn implies that for every
j\geq t
,

\displaystyle\Big|\sum_{k=1}^{t-1}\mu_{t}^{k}\phi_{k}\Big|_{(j)}^{2}\leq\frac{1}{t}\sum_{i=1}^{t}\Big|\sum_{k=1}^{t-1}\mu_{t}^{k}\phi_{k}\Big|_{(i)}^{2}\lesssim\frac{\log{n}}{n}.

With these two inequalities in mind, we can deduce that

\displaystyle\Big\|\sum_{k=1}^{t-1}\mu_{t}^{k}\phi_{k}\circ\Gamma(m)\Big\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i=1}^{t}\Big|\sum_{k=1}^{t-1}\mu_{t}^{k}\phi_{k}\Big|_{(i)}^{2}+\Big|\sum_{k=1}^{t-1}\mu_{t}^{k}\phi_{k}\Big|_{(t+1)}^{2}\cdot\big\|\Gamma(m)\big\|_{1}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{t\log n}{n}+\frac{\log n}{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\ind\Big(\Big|\alpha_{t}v^{\star}_{j}+\sum_{k=1}^{t-1}\beta_{t-1}^{k}\phi_{k,j}-m\Big|\leq|\xi_{t-1,j}|\Big).

(127)

This taken collectively with inequality (
126
) leads to

\displaystyle 2\rho\bigg\langle\bigg|\sum_{k=1}^{t-1}\mu_{t}^{k}\phi_{k}\bigg|,\,\Gamma\circ\big|\xi_{t-1}\big|\bigg\rangle

\displaystyle\lesssim\rho\sum_{m\in\mathcal{M}_{\mathsf{dc}}}\left(\sqrt{\frac{t\log n}{n}}+\sqrt{\frac{\log n}{n}}\sqrt{\sum_{j=1}^{n}\ind\Big(\Big|\alpha_{t}v^{\star}_{j}+\sum_{k=1}^{t-1}\beta_{t-1}^{k}\phi_{k,j}-m\Big|\leq|\xi_{t-1,j}|\Big)}\right)\big\|\xi_{t-1}\big\|_{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\rho\sum_{m\in\mathcal{M}_{\mathsf{dc}}}\left(\sqrt{\frac{t\log n}{n}}+\sqrt{\frac{\log n}{n}}\sqrt{\sum_{j=1}^{n}\ind\Big(\Big|\alpha_{t}v^{\star}_{j}+\sum_{k=1}^{t-1}\beta_{t-1}^{k}\phi_{k,j}-m\Big|\leq\theta(m)\Big)}\right)\big\|\xi_{t-1}\big\|_{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\rho\sqrt{\frac{(E_{t}+t)\log n}{n}}\big\|\xi_{t-1}\big\|_{2},

where the second inequality comes from (
123
), and the last inequality makes use of the definition (
14h
) of
E_{t}
.

Similar calculations lead to

\displaystyle\Big\{2\rho\langle\Gamma\rangle+2\rho_{1}\big\langle\Gamma\circ\big|\xi_{t-1}\big|\big\rangle\Big\}\bigg|\sum_{k=1}^{t-1}\mu_{t}^{k}\beta_{t-1}^{k}\bigg|\leq 2\Big(\rho+\rho_{1}\big\|\xi_{t-1}\big\|_{\infty}\Big)\langle\Gamma\rangle\big\|\mu_{t}\big\|_{2}\big\|\beta_{t-1}\big\|_{2}

\displaystyle\qquad=\frac{2(\rho+\rho_{1}\big\|\xi_{t-1}\big\|_{\infty})}{n}\Big\{\sum_{m\in\mathcal{M}_{\mathsf{dc}}}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\Gamma_{j}(m)\Big\}\big\|\beta_{t-1}\big\|_{2}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\frac{2(\rho+\rho_{1}\big\|\xi_{t-1}\big\|_{\infty})\big\|\beta_{t-1}\big\|_{2}}{n}\sum_{m\in\mathcal{M}_{\mathsf{dc}}}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\ind\Big(\Big|\alpha_{t}v^{\star}_{j}+\sum_{k=1}^{t-1}\beta_{t-1}^{k}\phi_{k,j}-m\Big|\leq|\xi_{t-1,j}|\Big)

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\frac{2(\rho+\rho_{1}\big\|\xi_{t-1}\big\|_{\infty})\big\|\beta_{t-1}\big\|_{2}}{n}\sum_{m\in\mathcal{M}_{\mathsf{dc}}}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\ind\Big(\Big|\alpha_{t}v^{\star}_{j}+\sum_{k=1}^{t-1}\beta_{t-1}^{k}\phi_{k,j}-m\Big|\leq\theta(m)\Big)

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\frac{2(\rho+\rho_{1}\big\|\xi_{t-1}\big\|_{\infty})E_{t}\big\|\beta_{t-1}\big\|_{2}}{n}

Taking the above pieces collectively, we demonstrate that

\displaystyle 2\rho\bigg\langle\bigg|\sum_{k=1}^{t-1}\mu_{t}^{k}\phi_{k}\bigg|,\,\Gamma\circ\big|\xi_{t-1}\big|\bigg\rangle+\Big\{2\rho\langle\Gamma\rangle+2\rho_{1}\big\langle\Gamma\circ\big|\xi_{t-1}\big|\big\rangle\Big\}\bigg|\sum_{k=1}^{t-1}\mu_{t}^{k}\beta_{t-1}^{k}\bigg|

\displaystyle\qquad\lesssim\rho\sqrt{\frac{(E_{t}+t)\log n}{n}}\big\|\xi_{t-1}\big\|_{2}+\frac{(\rho+\rho_{1}\big\|\xi_{t-1}\big\|_{\infty})E_{t}\big\|\beta_{t-1}\big\|_{2}}{n}

as claimed.

B.4
Proof of Lemma
5

Inequality (
69a
) and (
69c
) directly results from the Lipschitz property of
\eta_{t}
and the fact that
\|v^{\star}\|_{2}=1.

We then move on to proving inequality (
69b
).
Recall from (
65
) that
\delta_{t}
obeys

\displaystyle\Big|\delta_{t}

\displaystyle-\eta_{t}^{\prime}\Big(\alpha_{t}v^{\star}+\sum_{k=1}^{t-1}\beta_{t-1}^{k}\phi_{k}\Big)\circ\xi_{t-1}\Big|\leq\rho_{1}\big|\xi_{t-1}\big|^{2}+2\rho\Gamma\circ\big|\xi_{t-1}\big|.

(128)

In view of (
128
), we have

\displaystyle\Big|\big\langle\eta_{t}(v_{t}),\delta_{t}\big\rangle\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\Big|\big\langle\eta_{t}(v_{t}),\eta_{t}^{\prime}(v_{t})\circ\xi_{t-1}\big\rangle\Big|+\rho_{1}\Big|\big\langle\eta_{t}(v_{t}),\big|\xi_{t-1}\big|^{2}\big\rangle\Big|+2\rho\Big|\big\langle\eta_{t}(v_{t}),\Gamma\circ\big|\xi_{t-1}\big|\big\rangle\Big|

\displaystyle=\Big|\big\langle\eta_{t}(v_{t})\circ\eta_{t}^{\prime}(v_{t}),\xi_{t-1}\big\rangle\Big|+\rho_{1}\big\|\eta_{t}(v_{t})\big\|_{\infty}\Big\|\big|\xi_{t-1}\big|^{2}\Big\|_{1}+2\rho\Big|\big\langle\eta_{t}(v_{t})\circ\Gamma,\big|\xi_{t-1}\big|\big\rangle\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\big\|\eta_{t}(v_{t})\circ\eta_{t}^{\prime}(v_{t})\big\|_{2}\big\|\xi_{t-1}\big\|_{2}+\rho_{1}\big\|\eta_{t}(v_{t})\big\|_{\infty}\big\|\xi_{t-1}\big\|_{2}^{2}+2\rho\big\|\eta_{t}(v_{t})\big\|_{\infty}\big\|\Gamma\big\|_{2}\big\|\xi_{t-1}\big\|_{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim F_{t}\big\|\xi_{t-1}\big\|_{2}+\rho_{1}G_{t}\big\|\xi_{t-1}\big\|_{2}^{2}+\rho G_{t}\sqrt{E_{t}}\big\|\xi_{t-1}\big\|_{2}.

Here, the last line follows from Assumptions (
14e
) and (
14f
), as well as the fact that

\big\|\Gamma\big\|_{2}\leq\big\|\widetilde{\Gamma}\big\|_{2}\leq\sum_{m\in\mathcal{M}_{\mathsf{dc}}}\big\|\widetilde{\Gamma}(m)\big\|_{2}=\sum_{m\in\mathcal{M}_{\mathsf{dc}}}\bigg(\sum_{j=1}^{n}\widetilde{\Gamma}_{j}(m)\bigg)^{1/2},

where
\widetilde{\Gamma}(m)\coloneqq\big[\widetilde{\Gamma}_{j}(m)\big]_{1\leq j\leq n}\in\real^{n}

with

\widetilde{\Gamma}_{j}(m)=\ind\Big(\Big|\alpha_{t}v^{\star}_{j}+\sum\nolimits_{k=1}^{t-1}\beta_{t-1}^{k}\phi_{k,j}-m\Big|\leq\theta(m)\Big).

Further invoking
\sum_{m\in\mathcal{M}_{\mathsf{dc}}}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\widetilde{\Gamma}_{j}(m)\leq E_{t}
(see (
14h
)) and
|\mathcal{M}_{\mathsf{dc}}|=O(1)
gives

\displaystyle\big\|\Gamma\big\|_{2}\leq\sum_{m\in\mathcal{M}_{\mathsf{dc}}}\sqrt{E_{t}}\asymp\sqrt{E_{t}}.

This concludes the proof of the claim (
69b
).

Appendix C
Analysis for spectral initialization: Proof of Theorem
3

To establish Theorem
3
,
our strategy is to construct some auxiliary AMP sequences that are intimately connected to spectral initialization (obtained via a sequence of power iterations),
thus allowing us to analyze spectrally initialized AMP by means of the theory developed in Theorems
1
and
2
.
Note that the auxiliary AMP sequence to be introduced below is designed only for analysis purposes, and is not implemented during the execution of the real algorithm.

Throughout this section, we denote by
\lambda_{\max}
(resp.
\widehat{v}^{\star}
) the leading eigenvalue (resp. eigenvector) of
M
,
and let
\lambda_{i}(M)
represent the
i
-th largest eigenvalue (in magnitude) of
M
.

C.1
Preliminaries: non-asymptotic eigenvalue and eigenvector analysis

Understanding the performance of spectral methods requires careful control of the eigenvalues and eigenvectors of the random matrices of interest.
Before embarking on the proof, we gather several useful non-asymptotic eigenvalue/eigenvector perturbation bounds.

•

Bandeira and Van Handel, 2016
asserts that (by taking
\varepsilon
therein to be
(\log n/n)^{1/3}
)

\displaystyle\|W\|\leq 2+O\bigg(\Big(\frac{\log n}{n}\Big)^{1/3}\bigg),

(129a)

holds with probability at least
1-O(n^{-15})
. This combined with Weyl’s inequality further leads to

\displaystyle\big|\lambda_{2}(M)\big|\leq\|W\|\leq 2+O\bigg(\Big(\frac{\log n}{n}\Big)^{1/3}\bigg).

(129b)

•

Peng, 2012
establishes that, with probability at least
1-O(n^{-11})
one has

\displaystyle\lambda+\frac{1}{\lambda}-C_{9}\sqrt{\frac{\log n}{n(\lambda-1)^{5}}}\leq\lambda_{\max}\leq\lambda+\frac{1}{\lambda}+C_{9}\sqrt{\frac{\log n}{n}}

(130)

for some large enough constant
C_{9}>0
, provided that
1+\big(\frac{\log n}{n}\big)^{1/5}<\lambda=O(1)
.

•

Applying Weyl’s inequality (i.e.,
|\lambda_{2}(M)|\leq\|W\|
)
and
Simchowitz et al., 2018
(with
\epsilon=\frac{1}{8}\frac{\lambda+\frac{1}{\lambda}-2}{\lambda+\frac{1}{\lambda}}\min\big\{\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{\lambda^{2}-1}\big\}
and
\kappa=1/2
taken therein) yield

\displaystyle\lambda_{\max}-|\lambda_{2}(M)|

\displaystyle\geq\lambda_{\max}-\|W\|\geq\frac{\lambda+\frac{1}{\lambda}-2}{4}=\frac{(\lambda-1)^{2}}{4\lambda}

(131a)

\displaystyle\bigg|\lambda_{\max}-\lambda-\frac{1}{\lambda}\bigg|

\displaystyle\leq\min\left\{\frac{(\lambda-1)^{2}}{16\lambda},\frac{1}{8\lambda}\cdot\frac{\lambda-1}{\lambda+1}\right\}

(131b)

with probability at least
1-O(n^{-11})
, provided that

\displaystyle\lambda-1\geq C_{\lambda}\Big(\frac{\log(n\lambda)}{n}\Big)^{1/6}

(132)

for some sufficiently large constant
C_{\lambda}>0
.
A direct consequence of (
131b
) is that

\displaystyle\lambda_{\max}-2

\displaystyle\geq\lambda+\frac{1}{\lambda}-2-\frac{(\lambda-1)^{2}}{16\lambda}=\frac{15(\lambda-1)^{2}}{16\lambda}>0,

(133a)

\displaystyle\lambda_{\max}

\displaystyle\leq\lambda+\frac{1}{\lambda}+\frac{1}{8\lambda}\leq 3\lambda.

(133b)

•

In addition, we make note of an immediate consequence of (
131b
) as follows:

|\widetilde{\lambda}-\lambda|\leq\min\left\{C_{9}\sqrt{\frac{\log n}{n(\lambda-1)^{7}}},\frac{\lambda-1}{4},\frac{1}{2(\lambda+1)}\right\},

(134)

where we recall
\widetilde{\lambda}\coloneqq\frac{\lambda_{\max}+\sqrt{\lambda_{\max}^{2}-4}}{2}.

Proof of inequality (
134
).

In view of (
131b
), one can write
\lambda_{\max}=\lambda+\frac{1}{\lambda}+\Delta

for some
\Delta
with
|\Delta|\leq\min\big\{C_{9}\sqrt{\frac{\log n}{n(\lambda-1)^{5}}},\frac{(\lambda-1)^{2}}{16\lambda},\frac{1}{8\lambda}\frac{\lambda-1}{\lambda+1}\big\}
.
It is readily seen that

\displaystyle\Bigg|\frac{\lambda_{\max}+\sqrt{\lambda_{\max}^{2}-4}}{2}-\lambda\Bigg|

\displaystyle=\Bigg|\frac{\lambda+\frac{1}{\lambda}+\Delta+\sqrt{\left(\lambda+\frac{1}{\lambda}+\Delta\right)^{2}-4}}{2}-\lambda\Bigg|

\displaystyle=\Bigg|\frac{\sqrt{\left(\lambda+\frac{1}{\lambda}+\Delta\right)^{2}-4}-\sqrt{\left(\lambda+\frac{1}{\lambda}\right)^{2}-4}}{2}+\frac{\Delta}{2}\Bigg|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}\frac{|2\Delta\left(\lambda+\frac{1}{\lambda}\right)+\Delta^{2}|}{\sqrt{\left(\lambda+\frac{1}{\lambda}+\Delta\right)^{2}-4}+\sqrt{\left(\lambda+\frac{1}{\lambda}\right)^{2}-4}}+\frac{|\Delta|}{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{3|\Delta|\lambda}{\lambda-\frac{1}{\lambda}}+\frac{|\Delta|}{2}\leq\frac{4|\Delta|\lambda}{\lambda-1},

where the last line follows since
\lambda+\frac{1}{\lambda}\leq 2\lambda

and
|\Delta|\leq\lambda+\frac{1}{\lambda}
. This directly concludes the proof.
∎

Furthermore, the following lemma develops a non-asymptotic bound on the correlation between the leading eigenvector
\widehat{v}^{\star}
and the ground truth
v^{\star}
; the proof can be found in Section
C.5
.

Lemma 10
.

Suppose that
1+C_{\lambda}\big(\frac{\log n}{n}\big)^{1/9}\leq\lambda=O(1)
for some large enough constant
C_{\lambda}>0
.
The correlation between
v^{\star}
and the leading eigenvector
\widehat{v}^{\star}
of
M
satisfies

\big|\big\langle\widehat{v}^{\star},v^{\star}\big\rangle\big|=\sqrt{1-\frac{1}{\lambda^{2}}}+O\Big(\sqrt{\frac{\log n}{(\lambda-1)^{9}n}}\Big)

(135)

with probability at least
1-O(n^{-11})
.

C.2
Constructing an AMP-style basis that covers
\widehat{v}^{\star}
approximately

In this subsection, we design an auxiliary AMP sequence that allows us to construct a set of orthonormal vectors
\{y_{s}\}_{1\leq k\leq s}
,
whose span
approximately
covers the leading eigenvector
\widehat{v}^{\star}
of
M
.

Construction of auxiliary AMP iterates.

Let us produce the following iterative procedure initialized at the truth
v^{\star}
:

\displaystyle\omega_{t+1}=W\omega_{t}-\omega_{t-1},\qquad(t\geq 1)\qquad\text{with }~w_{0}=0~\text{ and }~w_{1}=v^{\star}.

(136)

This iterative procedure involves a power iteration
W\omega_{t}
in each iteration,
while at the same time it takes the form of AMP updates (by subtracting
\omega_{t-1}
and choosing the denoiser to be the identity function).
Note, however,
that the power iteration
W\omega_{t}
in (
136
) is concerned with only the noise matrix
W
,
which stands in stark contrast to (
3
) that consists of computing
(\lambda v^{\star}v^{\star\top}+W)\eta_{t}(x_{t})
and involves the signal component
\lambda v^{\star}v^{\star\top}
.
In fact, the signal component comes into play in (
136
) only through the initial vector
v_{1}=v^{\star}.

Other auxiliary sequences derived from
\{\omega_{k}\}
.

Akin to our proof of Theorem
1
(see Section
4.1
),
we find it useful to look at several auxiliary sequences
\{y_{k}\}_{k\geq 1}
,
\{\zeta_{k}^{\prime}\}
and
\{\psi_{k}\}
derived based on
\{\omega_{k}\}
,
which will assist in analyzing
\{\omega_{t}\}
.

(i)

Given that
\omega_{1}=v^{\star}
, we define

\displaystyle y_{1}\coloneqq\omega_{1}=v^{\star}\in\mathcal{S}^{n-1},\qquad\text{and}\qquad W_{1}^{\prime}\coloneqq W.

(137)

(ii)

For each
2\leq t<n
, concatenate
\{y_{k}\}
into a matrix
V_{t-1}\coloneqq[y_{k}]_{1\leq k\leq t-1}\in\real^{n\times(t-1)}

and define

\displaystyle y_{t}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\frac{\left(I-V_{t-1}V_{t-1}^{\top}\right)\omega_{t}}{\left\|\left(I-V_{t-1}V_{t-1}^{\top}\right)\omega_{t}\right\|_{2}},

(138)

\displaystyle W_{t}^{\prime}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\left(I-y_{t-1}y_{t-1}^{\top}\right)W_{t-1}^{\prime}\left(I-y_{t-1}y_{t-1}^{\top}\right).

According to Lemma
1
, the
y_{k}
’s constructed above are orthonormal, and
\omega_{t}\in\mathsf{span}\{y_{1},\ldots,y_{t}\}
.

(iii)

Additionally, if we generate
\{g_{i}^{k}\}_{1\leq i,k\leq n}
as i.i.d.
\mathcal{N}(0,\frac{1}{n})
and define

\displaystyle\zeta_{k}^{\prime}\coloneqq\Big(\frac{\sqrt{2}}{2}-1\Big)y_{k}y_{k}^{\top}W_{k}^{\prime}y_{k}+\sum_{i=1}^{k-1}g_{i}^{k}y_{i}\in\real^{n},\qquad 1\leq k\leq n,

(139)

then Lemma
2
reveals that the
\psi_{k}
’s constructed below are i.i.d. obeying

\displaystyle\psi_{k}\coloneqq W_{k}^{\prime}y_{k}+\zeta_{k}^{\prime}\overset{\text{i.i.d.}}{\sim}\mathcal{N}\left(0,\frac{1}{n}I_{n}\right),\qquad 1\leq k\leq n.

(140)

Clearly,
\{y_{k},W_{k}^{\prime},\zeta_{k}^{\prime},\psi_{k}\}
plays the same role as
\{z_{k},W_{k},\zeta_{k},\phi_{k}\}
in expression (
43
)
in the proof of Theorem
1
.

Connections between
\{\omega_{k}\}
and spectral initialization.

We now discuss some important connections between
\{\omega_{k}\}
and the leading eigenvector
\widehat{v}^{\star}
of
M
.
One basic fact to connect (
136
) with the power method
is that:
W^{t}v^{\star}
can be linearly represented by the iterates
\{\omega_{i}\}_{1\leq i\leq t+1}
,
as stated in the following lemma. Intuitively, this fact makes sense as the update rule (
136
) resembles that of the power method.

Lemma 11
.

For every
t\geq 0
,

W^{t}v^{\star}
is a linear combination of
\{\omega_{i}\}_{1\leq i\leq t+1}
.

Proof of Lemma
11
.

We shall establish this result by induction.
First, the claim is trivially true for
t=0
since
\omega_{1}=v^{\star}
.
Now, suppose the statement further holds true for
t-1
, i.e.,

\displaystyle W^{t-1}v^{\star}=\sum_{i=1}^{t}c_{t}^{i}\omega_{i}\qquad\text{for some coefficients }c_{t}^{i},

(141)

and we would like to extend it to
t
. Towards this, observe that

\displaystyle W^{t}v^{\star}=W\big(W^{t-1}v^{\star}\big)=W\sum_{i=1}^{t}c_{t}^{i}\omega_{i}=\sum_{i=1}^{t}c_{t}^{i}(\omega_{i+1}+\omega_{i-1}),

where the last step follows since
W\omega_{i}=\omega_{i+1}+\omega_{i-1}
(cf. (
136
)).
For notational simplicity, we shall also set

\displaystyle c_{t}^{i}=0\qquad\text{for any }i>t\text{ or }i=0.

(142)

As a result, we can write

W^{t}v^{\star}=\sum_{i=1}^{t+1}c_{t+1}^{i}\omega_{i}

(143a)

with the coefficients

\displaystyle c_{t+1}^{i}=c_{t}^{i+1}+c_{t}^{i-1};

(143b)

here, we have invoked (
142
).

This validates the claimed result.
∎

Moreover, it turns out that
\widehat{v}^{\star}
can be approximately
represented as (i) a linear combination of
\{y_{k}\}_{1\leq k\leq s}
,
and also (ii) a linear combination of the set of independent Gaussian vectors
\{\psi_{k}\}_{1\leq k\leq s}
(cf. (
140
)).
This is asserted by the following lemma, whose proof can be found in Section
C.6
.

Lemma 12
.

Let
s=\frac{C_{v}\lambda^{2}\log n}{(\lambda-1)^{2}}
for some sufficiently large constant
C_{v}>0
.
Assume that
1+C_{\lambda}(\frac{\log^{7}n}{n})^{1/6}<\lambda=O(1)
for some large enough constant
C_{\lambda}>0
.
With probability at least
1-O(n^{-11})
, there exist coefficients
c_{i}

(1\leq i\leq s)
such that

\displaystyle\bigg\|\widehat{v}^{\star}-\sum_{i=1}^{s}c_{i}y_{i}\bigg\|_{2}\lesssim\frac{\log^{3.5}n}{\sqrt{(\lambda-1)^{6}n}}\qquad\text{and}\qquad\bigg\|\widehat{v}^{\star}-c_{1}v^{\star}+\frac{1}{\widetilde{\lambda}}\sum_{i=1}^{s}c_{i}\psi_{i}\bigg\|_{2}\lesssim\frac{\log^{3.5}n}{\sqrt{(\lambda-1)^{6}n}},

(144)

where
\widetilde{\lambda}\coloneqq\frac{2}{\lambda_{\max}-\sqrt{\lambda_{\max}^{2}-4}}
, and
c_{i+1}=\widetilde{\lambda}^{-1}c_{i}
for all
i\geq 1
.

This approximate linear representation of
\widehat{v}^{\star}

plays a crucial role in explaining why spectrally initialized AMP yields a similar decomposition as
another AMP with independent initialization.

C.3
Constructing another AMP-style basis that covers
x_{1}
exactly

Next, we turn to our spectral estimate obtained through the power method:

x_{1}=a_{s}M^{s}\widetilde{v}\qquad\text{with }a_{s}=\frac{1}{\|M^{s}\widetilde{v}\|_{2}},

where
\widetilde{v}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\frac{1}{n}I_{n})
is the initial vector of the power method chosen randomly.
Based upon our results in Section
C.2
, we intend to further augment
\{y_{k}\}_{1\leq k\leq s}

into a set of
2s+1
orthonormal vectors
\{\widehat{y}_{t}\}_{1\leq t\leq 2s+1}

— again via a certain auxiliary AMP sequence —
such that
x_{1}
falls
perfectly
within
\mathsf{span}\{\widehat{y}_{1},\ldots,\widehat{y}_{2s+1}\}
.

Preliminaries about the power method.

Standard convergence analysis for the power method tells us that:
if we take
s\geq\widetilde{C}_{v}\frac{\lambda_{\max}\log n}{\lambda_{\max}-|\lambda_{2}(M)|}
for some constant
\widetilde{C}_{v}>0
large enough
and if
\widetilde{v}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\frac{1}{n}I_{n})
, then with probability exceeding
1-O(n^{-11})
we can guarantee that

\displaystyle\|x_{1}-\widehat{v}^{\star}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{n^{12}}\qquad\text{ and }\qquad

(145a)

\displaystyle a_{s}=\frac{1}{\|M^{s}\widetilde{v}\|_{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\lambda_{\max}^{s}|\langle\widehat{v}^{\star},\widetilde{v}\rangle|}\lesssim\frac{n^{11.5}}{\lambda_{\max}^{s}},

(145b)

where the last inequality is valid since

\langle\widehat{v}^{\star},\widetilde{v}\rangle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{d}}}{{=}}\frac{\langle g,\,v\rangle}{\|g\|_{2}},~g\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\frac{1}{n}I_{n})
and hence
|\langle\widehat{v}^{\star},\widetilde{v}\rangle|\gtrsim n^{-11.5}
with probability at least
1-O(n^{-11})
.
In addition, (
131a
) and (
131b
)
allow us to control
\frac{\lambda_{\max}}{\lambda_{\max}-|\lambda_{2}(M)|}
,
thus indicating that (
145
) is guaranteed to hold as long as

s\geq C_{v}\frac{\lambda\log n}{(\lambda-1)^{2}}

for some constant
C_{v}
large enough.

In addition, we remark that there exist a set of coefficients
a_{0},\ldots,a_{s-1}\in\real
that allow us to express

\displaystyle x_{1}=\sum_{i=0}^{s-1}a_{i}W^{i}v^{\star}+a_{s}W^{s}\widetilde{v},\qquad\text{with }a_{s}=\frac{1}{\|M^{s}\widetilde{v}\|_{2}}.

(146)

Proof of (
146
).

Recall that
x_{1}
is proportional to
(\lambda v^{\star}v^{\star\top}+W)^{s}\widetilde{v}
.
By expanding
(\lambda v^{\star}v^{\star\top}+W)^{s}
, we know that each term in the expansion takes one of the following forms:

\text{(i) }\lambda v^{\star}v^{\star\top}A_{1}\widetilde{v}\text{ for some matrix }A_{1};\quad\text{(ii) }W^{i}(\lambda v^{\star}v^{\star\top})A_{2}\widetilde{v}\text{ for some matrix }A_{2}\text{ and some }i;\quad\text{(iii) }W^{s}\widetilde{\nu}.

Clearly, in each case the term falls within the span of
\{v^{\star},W^{i}v^{\star},W^{s}\widetilde{v}\}
, thus concluding the proof.
∎

Construction of a set of basis vectors using another auxiliary AMP.

Based on the decomposition (
146
),
we intend to show that
x_{1}
can be linearly represented (in an exact manner) using a set of
2s+1
orthonormal basis vectors,
in a way similar to Lemma
12
.
Towards this end, we design another AMP-type algorithm (with the denoising functions taken as the identity function):

\displaystyle u_{t+1}=Wu_{t}-u_{t-1}\quad(t>s),\qquad\text{with }~u_{s}=0~\text{ and }~u_{s+1}=\widetilde{v},

(147)

Despite the use of the same update rule,
a key difference between (
147
) and (
136
) lies in that
u_{t}
starts from
\widetilde{v}
(i.e., the vector used to initialize the power method), while
\omega_{t}
starts from the ground-truth vector
v^{\star}
.

Akin to our analysis for Theorem
1
, we generate a sequence of orthonormal vectors
\{\widehat{y}_{k}\}
and auxiliary random matrices
\widehat{W}_{k}
as follows: recalling the sequence
\{y_{k},W_{k}\}
defined in (
138
), we take

\displaystyle\widehat{y}_{t}

\displaystyle\coloneqq y_{t},\qquad

\displaystyle 1\leq t\leq s,

(148)

\displaystyle\widehat{W}_{t}

\displaystyle\coloneqq W_{t}^{\prime},\qquad

\displaystyle 1\leq t\leq s,

\displaystyle\widehat{y}_{t}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\frac{\big(I-\widehat{V}_{t-1}\widehat{V}_{t-1}^{\top}\big)u_{t}}{\big\|\big(I-\widehat{V}_{t-1}\widehat{V}_{t-1}^{\top}\big)u_{t}\big\|_{2}},\qquad

\displaystyle s<t\leq 2s+1,

\displaystyle\widehat{W}_{t}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\big(I-\widehat{y}_{t-1}\widehat{y}_{t-1}^{\top}\big)\widehat{W}_{t-1}\big(I-\widehat{y}_{t-1}\widehat{y}_{t-1}^{\top}\big),\qquad

\displaystyle s<t\leq 2s+1,

where
\widehat{V}_{t-1}\coloneqq[\widehat{y}_{k}]_{1\leq k\leq t-1}\in\real^{n\times(t-1)}
.
The orthonormality of the sequence
\{\widehat{y}_{k}\}_{1\leq k\leq 2s+1}
can be seen by repeating the proof of Lemma
1
.
In addition, let us further generate the following vectors

\displaystyle\widehat{\psi}_{k}\coloneqq\widehat{W}_{k}\widehat{y}_{k}+\widehat{\zeta}_{k}\overset{\text{i.i.d.}}{\sim}\mathcal{N}\left(0,\frac{1}{n}I_{n}\right),\qquad\text{for all }1\leq k\leq n,

(149)

where

\displaystyle\widehat{\zeta}_{k}\coloneqq\Big(\frac{\sqrt{2}}{2}-1\Big)\widehat{y}_{k}\widehat{y}_{k}^{\top}\widehat{W}_{k}\widehat{y}_{k}+\sum_{i=1}^{k-1}g_{i}^{k}\widehat{y}_{i},

(150)

with the
g_{i}^{k}
’s independently drawn from
\mathcal{N}(0,\frac{1}{n})
.
Then Lemma
2
and its analysis immediately tell us that the
\widehat{\psi}_{k}
’s are statistically independent obeying

\displaystyle\widehat{\psi}_{k}\overset{\text{i.i.d.}}{\sim}\mathcal{N}\left(0,\frac{1}{n}I_{n}\right),\qquad\text{for all }1\leq k\leq n,

(151)

Linear representation of
x_{1}.

We are positioned to represent
x_{1}
over the set of basis vectors It turns out that
x_{1}
can be represented approximately as the linear combination of
\{\widehat{y}_{k}\}
, or the set of independent Gaussian vectors
\{\widehat{\psi}_{k}\}
.
Our result is formally stated as follows.

Lemma 13
.

With probability exceeding
1-O(n^{-11})
, we have

\displaystyle x_{1}=\sum_{i=1}^{2s+1}b_{i}\widehat{y}_{i}\qquad\text{and}\qquad\Bigg\|x_{1}-c_{1}v^{\star}-\frac{1}{\lambda}\sum_{i=1}^{2s+1}b_{i}\widehat{\psi}_{i}\Bigg\|_{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\log^{3.5}n}{\sqrt{(\lambda-1)^{7}n}}

(152)

with

b_{i}\coloneqq\langle\widehat{y}_{i},\,x_{1}\rangle\qquad\text{for any }1\leq i\leq 2s+1.

(153)

The proof of this lemma is deferred to Section
C.7

C.4
Analysis for spectrally initialized AMP

We are now positioned to develop non-asymptotic analysis for the spectrally initialized AMP, namely, the AMP sequence
\{x_{t}\}
(cf. (
3
)) when initialized to
x_{1}
(i.e., the output of the power method).

Auxiliary sequences derived from
\{x_{t}\}
.

Akin to the proof of Theorem
1
, we introduce a sequence of auxiliary vectors/matrices
\{z_{k},W_{k},\zeta_{k}\}_{-2s\leq k\leq n}
in a recursive manner in order to help understand the dynamics of
x_{t}
:

•

For any
k
with
-2s\leq k\leq 0
, set

\displaystyle z_{k}\coloneqq\widehat{y}_{k+2s+1},\quad W_{k}\coloneqq\widehat{W}_{k+2s+1}^{\prime},\quad\zeta_{k}\coloneqq\zeta_{k+2s+1}^{\prime},\quad\phi_{k}\coloneqq\widehat{\psi}_{k+2s+1},

where
\{\widehat{y}_{k},\widehat{W}_{k},\widehat{\psi}_{k}\}
have been introduced in Section
C.3
.

•

For any
1\leq k\leq n
, define

\displaystyle z_{1}\coloneqq\frac{\big(I-\widehat{V}_{2s+1}\widehat{V}_{2s+1}^{\top}\big)\eta_{1}(x_{1})}{\big\|\big(I-\widehat{V}_{2s+1}\widehat{V}_{2s+1}^{\top}\big)\eta_{1}(x_{1})\big\|_{2}}\in\real^{n},

(154a)

where we remind the readers that
\widehat{V}_{2s+1}=[\widehat{y}_{1},\ldots,\widehat{y}_{2s+1}]
.
Further, we take

\displaystyle U_{k-1}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\big[\widehat{V}_{2s+1},~[z_{i}]_{1\leq i\leq k-1}\big]\in\real^{n\times(k+2s)},

(154b)

\displaystyle z_{k}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\frac{\left(I-U_{k-1}U_{k-1}^{\top}\right)\eta_{k}(x_{k})}{\left\|\left(I-U_{k-1}U_{k-1}^{\top}\right)\eta_{k}(x_{k})\right\|_{2}},

\displaystyle W_{k}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\left(I-z_{k-1}z_{k-1}^{\top}\right)W_{k-1}\left(I-z_{k-1}z_{k-1}^{\top}\right).

With these definitions in place, we see that for each
t\geq 1
, the vectors
\{z_{k}\}^{t}_{-2s}
are orthonormal whose span contains
\eta_{t}(x_{t})
(see Lemma
1
and the text right after),
This allows us to decompose

\displaystyle\eta_{t}(x_{t})=\sum_{k=-2s}^{t}\beta_{t}^{k}z_{k},\qquad\text{with }\beta_{t}^{k}\coloneqq\langle\eta_{t}(x_{t}),z_{k}\rangle

(155)

and ensure that
\left\|\eta_{t}(x_{t})\right\|_{2}=\left\|\beta_{t}\right\|_{2}
with
\beta_{t}\coloneqq(\beta_{t}^{-2s},\ldots,\beta_{t}^{0},\beta_{t}^{1},\beta_{t}^{2},\ldots,\beta_{t}^{t})^{\top}\in\real^{t+2s+1}.

Additionally, we introduce the following vectors as in Lemma
2
:

\displaystyle\zeta_{k}\coloneqq\Big(\frac{\sqrt{2}}{2}-1\Big)z_{k}z_{k}^{\top}W_{k}z_{k}+\sum_{i=-2s}^{k-1}g_{i}^{k}z_{i}\qquad\text{for }k\geq 1,

(156)

with each
g_{i}^{k}
independently generated from
\mathcal{N}(0,\frac{1}{n})
, and we set

\displaystyle\phi_{k}\coloneqq W_{k}z_{k}+\zeta_{k},\qquad\text{for all }-2s\leq k<n-2s.

(157)

Consequently, repeating exactly the same argument as in the proof of Lemma
2
reveals that

\displaystyle\phi_{k}\overset{\text{i.i.d.}}{\sim}\mathcal{N}\left(0,\frac{1}{n}I_{n}\right),\qquad\text{for all }-2s\leq k<n-2s.

(158)

Analyzing spectrally initialized AMP via our general recipe.

Recall that Theorem
1
offers a general recipe in deriving the decomposition for
x_{t}
.
As it turns out, the same induction-based proof idea developed for Theorem
1
continues to work for analyzing spectrally initialized AMP.
In fact, assuming validity for the initialization (which we shall justify momentarily), such proof arguments lead to:

\displaystyle x_{t}\coloneqq\alpha_{t}v^{\star}+\sum_{k=-2s}^{t-1}\beta_{t-1}^{k}\phi_{k}+\xi_{t-1},\qquad\text{for }1\leq t<n-2s;

(159)

here,
\alpha_{t+1}=\lambda v^{\star\top}\eta_{t}(x_{t})
, and
\xi_{t-1}
is the residual term obeying

\displaystyle\xi_{t}

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=-2s}^{t-1}z_{k}\bigg[\Big\langle\phi_{k},\eta_{t}\big(\alpha_{t}v^{\star}+\sum_{k=-2s}^{t-1}\beta_{t-1}^{k}\phi_{k}+\xi_{t-1}\big)\Big\rangle-\langle\eta_{t}^{\prime}(x_{t})\rangle\beta_{t-1}^{k}-(\sqrt{2}-1)\beta_{t}^{k}z_{k}^{\top}W_{k}z_{k}-\sum_{i=-2s}^{k-1}\beta_{t}^{i}g_{i}^{k}-\sum_{i=k+1}^{t}\beta_{t}^{i}g_{k}^{i}\bigg].

Following Steps 2 and 3 verbatim in Section
4.1
, we see that (
159
)
holds true for
t+1
and satisfies

\displaystyle\|\xi_{t}\|_{2}

\displaystyle=\Big\langle\sum_{k=-2s}^{t-1}\mu_{t}^{k}\phi_{k},\delta_{t}\Big\rangle-\langle\delta_{t}^{\prime}\rangle\sum_{k=-2s}^{t-1}\mu_{t}^{k}\beta_{t-1}^{k}+\Delta_{t}-\sum_{k=-2s}^{t-1}\mu_{t}^{k}\left[(\sqrt{2}-1)\beta_{t}^{k}z_{k}^{\top}W_{k}z_{k}+\sum_{i=-2s}^{k-1}\beta_{t}^{i}g_{i}^{k}+\sum_{i=k+1}^{t}\beta_{t}^{i}g_{k}^{i}\right],

where
\Delta_{t}
,
\delta_{t}
and
\delta_{t}^{\prime}
are defined in (
23
).
Further, taking this collectively with Lemma
3
establishes (
21
).

We still need to verify that the spectral estimate
x_{1}
also satisfies the desired decomposition (
159
).
Towards this, note that if we choose
x_{1}=\lambda\eta_{0}(x_{0})

(e.g., taking
x_{1}=\lambda x_{0}
and choosing
\eta_{0}
to be identity),
then

\displaystyle\beta_{0}^{k}\coloneqq\frac{1}{\lambda}b_{k+2s+1}=\frac{1}{\lambda}\langle x_{1},\,\widehat{y}_{k+2s+1}\rangle=\frac{1}{\lambda}\langle x_{1},\,z_{k}\rangle=\big\langle\eta_{0}(x_{0}),z_{k}\big\rangle,

(160)

where we have used
(
153
)
. This combined with inequality (
152
) gives

\displaystyle\bigg\|x_{1}-c_{1}v^{\star}-\sum_{i=-2s}^{0}\beta_{0}^{k}\phi_{k}\bigg\|_{2}=\bigg\|x_{1}-c_{1}v^{\star}-\frac{1}{\lambda}\sum_{i=-2s}^{0}b_{k+2s+1}\widehat{\psi}_{k+2s+1}\bigg\|_{2}\lesssim\frac{\log^{3.5}n}{\sqrt{(\lambda-1)^{7}n}},

In addition, Lemma
12
tells us that

\big|c_{1}-\big\langle y_{1},\widehat{v}^{\star}\big\rangle\big|=\bigg|\Big\langle y_{1},\sum_{i=1}^{s}c_{i}y_{i}\Big\rangle-\big\langle y_{1},\widehat{v}^{\star}\big\rangle\bigg|\leq\bigg\|\sum_{i=1}^{s}c_{i}y_{i}-\widehat{v}^{\star}\bigg\|_{2}\lesssim\frac{\log^{3.5}n}{\sqrt{(\lambda-1)^{6}n}},

where we have used the fact that
y_{1}=v^{\star}
and the orthonormality of
\{y_{i}\}
.
Combining this with Lemma
10
implies that

\Big|c_{1}-\sqrt{1-\frac{1}{\lambda^{2}}}\Big|\leq\big|c_{1}-\big\langle y_{1},\widehat{v}^{\star}\big\rangle\big|+\Big|\big\langle y_{1},\widehat{v}^{\star}\big\rangle-\sqrt{1-\frac{1}{\lambda^{2}}}\Big|\lesssim\frac{\log^{3.5}n}{\sqrt{(\lambda-1)^{9}n}}.

Putting the preceding results together, we can express

\displaystyle x_{1}=\sqrt{1-\frac{1}{\lambda^{2}}}\,v^{\star}+\sum_{i=-2s}^{0}\beta_{0}^{k}\phi_{k}+\xi_{0}

(161)

where
\|\xi_{0}\|_{2}\lesssim\frac{\log^{3.5}n}{\sqrt{(\lambda-1)^{9}n}}
.
This justifies the validity of (
159
) for the base case with
t=1
, thus concluding the proof of Theorem
3
.

C.5
Proof of Lemma
10

Given the rotational invariance of the Wigner matrix
W
, we shall
assume without loss of generality that
v^{\star}=e_{1}
throughout
this proof. We also introduce the convenient notation
W=\left[\begin{array}[]{cc}W_{1,1}&w_{n-1}^{\top}\\
w_{n-1}&W_{n-1}\end{array}\right]
, where
W_{n-1}\in\mathbb{R}^{(n-1)\times(n-1)}
and
w_{n-1}\in\mathbb{R}^{n-1}

are statistically independent.

To begin with, it is readily seen from (
131a
)
that
\lambda_{\max}I_{n-1}-W_{n-1}
is invertible with probability
at least
1-O(n^{-11})
. Apply
Li et al., 2021

to show that

\big|\big\langle\widehat{v}^{\star},v^{\star}\big\rangle\big|^{2}=\frac{1}{1+\big\|\big(\lambda_{\max}I_{n-1}-W_{n-1}\big)^{-1}w_{n-1}\big\|_{2}^{2}}\eqqcolon\frac{1}{1+\big\|\sqrt{n}R(\lambda_{\max})w_{n-1}\big\|_{2}^{2}},

where for notational simplicity we define, for any
\lambda_{0}>\|W\|
,

R(\lambda_{0})\coloneqq\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\big(\lambda_{0}I_{n-1}-W_{n-1}\big)^{-1}.

In what follows, let us first analyze the target quantity for any
fixed
\lambda_{0}\in\big[\frac{7}{8}\big(\lambda+\frac{1}{\lambda}\big)+\frac{1}{4},3\lambda]
.

•

First of all,
Peng, 2012
combined
with a little algebra implies that

\Bigg|\big\|R(\lambda_{0})\big\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}-\frac{1}{\big(\frac{\lambda_{0}+\sqrt{\lambda_{0}^{2}-4}}{2}\big)^{2}-1}\Bigg|=\Bigg|\mathsf{Tr}\Big[\big(\lambda_{0}I_{n-1}-W_{n-1}\big)^{-2}\Big]-\frac{1}{\big(\frac{\lambda_{0}+\sqrt{\lambda_{0}^{2}-4}}{2}\big)^{2}-1}\Bigg|\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{\log n}{(\lambda-1)^{8}n}}

holds with probability at least
1-O(n^{-15})
, provided that
1<\lambda=O(1)
.
As a result,

\big\|R(\lambda_{0})\big\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}=\frac{1}{\big(\frac{\lambda_{0}+\sqrt{\lambda_{0}^{2}-4}}{2}\big)^{2}-1}+O\bigg(\sqrt{\frac{\log n}{(\lambda-1)^{8}n}}\bigg)\lesssim\frac{1}{\lambda-1},

(162)

where the last relation holds since
\lambda_{0}\geq\lambda+\frac{1}{\lambda}-\frac{1}{4}\big(\lambda+\frac{1}{\lambda}-2\big)
and hence

\frac{\lambda_{0}+\sqrt{\lambda_{0}^{2}-4}}{2}-1\gtrsim\lambda-1
(by repeating the proof of inequality (
134
) with
\Delta=\frac{1}{4}\big(\lambda+\frac{1}{\lambda}-2
).
In addition, it follows from (
129
) that

\|R(\lambda_{0})\|\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\frac{1}{\lambda_{0}-\|W_{n-1}\|}\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\cdot\frac{1}{\frac{7}{8}\big(\lambda+\frac{1}{\lambda}\big)+\frac{1}{4}-2-O\big((\frac{\log n}{n})^{1/3}\big)}\lesssim\frac{1}{(\lambda-1)^{2}\sqrt{n}}.

(163)

•

Further, invoking
Rudelson and Vershynin, 2013
(with
\varepsilon

therein taken to be
C_{9}\frac{\|R(\lambda_{0})\|\sqrt{\log n}}{\|R(\lambda_{0})\|_{\mathrm{F}}}

for some large enough constant
C_{9}>0
) reveals that, conditional
on
W_{n-1}
,

\Big|\big\|\sqrt{n}R(\lambda_{0})w_{n-1}\big\|^{2}-\|R(\lambda_{0})\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\Big|\leq C_{9}\|R(\lambda_{0})\|_{\mathrm{F}}\|R(\lambda_{0})\|\sqrt{\log n}\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{\log n}{(\lambda-1)^{5}n}}

holds with probability at least
1-O(n^{-15})
, provided that
C_{9}\frac{\|R(\lambda_{0})\|\sqrt{\log n}}{\|R(\lambda_{0})\|_{\mathrm{F}}}<1

(which is guaranteed to hold due to (
162
) and (
163
)).

•

Combine the above results to yield, with probability at least
1-O(n^{-15})
,

\displaystyle\Bigg|\frac{1}{1+\big\|\sqrt{n}R(\lambda_{0})w_{n-1}\big\|_{2}^{2}}-\frac{1}{1+\frac{1}{\big(\frac{\lambda_{0}+\sqrt{\lambda_{0}^{2}-4}}{2}\big)^{2}-1}}\Bigg|

\displaystyle\leq\Bigg|\big\|\sqrt{n}R(\lambda_{0})w_{n-1}\big\|_{2}^{2}-\frac{1}{\big(\frac{\lambda_{0}+\sqrt{\lambda_{0}^{2}-4}}{2}\big)^{2}-1}\Bigg|\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{\log n}{(\lambda-1)^{8}n}}.

Next, invoke standard epsilon-net argument
(
Vershynin, 2018
, Chapter 4.2)

to show that

\displaystyle\Bigg|\frac{1}{1+\big\|\sqrt{n}R(\lambda_{0})w_{n-1}\big\|_{2}^{2}}-\frac{1}{1+\frac{1}{\big(\frac{\lambda_{0}+\sqrt{\lambda_{0}^{2}-4}}{2}\big)^{2}-1}}\Bigg|

\displaystyle\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{\log n}{(\lambda-1)^{8}n}},\qquad\forall\lambda_{0}\in\Big[\frac{7}{8}\big(\lambda+\frac{1}{\lambda}\big)+\frac{1}{4},3\lambda\Big]

with probability at least
1-O(n^{-11})
; we omit this standard argument
here for the sake of brevity. Recognizing that
\lambda_{\max}\in\big[\frac{7}{8}\big(\lambda+\frac{1}{\lambda}\big)+\frac{1}{4},3\lambda]

(see (
131b
) and (
133
)) and defining
\widetilde{\lambda}\coloneqq\frac{\lambda_{\max}+\sqrt{\lambda_{\max}^{2}-4}}{2}
,
we immediately obtain

\displaystyle\big|\big\langle\widehat{v}^{\star},v^{\star}\big\rangle\big|^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{1+\big\|\sqrt{n}R(\lambda_{\max})w_{n-1}\big\|_{2}^{2}}=\frac{1}{1+\frac{1}{\widetilde{\lambda}^{2}-1}}+O\Big(\sqrt{\frac{\log n}{(\lambda-1)^{8}n}}\Big)=1-\frac{1}{\widetilde{\lambda}^{2}}+O\Big(\sqrt{\frac{\log n}{(\lambda-1)^{8}n}}\Big)

\displaystyle=1-\frac{1}{\lambda^{2}}+O\Big(\frac{|\lambda-\widetilde{\lambda}|}{\lambda\widetilde{\lambda}}\Big)+O\Big(\sqrt{\frac{\log n}{(\lambda-1)^{8}n}}\Big)=1-\frac{1}{\lambda^{2}}+O\Big(\sqrt{\frac{\log n}{(\lambda-1)^{8}n}}\Big),

where the last inequality comes from (
134
)
and
\lambda\asymp 1
. Consequently, we arrive at

\Bigg|\big|\big\langle\widehat{v}^{\star},v^{\star}\big\rangle\big|-\sqrt{1-\frac{1}{\lambda^{2}}}\Bigg|=\frac{\Big|\big|\big\langle\widehat{v}^{\star},v^{\star}\big\rangle\big|^{2}-\big(1-\frac{1}{\lambda^{2}}\big)\Big|}{\big|\big\langle\widehat{v}^{\star},v^{\star}\big\rangle\big|+\sqrt{1-\frac{1}{\lambda^{2}}}}\lesssim\frac{\sqrt{\frac{\log n}{(\lambda-1)^{8}n}}}{\sqrt{1-\frac{1}{\lambda^{2}}}}\asymp\sqrt{\frac{\log n}{(\lambda-1)^{9}n}}.

C.6
Proof of Lemma
12

Recall that the leading eigenvector
\widehat{v}^{\star}
of
M
satisfies
M\widehat{v}^{\star}=\lambda_{\max}\widehat{v}^{\star}
.
In view of the Neumann expansion for eigenvectors (see, e.g.
(
Chen et al., 2021a
, Theorem 2)
),
\widehat{v}^{\star}
admits the following expansion:

\displaystyle\widehat{v}^{\star}=\widetilde{c}_{0}\sum_{t=0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{\lambda_{\max}^{t}}W^{t}v^{\star},\qquad\text{with }\widetilde{c}_{0}=\frac{\lambda}{\lambda_{\max}}\langle v^{\star},\widehat{v}^{\star}\rangle,

(164)

with the proviso that
\|W\|<\lambda_{\max}
— a condition that has been guaranteed in (
131a
).
Clearly, one has

|\widetilde{c}_{0}|=\frac{\lambda}{\lambda_{\max}}\big|\langle v^{\star},\widehat{v}^{\star}\rangle\big|\leq\big|\langle v^{\star},\widehat{v}^{\star}\rangle\big|\leq 1.

(165)

Next, it follows from Lemma
11
that
W^{t}v^{\star}
can be written as a linear combination of
\{\omega_{i}\}_{1\leq i\leq t+1}
with
\omega_{i}
defined in (
136
).
Substituting (
143
) into expression (
164
) yields

\displaystyle\widehat{v}^{\star}

\displaystyle=\widetilde{c}_{0}\sum_{t=0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{\lambda_{\max}^{t}}W^{t}v^{\star}=\widetilde{c}_{0}\sum_{t=0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{\lambda_{\max}^{t}}\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{t+1}c_{t+1}^{i}\omega_{i}\Big)=\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}\Big(\widetilde{c}_{0}\sum_{t=i-1}^{\infty}\lambda_{\max}^{-t}c_{t+1}^{i}\Big)\omega_{i}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}\Big(\widetilde{c}_{0}\sum_{t=0}^{\infty}\lambda_{\max}^{-t}c_{t+1}^{i}\Big)\omega_{i}\eqqcolon\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}c_{i}\omega_{i},\qquad\text{with }c_{i}\coloneqq\widetilde{c}_{0}\sum_{t=0}^{\infty}\lambda_{\max}^{-t}c_{t+1}^{i}\text{ for }i\geq 1,

(166)

where the second line has made use of (
142
).
To proceed, let us claim for the moment that the following relations hold true for all
t
obeying
\frac{t^{5}\log n}{n}=o(1)
:

\displaystyle c_{i}=\widetilde{\lambda}^{-1}\cdot c_{i-1},\qquad\text{where }\widetilde{\lambda}^{-1}\coloneqq\frac{\lambda_{\max}-\sqrt{\lambda_{\max}^{2}-4}}{2},

(167a)

\displaystyle\|\omega_{t}\|_{2}=1+O\Big(\sqrt{\frac{t^{5}\log n}{n}}\Big),

(167b)

\displaystyle\big\|\omega_{t}-\psi_{t-1}\big\|_{2}\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{t^{5}\log n}{n}}\quad\text{and}\quad\big\|\omega_{t}-y_{t}\big\|_{2}\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{t^{5}\log n}{n}},

(167c)

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{\frac{C_{v}\log n}{\widetilde{\lambda}-1}}(c_{i})^{2}\leq 4.

(167d)

In particular, when
\lambda=O(1)
, it follows from (
134
) that, with probability at least
1-O(n^{-11})
,

\big|\widetilde{\lambda}-\lambda\big|=\bigg|\frac{\lambda_{\max}+\sqrt{\lambda_{\max}^{2}-4}}{2}-\lambda\bigg|\leq\frac{\lambda-1}{4},

(168a)

and as a result,

\widetilde{\lambda}-1\geq\lambda-\frac{\lambda-1}{4}-1=\frac{3(\lambda-1)}{4}.

(168b)

The preceding claims in (
167
) allow us to complete the proof of Lemma
12
.
To see this, note that by virtue of expression (
167a
) and (
167b
),
we can truncate the infinite sum by keeping the first
\frac{C_{v}\log n}{\widetilde{\lambda}-1}
terms for some large enough constant
C_{v}>0
, namely,

\displaystyle\bigg\|\widehat{v}^{\star}-\sum_{i=1}^{\frac{C_{v}\log n}{\widetilde{\lambda}-1}}c_{i}\omega_{i}\bigg\|_{2}

\displaystyle=\bigg\|\widetilde{c}_{0}\sum_{i=\frac{C_{v}\log n}{\widetilde{\lambda}-1}+1}^{\infty}c_{i}\omega_{i}\bigg\|_{2}\leq O\bigg(|\widetilde{c}_{0}|\sum_{i=\frac{C_{v}\log n}{\widetilde{\lambda}-1}}^{\infty}\widetilde{\lambda}^{-i}\bigg)\leq O\Big(\frac{|\widetilde{c}_{0}|\widetilde{\lambda}^{-\frac{C_{v}\log n}{\widetilde{\lambda}-1}}}{\widetilde{\lambda}-1}\Big)

\displaystyle=O\Big(\frac{|\widetilde{c}_{0}|n^{-\frac{C_{v}\log\widetilde{\lambda}}{\widetilde{\lambda}-1}}}{\widetilde{\lambda}-1}\Big)=O\Big(\frac{1}{(\widetilde{\lambda}-1)n}\Big),

(169)

where the last line holds when
C_{v}
is large enough and uses the fact that
|\widetilde{c}_{0}|\leq 1
(cf. (
165
)).
Notice that here we truncate at the first
\frac{C_{v}\log n}{\widetilde{\lambda}-1}
terms. If one decides to keep the first
s
terms for
s\geq\frac{C_{v}\log n}{\widetilde{\lambda}-1}
, it only results in a smaller truncation error.

Taking the relation (
167c
) and (
169
) together allows us to demonstrate that

\displaystyle\bigg\|\widehat{v}^{\star}-\sum_{i=1}^{\frac{C_{v}\log n}{\widetilde{\lambda}-1}}c_{i}y_{i}\bigg\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\bigg\|\widehat{v}^{\star}-\sum_{i=1}^{\frac{C_{v}\log n}{\widetilde{\lambda}-1}}c_{i}\omega_{i}\bigg\|_{2}+O\bigg(\sum_{i=1}^{\frac{C_{v}\log n}{\widetilde{\lambda}-1}}|c_{i}|\sqrt{\frac{i^{5}\log n}{n}}\bigg)

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{(\widetilde{\lambda}-1)n}+\bigg(\sum_{i=1}^{\frac{C_{v}\log n}{\widetilde{\lambda}-1}}|c_{i}|^{2}\bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}}\bigg(\sum_{i=1}^{\frac{C_{v}\log n}{\widetilde{\lambda}-1}}\frac{i^{5}\log n}{n}\bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\bigg(\frac{\log^{7}n}{(\widetilde{\lambda}-1)^{6}n}\bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}}\lesssim\frac{\log^{3.5}n}{\sqrt{(\lambda-1)^{6}n}},

where the penultimate relation comes from (
167d
),
and the last relation results from (
168b
).
Also, similar to the arguments in (
169
) we can obtain

\bigg\|\widetilde{c}_{0}\sum_{i=\frac{C_{v}\log n}{\widetilde{\lambda}-1}+1}^{s}c_{i}\omega_{i}\bigg\|_{2}\lesssim\frac{1}{(\widetilde{\lambda}-1)n}\qquad\text{and}\qquad\bigg\|\widetilde{c}_{0}\sum_{i=\frac{C_{v}\log n}{\widetilde{\lambda}-1}+1}^{s}c_{i}y_{i}\bigg\|_{2}\lesssim\frac{1}{(\widetilde{\lambda}-1)n}.

As a result, we arrive at

\bigg\|\widehat{v}^{\star}-\sum_{i=1}^{s}c_{i}y_{i}\bigg\|_{2}\leq\bigg\|\widehat{v}^{\star}-\sum_{i=1}^{\frac{C_{v}\log n}{\widetilde{\lambda}-1}}c_{i}\omega_{i}\bigg\|_{2}+\bigg\|\widetilde{c}_{0}\sum_{i=\frac{C_{v}\log n}{\widetilde{\lambda}-1}+1}^{\infty}c_{i}\omega_{i}\bigg\|_{2}\lesssim\frac{\log^{3.5}n}{\sqrt{(\lambda-1)^{6}n}}.

Repeating the same argument and recognizing that
y_{1}=v^{\star}
lead to

\bigg\|\widehat{v}^{\star}-c_{1}v^{\star}-\frac{1}{\widetilde{\lambda}}\sum_{i=1}^{s}c_{i}\psi_{i}\bigg\|_{2}=\bigg\|\widehat{v}^{\star}-c_{1}y_{1}-\sum_{i=2}^{s+1}c_{i}\psi_{i-1}\bigg\|\lesssim\frac{\log^{3.5}n}{\sqrt{(\lambda-1)^{6}n}}.

(170)

This concludes the proof of Lemma
12
, as long as the claims in (
167
) can be justified.
As a consequence, the remainder of this section is dedicated to proving (
167
).

C.6.1
Proof of claim (
167
)

Proof of recurrence relation (
167a
).

For any
i\geq 2
, it follows from the definition (
166
) of
c_{i}
and the relation (
143b
) that

\displaystyle c_{i+1}+c_{i-1}=\widetilde{c}_{0}\sum_{t=0}^{\infty}\lambda_{\max}^{-t}(c_{t+1}^{i+1}+c_{t+1}^{i-1})=\widetilde{c}_{0}\sum_{t=0}^{\infty}\lambda_{\max}^{-t}c_{t+2}^{i}=\lambda_{\max}\widetilde{c}_{0}\sum_{t=1}^{\infty}\lambda_{\max}^{-t}c_{t+1}^{i}=\lambda_{\max}c_{i},

(171)

where the last step is valid since
c_{t}^{i}=0
for
i>t.

To analyze this recurrence relation (
171
),
let us look at the two roots of the characteristic equation
r^{2}-\lambda_{\max}r+1=0
, namely,

r_{1}=\frac{\lambda_{\max}-\sqrt{\lambda_{\max}^{2}-4}}{2}
,
r_{2}=\frac{\lambda_{\max}+\sqrt{\lambda_{\max}^{2}-4}}{2}
.
It is well known that the solution to (
171
) can be expressed via these two roots as follows:

\displaystyle c_{i}=a_{1}(r_{1})^{i}+a_{2}(r_{2})^{i},\qquad i\geq 0

(172)

for some coefficients
a_{1},a_{2}\in\real
.

In view of (
134
), one has

r_{2}\geq 1+(\lambda-1)-\bigg|\frac{\lambda_{\max}+\sqrt{\lambda_{\max}^{2}-4}}{2}-\lambda\bigg|\geq 1+\frac{3(\lambda-1)}{4}>1,

which also indicates that
r_{1}=1/r_{2}<1
.
In addition, we claim that

\displaystyle 0\leq c_{t}^{i}\leq 2^{t}\qquad t\geq 0,~i\geq 0.

(173)

This relation can be easily shown by induction: (i) we first learn
from Lemma
11
that
v^{\star}=c_{1}^{1}\omega_{1}=c_{1}^{1}v^{\star}
and hence
c_{1}^{1}=1
, which together with
c_{1}^{i}=0
(
i=0
or
i>1
)
justifies (
173
) when
t=1
;
(ii) if (
173
) is valid for
t
, then it follows from
(
143b
) that
0\leq c_{t+1}^{i}=c_{t}^{i+1}+c_{t}^{i-1}\leq 2^{t}+2^{t}\leq 2^{t+1}
, thus establishing (
173
) for
t+1
— and hence its validity for all
t\geq 0
.
Combine (
173
) with (
166
) to show the boundedness of
c_{i}
in the sense that:

\displaystyle|c_{i}|

\displaystyle=|\widetilde{c}_{0}|\sum_{t=0}^{\infty}\lambda_{\max}^{-t}c_{t+1}^{i}\leq 2|\widetilde{c}_{0}|\sum_{t=0}^{\infty}(\lambda_{\max}/2)^{-t}=\frac{4|\widetilde{c}_{0}|\lambda_{\max}}{\lambda_{\max}-2}\leq\frac{64\lambda^{2}}{5(\lambda-1)^{2}},

(174)

which relies on (
165
) and (
133
).
The boundedness of
c_{i}
for any
i\geq 0
necessarily implies that
a_{2}=0
in (
172
) (otherwise
c_{i}
will blow up as
i
grows given that
r_{2}>1
).
We can thus conclude that
c_{i}=a_{1}r_{1}^{i}

(i\geq 0)
holds for some
a_{1}\neq 0
, thus implying that

\displaystyle\frac{c_{i}}{c_{i-1}}=r_{1}=\frac{\lambda_{\max}-\sqrt{\lambda_{\max}^{2}-4}}{2}.

Proof of inequality (
167b
).

As discussed previously, the iterates
\{\omega_{t}\}
in (
136
) form another sequence of AMP updates with the denoising functions taken to be the identity function.
In view of Theorem
1
, the iterates
\{\omega_{t}\}
admit the decomposition

\displaystyle\omega_{t}=\sum_{k=1}^{t-1}\beta_{t-1}^{k}\psi_{k}+\xi_{t-1};

(175)

here, we abuse the notation by taking
\beta_{t}^{k}\coloneqq\langle\omega_{t},y_{k}\rangle
(cf. (
44
))
(which satisfies
\|\beta_{t}\|_{2}=\|\omega_{t}\|_{2}
) and letting
\xi_{t-1}
denote the residual term.

In order to control
\omega_{t}
, we need to bound the size of
\xi_{t-1}
.
Specializing the expression (
56
) to the special choice of
\eta_{t}
(i.e., the identity function),
we obtain

\displaystyle\|\xi_{t}\|_{2}

\displaystyle=\Big\langle\sum_{k=1}^{t-1}\mu_{t}^{k}\psi_{k},\xi_{t-1}\Big\rangle+\sum_{k=1}^{t-1}\mu_{t}^{k}\bigg[\Big\langle\psi_{k},\sum_{j=1}^{t-1}\beta_{t-1}^{j}\psi_{j}\Big\rangle-\beta_{t-1}^{k}-(\sqrt{2}-1)\beta_{t}^{k}y_{k}^{\top}W_{k}^{\prime}y_{k}-\sum_{i=1}^{k-1}\beta_{t}^{i}g_{i}^{k}-\sum_{i=k+1}^{t}\beta_{t}^{i}g_{k}^{i}\bigg].

(176)

Here,
\{y_{k},W_{k}^{\prime}\}
have been defined in expression (
138
), whereas
\mu_{t}
is a unit vector in
\mathcal{S}^{t-2}.

We then control each term in (
176
) separately.
First, observe that with probability at least
1-O(n^{-11})
,

\displaystyle\Big|\Big\langle\sum_{k=1}^{t-1}\mu_{t}^{k}\psi_{k},\xi_{t-1}\Big\rangle\Big|\leq\Big\|\sum_{k=1}^{t-1}\mu_{t}^{k}\psi_{k}\Big\|_{2}\cdot\|\xi_{t-1}\|_{2}\leq\Big(1+O\Big(\sqrt{\frac{t\log n}{n}}\Big)\Big)\|\xi_{t-1}\|_{2}

(177)

holds for every
t\in[n]
, where the last inequality follows from (
99
).
In view of Lemma
3
, with probability at least
1-O(n^{-11})
one has

\displaystyle\bigg|\sum_{k=1}^{t-1}(\sqrt{2}-1)\mu_{t}^{k}\Big[\beta_{t}^{k}y_{k}^{\top}W_{k}^{\prime}y_{k}+\sum_{i=1}^{k-1}\beta_{t}^{i}g_{i}^{k}+\sum_{i=k+1}^{t}\beta_{t}^{i}g_{k}^{i}\Big]\bigg|\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{t\log n}{n}}\|\beta_{t}\|_{2}.

(178)

In addition, if we write matrix
\Psi\coloneqq[\psi_{1},\ldots,\psi_{t-1}]\in\real^{n\times(t-1)}
, then property (
98
) and
\|u_{t}\|_{2}=1
give

\displaystyle\bigg|\Big\langle\sum_{k=1}^{t-1}\mu_{t}^{k}\psi_{k},\sum_{j=1}^{t-1}\beta_{t}^{j}\psi_{j}\Big\rangle-\sum_{k=1}^{t-1}\mu_{t}^{k}\beta_{t}^{k}\bigg|

\displaystyle=\Big|\mu_{t}^{\top}\Psi^{\top}\Psi\beta_{t}-\mu_{t}^{\top}I_{t-1}\beta_{t}\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\|\mu_{t}\|_{2}\|\beta_{t}\|_{2}\big\|\Psi^{\top}\Psi-I_{t-1}\big\|\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{t\log n}{n}}\|\beta_{t}\|_{2}

(179)

holds with probability at least
1-O(n^{-11})
.
Taking the decomposition (
176
) collectively with (
177
), (
178
) and (
179
)
and using
\|\omega_{t-1}\|_{2}=\|\beta_{t-1}\|_{2}
,
we arrive at

\displaystyle\|\xi_{t}\|_{2}\leq\Big(1+C_{3}\sqrt{\frac{t\log n}{n}}\Big)\|\xi_{t-1}\|_{2}+C_{3}\sqrt{\frac{t\log n}{n}}\|\omega_{t}\|_{2}

(180)

for some large enough constant
C_{3}>0
.

Additionally, invoke (
175
) to obtain

\displaystyle\|\omega_{t}\|_{2}\leq\Big\|\sum_{k=1}^{t-1}\beta_{t-1}^{k}\psi_{k}\Big\|_{2}+\|\xi_{t-1}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(\textrm{i})}}{{\leq}}\Big(1+O\Big(\sqrt{\frac{t\log n}{n}}\Big)\Big)\|\beta_{t-1}\|_{2}+\|\xi_{t-1}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\Big(1+C_{3}\sqrt{\frac{t\log n}{n}}\Big)\|\omega_{t-1}\|_{2}+\|\xi_{t-1}\|_{2},

(181)

provided that the constant
C_{3}>0
is large enough.
Here, (i) comes from (
99
),
and we remind the readers that
\|\omega_{t-1}\|_{2}=\|\beta_{t-1}\|_{2}
and
\|\omega_{1}\|_{2}=1
.

Clearly,
the inequalities (
180
) and (
C.6.1
) taken together
lead to a recurrence relation involving
\|\xi_{t}\|_{2}
and
\|\omega_{t}\|_{2}
.
Based on this, we claim that for all
t
obeying
\frac{t^{5}\log n}{n}=o(1)
, one has

\|\xi_{t}\|_{2}\leq C_{5}\sqrt{\frac{t^{3}\log n}{n}}\qquad\text{and}\qquad\|\omega_{t}\|_{2}\leq 1+C_{5}\sqrt{\frac{t^{5}\log n}{n}}

(182)

for some universal constant
C_{5}=2C_{3}
. Clearly, (
182
) is satisfied
when
t=1
, given that
\xi_{1}=0
(as
\omega_{2}=Wv^{\star}
) and
\|\omega_{1}\|_{2}=\|v^{\star}\|_{2}=1
.
Suppose now that (
182
) is valid for
the
t
-th iteration, then we can deduce that

\displaystyle\|\omega_{t+1}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\Big(1+C_{3}\sqrt{\frac{(t+1)\log n}{n}}\Big)\|\omega_{t}\|_{2}+\|\xi_{t}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\Big(1+C_{3}\sqrt{\frac{(t+1)\log n}{n}}\Big)\left(1+C_{5}\sqrt{\frac{t^{5}\log n}{n}}\right)+C_{5}\sqrt{\frac{t^{3}\log n}{n}}

\displaystyle=1+C_{5}\sqrt{\frac{(t+1)\log n}{n}}\left(\frac{C_{3}}{C_{5}}+t^{2}+C_{3}\sqrt{\frac{t^{5}\log n}{n}}+t\right)

\displaystyle\leq 1+C_{5}\sqrt{\frac{(t+1)\log n}{n}}\left(t+1\right)^{2}=1+C_{5}\sqrt{\frac{(t+1)^{5}\log n}{n}};

(183)

\displaystyle\|\xi_{t+1}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\Big(1+C_{3}\sqrt{\frac{(t+1)\log n}{n}}\Big)\|\xi_{t}\|_{2}+C_{3}\sqrt{\frac{(t+1)\log n}{n}}\,\|\omega_{t+1}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C_{5}\Big(1+C_{3}\sqrt{\frac{(t+1)\log n}{n}}\Big)\sqrt{\frac{t^{3}\log n}{n}}+C_{3}\sqrt{\frac{(t+1)\log n}{n}}\left(1+C_{5}\sqrt{\frac{t^{5}\log n}{n}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq C_{5}\sqrt{\frac{(t+1)\log n}{n}}\left(t+\frac{C_{3}}{C_{5}}+2C_{3}\sqrt{\frac{t^{5}\log n}{n}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq C_{5}\sqrt{\frac{(t+1)\log n}{n}}(t+1)=C_{5}\sqrt{\frac{(t+1)^{3}\log n}{n}};

(184)

here, the last lines in both of the above bounds hold true since
C_{3}/C_{5}=1/2

and
\frac{t^{5}\log n}{n}=o(1)
. This in turn justifies the validity
of the claim (
182
) for the
(t+1)
-th iteration. Hence, by induction, we have established (
182
)
for all
t
obeying
\frac{t^{5}\log n}{n}=o(1)
.

Armed with (
182
), we can invoke (
175
) again to derive

\displaystyle\|\omega_{t}\|_{2}\geq\Big\|\sum_{k=1}^{t-1}\beta_{t-1}^{k}\psi_{k}\Big\|_{2}-\|\xi_{t-1}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(\textrm{i})}}{{\geq}}\Big(1-O\Big(\sqrt{\frac{t\log n}{n}}\Big)\Big)\|\beta_{t-1}\|_{2}-\|\xi_{t-1}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\geq\Big(1-C_{3}\sqrt{\frac{t\log n}{n}}\Big)\|\omega_{t-1}\|_{2}-C_{5}\sqrt{\frac{t^{3}\log n}{n}}

for some large enough constant
C_{3}>0
. Repeat the argument in (
183
) to yield

\|\omega_{t}\|_{2}\geq 1-C_{5}\sqrt{\frac{t^{5}\log n}{n}}.

This taken collectively with (
182
) finishes the proof of inequality (
167b
).

Proof of inequality (
167c
).

To streamline the presentation of our proof, let us first make note of the following result,
the proof of which is deferred to the end of this section:

\displaystyle\big\|\big[\beta_{t}^{1},\beta_{t}^{2},\cdots,\beta_{t}^{t-1}\big]\big\|_{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{t^{5}\log n}{n}}.

(185)

With this result, (
182
) and (
167b
) in mind, we are ready to prove (
167c
).
First, it follows from (
175
) that

\displaystyle\big\|\omega_{t}-\psi_{t-1}\big\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\big\|(\beta_{t-1}^{t-1}-1)\psi_{t-1}\big\|_{2}+\Big\|\sum_{k=1}^{t-2}\beta_{t-1}^{k}\psi_{k}\Big\|_{2}+\|\xi_{t-1}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\bigg(1+O\Big(\sqrt{\frac{t\log n}{n}}\Big)\bigg)\Big(\big|\beta_{t-1}^{t-1}-1\big|+\big\|\big[\beta_{t-1}^{1},\cdots,\beta_{t-1}^{t-2}\big]\big\|_{2}+\|\xi_{t-1}\|_{2}\Big),

\displaystyle\leq\bigg(1+O\Big(\sqrt{\frac{t\log n}{n}}\Big)\bigg)\Big(\big|\|\omega_{t-1}\|_{2}-1\big|+2\big\|\big[\beta_{t-1}^{1},\cdots,\beta_{t-1}^{t-2}\big]\big\|_{2}+\|\xi_{t-1}\|_{2}\Big),

where the second line results from the properties (
79a
) and (
98
),
and the last line is valid since

\beta_{t-1}^{t-1}=\big\langle\omega_{t-1},y_{t-1}\big\rangle=\frac{\omega_{t-1}^{\top}(I-V_{t-2}V_{t-2}^{\top})\omega_{t-1}}{\|(I-V_{t-2}V_{t-2}^{\top})\omega_{t-1}\|_{2}}\geq 0

\Longrightarrow\quad\big|\beta_{t-1}^{t-1}-1\big|=\big||\beta_{t-1}^{t-1}|-1\big|\leq\big|\|\beta_{t-1}\|_{2}-1\big|+\big\|\big[\beta_{t-1}^{1},\cdots,\beta_{t-1}^{t-2}\big]\big\|_{2}=\big|\|\omega_{t-1}\|_{2}-1\big|+\big\|\big[\beta_{t-1}^{1},\cdots,\beta_{t-1}^{t-2}\big]\big\|_{2}.

Taking this collectively with (
182
), (
185
) and (
167b
) yields the first part of the advertised bound (
167c
).

Regarding the second part of (
167c
), reorganizing the expression (
138
) of
y_{t}
gives

\displaystyle y_{t}

\displaystyle=\frac{\omega_{t}-V_{t-1}V_{t-1}^{\top}\omega_{t}}{\big\|\omega_{t}-V_{t-1}V_{t-1}^{\top}\omega_{t}\big\|_{2}}=\omega_{t}+\bigg(\frac{1-\big\|\omega_{t}-V_{t-1}V_{t-1}^{\top}\omega_{t}\big\|_{2}}{\big\|\omega_{t}-V_{t-1}V_{t-1}^{\top}\omega_{t}\big\|_{2}}\bigg)\omega_{t}-\frac{V_{t-1}V_{t-1}^{\top}\omega_{t}}{\big\|\omega_{t}-V_{t-1}V_{t-1}^{\top}\omega_{t}\big\|_{2}}.

In view of relations (
185
) and (
167b
), we can deduce that

\displaystyle\big\|V_{t-1}V_{t-1}^{\top}\omega_{t}\big\|_{2}

\displaystyle=\big\|V_{t-1}^{\top}\omega_{t}\big\|_{2}=\big\|\big[\beta_{t}^{1},\cdots,\beta_{t}^{t-1}\big]\big\|_{2}\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{t^{5}\log n}{n}}

\displaystyle\big\|\omega_{t}-V_{t-1}V_{t-1}^{\top}\omega_{t}\big\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\|\omega_{t}\|_{2}+\big\|V_{t-1}^{\top}\omega_{t}\big\|_{2}=1+O\Big(\sqrt{\frac{t^{5}\log n}{n}}\Big),

\displaystyle\big\|\omega_{t}-V_{t-1}V_{t-1}^{\top}\omega_{t}\big\|_{2}

\displaystyle\geq\|\omega_{t}\|_{2}-\big\|V_{t-1}^{\top}\omega_{t}\big\|_{2}\geq 1-O\Big(\sqrt{\frac{t^{5}\log n}{n}}\Big).

Taking the preceding bounds collectively and using (
167b
) once again, we immediately reach

\displaystyle\big\|y_{t}-\omega_{t}\big\|_{2}\leq O\Big(\sqrt{\frac{t^{5}\log n}{n}}\Big),

thus validating the second part of inequality (
167c
).

Proof of inequality (
167d
).

It follows from (
169
) and the triangle inequality that

\displaystyle\bigg\|\widehat{v}^{\star}-\sum_{i=1}^{\frac{C_{v}\log n}{\widetilde{\lambda}-1}}c_{i}y_{i}\bigg\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\bigg\|\widehat{v}^{\star}-\sum_{i=1}^{\frac{C_{v}\log n}{\widetilde{\lambda}-1}}c_{i}\omega_{i}\bigg\|_{2}+\bigg\|\sum_{i=1}^{\frac{C_{v}\log n}{\widetilde{\lambda}-1}}c_{i}(\omega_{i}-y_{i})\bigg\|_{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{(\widetilde{\lambda}-1)n}+\bigg(\sum_{i=1}^{\frac{C_{v}\log n}{\widetilde{\lambda}-1}}|c_{i}|^{2}\bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}}\bigg(\sum_{i=1}^{\frac{C_{v}\log n}{\widetilde{\lambda}-1}}\big\|\omega_{i}-y_{i}\big\|_{2}^{2}\bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{(\widetilde{\lambda}-1)n}+\bigg(\sum_{i=1}^{\frac{C_{v}\log n}{\widetilde{\lambda}-1}}|c_{i}|^{2}\bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}}\bigg(\sum_{i=1}^{\frac{C_{v}\log n}{\widetilde{\lambda}-1}}\frac{i^{5}\log n}{n}\bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{(\lambda-1)n}+\bigg(\sum_{i=1}^{\frac{C_{v}\log n}{\widetilde{\lambda}-1}}|c_{i}|^{2}\bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}}\sqrt{\frac{\log^{7}n}{(\lambda-1)^{6}n}},

where the penultimate inequality invokes (
167c
), and the last line results from (
168b
).
This combined with the orthonormality of
\{y_{i}\}
implies that

\displaystyle\bigg(\sum_{i=1}^{\frac{C_{v}\log n}{\widetilde{\lambda}-1}}(c_{i})^{2}\bigg)^{1/2}

\displaystyle=\bigg\|\sum_{i=1}^{\frac{C_{v}\log n}{\widetilde{\lambda}-1}}c_{i}y_{i}\bigg\|_{2}\leq\big\|\widehat{v}^{\star}\big\|_{2}+\bigg\|\widehat{v}^{\star}-\sum_{i=1}^{\frac{C_{v}\log n}{\widetilde{\lambda}-1}}c_{i}y_{i}\bigg\|_{2}\leq 1+O\Bigg(\bigg(\sum_{i=1}^{\frac{C_{v}\log n}{\widetilde{\lambda}-1}}|c_{i}|^{2}\bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}}\sqrt{\frac{\log^{7}n}{(\lambda-1)^{6}n}}\Bigg)

\displaystyle\leq 1+\frac{1}{2}\bigg(\sum_{i=1}^{\frac{C_{v}\log n}{\widetilde{\lambda}-1}}|c_{i}|^{2}\bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}},

where the last line is valid as long as
n(\lambda-1)^{6}/\log^{7}n

is sufficiently large. Rearranging terms, we are left with
\sum_{i=1}^{\frac{C_{v}\log n}{\widetilde{\lambda}-1}}(c_{i})^{2}\leq 4

as claimed.

Proof of inequality (
185
).

Finally, we finish the proof by establishing inequality (
185
).
From the definition of
\beta_{t}
and (
175
), one can derive a recursive relation as follows:

\displaystyle\big\|\big[\beta_{t}^{1},\ldots,\beta_{t}^{t-1}\big]\big\|_{2}

\displaystyle=\|V_{t-1}^{\top}\omega_{t}\|_{2}=\bigg\|V_{t-1}^{\top}\Big(\sum_{k=1}^{t-1}\beta_{t-1}^{k}\psi_{k}+\xi_{t-1}\Big)\bigg\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\Big\|\sum_{k=1}^{t-2}\beta_{t-1}^{k}\psi_{k}\Big\|_{2}+|\beta_{t-1}^{t-1}|\cdot\big\|V_{t-1}^{\top}\psi_{t-1}\big\|_{2}+\big\|\xi_{t-1}\big\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\Big(1+C_{4}\sqrt{\frac{t\log n}{n}}\Big)\big\|\big[\beta_{t-1}^{1},\ldots,\beta_{t-1}^{t-2}\big]\big\|_{2}+\|\omega_{t-1}\|_{2}\cdot\big\|V_{t-1}^{\top}\psi_{t-1}\big\|_{2}+\big\|\xi_{t-1}\big\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\Big(1+C_{4}\sqrt{\frac{t\log n}{n}}\Big)\big\|\big[\beta_{t-1}^{1},\ldots,\beta_{t-1}^{t-2}\big]\big\|_{2}+C_{4}\|\omega_{t-1}\|_{2}\sqrt{\frac{t\log n}{n}}+\big\|\xi_{t-1}\big\|_{2}

(186)

for some large enough constant
C_{4}>0
.
Here, the penultimate inequality uses (
99
) and the fact
|\beta_{t-1}^{t-1}|\leq\|\beta_{t-1}\|_{2}=\|\omega_{t-1}\|_{2}
,
while the last inequality would be guaranteed if we could establish the following result:

\displaystyle\|V_{t-1}^{\top}\psi_{t-1}\|_{2}\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{t\log n}{n}}.

(187)

We shall assume the validity of (
187
) for the moment, and return to prove it shortly.
Taking (
186
) together with (
182
) yields

\displaystyle\big\|\big[\beta_{t}^{1},\ldots,\beta_{t}^{t-1}\big]\big\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\Big(1+C_{4}\sqrt{\frac{t\log n}{n}}\Big)\big\|\big[\beta_{t-1}^{1},\ldots,\beta_{t-1}^{t-2}\big]\big\|_{2}+C_{6}\sqrt{\frac{t^{3}\log n}{n}}

(188)

for some sufficiently large constant
C_{6}>0
.

We then claim that for all
t
obeying
\frac{t^{3}\log n}{n}=o(1)
,

\big\|\big[\beta_{t}^{1},\ldots,\beta_{t}^{t-1}\big]\big\|_{2}\leq C_{7}\sqrt{\frac{t^{5}\log n}{n}}

(189)

holds for some large enough constant
C_{7}>0
.
Regarding the base case, we observe that

\displaystyle|\beta_{2}^{1}|=\big|\langle\omega_{2},\,y_{1}\rangle\big|=\big|v^{\star\top}Wv^{\star}\big|\leq C_{7}\sqrt{\frac{\log n}{n}}

(190)

with probability at least
1-O(n^{-12})
, provided that
C_{7}>0
is large enough.
Assuming that (
189
) is valid for the
(t-1)
-th iteration, we further have

\displaystyle\big\|\big[\beta_{t}^{1},\ldots,\beta_{t}^{t-1}\big]\big\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C_{7}\Big(1+C_{4}\sqrt{\frac{t\log n}{n}}\Big)\sqrt{\frac{(t-1)^{5}\log n}{n}}+C_{6}\sqrt{\frac{t^{3}\log n}{n}}

\displaystyle=C_{7}\sqrt{\frac{t^{3}\log n}{n}}\left\{\Big(1+C_{4}\sqrt{\frac{t\log n}{n}}\Big)\left(t-1\right)+\frac{C_{6}}{C_{7}}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq C_{7}\sqrt{\frac{t^{3}\log n}{n}}\cdot\left\{t-1+C_{4}\sqrt{\frac{t^{3}\log n}{n}}+\frac{C_{6}}{C_{7}}\right\}\leq C_{7}\sqrt{\frac{t^{5}\log n}{n}},

where the last inequality holds true as long as
C_{7}\geq 2C_{6}

and
\frac{t^{3}\log n}{n}=o(1)
.
This justifies the claim (
189
) for the
t
-th iteration. The standard induction argument then establishes (
189
) for all
t
obeying
\frac{t^{3}\log n}{n}=o(1)
.

We now come back to prove (
187
). Towards this, we first note that: by construction,
\psi_{t-1}
is independent of
V_{t-1}
. To justify this, recall that it has been established in the last paragraph of Section
B.1
that:

\psi_{t-1}
follows a Gaussian distribution
\mathcal{N}(0,\frac{1}{n}I_{n})
no matter what value the sequence
\{y_{k}\}_{1\leq k\leq t-1}
takes;
therefore, in view of the definition of statistical independence,
\psi_{t-1}
is independent of
\{y_{k}\}_{1\leq k\leq t-1}
and hence
V_{t-1}
(as
V_{t-1}
is obtained by simply concatenating
y_{1},\ldots,y_{t-1}
).
Therefore,
V_{t-1}^{\top}\psi_{t-1}
is essentially
\mathcal{N}(0,\frac{1}{n}I_{t-1})
,
and hence standard Gaussian concentration results
(
Vershynin, 2018
, Chapter 4.4)
imply that

\mathbb{P}\Big(\big\|V_{t-1}^{\top}\psi_{t-1}\big\|_{2}\geq 5\sqrt{\frac{t\log n}{n}}\Big)\leq O(n^{-11})

as claimed. This concludes the proof.

C.7
Proof of Lemma
13

Repeating the proof of Lemma
11
,
we can show that each
W^{s}\widetilde{v}
is a linear combination of
\{u_{s+1},\ldots,u_{2s+1}\}.

Taking this together with the decomposition (
146
) and Lemma
11

reveals that
x_{1}
can be expressed as

\displaystyle x_{1}=\sum_{i=1}^{2s+1}b_{i}\widehat{y}_{i},\qquad\text{with }b_{i}=\langle x_{1},\,\widehat{y}_{i}\rangle,

(191)

given that
\{\widehat{y}_{i}\}_{i=1}^{2s+1}
are orthonormal and span the subspace containing
\{\omega_{i}\}_{i=1}^{s}
and
\{u_{i}\}_{i=s+1}^{2s+1}.

Next, we move on to show that
\|[b_{s+1},\ldots,b_{2s+1}]\|_{2}
is small.
More specifically, recall from Lemma
11
that

\sum_{i=0}^{s-1}a_{i}W^{i}v^{\star}\in\mathsf{span}\{\omega_{1},\cdots,\omega_{s}\}=\mathsf{span}\big\{\widehat{y}_{1},\cdots,\widehat{y}_{s}\big\},

and hence by virtue of (
146
),

\displaystyle\big\|[b_{s+1},\cdots,b_{2s+1}]\big\|_{2}

\displaystyle=\Big\|x_{1}-\sum_{i=1}^{s}b_{i}\widehat{y}_{i}\Big\|_{2}\overset{\mathrm{(i)}}{\leq}\Big\|x_{1}-\sum_{i=0}^{s-1}a_{i}W^{i}v^{\star}\Big\|_{2}=\big\|a_{s}W^{s}\widetilde{v}\big\|_{2}\overset{\mathrm{(ii)}}{\lesssim}\frac{\|W\|^{s}n^{11.5}}{\lambda_{\max}^{s}}

\displaystyle\asymp\bigg(1-\frac{\lambda_{\max}-\|W\|}{\lambda_{\max}}\bigg)^{s}n^{11.5}\lesssim\frac{1}{n},

(192)

where (i) follows since
\sum_{i=1}^{s}b_{i}\widehat{y}_{i}
is the
Euclidean projection of
x_{1}
onto
\mathsf{span}\big\{\widehat{y}_{1},\cdots,\widehat{y}_{s}\big\}
while
\sum_{i=0}^{s-1}a_{i}W^{i}v^{\star}\in\mathsf{span}\big\{\widehat{y}_{1},\cdots,\widehat{y}_{s}\big\}
;
(ii) makes use of (
145
), and the last inequality
invokes (
131a
) and (
131b
)
and is valid if
s\geq\frac{C_{v}\lambda^{2}\log n}{(\lambda-1)^{2}}
for some
sufficiently large constant
C_{v}>0
.
Moreover, putting expressions (
191
) and (
144
) together yields

\displaystyle\big\|[b_{1},\cdots b_{s}]-[c_{1},\cdots,c_{s}]\big\|_{2}

\displaystyle=\Big\|\sum_{i=1}^{s}(b_{i}-c_{i})\widehat{y}_{i}\Big\|_{2}\leq\Big\|\sum_{i=1}^{s}(b_{i}-c_{i})\widehat{y}_{i}+\sum_{i=s+1}^{2s+1}b_{i}\widehat{y}_{i}\Big\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\big\|x_{1}-\widehat{v}^{\star}\big\|_{2}+\Big\|\widehat{v}^{\star}-\sum_{i=1}^{s}c_{i}\widehat{y}_{i}\Big\|_{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{n^{12}}+\frac{\log^{3.5}n}{\sqrt{(\lambda-1)^{6}n}}\asymp\frac{\log^{3.5}n}{\sqrt{(\lambda-1)^{6}n}}

(193)

with probability at least
1-O(n^{-11})
.
In light of the above two relations, we can further derive

\displaystyle\Big\|\sum_{i=1}^{2s+1}b_{i}\widehat{\psi}_{i}-\sum_{i=1}^{s}c_{i}\psi_{i}\Big\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\Big\|\sum_{i=1}^{s}b_{i}\widehat{\psi}_{i}-\sum_{i=1}^{s}c_{i}\psi_{i}\Big\|_{2}+\Big\|\sum_{i=s+1}^{2s+1}b_{i}\widehat{\psi}_{i}\Big\|_{2}

\displaystyle=\Big\|\sum_{i=1}^{s}b_{i}{\psi}_{i}-\sum_{i=1}^{s}c_{i}\psi_{i}\Big\|_{2}+\Big\|\sum_{i=s+1}^{2s+1}b_{i}\widehat{\psi}_{i}\Big\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\Big(1+O\Big(\sqrt{\frac{t\log n}{n}}\Big)\Big)\Big(\big\|[b_{1},\cdots b_{s}]-[c_{1},\cdots,c_{s}]\big\|_{2}+\big\|[b_{s+1},\ldots,b_{2s+1}]\big\|_{2}\Big)

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\log^{3.5}n}{\sqrt{(\lambda-1)^{6}n}}

with probability at least
1-O(n^{-11})
,
where the penultimate line applies the concentration result (
99
).
Hence, taking this collectively with (
145
) and Lemma
12
, we can demonstrate that

\displaystyle\Bigg\|x_{1}-c_{1}v^{\star}-\frac{1}{\widetilde{\lambda}}\sum_{i=1}^{2s+1}b_{i}\widehat{\psi}_{i}\Bigg\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\Bigg\|x_{1}-c_{1}v^{\star}-\frac{1}{\widetilde{\lambda}}\sum_{i=1}^{s}c_{i}\psi_{i}\Bigg\|_{2}+\frac{1}{\widetilde{\lambda}}\Big\|\sum_{i=1}^{2s+1}b_{i}\widehat{\psi}_{i}-\sum_{i=1}^{s}c_{i}\psi_{i}\Big\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\Bigg\|\widehat{v}^{\star}-c_{1}v^{\star}-\frac{1}{\widetilde{\lambda}}\sum_{i=1}^{s}c_{i}\psi_{i}\Bigg\|_{2}+\big\|x_{1}-\widehat{v}^{\star}\big\|_{2}+O\bigg(\frac{\log^{3.5}n}{\sqrt{(\lambda-1)^{6}n}}\bigg)

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\log^{3.5}n}{\sqrt{(\lambda-1)^{6}n}}+\frac{1}{n^{12}}+\frac{\log^{3.5}n}{\sqrt{(\lambda-1)^{6}n}}\asymp\frac{\log^{3.5}n}{\sqrt{(\lambda-1)^{6}n}}.

(194)

Finally, it results from (
134
) and
(
99
) that

\displaystyle\bigg\|\frac{1}{\lambda}\sum_{i=1}^{2s+1}b_{i}\widehat{\psi}_{i}-\frac{1}{\widetilde{\lambda}}\sum_{i=1}^{2s+1}b_{i}\widehat{\psi}_{i}\bigg\|_{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{|\lambda-\widetilde{\lambda}|}{\lambda^{2}}\bigg\|\sum_{i=1}^{2s+1}b_{i}\widehat{\psi}_{i}\bigg\|\lesssim\bigg(1+O\Big(\sqrt{\frac{s}{n}}\Big)\bigg)|\lambda-\widetilde{\lambda}|\cdot\big\|[b_{1},\cdots,b_{2s+1}]\big\|_{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{\log n}{n(\lambda-1)^{7}}},

where we also use the fact that
\big\|[b_{1},\cdots,b_{2s+1}]\big\|_{2}\asymp 1

(a direct consequence of (
191
) and the orthonormality
of
\{\widehat{y}_{i}\}
).
This together with (
194
) and the triangle inequality immediately concludes the proof.

Appendix D
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
synchronization: Proof of Theorem
4

With the denoising functions selected as in (
28b
), we first point out that

\displaystyle\|\beta_{t}\|_{2}=\|\eta_{t}(x_{t})\|_{2}=1,\qquad t\geq 1

(195)

throughout the execution of AMP.
This basic fact helps simplify the analysis,
as there is no need to control the related quantity
\Delta_{\beta,t}
(see (
16b
)) given that
\|\beta_{t-1}\|_{2}
is fixed.
As a result, this section focuses attention on characterizing the dynamics of
\alpha_{t}.

Induction hypotheses.

The proof of Theorem
4
is built upon Theorem
3
as well as the analysis framework laid out in
Theorem
2
(or Corollary
1
).
The proof is inductive in nature;
more specifically, we aim to show, by induction, that for every
t
obeying (
30
),
the AMP iterates
\{x_{t}\}
satisfy the desired decomposition (
30
) in Theorem
4

while satisfying the following properties:

\displaystyle\big(1+o(1)\big)\lambda

\displaystyle\geq\alpha_{t}\geq\big(1+o(1)\big)\sqrt{\lambda^{2}-1}

(196a)

\displaystyle\|\xi_{t-1}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C_{1}\sqrt{\frac{(t+s)\log n}{(\lambda-1)^{3}n}}+C_{1}\left(1-\frac{1}{40}(\lambda-1)\right)^{t-1}\frac{\log^{3.5}n}{\sqrt{(\lambda-1)^{9}n}}\eqqcolon S_{t}

(196b)

for some large enough constant
C_{1}>0
.

Given that
s\asymp\frac{\log n}{(\lambda-1)^{2}}
, we find it helpful to note

\displaystyle S_{t}\leq\underset{\eqqcolon\,\widetilde{S}_{t}}{\underbrace{C_{1}\sqrt{\frac{(t+s)\log n}{(\lambda-1)^{3}n}}+C_{1}\frac{\log^{3.5}n}{\sqrt{(\lambda-1)^{9}n}}}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{1}\sqrt{\frac{t\log n}{(\lambda-1)^{3}n}}+C_{1}\sqrt{\frac{s\log n}{(\lambda-1)^{3}n}}+C_{1}\frac{\log^{3.5}n}{\sqrt{(\lambda-1)^{9}n}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{t\log n}{(\lambda-1)^{3}n}}+\sqrt{\frac{\log^{7}n}{(\lambda-1)^{9}n}},

(197)

where the first line uses the basic inequality that
\sqrt{a+b}\leq\sqrt{a}+\sqrt{b}
for any positive numbers
a,b
; for the second line, we make use of the inequality
\sqrt{\frac{s\log n}{(\lambda-1)^{3}n}}\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{\log^{7}n}{(\lambda-1)^{9}n}}
that holds under the condition
s\asymp\frac{\log n}{(\lambda-1)^{2}}.

We first verify these hypotheses for the base case.
In view of Theorem
3
, the spectral initialization
x_{1}
(defined in (
28c
))
admits the decomposition (
30
) and satisfies

\displaystyle\alpha_{1}=\sqrt{\lambda^{2}-1},\qquad\|\beta_{0}\|_{2}=1,\qquad\|\xi_{0}\|_{2}\lesssim\frac{\log^{3.5}n}{\sqrt{(\lambda-1)^{9}n}}.

(198)

This validates the induction hypotheses (
196
) for the base case with
t=1
.
In order to carry out the induction argument,
we shall — throughout the rest of the proof — assume that the induction hypotheses (
196
) hold true for every iteration
k\leq t
,
and attempt to show their validity for the
(t+1)
-th iteration.

Organization of the proof.

The proof is organized as follows.
Section
D.1
collects a couple of preliminary facts (e.g., basic concentration inequalities, derivatives of the denoising function, and tight estimates of
\pi_{t}
and
\gamma_{t}
) that will be used throughout the induction argument.
Section
D.2
develops upper bounds on several key quantities (e.g.,
A_{t},B_{t},D_{t}
) that underlie our analysis framework in Theorem
2
and Corollary
1
.
The main recursion is established in Section
D.3
; specifically, Section
D.3.1
is devoted to establishing the bound for
\|\xi_{t}\|_{2}
, Section
D.3.2
studies the size of
\Delta_{\alpha,t}
, while Section
D.3.3
is dedicated to the analysis of
\alpha_{t}
.

D.1
Preliminary facts

Before embarking on the main proof of Theorem
4
, let us gather some preliminary facts that shall be used multiple times throughout the proof.

D.1.1
Basic concentration results

We begin by stating some concentration results that follow directly from the results in Section
A
.
Recall that the
\phi_{k}
’s are i.i.d. drawn from
\phi_{k}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{i.i.d}}}{{\sim}}\mathcal{N}(0,\frac{1}{n}I_{n})
,
and for every
x\in\real^{n}
we denote by
|x|_{(i)}
its
i
-th largest entry in magnitude.
In the statement of Lemma
6
, we mention some convex set
\mathcal{E}
, which we shall select as follows.
For any fixed
1\leq t<n-2s
and
1\leq\tau\leq n
, let us define the following set:

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{\tau}\coloneqq\left\{\{\phi_{k}\}:\max_{-2s\leq k\leq t-1}\|\phi_{k}\|_{2}<1+C_{5}\sqrt{\frac{\log\frac{n}{\delta}}{n}}\right\}\bigcap\left\{\{\phi_{k}\}:\sup_{a\in\mathcal{S}^{2s+t-1}}\Big\|\sum_{k=-2s}^{t-1}a_{k}\phi_{k}\Big\|_{2}<1+C_{5}\sqrt{\frac{(t+s)\log\frac{n}{\delta}}{n}}\right\}

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt\bigcap\left\{\{\phi_{k}\}:\sup_{a=[a_{k}]_{-2s\leq k<t}\in\mathcal{S}^{2s+t-1}}\sum_{i=1}^{\tau}\Big|\sum_{k=-2s}^{t-1}a_{k}\phi_{k}\Big|_{(i)}^{2}<\frac{C_{5}(t+s+\tau)\log\frac{n}{\delta}}{n}\right\}

(199)

for some large enough constant
C_{5}>0
.
It is easily seen that
\mathcal{E}_{\tau}
is a convex set with respect to
(\phi_{-2s},\ldots,\phi_{t-1})
.
Additionally, Lemma
8
together with the union bound reveals that
\{\mathcal{E}_{\tau}\}
is a set of high-probability events:

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\{\phi_{k}\}\in\mathcal{E})\geq 1-\delta,\qquad\text{with }\mathcal{E}\coloneqq\bigcap_{\tau=1}^{n}\mathcal{E}_{\tau}

(200)

In addition, Lemma
6
and Corollary
2
entail
bounding the expected difference between a function
f
and its projection onto
\mathcal{E}
(see (
71
)).
Here, we state a simple result that leads to a useful bound in this regard.
Specifically, denote
\Phi\coloneqq\sqrt{n}(\phi_{-2s},\ldots,\phi_{t-1})
,
and consider any given function
f:\real^{n\times(2s+t)}\to\real
obeying

|f(\Phi)|\lesssim n^{100}\Big(\max_{k}\|\phi_{k}\|_{2}\Big)^{100}.

(201)

Denoting by
\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{E}}(\cdot)
the Euclidean projection onto the set

\mathcal{E}
and taking
\delta\asymp n^{-300}
,
we assert that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[\big|f(\Phi)-f(\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{E}}(\Phi))\big|\big]\lesssim n^{-100}.

(202)

In light of this result, we shall choose the set
\mathcal{E}
with
\delta\asymp n^{-300}
throughout the rest of this section.

Proof of inequality (
202
).

We divide into two cases depending on the value of
\max_{k}\|\phi_{k}\|_{2}
, namely,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[\big|f(\Phi)-f(\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{E}}(\Phi))\big|\big]
