\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Big[\big|f(\Phi)-f(\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{E}}(\Phi))\big|\ind\left(\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{c}}\right)\Big]\lesssim\mathbb{E}\left[n^{100}\Big(\max_{k}\|\phi_{k}\|_{2}\Big)^{100}\ind\left(\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{c}}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathbb{E}\left[n^{100}\Big(\max_{k}\|\phi_{k}\|_{2}\Big)^{100}\ind\left(\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{c}}\right)\ind\bigg(\max_{k}\|\phi_{k}\|_{2}\leq 1+C_{5}\sqrt{\frac{\log\frac{n}{\delta}}{n}}\bigg)\right]

\displaystyle\qquad+\mathbb{E}\left[n^{100}\Big(\max_{k}\|\phi_{k}\|_{2}\Big)^{100}\ind\left(\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{c}}\right)\ind\bigg(\max_{k}\|\phi_{k}\|_{2}>1+C_{5}\sqrt{\frac{\log\frac{n}{\delta}}{n}}\bigg)\right].

First, it is easily seen from (
200
) that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[n^{100}\Big(\max_{k}\|\phi_{k}\|_{2}\Big)^{100}\ind\left(\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{c}}\right)\ind\Big(\max_{k}\|\phi_{k}\|_{2}\leq 1+C_{5}\sqrt{\frac{\log\frac{n}{\delta}}{n}}\Big)\right]\lesssim n^{200}\delta,

provided that
\log\frac{1}{\delta}\lesssim\log n
.
In addition, one can deduce that

\displaystyle n^{100}\sum_{k}\mathbb{E}\left[\|\phi_{k}\|_{2}^{100}\ind\left(\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{c}}\right)\ind\Big(\max_{k}\|\phi_{k}\|_{2}>1+C_{5}\sqrt{\frac{\log\frac{n}{\delta}}{n}}\Big)\right]

\displaystyle\qquad\lesssim n^{100}\sum_{k}\mathbb{E}\left[\|\phi_{k}\|_{2}^{100}\ind\left(\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{c}}\right)\ind\Big(\|\phi_{k}\|_{2}\leq 1+C_{5}\sqrt{\frac{\log\frac{n}{\delta}}{n}}\Big)\ind\Big(\max_{k}\|\phi_{k}\|_{2}>1+C_{5}\sqrt{\frac{\log\frac{n}{\delta}}{n}}\Big)\right]

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+n^{100}\sum_{k}\mathbb{E}\left[\|\phi_{k}\|_{2}^{100}\ind\Big(\|\phi_{k}\|_{2}>1+C_{5}\sqrt{\frac{\log\frac{n}{\delta}}{n}}\Big)\right]

\displaystyle\qquad\lesssim n^{100}\sum_{k}\left(1+C_{5}\sqrt{\frac{\log\frac{n}{\delta}}{n}}\right)^{100}\delta+n^{100}\sum_{k}\int_{C_{5}\sqrt{\frac{\log\frac{n}{\delta}}{n}}}^{\infty}(1+x)^{100}\exp(-\frac{nx^{2}}{2})\mathrm{d}x\leq n^{200}\delta+n^{-100},

provided that
\log\frac{1}{\delta}\lesssim\log n
and that
C_{5}
is large enough.
Putting these two cases together and choosing
\delta\asymp n^{-300}
finish the proof.
∎

D.1.2
Properties about the denoising function
\eta_{t}

Recall that the denoising function is

\eta_{t}(x)=\gamma_{t}\tanh(\pi_{t}x)\qquad\text{with }\pi_{t}=\sqrt{n(\|x_{t}\|_{2}^{2}-1)}\text{ and }\gamma_{t}=\left\|\tanh\left(\pi_{t}x_{t}\right)\right\|_{2}^{-1}.

In this subsection, we single out several useful properties related to
\eta_{t}(\cdot)
.

Tight estimates of
\pi_{t}
and
\gamma_{t}
.

Given that
\eta_{t}(\cdot)
involves two quantities
\pi_{t}
and
\gamma_{t}
,
we first develop tight bounds on the sizes of them in the following,
which are legitimate under the induction hypotheses (
196
). The proof is deferred to Section
D.1.3
.

Lemma 14
.

Under the induction hypotheses (
196
), we have

\displaystyle\pi_{t}

\displaystyle=\bigg(1+O\bigg(\frac{S_{t}}{\alpha^{2}_{t}}\bigg)\bigg)\alpha_{t}\sqrt{n}=\big(1+o(1)\big)\alpha_{t}\sqrt{n}

(203a)

\displaystyle\gamma_{t}^{-2}

\displaystyle=\left(1+O\bigg(\frac{S_{t}}{\alpha_{t}}+\frac{S_{t}}{\alpha_{t}^{3}}\bigg)\right)n\int\tanh\big(\alpha_{t}(\alpha_{t}+x)\big)\varphi(\mathrm{d}x)\asymp\alpha_{t}^{2}n

(203b)

with probability exceeding
1-O(n^{-11})
.

Bounds on derivatives and gradients.

Next, we look at the derivatives and gradients of the denoising function.
As can be straightforwardly seen,
the function
\eta_{t}(x)\coloneqq\gamma_{t}\tanh(\pi_{t}x)
is smooth everywhere, whose first three derivatives are given by

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \eta_{t}^{\prime}(x)&=\gamma_{t}\pi_{t}\big(1-\tanh^{2}(\pi_{t}x)\big)\\
\eta_{t}^{\prime\prime}(x)&=-2\gamma_{t}\pi_{t}^{2}\tanh(\pi_{t}x)\big(1-\tanh^{2}(\pi_{t}x)\big)\\
\eta_{t}^{(\prime\prime\prime)}(x)&=-2\gamma_{t}\pi_{t}^{3}\big(1-\tanh^{2}(\pi_{t}x)\big)\big(1-3\tanh^{2}(\pi_{t}x)\big)\end{aligned}

(204)

for any
x\in\real
. Combining the identities with (
203
) and the fact
|\tanh(x)|\leq 1
, we can easily validate that

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &|\eta_{t}(x)|\lesssim\frac{1}{\alpha_{t}\sqrt{n}},\qquad\qquad&&|\eta_{t}^{\prime}(x)|\lesssim 1\eqqcolon\rho,\\
&|\eta_{t}^{\prime\prime}(x)|\lesssim\alpha_{t}\sqrt{n}\lesssim\sqrt{n}\eqqcolon\rho_{1},\qquad&&|\eta_{t}^{(\prime\prime\prime)}(x)|\lesssim\alpha_{t}^{2}n\lesssim n\eqqcolon\rho_{2}.\end{aligned}

(205)

Next, let us consider any given vectors
\mu=[\mu^{k}]_{-2s\leq k\leq t-1}\in\mathcal{S}^{t+2s-1}
,
\beta=[\beta^{k}]_{-2s\leq k\leq t-1}\in\mathcal{S}^{t+2s-1}
,
and any given
\alpha\in\real
obeying
\lambda\geq\alpha\geq\sqrt{\lambda^{2}-1}
(note that, for the moment, we shall treat them as fixed parameters independent of
\{\phi_{k}\}
).
We shall also define

\displaystyle\eta_{t}^{(i)}\big(v(\alpha,\beta)\big)\coloneqq\eta_{t}^{(i)}\Big(\alpha v^{\star}+\sum_{k=-2s}^{t-1}\beta^{k}\phi_{k}\Big)\in\real^{n},\quad\text{with }v(\alpha,\beta)\coloneqq\alpha v^{\star}+\sum_{k=-2s}^{t-1}\beta^{k}\phi_{k},

where the superscript
i
denotes the
i
-th derivative (computed in an entrywise manner).
In what follows, we collect several elementary results that are useful for our main proof.

\displaystyle\Big\|\nabla_{\phi_{j}}\Big\langle\sum_{k=-2s}^{t-1}\mu^{k}\phi_{k},a\Big\rangle\Big\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq|\mu^{j}|\cdot\|a\|_{2},\qquad

\displaystyle\text{for any given }a\in\real^{n}

(206a)

\displaystyle\Big\|\nabla_{\phi_{j}}\big\langle\eta_{t}^{(s)}\big(v(\alpha,\beta)\big),a\big\rangle\Big\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq|\beta^{j}|\cdot\big\|\eta_{t}^{(s+1)}\big(v(\alpha,\beta)\big)\circ a\big\|_{2},\qquad

\displaystyle\text{for any given }a\in\real^{n}

(206b)

\displaystyle\Big\|\nabla_{\mu}\Big\langle\sum_{k=-2s}^{t-1}\mu^{k}\phi_{k},a\Big\rangle\Big\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\|a\|_{2}\sum_{k=-2s}^{t-1}\|\phi_{k}\|_{2},\qquad

\displaystyle\text{for any given }a\in\real^{n}

(206c)

\displaystyle\Big\|\nabla_{\mu,\beta}\Big(\sum_{k=-2s}^{t-1}\mu^{k}\beta^{k}\Big)\Big\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\|\mu\|_{2}+\|\beta\|_{2}=2,

(206d)

\displaystyle\Big\|\nabla_{\beta}\Big\langle\eta_{t}^{(s)}\big(v(\alpha,\beta)\big),a\Big\rangle\Big\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\|a\|_{2}\cdot\big\|\eta_{t}^{(s+1)}\big(v(\alpha,\beta)\big)\big\|_{2}\cdot\sum_{k=-2s}^{t-1}\|\phi_{k}\|_{2},\qquad

\displaystyle\text{for any given }a\in\real^{n}.

(206e)

The proofs of these results are fairly elementary and are hence omitted for the sake of brevity.

D.1.3
Proof of tight estimates of
\pi_{t}
and
\gamma_{t}
(Lemma
14
)

Bounding quantity
\pi_{t}
.

In view of Lemma
8
and the fact that
v^{\star\top}\big[\phi_{1},\cdots,\phi_{t-1}\big]\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\frac{1}{n}I_{t-1})
, we have

\displaystyle\left\|\sum_{k=-2s}^{t-1}\beta_{t-1}^{k}\phi_{k}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle=1+O\left(\sqrt{\frac{(t+s)\log n}{n}}\right),

\displaystyle\Big|\Big\langle v^{\star},\sum_{k=-2s}^{t-1}\beta_{t-1}^{k}\phi_{k}\Big\rangle\Big|

\displaystyle=\Big|\Big\langle v^{\star\top}\big[\phi_{-2s},\cdots,\phi_{t-1}\big],\big[\beta_{t-1}^{-2s},\cdots\beta_{t-1}^{t-1}\big]\Big\rangle\Big|\leq\Big\|v^{\star\top}\big[\phi_{-2s},\cdots,\phi_{t-1}\big]\Big\|_{2}\|\beta_{t-1}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{(t+s)\log n}{n}}

with probability exceeding
1-O(n^{-11})
, where we recall that
\|\beta_{t-1}\|_{2}=1
.
As a result, recalling the induction hypothesis that
|\alpha_{t}|\leq\lambda\lesssim 1
(see (
196a
)), we arrive at

\Big\|\alpha_{t}v^{\star}+\sum_{k=-2s}^{t-1}\beta_{t-1}^{k}\phi_{k}\Big\|_{2}^{2}=\alpha_{t}^{2}+2\alpha_{t}\Big\langle v^{\star},\sum_{k=-2s}^{t-1}\beta_{t-1}^{k}\phi_{k}\Big\rangle+\Big\|\sum_{k=-2s}^{t-1}\beta_{t-1}^{k}\phi_{k}\Big\|_{2}^{2}=\alpha_{t}^{2}+1+O\Big(\sqrt{\frac{(t+s)\log n}{n}}\Big)\lesssim 1.

Invoke the other induction hypothesis (
196b
) and the condition
s\asymp\frac{\log n}{(\lambda-1)^{2}}
to obtain

\displaystyle\|x_{t}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\Big\|\alpha_{t}v^{\star}+\sum_{k=-2s}^{t-1}\beta_{t-1}^{k}\phi_{k}+\xi_{t-1}\Big\|_{2}^{2}=\Big\|\alpha_{t}v^{\star}+\sum_{k=-2s}^{t-1}\beta_{t-1}^{k}\phi_{k}\Big\|_{2}^{2}+2\Big\langle\xi_{t-1},\alpha_{t}v^{\star}+\sum_{k=-2s}^{t-1}\beta_{t-1}^{k}\phi_{k}\Big\rangle+\|\xi_{t-1}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\alpha_{t}^{2}+1+O\Big(\sqrt{\frac{(t+s)\log n}{n}}\Big)+O\bigg(\big\|\xi_{t-1}\big\|_{2}\Big\|\alpha_{t}v^{\star}+\sum_{k=-2s}^{t-1}\beta_{t-1}^{k}\phi_{k}\Big\|_{2}\bigg)+\|\xi_{t-1}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\alpha_{t}^{2}+1+O\Big(\sqrt{\frac{(t+s)\log n}{n}}\Big)+O\left(\|\xi_{t-1}\|_{2}\right)=\alpha_{t}^{2}+1+O(S_{t}).

Therefore, we can conclude that

\displaystyle\pi_{t}=\sqrt{n(\|x_{t}\|_{2}^{2}-1)}

\displaystyle=\alpha_{t}\sqrt{n}\left(1+O\left(\frac{S_{t}}{\alpha^{2}_{t}}\right)\right),

(207)

where we use the induction hypothesis (
196
).
This establishes the advertised relation (
203a
) about
\pi_{t}
.

Bounding quantity
\gamma_{t}
.

Before proceeding, we find it helpful to first establish a connection between

\|\tanh\left(\pi_{t}x_{t}\right)\|_{2}
and
\|\tanh(\pi_{t}v_{t})\|_{2}
, where we recall that
x_{t}=v_{t}+\xi_{t-1}
.
Recognizing that
|\tanh(x)|\leq 1
and
|\tanh^{\prime}(x)|\leq 1
, we can guarantee that, for each
1\leq i\leq n
,

\displaystyle\big|\tanh\left(\pi_{t}x_{t,i}\right)-\tanh\left(\pi_{t}v_{t,i}\right)\big|\leq\pi_{t}|{\xi}_{t-1,i}|\lesssim\alpha_{t}\sqrt{n}\cdot|{\xi}_{t-1,i}|,

where the last inequality follows from (
207
).
By virtue of the the induction hypothesis (
196b
),
we can obtain

\displaystyle\left|\big\|\tanh\left(\pi_{t}x_{t}\right)\big\|_{2}^{2}-\big\|\tanh\left(\pi_{t}v_{t}\right)\big\|_{2}^{2}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\big\|\tanh\left(\pi_{t}x_{t}\right)-\tanh\left(\pi_{t}v_{t}\right)\big\|_{2}\cdot\big\|\tanh\left(\pi_{t}x_{t}\right)+\tanh\left(\pi_{t}v_{t}\right)\big\|_{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\alpha_{t}n\|\xi_{t-1}\|_{2}\lesssim\alpha_{t}nS_{t}.

(208)

The above relation (
208
)
allows us to turn attention to the quantity
\left\|\tanh\left(\pi_{t}v_{t}\right)\right\|_{2}^{2}
,
towards which we would like to invoke Lemma
6

to control the following quantity

\displaystyle\big\|\tanh\left(\pi_{t}v_{t}\right)\big\|_{2}^{2}-n\int\tanh^{2}\left(\frac{\pi_{t}}{\sqrt{n}}\left(\alpha_{t}+x\right)\right)\varphi(\mathrm{d}x).

Given that for any coordinate
1\leq i\leq n
, one has
\sqrt{n}v^{\star}_{i}\in\{+1,-1\}
and hence (due to symmetry)

\displaystyle\int\tanh^{2}\left(\pi_{t}\left(\alpha_{t}v^{\star}_{i}+\frac{x}{\sqrt{n}}\right)\right)\varphi(\mathrm{d}x)=\int\tanh^{2}\left(\frac{\pi_{t}}{\sqrt{n}}\left(\alpha_{t}+x\right)\right)\varphi(\mathrm{d}x),

we are motivated to look at the following function

\displaystyle f_{\theta}(\Phi)\coloneqq\big\|\tanh(\pi v)\big\|_{2}^{2}-\int\bigg\|\tanh\left(\pi\left(\alpha v^{\star}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}x\right)\right)\bigg\|_{2}^{2}\varphi_{n}(\mathrm{d}x)\qquad\text{where }v\coloneqq\alpha v^{\star}+\sum_{k=-2s}^{t-1}\beta^{k}\phi_{k}.

where we define

\Phi=\sqrt{n}\big[\phi_{-2s},\ldots,\phi_{t-1}\big],\qquad\theta=[\alpha,\beta,\pi]\in\real^{t+2s+2}\qquad\text{and}\qquad\beta=[\beta^{-2s},\cdots,\beta^{t-1}].

Clearly,
in order to bound
\big\|\tanh(\pi_{t}v_{t})\big\|_{2}^{2}-\int\big\|\tanh\big(\pi_{t}\big(\alpha_{t}v^{\star}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}x\big)\big)\big\|_{2}^{2}\varphi_{n}(\mathrm{d}x)
,
it suffices to develop a bound on
f_{\theta}(\Phi)
uniformly over all
\theta
within the following set:

\Theta\coloneqq\Big\{\theta=(\alpha,\beta,\pi)\mid\|\beta\|_{2}=1,\sqrt{\lambda-1}\lesssim\alpha\lesssim 1,\pi\asymp\alpha\sqrt{n}\Big\}.

(209)

Towards this end, observe that

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla_{\Phi}f_{\theta}(\Phi)\right\|_{2}\leq\frac{2\pi\|\beta\|_{2}}{\sqrt{n}}\left\|\tanh(\pi v)\circ\tanh^{\prime}(\pi v)\right\|_{2}\leq 2\pi\|\beta\|_{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\alpha\sqrt{n},

where we have used the facts that
\|\beta\|_{2}=1
, and
\pi\asymp\alpha\sqrt{n}
.
Additionally, it is straightforward to check that
f_{\theta}(\Phi)
obeys
\|\nabla_{\theta}f_{\theta}(\Phi)\|_{2}\lesssim n^{100}

for all
Z\in\mathcal{E}
and
|f_{\theta}(\Phi)|\lesssim n^{100}\big(\max_{k}\|\phi_{k}\|_{2}\big)^{100}
.
For any fixed
\theta
, it is readily seen that
\mathbb{E}[f_{\theta}(\Phi)]=0
.
Applying Corollary
2
in conjunction with (
202
) yields

\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}\bigg|\frac{1}{\alpha}f_{\theta}(\Phi)\bigg|\lesssim\sqrt{n(t+s)\log n}

with probability at least
1-O(n^{-11})
. This in turn leads to

\displaystyle\left|\big\|\tanh(\pi_{t}v_{t})\big\|_{2}^{2}-\int\Big\|\tanh\left(\pi_{t}\left(\alpha_{t}v^{\star}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}x\right)\right)\Big\|_{2}^{2}\varphi(\mathrm{d}x)\right|\leq\alpha_{t}\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}\frac{1}{\alpha}\left|f_{\theta}(\Phi)\right|\lesssim\alpha_{t}\sqrt{(t+s)n\log n}.

(210)

Putting (
207
), (
208
), and (
210
) together leads to

\displaystyle\|\tanh(\pi_{t}x_{t})\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=n\int\tanh^{2}\left(\frac{\pi_{t}}{\sqrt{n}}\left(\alpha_{t}+x\right)\right)\varphi(\mathrm{d}x)+O\Big(\alpha_{t}nS_{t}+\alpha_{t}\sqrt{(t+s)n\log n}\Big)

\displaystyle=n\int\tanh^{2}\left(\frac{\pi_{t}}{\sqrt{n}}\left(\alpha_{t}+x\right)\right)\varphi(\mathrm{d}x)+O\big(\alpha_{t}nS_{t}\big).

(211)

In view of the mean value theorem and the fact that
|(\tanh^{2})^{\prime}(w)|=|2\tanh(w)\tanh^{\prime}(w)|\leq 2
(and hence
\tanh^{2}
is 2-Lipschitz continuous), we have

\displaystyle\bigg|\tanh^{2}\Big(\frac{\pi_{t}}{\sqrt{n}}(\alpha_{t}+x)\Big)-\tanh^{2}\Big(\alpha_{t}(\alpha_{t}+x)\Big)\bigg|

\displaystyle\leq 2\left|\Big(\frac{\pi_{t}}{\sqrt{n}}-\alpha_{t}\Big)(\alpha_{t}+x)\right|\leq 2\left|\frac{\pi_{t}}{\sqrt{n}}-\alpha_{t}\right|\big(\alpha_{t}+|x|\big),

(212)

which together with (
207
) yields

\displaystyle\bigg|{\displaystyle\int}\tanh^{2}\Big(\frac{\pi_{t}}{\sqrt{n}}(\alpha_{t}+x)\Big)\varphi(\mathrm{d}x)-{\displaystyle\int}\tanh^{2}\Big(\alpha_{t}(\alpha_{t}+x)\Big)\varphi(\mathrm{d}x)\bigg|

\displaystyle\qquad\leq 2\left|\frac{\pi_{t}}{\sqrt{n}}-\alpha_{t}\right|\left({\displaystyle\int}\alpha_{t}\varphi(\mathrm{d}x)+{\displaystyle\int}|x|\varphi(\mathrm{d}x)\right)\lesssim\left|\frac{\pi_{t}}{\sqrt{n}}-\alpha_{t}\right|\lesssim\frac{S_{t}}{\alpha_{t}}.

(213)

Substitution into (
211
) gives

\displaystyle\big\|\tanh(\pi_{t}x_{t})\big\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=n\int\tanh^{2}\big(\alpha_{t}(\alpha_{t}+x)\big)\varphi(\mathrm{d}x)+O\bigg(\alpha_{t}nS_{t}+\frac{nS_{t}}{\alpha_{t}}\bigg)

\displaystyle=n\int\tanh\big(\alpha_{t}(\alpha_{t}+x)\big)\varphi(\mathrm{d}x)+O\left(\alpha_{t}^{2}n\bigg(\frac{S_{t}}{\alpha_{t}}+\frac{S_{t}}{\alpha_{t}^{3}}\bigg)\right),

(214)

where the last line follows from
Deshpande et al., 2017
.

Finally, we justify that
\int\tanh\left(\alpha_{t}\left(\alpha_{t}+x\right)\right)\varphi(\mathrm{d}x)\asymp\alpha_{t}^{2}
.
Towards this, we make the observation that

\displaystyle\int\tanh(\alpha_{t}^{2}+\alpha_{t}x)\varphi(\mathrm{d}x)

\displaystyle\qquad=\int_{0}^{2\alpha_{t}}\tanh(\alpha_{t}^{2}+\alpha_{t}x)\varphi(\mathrm{d}x)+\int_{\alpha_{t}}^{\infty}\left\{\tanh\big(\alpha_{t}^{2}+\alpha_{t}(\alpha_{t}+z)\big)+\tanh\big(\alpha_{t}^{2}+\alpha_{t}(\alpha_{t}-z)\big)\right\}\varphi(\mathrm{d}x)

\displaystyle\qquad\geq\int_{0}^{2\alpha_{t}}\tanh(\alpha_{t}^{2}+\alpha_{t}x)\varphi(\mathrm{d}x)\geq\int_{0}^{2\alpha_{t}}\tanh^{\prime}(3\alpha_{t}^{2})(\alpha_{t}^{2}+\alpha_{t}x)\varphi(\mathrm{d}x)

\displaystyle\qquad\geq\Big(1-\tanh^{2}(3\alpha_{t}^{2})\Big)\Big(2\alpha_{t}^{3}+\varphi(2\alpha_{t})2\alpha_{t}^{2}\Big)\asymp\alpha_{t}^{2},

where the first inequality follows since
\tanh\big(\alpha_{t}^{2}+\alpha_{t}(\alpha_{t}+z)\big)+\tanh\big(\alpha_{t}^{2}+\alpha_{t}(\alpha_{t}-z)\big)\geq 0

for any
z\geq 0
,
the second inequality holds since
\tanh^{\prime}(0)=0
and
\tanh^{\prime}(w)
is decreasing in
w
for
w\geq 0
,
and the last line uses
\tanh^{\prime}(w)=1-\tanh^{2}(w)
and the induction hypothesis that
\alpha_{t}\lesssim 1
(cf. (
196a
)).
Additionally, it results from the Taylor expansion as well as the
facts
\tanh(0)=0
and
|\tanh^{\prime\prime}(w)|\leq 1
that

\displaystyle\int\tanh(\alpha_{t}^{2}+\alpha_{t}x)\varphi(\mathrm{d}x)

\displaystyle\leq\int(\alpha_{t}^{2}+\alpha_{t}x)\varphi(\mathrm{d}x)+\frac{1}{2}\int|\alpha_{t}^{2}+\alpha_{t}x|^{2}\varphi(\mathrm{d}x)

\displaystyle\leq\int(\alpha_{t}^{2}+\alpha_{t}x)\varphi(\mathrm{d}x)+\int\alpha_{t}^{4}\varphi(\mathrm{d}x)+\int\alpha_{t}^{2}x^{2}\varphi(\mathrm{d}x)=2\alpha_{t}^{2}+\alpha_{t}^{4}.

Consequently, we have justified that

\int\tanh(\alpha_{t}^{2}+\alpha_{t}x)\varphi(\mathrm{d}x)\asymp\alpha_{t}^{2}

(215)

given that
\alpha_{t}\lesssim 1
(cf. (
196a
)).
Combining this with (
214
) and the induction hypothesis that
\sqrt{\lambda-1}\lesssim\alpha_{t}\lesssim 1
(cf. (
196a
)), we reach

\displaystyle\big\|\tanh(\pi_{t}x_{t})\big\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\left(1+O\bigg(\frac{S_{t}}{\alpha_{t}}+\frac{S_{t}}{\alpha_{t}^{3}}\bigg)\right)n\int\tanh\big(\alpha_{t}(\alpha_{t}+x)\big)\varphi(\mathrm{d}x)\asymp n\alpha_{t}^{2},

(216)

provided that
\frac{S_{t}}{\alpha_{t}}+\frac{S_{t}}{\alpha_{t}^{3}}\ll 1
.
This concludes the proof of Lemma
14
.

D.2
Controlling several key quantities
A_{t},B_{t},D_{t}

By virtue of Theorem
2
or Corollary
1
,
the behavior of
\alpha_{t+1}
is governed by a couple of key quantities as defined in Assumptions
2
(except that those sums w.r.t.
\sum_{k=1}^{t-1}
there should be replaced with
\sum_{k=-2s}^{t-1}
to account for spectral initialization).
Several immediate remarks are in order.

•

As alluded to previously, there is no need to bound
\Delta_{\beta,t}
given that
\|\beta_{t}\|_{2}
is fixed. As a result,
there is no need in controlling
C_{t},F_{t}
and
G_{t}
.

•

Given that the denoising function is smooth everywhere, we clearly have
E_{t}=0
.

With these remarks in mind,
the proof of Theorem
4
largely consists of identifying sufficiently small quantities
A_{t},B_{t},D_{t}
such that (
14a
), (
14b
) and (
14d
) are satisfied with high probability, which forms the main content of this subsection.
The analysis in this subsection operates under the induction hypotheses (
196
).

D.2.1
Quantity
A_{t}
in (
14a
)

Recall that this part is concerned with bounding the following quantity

\displaystyle\Big\langle\sum_{k=-2s}^{t-1}\mu^{k}_{t}\phi_{k},\eta_{t}(v_{t})\Big\rangle-\left\langle\eta_{t}^{\prime}(v_{t})\right\rangle\sum_{k=-2s}^{t-1}\mu^{k}_{t}\beta_{t-1}^{k};

(217)

note that the summation starts from
k=-2s
in order to take into account spectral initialization.
In order to analyze this quantity, we introduce

\Phi\coloneqq\sqrt{n}(\phi_{-2s},\ldots,\phi_{t-1}),\qquad\theta\coloneqq(\mu,\alpha,\beta,\pi,\gamma)\in\mathcal{S}^{2s+t-1}\times\real\times\mathcal{S}^{2s+t-1}\times\real\times\real,

(218)

and define the following function

\displaystyle f_{\theta}(\Phi)\coloneqq\Big\langle\sum_{k=-2s}^{t-1}\mu^{k}\phi_{k},\eta\Big(\alpha v^{\star}+\sum_{k=-2s}^{t-1}\beta^{k}\phi_{k}\Big)\Big\rangle-\Big\langle\eta^{\prime}\Big(\alpha v^{\star}+\sum_{k=-2s}^{t-1}\beta^{k}\phi_{k}\Big)\Big\rangle\sum_{k=-2s}^{t-1}\mu^{k}\beta^{k},

where

\eta(w)\coloneqq\gamma^{-1}\tanh(\pi w);

(219)

here, we suppress the dependency on
\alpha
,
\pi
and
\gamma
in the notation
\eta(\cdot)
for simplicity.
Clearly, the quantity (
217
) can be expressed as
f_{\theta}(\Phi)
with
\alpha=\alpha_{t}
,
\mu=\mu_{t},\beta=\beta_{t-1},\pi=\pi_{t}
and
\gamma=\gamma_{t}
;
these parameters, however, are statistically dependent on
\Phi
. As a result, we resort to Lemma
6
in order to obtain a uniform control over all parameters
within a suitable region

\Theta\coloneqq\Big\{\theta=(\mu,\alpha,\beta,\pi,\gamma)\mid\|\mu\|_{2}=\|\beta\|_{2}=1,\sqrt{\lambda-1}\lesssim\alpha\lesssim 1,\pi\asymp\gamma^{-1}\asymp\alpha\sqrt{n}\Big\}.

(220)

It follows immediately from the calculation in Section
D.1.2
that, for any
\theta\in\Theta
and any
x\in\real
,

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &|\eta(x)|\lesssim\frac{1}{\alpha\sqrt{n}},\qquad|\eta^{\prime}(x)|\lesssim 1,\qquad|\eta^{\prime\prime}(x)|\lesssim\alpha\sqrt{n},\qquad&&|\eta^{\prime\prime\prime}(x)|\lesssim\alpha^{2}n.\end{aligned}

(221)

Clearly, we can see that (i)
\|\theta\|_{2}\lesssim\sqrt{n}
for any
\theta\in\Theta
,
(ii)
\|\nabla_{\theta}f_{\theta}(Z)\|_{2}\lesssim n^{100}
for any
Z\in\mathcal{E}
(see (
200
)),
and (iii)
|f_{\theta}(\Phi)|\lesssim n^{100}\big(\max_{k}\|\phi_{k}\|_{2}\big)^{100}
.
Then according to (
202
),
it would be natural to invoke Corollary
2
to obtain uniform control of
f_{\theta}(\Phi)
.
The main step then boils down to bounding
\nabla_{\Phi}f_{\theta}(\Phi)
, which we accomplish in what follows.

Letting
v\coloneqq\alpha v^{\star}+\sum_{k=-2s}^{t-1}\beta^{k}\phi_{k}
for notational simplicity,
we can directly bound the derivative of
f
w.r.t.
\Phi
as follows:

\displaystyle\big\|\nabla_{\Phi}f_{\theta}(\Phi)\big\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\|\mu\|_{2}}{\sqrt{n}}\left\|\eta_{t}(v)\right\|_{2}+\frac{\|\beta\|_{2}}{\sqrt{n}}\Big\|\sum_{k=-2s}^{t-1}\mu^{k}\phi_{k}\circ\eta^{\prime}(v)\Big\|_{2}+\bigg(\frac{\|\beta\|_{2}}{n\sqrt{n}}\left\|\eta^{\prime\prime}(v)\right\|_{2}\bigg)\big(\|\mu\|_{2}\|\beta\|_{2}\big)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\left\|\eta_{t}(v)\right\|_{2}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\Big\|\sum_{k=-2s}^{t-1}\mu^{k}\phi_{k}\circ\eta^{\prime}(v)\Big\|_{2}+\frac{1}{n\sqrt{n}}\left\|\eta^{\prime\prime}(v)\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{\alpha\sqrt{n}},

where the first inequality follows from (
206a
) and (
206b
),
the second line relies on the condition
\|\mu\|_{2}=\|\beta\|_{2}=1
,
and the last inequality makes use of (
205
), the condition
\sqrt{\lambda-1}\lesssim\alpha\lesssim 1
, and the fact that
\big\|\sum_{k=-2s}^{t-1}\mu^{k}\phi_{k}\big\|_{2}\lesssim 1
(see the second pair of curly brackets in (
199
)).
Applying Corollary
2
then gives

\displaystyle\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}\big|\alpha f_{\theta}(\Phi)-\alpha\mathbb{E}[f_{\theta}(\Phi)]\big|\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{(t+s)\log n}{n}}

(222)

with probability at least
1-O(n^{-11})
.

In addition, we observe that: for any fixed parameter
\theta
, Stein’s lemma reveals that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[f_{\theta}(\Phi)\big]=\mathbb{E}\left[\Big\langle\sum_{k=-2s}^{t-1}\mu^{k}\phi_{k},\eta_{t}\Big(\alpha v^{\star}+\sum_{k=-2s}^{t-1}\beta^{k}\phi_{k}\Big)\Big\rangle-\Big\langle\eta_{t}^{\prime}\Big(\alpha v^{\star}+\sum_{k=-2s}^{t-1}\beta^{k}\phi_{k}\Big)\Big\rangle\sum_{k=-2s}^{t-1}\mu^{k}\beta^{k}\right]=0,

which together with (
223
) gives

\displaystyle\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}\Big\{\alpha\,\big|f_{\theta}(\Phi)\big|\Big\}\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{(t+s)\log n}{n}}

(223)

with probability at least
1-O(n^{-11})
.
Consequently, it is sufficient to take

\displaystyle A_{t}\asymp\frac{1}{\alpha_{t}}\sqrt{\frac{(t+s)\log n}{n}}.

(224)

D.2.2
Quantity
B_{t}
in (
14b
)

Regarding the quantity
B_{t}
, we need to examine the following function

\displaystyle f_{\theta}(\Phi)\coloneqq v^{\star\top}\eta(v),\qquad\text{with }v\coloneqq\alpha v^{\star}+\sum_{k=-2s}^{t-1}\beta^{k}\phi_{k},

where
\Phi
and
\theta
are defined in (
218
), and
\eta(\cdot)
is defined in (
219
).
Clearly, the target quantity on the left-hand side of (
14b
)
can be viewed as
f_{\theta}(\Phi)
with
\alpha=\alpha_{t},\beta=\beta_{t-1},\mu=\mu_{t},\pi=\pi_{t}
and
\gamma=\gamma_{t}
.
When it comes to the convex set
\mathcal{E}
(cf. (
200
)) and the parameter set
\Theta
(cf. (
220
)),
it is straightforward to verify that
\|\nabla_{\theta}f_{\theta}(Z)\|_{2}\lesssim n^{100}
for any
Z\in\mathcal{E}

and
|f_{\theta}(Z)|\lesssim n^{100}\big(\|Z\|_{\mathrm{F}}\big)^{100}
.
Therefore,
in view of (
202
),
we shall resort to Corollary
2
to obtain uniform control of
f_{\theta}(\Phi)
over all
\theta\in\Theta
.

Invoking inequality (
206b
) yields

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla_{\Phi}f_{\theta}(\Phi)\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\|\beta\|_{2}}{\sqrt{n}}\left\|v^{\star}\circ\eta^{\prime}(v)\right\|_{2}\lesssim\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}},

where the last inequality holds since, according to (
221
),

\displaystyle\|\eta^{\prime}(v)\circ v^{\star}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|v^{\star}\|_{2}=1.

(225)

Apply Corollary
2
to arrive at

\displaystyle\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}\left|v^{\star\top}\eta(v)-\mathbb{E}[v^{\star\top}\eta(v)]\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{(t+s)\log n}{n}}

with probability at least
1-O(n^{-11})
.
In addition, for every given
\theta
we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[v^{\star\top}\eta(v)]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[v^{\star\top}\eta\Big(\alpha v^{\star}+\sum_{k=-2s}^{t-1}\beta^{k}\phi_{k}\Big)\right]=v^{\star\top}\int\eta\left(\alpha v^{\star}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}x\right)\varphi_{n}(\mathrm{d}x),

where
\varphi_{n}(\cdot)
is the CDF of
\mathcal{N}(0,I_{n})
.
Therefore, in view of the definition (
14b
), it is sufficient to set

\displaystyle B_{t}\asymp\sqrt{\frac{(t+s)\log n}{n}}.

(226)

D.2.3
Quantity
D_{t}
in (
14d
)

With regards to quantity
D_{t}
, we aim to justify that

\displaystyle\Big\|\sum_{k=-2s}^{t-1}\mu_{t}^{k}\phi_{k}\circ\eta_{t}^{\prime}(v_{t})-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{k=-2s}^{t-1}\mu_{t}^{k}\beta_{t-1}^{k}\eta_{t}^{\prime\prime}(v_{t})\Big\|_{2}^{2}-\kappa_{t}^{2}\lesssim D_{t}\asymp\sqrt{\frac{(t+s)\log^{2}n}{n}}.

(227)

In order to prove this, let us introduce the following function

\displaystyle f_{\theta}(\Phi)\coloneqq\Big\|\sum_{k=-2s}^{t-1}\mu^{k}\phi_{k}\circ\eta^{\prime}(v)-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{k=-2s}^{t-1}\mu^{k}\beta^{k}\eta^{\prime\prime}(v)\Big\|_{2}^{2}-\kappa^{2},

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\text{with }v\coloneqq\alpha v^{\star}+\sum_{k=-2s}^{t-1}\beta^{k}\phi_{k};

here,
\Phi
and
\theta
are defined in (
218
),
\eta(\cdot)
is defined in (
219
),
whereas
\kappa
is defined such that

\displaystyle\kappa^{2}\coloneqq\max\Bigg\{\Bigg\langle\int\Big[x\eta^{\prime}\Big(\alpha v^{\star}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}x\Big)-\frac{\|\beta\|_{2}}{\sqrt{n}}\eta^{\prime\prime}\Big(\alpha v^{\star}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}x\Big)\Big]^{2}\varphi_{n}(\mathrm{d}x)\Bigg\rangle,~

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\bigg\langle\int\Big[\eta^{\prime}\Big(\alpha v^{\star}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}x\big)\Big]^{2}\varphi_{n}(\mathrm{d}x)\bigg\rangle\Bigg\}.

(228)

We shall also introduce the set
\mathcal{E}
(resp.
\Theta
) as in (
200
) (resp. (
220
)).
Once again, it is easily seen that

\|\nabla_{\theta}f_{\theta}(Z)\|_{2}\lesssim n^{100}
holds for any
Z\in\mathcal{E}

and
|f_{\theta}(Z)|\lesssim n^{100}\big(\|Z\|_{\mathrm{F}}\big)^{100}
.
In light of (
202
),
it is natural to apply Corollary
2
to obtain uniform control of
f_{\theta}(\Phi)
over all
\theta\in\Theta
,
which we detail as follows.

To begin with, we can take the derivative and use
\|\mu\|_{2}=\|\beta\|_{2}=1
to obtain

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla_{\Phi}f_{\theta}(\Phi)\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{2}{\sqrt{n}}\left\|\sum_{k=-2s}^{t-1}\mu^{k}\phi_{k}\circ\eta^{\prime}(v)\circ\eta^{\prime}(v)\right\|_{2}+\frac{2}{\sqrt{n}}\left\|\sum_{k=-2s}^{t-1}\mu^{k}\phi_{k}\circ\sum_{k=-2s}^{t-1}\mu^{k}\phi_{k}\circ\eta^{\prime}(v)\circ\eta^{\prime\prime}(v)\right\|_{2}+\frac{2}{n^{2}\sqrt{n}}\left\|\eta^{\prime\prime}(v)\circ\eta^{\prime\prime\prime}(v)\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{2}{n\sqrt{n}}\left\|\eta^{\prime}(v)\circ\eta^{\prime\prime}(v)\right\|_{2}+\frac{2}{n\sqrt{n}}\left\|\sum_{k=-2s}^{t-1}\mu^{k}\phi_{k}\circ\eta^{\prime\prime}(v)\circ\eta^{\prime\prime}(v)\right\|_{2}+\frac{2}{n\sqrt{n}}\left\|\sum_{k=-2s}^{t-1}\mu^{k}\phi_{k}\circ\eta^{\prime}(v)\circ\eta^{\prime\prime\prime}(v)\right\|_{2},

(229)

as a consequence of (
206a
) and (
206b
).
With this in place, we can further deduce that

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla_{\Phi}f_{\theta}(\Phi)\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{\log n}{n}},

(230)

whose proof is deferred to the end of this subsection.

Applying Corollary
2
then reveals that: with probability at least
1-O(n^{-11})
,

\displaystyle\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}\Big\{f_{\theta}(\Phi)-\mathbb{E}[f_{\theta}(\Phi)]\Big\}\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{(t+s)\log^{2}n}{n}}.

(231)

Taking
\theta=(\mu_{t},\alpha_{t},\beta_{t-1},\pi_{t},\gamma_{t})

in the above inequality (
231
) and making use of the following observation

\displaystyle\Big\|\sum_{k=-2s}^{t-1}\mu_{t}^{k}\phi_{k}\circ\eta_{t}^{\prime}(v_{t})-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{k=-2s}^{t-1}\mu_{t}^{k}\beta_{t-1}^{k}\eta_{t}^{\prime\prime}(v_{t})\Big\|_{2}^{2}-\kappa_{t}^{2}-\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}\mathbb{E}[f_{\theta}(\Phi)]\leq\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}\Big\{f_{\theta}(\Phi)-\mathbb{E}[f_{\theta}(\Phi)]\Big\},

we arrive at

\displaystyle\Big\|\sum_{k=-2s}^{t-1}\mu_{t}^{k}\phi_{k}\circ\eta_{t}^{\prime}(v_{t})-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{k=-2s}^{t-1}\mu_{t}^{k}\beta_{t-1}^{k}\eta_{t}^{\prime\prime}(v_{t})\Big\|_{2}^{2}-\kappa_{t}^{2}-\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}\mathbb{E}[f_{\theta}(\Phi)]\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{(t+s)\log^{2}n}{n}}.

In order to conclude the proof of (
227
), it suffices to show that for every
\theta\in\Theta
, one has
\mathbb{E}\left[f_{\theta}(\Phi)\right]\leq 0
.
To see this, consider any fixed
\theta
, and use
\varrho
to denote the angle between the two unit vectors
\mu
and
\beta
(so that
\cos\varrho=\langle\mu,\,\beta\rangle
).
Hence, one can write

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\sum_{k=-2s}^{t-1}\mu^{k}\phi_{k}\circ\eta^{\prime}(v)-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{k=-2s}^{t-1}\mu^{k}\beta^{k}\eta^{\prime\prime}(v)\right\|_{2}^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{X,Y\overset{\text{i.i.d.}}{\sim}\mathcal{N}(0,I_{n})}\left[\left\|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}(X\cos\varrho+Y\sin\varrho)\circ\eta^{\prime}\left(\alpha v^{\star}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}X\right)-\frac{\cos\varrho}{n}\eta^{\prime\prime}\left(\alpha v^{\star}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}X\right)\right\|_{2}^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=\cos^{2}\varrho\cdot\mathbb{E}_{X\sim\mathcal{N}(0,I_{n})}\left[\left\|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}X\circ\eta^{\prime}\left(\alpha v^{\star}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}X\right)-\frac{1}{n}\eta^{\prime\prime}\left(\alpha v^{\star}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}X\right)\right\|_{2}^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\qquad+\sin^{2}\varrho\cdot\mathbb{E}_{X\sim\mathcal{N}(0,I_{n})}\left[\left\|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\eta^{\prime}\left(\alpha v^{\star}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}X\right)\right\|_{2}^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\kappa^{2},

(232)

where the last line follows directly from the definition of
\kappa.
This in turn implies that
\mathbb{E}\left[f_{\theta}(\Phi)\right]\leq 0
.

Putting the above pieces together justifies the desired inequality (
227
), provided that (
230
) is valid.

Proof of inequality (
230
).

In the sequel, let us first carry out the calculations for the dominant term — namely, the second term of expression (
D.2.3
);
Note that the
(t+s)
-th largest entry (in magnitude) of
\sum_{k=-2s}^{t-1}\mu^{k}\phi_{k}
obeys

\displaystyle(t+s)\left|\sum_{k=-2s}^{t-1}\mu^{k}\phi_{k}\right|_{(t+1)}^{2}\leq\sum_{i=1}^{t+s}\left|\sum_{k=-2s}^{t-1}\mu^{k}\phi_{k}\right|_{(i)}^{2}\lesssim\frac{(t+s)\log n}{n},

(233a)

which follows from the definition of the event
\mathcal{E}
(cf. (
200
)).
This implies that

\displaystyle\left|\sum_{k=-2s}^{t-1}\mu^{k}\phi_{k}\right|_{(t+s)}\leq C_{7}\sqrt{\frac{\log n}{n}}

(233b)

for some large enough constant
C_{7}>0
.
By virtue of (
221
), it holds that

\displaystyle\bigg\|\sum_{k=-2s}^{t-1}\mu^{k}\phi_{k}\circ\sum_{k=-2s}^{t-1}\mu^{k}\phi_{k}\circ\eta^{\prime}(v)\circ\eta^{\prime\prime}(v)\bigg\|_{2}\lesssim\alpha\sqrt{n}\,\bigg\|\sum_{k=-2s}^{t-1}\mu^{k}\phi_{k}\circ\sum_{k=-2s}^{t-1}\mu^{k}\phi_{k}\bigg\|_{2}

\displaystyle\qquad\lesssim\sqrt{n}\,\bigg\|\sum_{k=-2s}^{t-1}\mu^{k}\phi_{k}\bigg\|_{\infty}\bigg(\sum_{i=1}^{t}\Big|\sum_{k=-2s}^{t-1}\mu^{k}\phi_{k}\Big|_{(i)}^{2}\bigg)^{1/2}+\sqrt{n}\left|\sum_{k=-2s}^{t-1}\mu^{k}\phi_{k}\right|_{(t+1)}\bigg\|\sum_{k=-2s}^{t-1}\mu^{k}\phi_{k}\bigg\|_{2}

\displaystyle\qquad\lesssim\sqrt{n}\sqrt{\frac{(t+s)\log n}{n}}\cdot\sqrt{\frac{(t+s)\log n}{n}}+\sqrt{n}\sqrt{\frac{\log n}{n}}\bigg(1+\sqrt{\frac{(t+s)\log n}{n}}\bigg)

\displaystyle\qquad\lesssim\sqrt{\log n}.

This leads to the desired bound for the second term of (
D.2.3
).
The other terms can be bounded in a similar manner, which we omit here for brevity.

D.3
Establishing the induction hypotheses for the next iteration

In this subsection, we move on to establish the induction hypotheses (
196
) for the
(t+1)
-th iteration,
in addition to controlling several intermediate quantities.
For this purpose, Theorem
2
offers a general recipe to control the residual terms
\|\xi_{t}\|_{2}
and
|\Delta_{\alpha,t}|

by means of the key quantities
A_{t},B_{t},D_{t}
that have been analyzed in Section
D.2
.
Direct application of Theorem
2
or Corollary
1
already leads to non-asymptotic performance bounds.
It turns out that for the problem of
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
synchronization,
we might be able to obtain tighter error bounds (i.e.,
\sqrt{t/n}
vs.
\sqrt{t^{2}/n}
)
if we slightly refine the analysis of Theorem
2
by exploiting the problem-specific structure,
which we shall detail as follows.

D.3.1
Induction step for bounding
\|\xi_{t}\|_{2}

In this subsection, we aim to establish the induction hypothesis (
196b
) for the next iteration (namely, showing that

\|\xi_{t}\|_{2}\leq S_{t+1}
. In view of Theorem
3
and (
14a
), the residual term
\xi_{t}
obeys

\displaystyle\|\xi_{t}\|_{2}\leq\Big\langle\sum_{k=-2s}^{t-1}\mu_{t}^{k}\phi_{k},\delta_{t}\Big\rangle-\langle\delta_{t}^{\prime}\rangle\sum_{k=-2s}^{t-1}\mu_{t}^{k}\beta_{t-1}^{k}+A_{t}+O\Big(\sqrt{\frac{(t+s)\log n}{n}}\Big),

(234)

where
\delta_{t}
and
\delta_{t}^{\prime}
are defined as

\displaystyle\delta_{t}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\eta_{t}\Big(\alpha_{t}v^{\star}+\sum_{k=-2s}^{t-1}\beta_{t-1}^{k}\phi_{k}+\xi_{t-1}\Big)-\eta_{t}\Big(\alpha_{t}v^{\star}+\sum_{k=-2s}^{t-1}\beta_{t-1}^{k}\phi_{k}\Big),

\displaystyle\delta_{t}^{\prime}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\eta_{t}^{\prime}\Big(\alpha_{t}v^{\star}+\sum_{k=-2s}^{t-1}\beta_{t-1}^{k}\phi_{k}+\xi_{t-1}\Big)-\eta_{t}^{\prime}\Big(\alpha_{t}v^{\star}+\sum_{k=-2s}^{t-1}\beta_{t-1}^{k}\phi_{k}\Big).

We have already bounded
A_{t}
in Section
D.2.1
. As a result, it comes down to bounding
\delta_{t}
and
\delta_{t}^{\prime}
.

As alluded to previously, we can obtain slightly tighter bounds than directly invoking Theorem
2
or Corollary
1
,
by improving the proof of Theorem
2
a little a bit with the aid of the special structure of
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
synchronization.
Specifically, recall from (
205
) that

|\eta_{t}(w)|\lesssim\frac{1}{\alpha_{t}\sqrt{n}}\qquad\text{and}\qquad|\eta_{t}^{\prime}(w)|\lesssim 1\qquad\text{for any }w\in\real.

These two basic bounds allow us to strengthen the (
65
) and (
66
) as follows in the proof of Theorem
2
:

\displaystyle\Big|\delta_{t}-\eta_{t}^{\prime}\Big(\alpha_{t}v^{\star}+\sum_{k=1}^{t-1}\beta_{t-1}^{k}\phi_{k}\Big)\circ\xi_{t-1}\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\rho_{1}\big|\xi_{t-1}\big|^{2},

(235a)

\displaystyle\|\delta_{t}\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{\alpha_{t}\sqrt{n}},

(235b)

\displaystyle\bigg|\delta_{t}^{\prime}-\eta_{t}^{\prime\prime}\Big(\alpha_{t}v^{\star}+\sum_{k=1}^{t-1}\beta_{t-1}^{k}\phi_{k}\Big)\circ\xi_{t-1}\bigg|

\displaystyle\leq\rho_{2}\big|\xi_{t-1}\big|^{2},

(235c)

\displaystyle\|\delta_{t}^{\prime}\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\lesssim 1,

(235d)

where we recall that
\Gamma=0
in
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
synchronization (as there is no discontinuous point in
\tanh(\cdot)
).

To help further bound (
235
), we make note of some preliminary facts below. Let us introduce the following index set:

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}\coloneqq\left\{i:\bigg|\sum_{k=-2s}^{t-1}\mu_{t}^{k}\phi_{k,i}\bigg|>C_{7}\sqrt{\frac{\log n}{n}}\right\},

where
C_{7}>0
is a large enough constant employed in (
233
).
By virtue of (
233
), one has

\displaystyle|\mathcal{I}|\leq t+s.

For notational simplicity, we overload the notation by introducing two vectors:

\ind_{\mathcal{I}}\coloneqq\big[\ind_{\mathcal{I}}(i)\big]_{1\leq i\leq n}\in\real^{n}\qquad\text{and}\qquad\ind_{\mathcal{I}^{\mathrm{c}}}\coloneqq\big[\ind_{\mathcal{I}^{\mathrm{c}}}(i)\big]_{1\leq i\leq n}\in\real^{n}.

In addition, let us define

\widehat{\xi}_{t-1}\coloneqq\xi_{t-1}\circ\ind_{\mathcal{I}^{\mathrm{c}}}.

Based on this set of notation, we can readily derive from (
235
) that

\displaystyle\Big|\delta_{t}-\eta_{t}^{\prime}\Big(\alpha_{t}v^{\star}+\sum_{k=1}^{t-1}\beta_{t-1}^{k}\phi_{k}\Big)\circ\widehat{\xi}_{t-1}\Big|

\displaystyle\lesssim\rho_{1}\big|\widehat{\xi}_{t-1}\big|^{2}+\frac{1}{\alpha_{t}\sqrt{n}}\ind_{\mathcal{I}},

(236a)

\displaystyle\bigg|\delta_{t}^{\prime}-\eta_{t}^{\prime\prime}\Big(\alpha_{t}v^{\star}+\sum_{k=1}^{t-1}\beta_{t-1}^{k}\phi_{k}\Big)\circ\xi_{t-1}\bigg|

\displaystyle\leq\rho_{2}\big|\xi_{t-1}\big|^{2}.

(236b)

We aim to control the right-hand side of expression (
234
), which boils down to bounding
\Big\langle\sum_{k=-2s}^{t-1}\mu_{t}^{k}\phi_{k},\delta_{t}\Big\rangle-\langle\delta_{t}^{\prime}\rangle\sum_{k=-2s}^{t-1}\mu_{t}^{k}\beta_{t-1}^{k}
.
Substitution of (
236
) into (
234
) leads to

\displaystyle\|\xi_{t}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\bigg\langle\bigg|\sum_{k=-2s}^{t-1}\mu_{t}^{k}\phi_{k}\bigg|,\rho_{1}\widehat{\xi}_{t-1}^{2}+\frac{1}{\alpha_{t}\sqrt{n}}\ind_{\mathcal{I}}\bigg\rangle+\bigg\langle\sum_{k=-2s}^{t-1}\mu_{t}^{k}\phi_{k},\eta_{t}^{\prime}\Big(\alpha_{t}v^{\star}+\sum_{k=-2s}^{t-1}\beta_{t-1}^{k}\phi_{k}\Big)\circ\widehat{\xi}_{t-1}\bigg\rangle

\displaystyle-\bigg\langle\eta_{t}^{\prime\prime}\Big(\alpha_{t}v^{\star}+\sum_{k=-2s}^{t-1}\beta_{t-1}^{k}\phi_{k}\Big)\circ\xi_{t-1}\bigg\rangle\sum_{k=-2s}^{t-1}\mu_{t}^{k}\beta_{t-1}^{k}+\left\langle\rho_{2}\xi_{t-1}^{2}\right\rangle\bigg|\sum_{k=-2s}^{t-1}\mu_{t}^{k}\beta_{t-1}^{k}\bigg|+A_{t}+O\Big(\sqrt{\frac{(t+s)\log n}{n}}\Big).

(237)

Next, we shall control each term in (
237
) separately.

•

We begin with the first term in (
237
). Recalling the definition of set
\mathcal{I}
and using
\rho_{1}\lesssim\sqrt{n}
, we have

\displaystyle\Bigg\langle\bigg|\sum_{k=-2s}^{t-1}\mu_{t}^{k}\phi_{k}\bigg|,\rho_{1}\widehat{\xi}_{t-1}^{2}+\frac{1}{\alpha_{t}\sqrt{n}}\ind_{\mathcal{I}}\Bigg\rangle

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{\alpha_{t}\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}\Big|\sum_{k=-2s}^{t-1}\mu_{t}^{k}\phi_{k,i}\Big|+\rho_{1}\sum_{i\notin\mathcal{I}}\Big|\sum_{k=-2s}^{t-1}\mu_{t}^{k}\phi_{k,i}\xi_{t-1,i}^{2}\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\alpha_{t}\sqrt{n}}\sqrt{\big|\mathcal{I}\big|\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}\Big|\sum_{k=-2s}^{t-1}\mu_{t}^{k}\phi_{k,i}\Big|^{2}}+(\sqrt{n})C_{7}\sqrt{\frac{\log n}{n}}\sum_{i\notin\mathcal{I}}\xi_{t-1,i}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{\alpha_{t}\sqrt{n}}\sqrt{\big|\mathcal{I}\big|\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}\Big|\sum_{k=-2s}^{t-1}\mu_{t}^{k}\phi_{k,i}\Big|^{2}}+\sqrt{\log n}\,\|\xi_{t-1}\|_{2}^{2},

where the second line follows from Cauchy-Schwarz and the definition
of
\mathcal{I}
.
Recalling that
\{\phi_{k}\}
fall within the set
\mathcal{E}
(cf. (
199
)) with high probability and using
|\mathcal{I}|\lesssim t+s
, we can further derive

\displaystyle\Bigg\langle\bigg|\sum_{k=-2s}^{t-1}\mu_{t}^{k}\phi_{k}\bigg|,\rho_{1}\widehat{\xi}_{t-1}^{2}+\frac{1}{\alpha_{t}\sqrt{n}}\ind_{\mathcal{I}}\Bigg\rangle

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{(t+s)\sqrt{\log n}}{\alpha_{t}n}+\sqrt{\log n}\,\|\xi_{t-1}\|_{2}^{2}

(238)

with probability at least
1-O(n^{-11})
.

•

Regarding the fourth term in (
237
), one can use
|\mu_{t}^{\top}\beta_{t-1}|\leq\|\mu_{t}\|_{2}\|\beta_{t-1}\|_{2}=1
and
\rho_{2}\lesssim n
to get

\displaystyle\left\langle\rho_{2}\xi_{t-1}^{2}\right\rangle\bigg|\sum_{k=-2s}^{t-1}\mu_{t}^{k}\beta_{t-1}^{k}\bigg|\lesssim\|\xi_{t-1}\|_{2}^{2}.

(239)

•

With regards to the second and third term in (
237
), direct calculations yield

\displaystyle\bigg|\Big\langle\sum_{k=-2s}^{t-1}\mu_{t}^{k}\phi_{k},\eta_{t}^{\prime}\Big(\alpha_{t}v^{\star}+\sum_{k=-2s}^{t-1}\beta_{t-1}^{k}\phi_{k}\Big)\circ\widehat{\xi}_{t-1}\Big\rangle-\Big\langle\eta_{t}^{\prime\prime}\Big(\alpha_{t}v^{\star}+\sum_{k=-2s}^{t-1}\beta_{t-1}^{k}\phi_{k}\Big)\circ\xi_{t-1}\Big\rangle\sum_{k=-2s}^{t-1}\mu_{t}^{k}\beta_{t-1}^{k}\bigg|

\displaystyle=\bigg|\Big\langle\sum_{k=-2s}^{t-1}\mu_{t}^{k}\phi_{k}\circ\eta_{t}^{\prime}\Big(\alpha_{t}v^{\star}+\sum_{k=-2s}^{t-1}\beta_{t-1}^{k}\phi_{k}\Big)\circ\ind_{\mathcal{I}^{\mathrm{c}}}-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{k=-2s}^{t-1}\mu_{t}^{k}\beta_{t-1}^{k}\eta_{t}^{\prime\prime}\Big(\alpha_{t}v^{\star}+\sum_{k=-2s}^{t-1}\beta_{t-1}^{k}\phi_{k}\Big),\xi_{t-1}\Big\rangle\bigg|

\displaystyle\leq\Big\|\sum_{k=-2s}^{t-1}\mu_{t}^{k}\phi_{k}\circ\eta_{t}^{\prime}\Big(\alpha_{t}v^{\star}+\sum_{k=-2s}^{t-1}\beta_{t-1}^{k}\phi_{k}\Big)-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{k=-2s}^{t-1}\mu_{t}^{k}\beta_{t-1}^{k}\eta_{t}^{\prime\prime}\Big(\alpha_{t}v^{\star}+\sum_{k=-2s}^{t-1}\beta_{t-1}^{k}\phi_{k}\Big)\Big\|_{2}\|\xi_{t-1}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\qquad+\Big\|\sum_{k=-2s}^{t-1}\mu_{t}^{k}\phi_{k}\circ\eta_{t}^{\prime}\Big(\alpha_{t}v^{\star}+\sum_{k=-2s}^{t-1}\beta_{t-1}^{k}\phi_{k}\Big)\circ\ind_{\mathcal{I}}\Big\|_{2}\|\xi_{t-1}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\kappa_{t}^{2}+D_{t}}\,\|\xi_{t-1}\|_{2}+O\bigg(\sqrt{\frac{(t+s)\log n}{n}}\bigg)\|\xi_{t-1}\|_{2},

(240)

where we invoke the assumption (
14d
), the inequality (
99
) and the bound (
205
).

Substituting the above bounds into inequality (
237
) gives

\displaystyle\|\xi_{t}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\Bigg(\sqrt{\kappa_{t}^{2}+D_{t}}+O\bigg(\sqrt{\frac{(t+s)\log n}{n}}\bigg)\Bigg)\|\xi_{t-1}\|_{2}+O\Bigg(\sqrt{\frac{(t+s)\log n}{n}}+A_{t}+\frac{(t+s)\sqrt{\log n}}{\alpha_{t}n}+\sqrt{\log n}\,\|\xi_{t-1}\|_{2}^{2}\Bigg)

\displaystyle\leq\left(\sqrt{\kappa_{t}^{2}+O\bigg(\sqrt{\frac{(t+s)\log^{2}n}{n}}\bigg)}+O\left(\sqrt{\frac{(t+s)\log n}{n}}+\sqrt{\log n}\,S_{t}\right)\right)\|\xi_{t-1}\|_{2}+O\left(\sqrt{\frac{(t+s)\log n}{(\lambda-1)n}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\left(1-\frac{1}{40}(\lambda-1)\right)\|\xi_{t-1}\|_{2}+O\left(\sqrt{\frac{(t+s)\log n}{(\lambda-1)n}}\right);

(241)

here, the penultimate step follows from the inequalities (
224
), (
227
) and induction hypothesis (
196
) for
\|\xi_{t-1}\|_{2}
,
while the last line makes use of the induction hypothesis and is valid if
\sqrt{\frac{(t+s)\log^{2}n}{n}}\ll\lambda-1
and if

\kappa_{t}\leq 1-\frac{1}{15}(\lambda-1).

(242)

The proof of this inequality (
242
) is postponed to Section
D.3.4
.
Invoking the induction the hypothesis (
196b
) for
\|\xi_{t-1}\|_{2}

in the above inequality (
241
), we arrive at

\displaystyle\|\xi_{t}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\left(1-\frac{1}{40}(\lambda-1)\right)\|\xi_{t-1}\|_{2}+C_{3}\sqrt{\frac{(t+s)\log n}{(\lambda-1)n}}

\displaystyle\leq\left(1-\frac{1}{40}(\lambda-1)\right)\left\{C_{1}\sqrt{\frac{(t+s)\log n}{(\lambda-1)^{3}n}}+C_{1}\left(1-\frac{1}{40}(\lambda-1)\right)^{t-1}\frac{\log^{3.5}n}{\sqrt{(\lambda-1)^{9}n}}\right\}+C_{3}\sqrt{\frac{(t+s)\log n}{(\lambda-1)n}}

\displaystyle=C_{1}\left(1-\frac{1}{15}(\lambda-1)\right)^{t}\frac{\log^{3.5}n}{\sqrt{(\lambda-1)^{9}n}}+\left\{C_{1}\left(1-\frac{1}{40}(\lambda-1)\right)\sqrt{\frac{(t+s)\log n}{(\lambda-1)^{3}n}}+C_{3}(\lambda-1)\sqrt{\frac{(t+s)\log n}{(\lambda-1)^{3}n}}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq C_{1}\left(1-\frac{1}{15}(\lambda-1)\right)^{t}\frac{\log^{3.5}n}{\sqrt{(\lambda-1)^{9}n}}+C_{1}\sqrt{\frac{(t+s)\log n}{(\lambda-1)^{3}n}},

provided that the ratio
C_{1}/C_{3}
is sufficiently large.
This validates the induction hypothesis (
196b
) for
\|\xi_{t}\|_{2}
,
thereby completing the induction step for
\|\xi_{t}\|_{2}.

D.3.2
Bounding the residual term
\Delta_{\alpha,t}

Note that the denoising function in
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
synchronization is smooth everywhere,
and hence
E_{t}=0
(see (
14h
)).
The bound (
16a
) then yields

\displaystyle|\Delta_{\alpha,t}|

\displaystyle\lesssim B_{t}+\left(\rho+\rho_{1}\|v^{\star}\|_{\infty}\|\xi_{t-1}\|_{2}\right)\cdot\|\xi_{t-1}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim B_{t}+\|\xi_{t-1}\|_{2}+\|\xi_{t-1}\|_{2}^{2},

where the last line follows by relation (
205
).
Recall that our induction hypothesis says
\|\xi_{t-1}\|_{2}\leq S_{t}
,
and that we have bounded
B_{t}
in (
226
).
These taken together imply that

\displaystyle|\Delta_{\alpha,t}|

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\xi_{t-1}\|_{2}+\sqrt{\frac{(t+s)\log n}{n}}\lesssim S_{t},

(243)

where the last inequality comes from (
196b
).

D.3.3
Bounding
\alpha_{t}
and understanding state evolution

Next, we turn to the induction step for establishing (
196a
) and (
31b
) regarding
\alpha_{t}
.
More precisely, under the induction hypothesis (
196
) for the
t
-th iteration,
we would like to show that (
196a
) and (
31b
) hold for the
(t+1)
-th iteration w.r.t.
\alpha_{t+1}
.

Figure 1:

Numerical illustrations for quantities regarding
\kappa_{t}
(the left panel) and
\alpha_{t}
(the middle and the right panel).
Left panel: quantity associated with (
264
) as a function of
\lambda
within the range
(1,1.2]
;
middle panel: the quantity (
256
) as a function of
\lambda
within the range
(1,1.2]
;
right panel: the derivative of (
248
) as a function of
\tau
within the range of
[0,1.44]
.

From the definition of
\alpha_{t+1}
(see (
20
)), we have

\displaystyle\alpha_{t+1}^{2}

\displaystyle=\lambda^{2}\langle v^{\star},\eta_{t}(x_{t})\rangle^{2}=\frac{\lambda^{2}\langle v^{\star},\tanh(\pi_{t}x_{t})\rangle^{2}}{\|\tanh(\pi_{t}x_{t})\|_{2}^{2}}.

(244)

To understand the dynamics of
\alpha_{t}
,
let us look at the state evolution recursion — namely, a sequence of scalars
\{\tau_{t}\}
defined recursively as follows:

\displaystyle\tau_{1}

\displaystyle=\lambda^{2}-1

(245a)

\displaystyle\tau_{t+1}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\frac{\lambda^{2}\left[\int\tanh(\tau_{t}+\sqrt{\tau_{t}}x)\varphi(\mathrm{d}x)\right]^{2}}{\int\tanh^{2}(\tau_{t}+\sqrt{\tau_{t}}x)\varphi(\mathrm{d}x)}=\lambda^{2}\int\tanh(\tau_{t}+\sqrt{\tau_{t}}x)\varphi(\mathrm{d}x).

(245b)

Here, the last line comes from
Deshpande et al., 2017
.
As it turns out, this scalar sequence (
245
) converges monotonically to a fixed point
\tau^{\star}
of the recursion (
245
),
namely,

\displaystyle\tau_{t}\nearrow\tau^{\star},\qquad\tau_{t},\tau^{\star}\in(\lambda^{2}-1,\lambda^{2}),\qquad\text{ where }\tau^{\star}\text{ obeys }\tau^{\star}=\lambda^{2}\int\tanh(\tau^{\star}+\sqrt{\tau^{\star}}x)\varphi(\mathrm{d}x).

(246)

This claim can be established as follows by studying the property of the function

g_{\lambda}(\tau)=\frac{\lambda^{2}\left[\int\tanh(\tau+\sqrt{\tau}x)\varphi(\mathrm{d}x)\right]^{2}}{\int\tanh^{2}(\tau+\sqrt{\tau}x)\varphi(\mathrm{d}x)}=\lambda^{2}\int\tanh^{2}(\tau+\sqrt{\tau}x)\varphi(\mathrm{d}x).

(247)

(i)

We first observe that, for any
1<\lambda\leq 1.2
, the following derivative

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\lambda^{2}}\cdot\frac{\mathrm{d}g_{\lambda}(\tau)}{\mathrm{d}\tau}=\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}\tau}\int\tanh(\tau+\sqrt{\tau}x)\varphi(\mathrm{d}x)=\int\left(1+\frac{x}{2\sqrt{\tau}}\right)\left(1-\tanh^{2}(\tau+\sqrt{\tau}x)\right)\varphi(\mathrm{d}x)

(248)

always obeys

\frac{\mathrm{d}g_{\lambda}(\tau)}{\mathrm{d}\tau}\in(0,\lambda^{2})

(249)

and is decreasing in
\tau
within the interval
\tau\in[\lambda^{2}-1,\lambda^{2}]\subseteq[0,1.44]

(given our assumption that
1<\lambda\leq 1.2
); this is numerically validated in the right panel of Figure
1
.

(ii)

Secondly, we observe that
g_{\lambda}(\tau_{1})>\tau_{1}
, where
\tau_{1}=\lambda^{2}-1
.
To prove this, consider the problem of estimating a Bernoulli random variable
X\sim\mathsf{Bernoulli}(1/2)\in\mathcal{\{}1,-1\}

based on the observation
Y=\sqrt{\tau}X+Z
, where
Z\sim\mathcal{N}(0,1)
is independent from
X
.
It is well known that
\tanh(\sqrt{\tau}Y)=\tanh(\tau X+\sqrt{\tau}Z)=\mathbb{E}[X\mid Y]

is the minimum mean square error (MMSE) estimator
(
Deshpande et al., 2017
, Appendix B.2)
.
In addition, the MMSE estimator
\mathbb{E}[X\mid Y]
is known to be the projection of
Y
onto the space of functions of
Y
,
and as a result, it achieves the largest correlation with
X
among all estimators based on
Y
.
This implies that the estimator
\tanh(\tau X+\sqrt{\tau}Z)
enjoys higher correlation with
X
compared to the other estimator

\tau X+\sqrt{\tau}Z
, thus leading to

\displaystyle\frac{\left[\int\tanh(\tau_{1}+\sqrt{\tau_{1}}x)\varphi(\mathrm{d}x)\right]^{2}}{\int\tanh^{2}(\tau_{1}+\sqrt{\tau_{1}}x)\varphi(\mathrm{d}x)}\geq\frac{\left[\int(\tau_{1}+\sqrt{\tau_{1}}x)\varphi(\mathrm{d}x)\right]^{2}}{\int(\tau_{1}+\sqrt{\tau_{1}}x)^{2}\varphi(\mathrm{d}x)}=\frac{\tau_{1}^{2}}{\tau_{1}^{2}+\tau_{1}}=\frac{\lambda^{2}-1}{\lambda^{2}}.

Given that the left-hand side of the above relation is given by
\frac{g_{\lambda}(\tau_{1})}{\lambda^{2}}
(cf. (
245b
)), we conclude that

\frac{g_{\lambda}(\tau_{1})}{\lambda^{2}}\geq\frac{\lambda^{2}-1}{\lambda^{2}}\qquad\Longrightarrow\qquad g_{\lambda}(\tau_{1})\geq\lambda^{2}-1=\tau_{1}.

(iii)

Thirdly, it is seen that
g_{\lambda}(\lambda^{2})<\lambda^{2}
, which follows from (
247
) and the fact that
|\tanh(w)|<1

(w\in\real)
.

(iv)

The above three properties immediately reveal that:

(a)

There exists a unique fixed point
\tau^{\star}
of
g_{\lambda}(\cdot)
within
(\lambda^{2}-1,\lambda^{2})
;

(b)

Starting from
\tau_{1}=\lambda^{2}-1
,
\tau_{t}
is monotonically increasing in
t
and keeps moving closer to (but remains below)
\tau^{\star}
. To see this, note that for any
\tau_{t}<\tau^{\star}
, one has
\tau_{t+1}=g_{\lambda}(\tau_{t})\leq g_{\lambda}(\tau^{\star})=\tau^{\star}
and
\tau_{t+1}=g_{\lambda}(\tau_{t})>\tau_{t}
(as
\tau_{t}<\tau^{\star}
and
\tau_{1}<g_{\lambda}(\tau_{1})
).

With the state evolution sequence
\{\tau_{t}\}
in place, we claim that for every
t
, it satisfies

\displaystyle\alpha_{t+1}^{2}=\left(1+O\Big(\frac{\widetilde{S}_{t+1}}{(\lambda-1)^{2.5}}\Big)\right)\tau_{t+1}=\big(1+o(1)\big)\tau_{t+1},

(250)

where
\widetilde{S}_{t}
is defined in (
D
).
If the claim (
250
) were valid, then one could readily conclude that

\displaystyle(1+o(1))\lambda^{2}

\displaystyle\geq\alpha_{t+1}^{2}=\left(1+o(1)\right)\tau_{t+1}\geq(1+o(1))\tau_{1}=(1+o(1))(\lambda^{2}-1),

\displaystyle\alpha_{t+1}^{2}

\displaystyle=\left(1+O\bigg(\sqrt{\frac{t\log n}{(\lambda-1)^{8}n}}+\frac{\log^{3.5}n}{\sqrt{(\lambda-1)^{14}n}}\bigg)\right)\tau_{t+1},

where we have used (
246
) and the definition (
D
) of
\widetilde{S}_{t}
.
Consequently, if we can establish inequality (
250
), we can finish the inductive step with respect to
\alpha_{t}
.

Proof of claim (
250
).

We intend to accomplish this via an induction argument.
Assuming that (
250
) is valid for the
t
-th iteration,
we would like to establish (
250
) for the
(t+1)
-th iteration as well.
Let

\displaystyle\varsigma_{t}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\alpha_{t}^{2}-\tau_{t},\qquad t\geq 1,

(251)

then it is equivalent to proving that

|\varsigma_{t+1}|\leq\left(\frac{C_{6}\widetilde{S}_{t+1}}{(\lambda-1)^{2.5}}\right)\tau_{t+1}

(252)

for some constant
C_{6}>0
large enough,
provided that

|\varsigma_{t}|\leq\left(\frac{C_{6}\widetilde{S}_{t}}{(\lambda-1)^{2.5}}\right)\tau_{t}.

(253)

Towards this end, let us define the following quantity:

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{1}\coloneqq\frac{\lambda^{2}\langle v^{\star},\tanh(\pi_{t}x_{t})\rangle^{2}}{\|\tanh(\pi_{t}x_{t})\|_{2}^{2}}-\frac{\lambda^{2}\left[\int\tanh(\alpha_{t}^{2}+\alpha_{t}x)\varphi(\mathrm{d}x)\right]^{2}}{\int\tanh^{2}(\alpha_{t}^{2}+\alpha_{t}x)\varphi(\mathrm{d}x)}.

We can then employ (
244
) and (
245
) to derive

\displaystyle\varsigma_{t+1}\coloneqq\alpha_{t+1}^{2}-\tau_{t+1}

\displaystyle=\lambda^{2}\int\tanh(\alpha_{t}^{2}+\alpha_{t}x)\varphi(\mathrm{d}x)-\lambda^{2}\int\tanh(\tau_{t}+\sqrt{\tau_{t}}x)\varphi(\mathrm{d}x)+\mathcal{T}_{1}

\displaystyle=\varsigma_{t}\,\underbrace{\lambda^{2}\int\left(1-\tanh^{2}(\tau_{t}+\sqrt{\tau_{t}}x)\right)\left(1+\frac{1}{2\sqrt{\tau_{t}}}x\right)\varphi(\mathrm{d}x)}_{=:\mathcal{T}_{2}}+O\left(\frac{\varsigma_{t}^{2}}{\tau_{t}^{3/2}}\right)+\mathcal{T}_{1},

(254)

where the last identity shall be established towards the end of this subsection.
In what follows, we shall look at
\mathcal{T}_{1}
and
\mathcal{T}_{2}
separately.

•

To control
\mathcal{T}_{2}
,
we first observe that
\mathcal{T}_{2}\geq 0
,
a direct consequence of (
249
) and (
248
).
In addition, we claim that,
for any
\tau\geq\lambda^{2}-1
and any
\lambda\in[1,1.2]
,

\displaystyle 0\leq\mathcal{T}_{2}(\lambda,\tau)\coloneqq\lambda^{2}\int\left(1-\tanh^{2}(\tau+\sqrt{\tau}x)\right)\left(1+\frac{1}{2\sqrt{\tau}}x\right)\varphi(\mathrm{d}x)\leq 1-(\lambda-1).

(255)

To see this,
we resort to the numerical verification. To be specific,
the middle panel of Figure
1
plots the following quantity

\displaystyle\frac{1-\sup_{\tau:\lambda^{2}-1\leq\tau\leq\lambda^{2}}\mathcal{T}_{2}(\lambda,\tau)}{\lambda-1}

(256)

as a function of
\lambda
; it is clearly seen from Figure
1
that this ratio is strictly above 1 for any
\lambda\in[1,1.2].

All this indicates that

0\leq\mathcal{T}_{2}\leq 1-(\lambda-1).

•

Next, we turn attention to
\mathcal{T}_{1}
. Repeating the same argument as in (
212
) and (
213
)
and recognizing that
|\tanh^{\prime}(w)|\leq 1
, we have

\bigg|{\displaystyle\int}\tanh\Big(\frac{\pi_{t}}{\sqrt{n}}(\alpha_{t}+x)\Big)\varphi(\mathrm{d}x)-{\displaystyle\int}\tanh\big(\alpha_{t}(\alpha_{t}+x)\big)\varphi(\mathrm{d}x)\bigg|\lesssim\frac{S_{t}}{\alpha_{t}}.

This taken together with the definition of
\Delta_{\alpha,t}
(cf. (
15a
)), the definition of
\alpha_{t+1}
(cf. (
20
)) and the fact that
\sqrt{n}v^{\star}_{i}\in\{1,-1\}
(
1\leq i\leq n
) gives

\displaystyle\langle v^{\star},\tanh(\pi_{t}x_{t})\rangle

\displaystyle=\sqrt{n}\int\tanh\left(\frac{\pi_{t}}{\sqrt{n}}(\alpha_{t}+x)\right)\varphi(\mathrm{d}x)+O\left(\gamma_{t}^{-1}\big|\Delta_{\alpha,t}\big|\right)

\displaystyle=\sqrt{n}\int\tanh(\alpha_{t}^{2}+\alpha_{t}x)\varphi(\mathrm{d}x)+O\left(\frac{S_{t}}{\alpha_{t}}\sqrt{n}+\alpha_{t}S_{t}\sqrt{n}\right)

\displaystyle=\sqrt{n}\int\tanh(\alpha_{t}^{2}+\alpha_{t}x)\varphi(\mathrm{d}x)+O\left(\frac{S_{t}}{\alpha_{t}}\sqrt{n}\right),

where the second line is due to (
243
) and (
203b
),
and the last line is valid since
\alpha_{t}\lesssim\lambda\leq 1
(cf. (
196a
)).
Additionally, (
214
) tells us that

\displaystyle\|\tanh(\pi_{t}x_{t})\|_{2}^{2}=n\int\tanh^{2}(\alpha_{t}^{2}+\alpha_{t}x)\varphi(\mathrm{d}x)+O\left(\alpha_{t}^{2}n\bigg(\frac{S_{t}}{\alpha_{t}^{3}}\bigg)\right).

Taking these two relations collectively with (
216
) and (
215
) ensures that

\displaystyle|\mathcal{T}_{1}|\lesssim\lambda^{2}\alpha_{t}^{2}\left(\frac{S_{t}}{\alpha_{t}^{3}}\right)\asymp\lambda^{2}\frac{S_{t}}{\alpha_{t}}.

Putting the above bounds together, we arrive at

|\varsigma_{t+1}|\leq\big(1-(\lambda-1)\big)|\varsigma_{t}|+O\bigg(\frac{\varsigma_{t}^{2}}{\tau_{t}^{3/2}}\bigg)+O\left(\lambda^{2}\frac{S_{t}}{\alpha_{t}}\right).

Given that
\tau_{t}
is increasing in
t
(see (
246
)), there exists some large enough constant
C_{8}>0
such that

\displaystyle\frac{|\varsigma_{t+1}|}{\tau_{t+1}}

\displaystyle\leq\big(1-(\lambda-1)\big)\frac{|\varsigma_{t}|}{\tau_{t}}+O\bigg(\frac{\varsigma_{t}^{2}}{\tau_{t}^{5/2}}\bigg)+O\left(\lambda^{2}\frac{S_{t}}{\alpha_{t}\tau_{t}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\big(1-(\lambda-1)\big)\frac{|\varsigma_{t}|}{\tau_{t}}+\frac{C_{8}}{\sqrt{\lambda-1}}\frac{\varsigma_{t}^{2}}{\tau_{t}^{2}}+\frac{C_{8}S_{t}}{(\lambda-1)^{1.5}}

\displaystyle\leq\big(1-(\lambda-1)\big)\left\{\frac{C_{6}\widetilde{S}_{t}}{(\lambda-1)^{2.5}}\right\}+\left(C_{8}\frac{C_{6}\widetilde{S}_{t}}{(\lambda-1)^{3}}\right)\frac{C_{6}\widetilde{S}_{t}}{(\lambda-1)^{2.5}}+\frac{C_{8}\widetilde{S}_{t}}{(\lambda-1)^{1.5}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{6}\widetilde{S}_{t}}{(\lambda-1)^{2.5}}\leq\frac{C_{6}\widetilde{S}_{t+1}}{(\lambda-1)^{2.5}},

where the second line holds since
\alpha_{t}^{2}=(1+o(1))\tau_{t}
and
\tau_{t}\geq\lambda^{2}-1\asymp\lambda-1
(cf. (
246
)),
the third line relies on (
253
) and
\tau_{t}\gtrsim\lambda-1
,
and the last line is valid provided that
\frac{\widetilde{S}_{t}}{(\lambda-1)^{4}}\ll 1
.
This in turn establishes (
252
) for the
(t+1)
-th iteration.

Proof of relation (
254
).

We first make the observation that

\displaystyle\Big|\tanh(\alpha_{t}^{2}+\alpha_{t}x)-\tanh(\tau_{t}+\sqrt{\tau_{t}}x)-\big(1-\tanh^{2}(\tau_{t}+\sqrt{\tau_{t}}x)\big)\Big(\alpha_{t}^{2}+\alpha_{t}x-\tau_{t}-\sqrt{\tau_{t}}x\Big)\Big|

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\frac{1}{2}(\alpha_{t}^{2}+\alpha_{t}x-\tau_{t}-\sqrt{\tau_{t}}x)^{2},

(257)

which follows due to Taylor expansion and the fact that
\tanh^{\prime}(w)=1-\tanh^{2}(w)\in[0,1]

for any
w\in\mathbb{R}
. In addition, one has

\displaystyle\alpha_{t}^{2}+\alpha_{t}x-\tau_{t}-\sqrt{\tau_{t}}x

\displaystyle=(\alpha_{t}^{2}-\tau_{t})+\frac{\alpha_{t}^{2}-\tau_{t}}{\alpha_{t}+\sqrt{\tau_{t}}}x=\varsigma_{t}\Big(1+\frac{1}{2\sqrt{\tau_{t}}}\Big)x+\varsigma_{t}\Big(\frac{1}{\alpha_{t}+\sqrt{\tau_{t}}}-\frac{1}{2\sqrt{\tau_{t}}}\Big)x

\displaystyle=\varsigma_{t}\Big(1+\frac{1}{2\sqrt{\tau_{t}}}\Big)x-\Big(\frac{\varsigma_{t}^{2}}{2(\alpha_{t}+\sqrt{\tau_{t}})^{2}\sqrt{\tau_{t}}}\Big)x,

which further implies that

\displaystyle\qquad\Big|\big(\alpha_{t}^{2}+\alpha_{t}x-\tau_{t}-\sqrt{\tau_{t}}x\big)-\varsigma_{t}\Big(1+\frac{1}{2\sqrt{\tau_{t}}}\Big)x\Big|\leq\frac{\varsigma_{t}^{2}}{2\tau_{t}^{3/2}}|x|

\displaystyle\Longrightarrow\qquad\big(\alpha_{t}^{2}+\alpha_{t}x-\tau_{t}-\sqrt{\tau_{t}}x\big)^{2}\leq 2\varsigma_{t}^{2}\Big(1+\frac{1}{2\sqrt{\tau_{t}}}\Big)^{2}x^{2}+\frac{\varsigma_{t}^{4}}{2\tau_{t}^{3}}x^{2}.

Substituting the preceding two inequalities into (
257
) yields

\displaystyle\Big|\tanh(\alpha_{t}^{2}+\alpha_{t}x)-\tanh(\tau_{t}+\sqrt{\tau_{t}}x)-\big(1-\tanh^{2}(\tau_{t}+\sqrt{\tau_{t}}x)\big)\varsigma_{t}\Big(1+\frac{1}{2\sqrt{\tau_{t}}}\Big)x\Big|

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\frac{\varsigma_{t}^{2}}{2\tau_{t}^{3/2}}|x|+\varsigma_{t}^{2}\Big(1+\frac{1}{2\sqrt{\tau_{t}}}\Big)^{2}x^{2}+\frac{\varsigma_{t}^{4}}{4\tau_{t}^{3}}x^{2}.

Taking the integral and using the facts that
\tau_{t}\leq\lambda^{2}\lesssim 1
(cf. (
246
)) and the induction hypothesis
|\varsigma_{t}|\lesssim\tau_{t}
then give

\displaystyle\left|{\displaystyle\int}\tanh(\alpha_{t}^{2}+\alpha_{t}x)\varphi(\mathrm{d}x)-{\displaystyle\int}\tanh(\tau_{t}+\sqrt{\tau_{t}}x)\varphi(\mathrm{d}x)-\varsigma_{t}\Big(1+\frac{1}{2\sqrt{\tau_{t}}}\Big){\displaystyle\int}\big(1-\tanh^{2}(\tau_{t}+\sqrt{\tau_{t}}x)\big)\varphi(\mathrm{d}x)\right|

\displaystyle\quad\leq{\displaystyle\int}\left\{\frac{\varsigma_{t}^{2}}{2\tau_{t}^{3/2}}|x|+\varsigma_{t}^{2}\Big(1+\frac{1}{2\sqrt{\tau_{t}}}\Big)^{2}x^{2}+\frac{\varsigma_{t}^{4}}{4\tau_{t}^{3}}x^{2}\right\}\varphi(\mathrm{d}x)\lesssim\frac{\varsigma_{t}^{2}}{\tau_{t}^{3/2}}+\frac{\varsigma_{t}^{2}}{\tau_{t}}+\frac{\varsigma_{t}^{4}}{\tau_{t}^{3}}\asymp\frac{\varsigma_{t}^{2}}{\tau_{t}^{3/2}}.

D.3.4
Bounding quantity
\kappa_{t}

Recall that the analysis in Section
D.3.1
requires bounding
\kappa_{t}
, which shall be done in this subsection with the assistance of expression (
250
).
First, combining (
203a
) and (
203b
) leads to

\displaystyle\gamma_{t}^{2}\pi_{t}^{2}=\frac{(1+o(\lambda-1))\alpha_{t}^{2}}{\int\tanh(\alpha_{t}^{2}+\alpha_{t}x)\varphi(\mathrm{d}x)}=\frac{(1+o(\lambda-1))\alpha_{t}^{2}}{(1+o(\lambda-1))\tau_{t+1}/\lambda^{2}}\leq\frac{(1+o(\lambda-1))\lambda^{2}\alpha_{t}^{2}}{(1+o(\lambda-1))\tau_{t}}=(1+o(\lambda-1))\lambda^{2},

(258)

provided that
\frac{\widetilde{S}_{t}}{(\lambda-1)^{3.5}}\ll 1
.
Here, the second identity holds due to (
245
), (
252
) and (
254
),
the inequality is valid since
\tau_{t}
is increasing in
t
(see (
246
)),
and the last identity comes from (
250
).
Recalling the definition of
\kappa_{t}
(cf. (
2.2
)) and the fact that
\|\beta_{t-1}\|_{2}=1
gives

\displaystyle\kappa_{t}^{2}=\max\Bigg\{\Bigg\langle\int\Bigg[x\eta_{t}^{\prime}\Big(\alpha_{t}v^{\star}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}x\Big)-\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\eta_{t}^{\prime\prime}\Big(\alpha_{t}v^{\star}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}x\Big)\Bigg]^{2}\varphi_{n}(\mathrm{d}x)\Bigg\rangle,~\bigg\langle\int\Big[\eta_{t}^{\prime}\Big(\alpha_{t}v^{\star}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}x\Big)\Big]^{2}\varphi_{n}(\mathrm{d}x)\bigg\rangle\Bigg\}.

(259)

In what follows, let us control each term in (
259
) separately.

•

To begin with, in view of the relations (
204
),
we obtain

\displaystyle\Bigg\langle\int\Bigg[x\eta_{t}^{\prime}\Big(\alpha_{t}v^{\star}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}x\Big)-\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\eta_{t}^{\prime\prime}\Big(\alpha_{t}v^{\star}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}x\Big)\Bigg]^{2}\varphi_{n}(\mathrm{d}x)\Bigg\rangle

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\int\left[\left(\gamma_{t}\pi_{t}x+\frac{2}{\sqrt{n}}\gamma_{t}\pi_{t}^{2}\tanh\Big(\pi_{t}\Big(\alpha_{t}v^{\star}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}x\Big)\Big)\right)\cdot\Big(1-\tanh^{2}\Big(\pi_{t}\Big(\alpha_{t}v^{\star}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}x\Big)\Big)\Big)\right]^{2}\varphi_{n}(\mathrm{d}x)

\displaystyle=\int\left[\left(\gamma_{t}\pi_{t}x+\frac{2}{\sqrt{n}}\gamma_{t}\pi_{t}^{2}\tanh\Big(\frac{\pi_{t}}{\sqrt{n}}\big(\alpha_{t}+x\big)\Big)\right)\cdot\Big(1-\tanh^{2}\Big(\frac{\pi_{t}}{\sqrt{n}}\big(\alpha_{t}+x\big)\Big)\Big)\right]^{2}\varphi(\mathrm{d}x),

(260)

where the last step follows from
\sqrt{n}v^{\star}_{i}\in\{+1,-1\}
and the symmetry of
\varphi(\cdot).

Reorganizing terms and recalling that
\pi_{t}=(1+o(\lambda-1))\alpha_{t}\sqrt{n}
(cf. (
203a
)) and
\gamma_{t}^{2}\pi_{t}^{2}\leq(1+o(\lambda-1))\lambda^{2}
(cf. (
258
)), we arrive at

(
260
)

\displaystyle=(1+o(\lambda-1))\gamma^{2}_{t}\pi^{2}_{t}\int\left[\left(x+2\alpha_{t}\tanh(\alpha_{t}^{2}+\alpha_{t}x)\right)\left(1-\tanh^{2}(\alpha_{t}^{2}+\alpha_{t}x)\right)\right]^{2}\varphi(\mathrm{d}x)

\displaystyle\leq(1+o(\lambda-1))\lambda^{2}\int\left[\left(x+2\sqrt{\tau_{t}}\tanh(\tau_{t}+\sqrt{\tau_{t}}x)\right)\left(1-\tanh^{2}(\tau_{t}+\sqrt{\tau_{t}}x)\right)\right]^{2}\varphi(\mathrm{d}x),

where the last line also relies on the relation (
250
).
As a result, we reach

\displaystyle\Bigg\langle\int\Bigg[x\eta_{t}^{\prime}\big(\alpha_{t}v^{\star}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}x\big)-\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\eta_{t}^{\prime\prime}\big(\alpha_{t}v^{\star}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}x\big)\Bigg]^{2}\varphi_{n}(\mathrm{d}x)\Bigg\rangle

\displaystyle\qquad\leq(1+o(\lambda-1))\lambda^{2}\int\left[\left(x+2\sqrt{\tau_{t}}\tanh(\tau_{t}+\sqrt{\tau_{t}}x)\right)\left(1-\tanh^{2}(\tau_{t}+\sqrt{\tau_{t}}x)\right)\right]^{2}\varphi(\mathrm{d}x).

(261)

•

Through similar calculations (for which we omit the details here), one can deduce that

\displaystyle\Big\langle\int\Big[\eta_{t}^{\prime}\Big(\alpha_{t}v^{\star}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}x\big)\Big]^{2}\varphi_{n}(\mathrm{d}x)\Big\rangle\leq(1+o(\lambda-1))\lambda^{2}\int\left[1-\tanh^{2}(\tau_{t}+\sqrt{\tau_{t}}x)\right]^{2}\varphi(\mathrm{d}x).

(262)

Finally,
let us look at the following function:

\displaystyle{\kappa}^{2}(\lambda,\tau)\coloneqq\lambda^{2}\max\bigg\{\int\Big[\left(x+2\sqrt{\tau}\tanh(\tau+\sqrt{\tau}x)\right)\left(1-\tanh^{2}(\tau+\sqrt{\tau}x)\right)\Big]^{2}\varphi(\mathrm{d}x),\int\left[1-\tanh^{2}(\tau+\sqrt{\tau}x)\right]^{2}\varphi(\mathrm{d}x)\bigg\}.

(263)

For any
1<\lambda<1.2
, we observe that

\displaystyle\sup_{\tau:\lambda^{2}-1\leq\tau\leq\lambda^{2}}\kappa(\lambda,\tau)\leq 1-\frac{\lambda-1}{12},

(264)

which has been numerically validated in the left panel of Figure
1
.
Thus, putting the above results together, we have demonstrated the advertised bound for
\kappa_{t}
:

\displaystyle\kappa_{t}^{2}\leq(1+o(1))\sup_{\tau:\lambda^{2}-1\leq\tau\leq\lambda^{2}}\kappa(\lambda,\tau)\leq 1-\frac{\lambda-1}{15}.

(265)

Appendix E
Initialization for sparse PCA

This section is dedicated to the study of AMP with two data-driven initialization schemes that achieve non-trivial correlation with the truth, with a focus on the scenario where the SNR is above the computational limit.

E.1
AMP with data-dependent initialization: strong SNR regime

Let us begin by considering the strong SNR regime where

\displaystyle\lambda\|v^{\star}\|_{\infty}\gtrsim\sqrt{\frac{k\log n}{n}}.

(266)

For instance, if
\|v^{\star}\|_{\infty}=O(\frac{1}{\sqrt{k}})
,
then (
266
) imposes a constraint on
\lambda
as
\lambda\gtrsim k\sqrt{\frac{\log n}{n}}
.

Initialization scheme #1: diagonal maximization.

Set
\eta_{0}(x_{0})=0
, and take

\displaystyle x_{1}=e_{\hat{s}},\qquad\text{with }~{\hat{s}}\coloneqq\arg\max_{i}\left|M_{ii}\right|,

(267)

where
e_{i}\in\real^{n}
denotes the
i
-th standard basis vector.
In words, this initialization simply identifies the largest diagonal entry of
M
, and forms a standard basis vector w.r.t. this entry.
Given the ambiguity of the global sign (i.e., one can only hope to recover
v^{\star}
up to global sign),
we shall assume
v^{\star}_{\hat{s}}\geq 0
without loss of generality.

As it turns out, in the strong SNR regime (
266
),
the algorithm (
267
) is guaranteed to find an index within the following index subset:

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}_{0}\coloneqq\Big\{s\in[n]~\mid~|v^{\star}_{s}|\geq\frac{1}{2}\|v^{\star}\|_{\infty}\Big\}.

(268)

Moreover, executing one iteration of AMP from
x_{1}=e_{\hat{s}}
is able to yield a nontrivial correlation with the truth
v^{\star}
.
These two facts are formally stated in the following proposition, with its proof deferred to Section
F.5
.

Proposition 1
.

Suppose the signal strength satisfies (
266
).
With probability at least
1-O(n^{-11})
, one has:

1.

the index
{\hat{s}}
(cf. (
267
)) satisfies
\hat{s}\in\mathcal{S}_{0}
;

2.

for every
s\in\mathcal{S}_{0}
, the AMP updates (
3
) initialized with
x_{1}=e_{s}

and
\eta_{0}(x_{0})=0
obey

\displaystyle\big|\big\langle v^{\star},\eta_{2}(x_{2})\big\rangle\big|\asymp 1.

(269)

Non-asymptotic theory of AMP when initialized by (
267
).

Despite the statistical dependency between
\hat{s}
and
W
,
Proposition
1
guarantees that it always comes from a fixed and small index subset.
Consequently, basic union bounding suffices in helping us analyze the subsequent AMP iterates.
This is summarized in the result below;
the proof can be found in Section
F
.

Corollary 4
.

If the signal strength satisfies (
266
),
then with probability at least
1-O(n^{-10})
, the AMP iterates (
3
) with initialization (
267
) obey (
39
) - (
41
) for
2\leq t\lesssim\frac{n\lambda^{2}}{\log^{3}n}
,
where
\alpha_{3}^{\star}\asymp\lambda
.

E.2
AMP with data-dependent initialization: weak signal regime

We now move on to the following regime that violates the condition (
266
):

\displaystyle\lambda\gtrsim\frac{k}{\sqrt{n}}~~\text{ and }~~\|v^{\star}\|_{\infty}=o\Big(\sqrt{\frac{\log n}{k}}\Big).

(270)

It is noteworthy that
\lambda
cannot be further reduced,
as a computational barrier has been widely conjectured that asserts that no polynomial algorithm can achieve consistent estimation if
\lambda=o(k/{\sqrt{n}})

(
Berthet and Rigollet, 2013a
;
Cai et al., 2015
;
Wang et al., 2016
;
Hopkins et al., 2017
)
.

Initialization scheme #2.

Before describing our next initialization scheme,
we give two remarks below.

•

As shown in the prior literature, there exists a computationally feasible algorithm that allows one to find an estimate
\widehat{v}_{\mathsf{oracle}}

that obeys
\|\widehat{v}_{\mathsf{oracle}}\|_{2}=1
and

\big|\big\langle\widehat{v}_{\mathsf{oracle}},v^{\star}\big\rangle\big|\asymp 1

(271)

with probability exceeding
1-O(n^{-10})
,
as long as
\lambda\gtrsim k/\sqrt{n}
in the model (
35
).
An example of this kind is the one based on covariance thresholding studied in
Deshpande and Montanari, 2014b
;
Krauthgamer et al., 2015
.
4
4

4

While
Deshpande and Montanari, 2014b
focused primarily on the spiked Wishart model, it is fairly easy to transfer the Wigner model (
35
) into the model therein, by using a simple Gaussian lifting trick to asymmetrize
M
.

In what follows, we shall call this algorithm as an oracle algorithm.

•

The estimate returned by the above oracle algorithm, however, exhibits complicated statistical dependency on
W
,
thus precluding us from directly invoking our AMP analysis framework.

In light of the above observations, we propose an initialization scheme based on sample splitting,
which repeats the following steps for
N\asymp\log n
rounds. In each round
j
:

1)

Randomly sample an index subset
\mathcal{I}_{j}
, independent of
M
, with mean size
|\mathcal{I}_{j}|=np
(each
i\in[n]
is included in
\mathcal{I}_{j}
with probability
p
)
and partition
M
into four independent blocks, namely,
M_{\mathcal{I}_{j},\mathcal{I}_{j}}
,

M_{\mathcal{I}_{j}^{c},\mathcal{I}_{j}}
,

M_{\mathcal{I}_{j},\mathcal{I}_{j}^{c}}
,

M_{\mathcal{I}_{j}^{c},\mathcal{I}_{j}^{c}}
.
Here and below,
M_{\mathcal{I},\mathcal{J}}
denotes the submatrix of
M
with rows (resp. columns) coming from those with indices in
\mathcal{I}
(resp.
\mathcal{J}
).

2)

Apply the oracle algorithm mentioned above with a little follow-up step to obtain a unit-norm estimate
x^{j}\in\real^{|\mathcal{I}_{j}^{\mathrm{c}}|}
(see Algorithm
1
for details).

3)

Run AMP on a smaller-dimensional (but independent) submatrix
M_{\mathcal{I}_{j}^{c},\mathcal{I}_{j}^{c}}
; the size of
\mathcal{I}_{j}
is chosen to be
o(n)
,
so that the efficiency of the AMP will not degrade much.

Finally, we select an index set
\mathcal{I}_{\widehat{j}}
based on the following criterion:

\displaystyle\widehat{j}\coloneqq\arg\!\max_{1\leq j\leq N}~\Big\{x^{j\top}M_{\mathcal{I}_{j}^{c},\mathcal{I}_{j}^{c}}x^{j}\Big\}.

In other words, we pick an index set such that its initial estimate has the largest correlation with the complement diagonal block.
The fact that
x^{j}
is statistically independent from
W_{\mathcal{I}_{\widehat{j}}^{c},\mathcal{I}_{\widehat{j}}^{c}}

plays a crucial role in the subsequent analysis.
The whole initialization scheme is summarized in Algorithm
1
.

We are then positioned to derive some key properties of the above initialization scheme.
For ease of exposition, let us define an index subset

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}_{1}\coloneqq\bigg\{j\in N:\frac{\big\langle v_{\mathcal{I}_{j}^{c}}^{\star},x^{j}\big\rangle}{\big\|v_{\mathcal{I}_{j}^{c}}^{\star}\big\|_{2}}\asymp 1\bigg\}.

(272)

We immediately make note of the following property, whose proof is provided in Section
F.6
.

Proposition 2
.

Consider the regime (
270
) with
k\gg\log n
, and set
p=C_{p}\frac{\log n}{k}
for some large constant
C_{p}
.
With probability at least
1-O(n^{-10})
, the vector
v^{\widehat{j}}
computed in (
275
) — with
\widehat{j}
chosen in (
277
) — satisfies

\displaystyle v^{\widehat{j}}\coloneqq M_{\mathcal{I}_{\widehat{j}}^{c},\mathcal{I}_{\widehat{j}}}v_{\mathcal{I}_{\widehat{j}}}=\alpha_{1}v_{\mathcal{I}_{\widehat{j}}^{c}}^{\star}+\phi_{0}\qquad\text{with }\alpha_{1}\asymp\lambda\sqrt{p},

(273)

where
\phi_{0}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\frac{1}{n}I_{|\mathcal{I}^{c}_{\widehat{j}}|})
is independent from
W_{\mathcal{I}_{\widehat{j}}^{c},\mathcal{I}_{\widehat{j}}^{c}}
conditional on
\mathcal{I}_{\widehat{j}}
.
Moreover, one has
\widehat{j}\in\mathcal{S}_{1}
with probability at least
1-O(n^{-10}).

Non-asymptotic theory of AMP as initialized in Algorithm
1
.

As revealed by Proposition
2
, the aforementioned initialization scheme provides an almost independent estimate that enjoys non-vanishing correlation with the truth.
We can then execute the AMP update rule (
36a
) on the submatrix
M_{\mathcal{I}_{\widehat{j}}^{c},\mathcal{I}_{\widehat{j}}^{c}}

in order to obtain an estimate
x_{t}
for the subvector of
v^{\star}
from the index subset
\mathcal{I}_{\widehat{j}}^{c}
;
details are summarized in Algorithm
1
.
With this in mind, our theory developed so far readily leads to finite-sample characterizations of this estimate
x_{t}
.
More specifically, Theorem
5
together with some basic union bounds reveals that with probability at least
1-O(n^{-10})
,
the estimate
x_{t}
returned by Algorithm
1
satisfies

\displaystyle x_{t+1,i}

\displaystyle=\alpha_{t+1}v^{\star}_{i}+\sum_{j=1}^{t}\beta_{t}^{j}\phi_{j,i}+\xi_{t,i},\qquad\text{for all }i\in\mathcal{I}^{c}_{\widehat{j}},

(274)

where again the
\phi_{j}
’s are i.i.d. drawn from
\mathcal{N}(0,\frac{1}{n}I_{|\mathcal{I}^{c}_{\widehat{j}}|})
, with the coefficients
\beta_{t}
,
\alpha_{t+1}
and
\|\xi_{t}\|_{2}
satisfying the predictions of Theorem
5
(except that
\alpha_{t+1}
should be rescaled by
\sqrt{1-p}
to account for the reduced signal size).

Remark 5
.

The careful reader might remark that Algorithm
1
only returns an estimate over the index subset
\mathcal{I}_{\widehat{j}}^{c}
.
One still needs to estimate the remaining entries of
v^{\star}
.
To do so, we can simply rerun the algorithm to generate different sampling sets, in the hope of producing another estimate that covers the remaining subvector (which is likely to happen given that
\mathcal{I}_{\widehat{j}}
is vanishingly small).
The two AMP outputs can then be merged easily to estimate the whole vector
v^{\star}
.
Details are omitted here as they are not the focus of the current paper.

Input:
data matrix
M
; an oracle algorithm as described in (
271
);
p\asymp\frac{\log n}{k}
;
\tau_{1}\asymp\sqrt{\frac{\log n}{n}}
.

Initialization:

1.

Set
N\asymp\log n
. For every
j\in[N]
, sample an index subset
\mathcal{I}_{j}\subset[n]
such that each

i\in[n]
belongs to
\mathcal{I}_{j}
independently with probability
p
.

2.

For each
j\in[N]
, partition
M
into four sub-matrices
M_{\mathcal{I}_{j},\mathcal{I}_{j}},M_{\mathcal{I}_{j},\mathcal{I}_{j}^{c}},M_{\mathcal{I}_{j}^{c},\mathcal{I}_{j}}
and
M_{\mathcal{I}_{j}^{c},\mathcal{I}_{j}^{c}}
.
Run the oracle algorithm to obtain a unit-norm estimate
v_{\mathcal{I}_{j}}
for
v^{\star}_{\mathcal{I}_{j}}
— the subvector of
v^{\star}
in the index set
\mathcal{I}_{j}
— based on
M_{\mathcal{I}_{j},\mathcal{I}_{j}}
,
which satisfies
\langle v^{\star}_{\mathcal{I}},v_{\mathcal{I}}\rangle\asymp\big\|v^{\star}_{\mathcal{I}}\big\|_{2}
with high probability.
Compute

\displaystyle v^{j}

\displaystyle\coloneqq M_{\mathcal{I}_{j}^{c},\mathcal{I}_{j}}\cdot v_{\mathcal{I}_{j}},

(275)

\displaystyle x^{j}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\frac{\mathsf{ST}_{\tau_{1}}(v^{j})}{\|\mathsf{ST}_{\tau_{1}}(v^{j})\|_{2}}.

(276)

3.

Compute

\displaystyle\widehat{j}\coloneqq\arg\!\max_{1\leq j\leq N}~\Big\{x^{j\top}M_{\mathcal{I}_{j}^{c},\mathcal{I}_{j}^{c}}x^{j}\Big\}\qquad\text{and}\qquad x_{1}=x^{\widehat{j}}.

(277)

AMP:
run AMP (
36a
) on
M_{\mathcal{I}_{\widehat{j}}^{c},\mathcal{I}_{\widehat{j}}^{c}}
with initialization
x_{1}
and
\eta_{0}(x_{0})=0
to obtain
x_{t}\in\real^{|\mathcal{I}_{\widehat{j}}^{c}|}
.

Algorithm 1

AMP with sample-split initialization.

Appendix F
Sparse PCA: Proofs of Theorem
5
and Corollary
4

Akin to the problem of
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
synchronization,
we always have
\|\beta_{t-1}\|_{2}=\|\eta_{t-1}(x_{t-1})\|_{2}=1

given our choices of the denoising functions (
36b
).
As a result, we shall focus attention on tracking
\alpha_{t}.

The proofs of Theorem
5
and Corollary
4
mainly follow from Theorem
2
,
with the assistance of an induction argument.
Specifically, our induction hypotheses for the
t
-th iteration are

\displaystyle\alpha_{t}\asymp\lambda\qquad\text{and}\qquad\|\xi_{t-1}\|_{2}\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{(t-1)\log^{3}n+k}{n}}.

(278)

In what follows, we shall assume the induction hypotheses (
278
) are valid for the
t
-th iteration,
and demonstrate their validity for the
(t+1)
-th iteration; the base case will be validated in Section
F.4
.
The only difference between Theorem
5
and Corollary
4
lies in the initialization step which
is detailed in Section
F.4.3
.

F.1
Preliminary facts

Before delving into the details of the main proof, we collect several preliminary results that shall be used repeatedly throughout this section.

F.1.1
Properties about the denoising functions

Recall that we adopt the following denoising functions: for any
x\in\real^{n}
,

\displaystyle\eta_{t}(x)\coloneqq\gamma_{t}\cdot\mathrm{sign}(x)\circ(|x|-\tau_{t}1)_{+}\qquad\text{where }\gamma_{t}\coloneqq\big\|\mathrm{sign}(x_{t})\circ(|x_{t}|-\tau_{t}1)_{+}\big\|_{2}^{-1}~\text{ and }~\tau_{t}\asymp\sqrt{\frac{\log n}{n}},

(279)

Here and throughout,
we abuse the notation to use it in an entrywise manner when applied to vectors, i.e.,

\mathrm{sign}(x)=\big[\mathrm{sign}(x_{i})\big]_{1\leq i\leq n}\qquad\text{and}\qquad(|x|-\tau 1)_{+}=\big[(|x_{i}|-\tau)_{+}\big]_{1\leq i\leq n}.

(280)

for any
x=[x_{i}]_{1\leq i\leq n}\in\real^{n}
.
The entrywise derivative of
\eta_{t}(x)
w.r.t.
x
is given by

\eta_{t}^{\prime}(x)=\gamma_{t}\mathds{1}(|x|>\tau_{t}1)\coloneqq\gamma_{t}\big[\mathds{1}(|x_{i}|>\tau_{t})\big]_{1\leq i\leq n}.

(281)

Here,
\eta_{t}^{\prime}(w)
is well-defined for all differentiable points
w\in\real
, with its value for the non-differentiable points (i.e.,
w=\pm\tau_{t}
) taken to be 0;
this works for our purpose given that the non-differentiable part are accounted for separately in Theorem
2
.

Next, consider a set of parameters
\mu=[\mu^{j}]_{1\leq j\leq t-1}\in\mathcal{S}^{t-2}
,
\alpha\in\real
and
\beta=[\beta^{j}]_{1\leq j\leq t-1}\in\real^{t-1}
independent of
\{\phi_{j}\}
,
and define the following vector (which is a function of
\alpha
and
\beta
):

v=\alpha v^{\star}+\sum_{j=1}^{t-1}\beta^{j}\phi_{j}\in\real^{n}.

We also define, for any positive numbers
\tau,\gamma\in\real
,

\eta(x;\tau)\coloneqq\gamma\cdot\mathrm{sign}(x)\circ(|x|-\tau 1)_{+}.

(282)

Elementary calculations together with
\|\mu\|_{2}=\|\beta\|_{2}=1
yield

\displaystyle\Big\|\nabla_{\phi_{j}}\Big\langle\sum_{j=1}^{t-1}\mu^{j}\phi_{j},a\Big\rangle\Big\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq|\mu^{j}|\|a\|_{2},\qquad

\displaystyle\text{for any given }a\in\mathbb{R}^{n}

(283a)

\displaystyle\Big\|\nabla_{\phi_{j}}\big\langle\eta(v;\tau),a\big\rangle\big\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq|\beta^{j}|\|\eta^{\prime}(v;\tau)\circ a\|_{2}\qquad

\displaystyle\text{for any given }a\in\mathbb{R}^{n}

(283b)

\displaystyle\Big\|\nabla_{\mu}\Big\langle\sum_{j=1}^{t-1}\mu^{j}\phi_{j},a\Big\rangle\Big\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\|a\|_{2}\sum_{j=1}^{t-1}\|\phi_{j}\|_{2}\qquad

\displaystyle\text{for any given }a\in\mathbb{R}^{n}

(283c)

\displaystyle\Big\|\nabla_{\mu,\beta}\Big(\sum_{j=1}^{t-1}\mu^{j}\beta^{j}\Big)\Big\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq 2

(283d)

\displaystyle\Big\|\nabla_{\alpha,\beta,\tau}\big\langle\eta(v;\tau),a\big\rangle\Big\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\|a\|_{2}\|\eta^{\prime}(v;\tau)\|_{2}\Big(1+\sum_{j}\|\phi_{j}\|_{2}\Big)\qquad

\displaystyle\text{for any given }a\in\mathbb{R}^{n}.

(283e)

F.1.2
Basic concentration results

Next, we collect some basic concentration results.
Similar to (
199
) in Section
D.1.1
,
we define

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{s}\coloneqq\left\{\{\phi_{j}\}:\max_{1\leq j\leq t-1}\|\phi_{j}\|_{2}<1+C_{5}\sqrt{\frac{\log\frac{n}{\delta}}{n}}\right\}\bigcap\left\{\{\phi_{j}\}:\sup_{a\in\mathcal{S}^{t-2}}\Big\|\sum_{j=1}^{t-1}a_{k}\phi_{j}\Big\|_{2}<1+C_{5}\sqrt{\frac{t\log\frac{n}{\delta}}{n}}\right\}

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt\bigcap\left\{\{\phi_{j}\}:\sup_{a\in\mathcal{S}^{t-2}}\sum_{i=1}^{s}\Big|\sum_{j=1}^{t-1}a_{k}\phi_{j}\Big|_{(i)}^{2}<\frac{C_{5}(t+s)\log\frac{n}{\delta}}{n}\right\}

(284)

for some sufficiently large constant
C_{5}>0
.
As discussed in (
200
), the convex set
\mathcal{E}_{s}
satisfies

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\{\phi_{j}\}\in\bigcap_{s=1}^{n}\mathcal{E}_{s}\right)\geq 1-\delta.

In addition, let us introduce an additional collection of convex sets: for any
1\leq s\leq n
,

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{s}\coloneqq\left\{\{\phi_{j}\}:\|\Phi_{s,:}\|_{2}\leq C_{5}\sqrt{(t-1)\log\frac{n}{\delta}}\right\},

(285)

where
\Phi_{s,:}
denotes the
s
-th row of matrix
\Phi=\sqrt{n}[\phi_{1},\cdots,\phi_{t-1}]\in\real^{n\times(t-1)}
.
Standard Gaussian concentration results
(
Vershynin, 2018
, Chapter 4.4)
reveal that
\{\phi_{j}\}
falls within
\bigcap_{s=1}^{n}\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{s}

with probability at least
1-\delta
, provided that
C_{5}
is large enough.
As a result, it is readily seen that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\{\phi_{j}\}\in\mathcal{E})\geq 1-2\delta,\qquad\text{for }\mathcal{E}\coloneqq\bigcap_{s=1}^{n}\big(\mathcal{E}_{s}\cap\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{s}\big).

(286)

Throughout the rest of the proof, we shall take
\delta
to be sufficiently small, say,
\delta\asymp n^{-300}
(similar to Section
D.1.1
).

F.1.3
Bounding the size of
\eta^{\prime}_{t}

As studied in the case of
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
synchronization (see (
233
)),
we know that conditional on the event
\mathcal{E}
,
the
t
-th largest entry of
\sum_{j=1}^{t-1}\beta^{j}\phi_{j}
for an arbitrary unit vector
