\beta=[\beta^{1},\cdots,\beta^{t-1}]\in\mathcal{S}^{t-2}
obeys

\displaystyle t\bigg|\sum_{j=1}^{t-1}\beta^{j}\phi_{j}\bigg|_{(t)}^{2}\leq\sum_{i=1}^{t}\bigg|\sum_{j=1}^{t-1}\beta^{j}\phi_{j}\bigg|_{(i)}^{2}\lesssim\frac{(t+t)\log n}{n}\asymp\frac{t\log n}{n},

where the last inequality uses the definition of
\mathcal{E}
.
It therefore implies that for every
l\geq t
, one has

\displaystyle\Big|\sum_{j=1}^{t-1}\beta^{j}\phi_{j}\Big|_{(l)}\leq\bigg|\sum_{j=1}^{t-1}\beta^{j}\phi_{j}\bigg|_{(t)}\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{\log n}{n}}

with probability at least
1-O(n^{-11})
.
Now consider the vector
v\coloneqq\alpha_{t}v^{\star}+\sum_{j=1}^{t-1}\beta^{j}\phi_{j}
.
Since
|v^{\star}|_{(k+1)}=0
(given that
v^{\star}
is
k
-sparse) and that
\tau_{t}\geq C_{3}\sqrt{\frac{\log n}{n}}

for some constant
C_{3}>0
large enough,
we can show that

\displaystyle\big|\eta_{t}^{\prime}(v)\big|_{(i)}=\big||v|-\tau 1\big|_{(i)}=0,\qquad\text{for }i>k+t

(287)

with probability exceeding
1-O(n^{-11})
.
As a direct consequence of (
287
), for any vector
a\in\real^{n}
one has

\displaystyle\left\|\eta_{t}^{\prime}(v)\circ a\right\|_{2}=\gamma_{t}\sqrt{\sum_{i=1}^{k+t}|a|_{(i)}^{2}}\lesssim\lambda^{-1}\sqrt{\sum_{i=1}^{k+t}|a|_{(i)}^{2}},

(288)

where the last relation comes from (
289
).

F.2
Tight estimate of
\gamma_{t}

In this subsection,
our goal is to show that under the induction hypotheses (
278
), we have

\displaystyle\gamma_{t}\coloneqq\big\|\mathrm{sign}(x_{t})(|x_{t}|-\tau_{t}1)_{+}\big\|_{2}^{-1}=\big\|(|x_{t}|-\tau_{t}1)_{+}\big\|_{2}^{-1}\asymp\lambda^{-1},

(289)

which would then imply that (see Assumption
1
)

\displaystyle\rho=\lambda^{-1}\qquad\text{and}\qquad\rho_{1}=0.

(290)

In order to show this, we resort to Corollary
2
.
Let us define

\displaystyle\Phi=\sqrt{n}\big[\phi_{1},\cdots,\phi_{t-1}\big]\qquad\text{and}\qquad\theta=(\alpha,\beta,\tau)\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{t-1}\times\mathbb{R}\quad\text{with }\beta=[\beta^{1},\cdots,\beta^{t-1}],

and consider the following function:

\displaystyle f_{\theta}(\Phi)\coloneqq\big\|\mathrm{sign}(v)\circ(|v|-\tau 1)_{+}\big\|_{2}^{2},\qquad\text{with }v\coloneqq\alpha v^{\star}+\sum_{j=1}^{t-1}\beta^{j}\phi_{j}.

(291)

Let us also introduce the following set of parameters:

\displaystyle\Theta\coloneqq\left\{\theta=(\alpha,\beta,\tau)\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{t-1}\times\mathbb{R}\,\Big|\,\alpha\asymp\lambda,\|\beta\|_{2}=1,C_{5}\sqrt{\frac{\log n}{n}}\leq\tau\asymp\sqrt{\frac{\log n}{n}}\right\}

for some large enough constant
C_{5}>0
.
Consequently,
\gamma_{t}
(cf. (
289
))
can be viewed as
f_{\theta}(\Phi)
with
\theta=(\alpha_{t},\beta_{t-1},\tau_{t})
,
and hence it suffices to develop a uniform bound on
f_{\theta}(\Phi)
over all
\theta\in\Theta
.

It is easily seen that
|f_{\theta}(\Phi)|\lesssim n^{100}\big(\max_{j}\|\phi_{j}\|_{2}\big)^{100}
,
and that
\|\nabla_{\theta}f(Z)\|_{2}\lesssim n^{100}
for all
Z\in\mathcal{E}
(cf. (
286
)).
In addition, given that
\log\frac{1}{\delta}\asymp\log n
, it follows from (
291
) and (
284
) that

\displaystyle\|v\|_{2}\leq|\alpha|\|v^{\star}\|_{2}+1+C_{5}\sqrt{\frac{t\log\frac{n}{\delta}}{n}}\asymp\lambda+1\asymp 1

(292)

over the set
\mathcal{E}
,
where the penultimate step is valid as long as
\frac{t\log n}{n}\lesssim 1
.
Moreover, we observe that

\displaystyle\big\|(|v|-\tau 1)_{+}\big\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i:\,v_{i}^{\star}\neq 0}\Big|\alpha v_{i}^{\star}+\sum_{j=1}^{t-1}\beta^{j}\phi_{j,i}\Big|^{2}+\sum_{i:\,v_{i}^{\star}=0}\bigg(\Big|\sum_{j=1}^{t-1}\beta^{j}\phi_{j,i}\Big|-\tau\bigg)^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\alpha^{2}\|v^{\star}\|_{2}^{2}+\sum_{i:\,v_{i}^{\star}\neq 0}\Big|\sum_{j=1}^{t-1}\beta^{j}\phi_{j,i}\Big|^{2}+\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bigg(\Big|\sum_{j=1}^{t-1}\beta^{j}\phi_{j,i}\Big|-\tau\bigg)^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\alpha^{2}+\sum_{i=1}^{2k+t}\Big|\sum_{j=1}^{t-1}\beta^{j}\phi_{j}\Big|_{(i)}^{2}\lesssim\alpha^{2}+\frac{(k+t)\log n}{n},

where the last line comes from (
287
) and the
definition (
284
) of
\mathcal{E}
.
This in turn allows us to calculate

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla_{\Phi}f_{\theta}(\Phi)\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq 2\frac{\|\beta\|_{2}}{\sqrt{n}}\big\|\mathrm{sign}(v)\circ(|v|-\tau 1)_{+}\circ\ind\left(|v|>\tau 1\right)\big\|_{2}\leq 2\frac{\big\|(|v|-\tau 1)_{+}\big\|_{2}}{\sqrt{n}}\lesssim\frac{\alpha+\sqrt{\frac{(k+t)\log n}{n}}}{\sqrt{n}}.

Therefore, Corollary
2
and (
202
) tell us that, with probability at least
1-O(n^{-11})
,

\displaystyle\left|\big\|(|v|-\tau 1)_{+}\big\|_{2}^{2}-\int\Big\|\Big(\Big|\alpha v^{\star}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}x\Big|-\tau 1\Big)_{+}\Big\|_{2}^{2}\varphi_{n}(\mathrm{d}x)\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\bigg(\alpha+\sqrt{\frac{(k+t)\log n}{n}}\bigg)\sqrt{\frac{t\log n}{n}}

holds simultaneously for all
\theta\in\Theta
.
This in turn implies that

\displaystyle\bigg|\big\|(|v_{t}|-\tau_{t}1)_{+}\big\|_{2}^{2}-\int\Big\|\left(\Big|\alpha_{t}v^{\star}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}x\Big|-\tau_{t}1\right)_{+}\Big\|_{2}^{2}\varphi_{n}(\mathrm{d}x)\bigg|

\displaystyle\lesssim\bigg(\alpha_{t}+\sqrt{\frac{(k+t)\log n}{n}}\bigg)\sqrt{\frac{t\log n}{n}}.

(293)

Next, let us assess the size of the quantity
\int\|(|\alpha_{t}v^{\star}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}x|-\tau_{t}1)_{+}\|_{2}^{2}\varphi_{n}(\mathrm{d}x)
.
For those indices
i
obeying
|\alpha_{t}v_{i}^{\star}|\geq 2\tau_{t}
,
it is easily seen from basic Gaussian properties that

\displaystyle\int\Big(\Big|\alpha_{t}v_{i}^{\star}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}x\Big|-\tau_{t}\Big)_{+}^{2}\varphi(\mathrm{d}x)\asymp\int_{-\sqrt{\log n}}^{\sqrt{\log n}}\big(\alpha_{t}v_{i}^{\star}\big)^{2}\varphi(\mathrm{d}x)\asymp\left(\alpha_{t}v_{i}^{\star}\right)^{2},

(294)

which together with the induction hypothesis
\alpha_{t}\asymp\lambda
gives

\displaystyle\sum_{i:\,|\alpha_{t}v_{i}^{\star}|\geq 2\tau_{t}\asymp\sqrt{\frac{\log n}{n}}}\int\Big(\Big|\alpha_{t}v_{i}^{\star}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}x\Big|-\tau_{t}\Big)_{+}^{2}\varphi(\mathrm{d}x)\asymp\lambda^{2}\sum_{i:\,|\alpha_{t}v_{i}^{\star}|\geq 2\tau_{t}\asymp\sqrt{\frac{\log n}{n}}}\left(v_{i}^{\star}\right)^{2}.

(295)

Additionally, it is observed that

\displaystyle 1\geq\sum_{i}\big(v_{i}^{\star}\big)^{2}\ind\Big(|\alpha_{t}v_{i}^{\star}|\geq 2\tau_{t}\Big)

\displaystyle\overset{(\mathrm{i})}{\geq}\sum_{i}\big(v_{i}^{\star}\big)^{2}\ind\Big(|v_{i}^{\star}|\geq\sqrt{\frac{1}{2k}}\Big)=1-\sum_{i}\big(v_{i}^{\star}\big)^{2}\ind\Big(0<|v_{i}^{\star}|<\sqrt{\frac{1}{2k}}\Big)

\displaystyle\geq 1-k\cdot\left(\sqrt{\frac{1}{2k}}\right)^{2}1=\frac{1}{2},

(296)

where (i) holds since for any
i
with
|v_{i}^{\star}|\geq\sqrt{\frac{1}{2k}}
,
one necessarily has
|\alpha_{t}v_{i}^{\star}|\asymp|\lambda v_{i}^{\star}|\geq 2\tau_{t}\asymp\sqrt{\frac{\log n}{n}}

as long as
\lambda\geq C_{2}\sqrt{\frac{k\log n}{n}}
for some large enough constant
C_{2}>0
.
Substitution into (
295
) yields

\displaystyle\sum_{i:\,|\alpha_{t}v_{i}^{\star}|\geq 2\tau_{t}\asymp\sqrt{\frac{\log n}{n}}}\int\Big(\Big|\alpha_{t}v_{i}^{\star}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}x\Big|-\tau_{t}\Big)_{+}^{2}\varphi(\mathrm{d}x)\asymp\lambda^{2}.

(297)

Moreover, when it comes to those indices
i
obeying
|\alpha_{t}v_{i}^{\star}|<2\tau_{t}\asymp\sqrt{\frac{\log n}{n}}
, one has

\sum_{i:\,|\alpha_{t}v_{i}^{\star}|<2\tau_{t}\asymp\sqrt{\frac{\log n}{n}}}\int\Big(\Big|\alpha_{t}v_{i}^{\star}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}x\Big|-\tau_{t}\Big)_{+}^{2}\varphi(\mathrm{d}x)\leq\sum_{i:\,|\alpha_{t}v_{i}^{\star}|<2\tau_{t}\asymp\sqrt{\frac{\log n}{n}}}\big(\alpha_{t}v_{i}^{\star}\big)^{2}\lesssim k\cdot\frac{\log n}{n}\lesssim\lambda^{2},

provided that
\lambda^{2}\gtrsim\frac{k\log n}{n}
.
This combined with (
297
) leads to

\displaystyle\int\Big\|\Big(\Big|\alpha_{t}v^{\star}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}x\Big|-\tau_{t}1\Big)_{+}\Big\|_{2}^{2}\varphi_{n}(\mathrm{d}x)

\displaystyle\asymp\lambda^{2}.

(298)

Taking this collectively with (
293
) gives

\displaystyle\bigg|\big\|(|v_{t}|-\tau_{t}1)_{+}\big\|_{2}-\bigg(\int\Big\|\left(\Big|\alpha_{t}v^{\star}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}x\Big|-\tau_{t}1\right)_{+}\Big\|_{2}^{2}\varphi_{n}(\mathrm{d}x)\bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}}\bigg|

\displaystyle\quad\quad\leq\frac{\Big|\big\|(|v_{t}|-\tau_{t}1)_{+}\big\|_{2}^{2}-\int\big\|\left(\big|\alpha_{t}v^{\star}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}x\big|-\tau_{t}1\right)_{+}\big\|_{2}^{2}\varphi_{n}(\mathrm{d}x)\Big|}{\Big(\int\big\|\big(\big|\alpha_{t}v^{\star}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}x\big|-\tau_{t}1\big)_{+}\big\|_{2}^{2}\varphi_{n}(\mathrm{d}x)\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}}\lesssim\frac{\alpha_{t}+\sqrt{\frac{(k+t)\log n}{n}}}{\lambda}\sqrt{\frac{t\log n}{n}}.

(299)

Now in order to control
\gamma_{t}
, we still need to establish a connection between
\|\mathrm{sign}(x_{t})\circ(|x_{t}|-\tau_{t})_{+}\|_{2}
and
\|\mathrm{sign}(v_{t})\circ(|v_{t}|-\tau_{t})_{+}\|_{2}.

Recognizing that
x_{t}=v_{t}+\xi_{t-1}
,
we can invoke the triangle inequality to obtain

\displaystyle\gamma_{t}^{-1}

\displaystyle=\big\|(|x_{t}|-\tau_{t}1)_{+}\big\|_{2}=\big\|(|v_{t}|-\tau_{t}1)_{+}\big\|_{2}+O(\|\xi_{t-1}\big\|_{2})

\displaystyle=\sqrt{\int\Big\|\Big(\Big|\alpha_{t}v^{\star}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}x\Big|-\tau_{t}1\Big)_{+}\Big\|_{2}^{2}\varphi_{n}(\mathrm{d}x)}+O\bigg(\frac{1}{\lambda}\sqrt{\frac{t\log n}{n}}+\|\xi_{t-1}\|_{2}\bigg)

\displaystyle=\left(1+O\Bigg(\frac{\alpha_{t}+\sqrt{\frac{(k+t)\log n}{n}}}{\lambda^{2}}\sqrt{\frac{t\log n}{n}}+\frac{\|\xi_{t-1}\|_{2}}{\lambda}\Bigg)\right)\sqrt{\int\Big\|\Big(\Big|\alpha_{t}v^{\star}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}x\Big|-\tau_{t}1\Big)_{+}\Big\|_{2}^{2}\varphi_{n}(\mathrm{d}x)}\asymp\lambda,

(300)

where the penultimate step follows from inequality (
299
),
and the last line makes use of (
298
).
This establishes the claimed result in (
289
).

F.3
Controlling key quantities
A_{t},B_{t},D_{t},E_{t}
and
\kappa_{t}

In order to apply Theorem
2
for the sparse spiked Wigner model,
a key step lies in bounding the multiple key quantities
A_{t},\ldots,G_{t}
(see (
14a
)-(
14f
)) as specified in Assumption
2
,
which we aim to accomplish in this subsection. Note that we do not need to bound
C_{t},F_{t}
and
G_{t}
as they only appear in the bound on
\Delta_{\beta,t}
, which is irrelevant in this case.
The rest of the section is dedicated to bounding
A_{t},B_{t},D_{t},E_{t}
.
Along the way, we shall also control
\kappa_{t}
, which is needed when calculating
D_{t}
.

F.3.1
Quantity
A_{t}
in (
14a
)

Unlike the case of
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
synchronization where the denoising functions are smooth everywhere, caution needs to be exercised when handling discontinuity points in sparse spiked Wigner models.
Consider any given
\mu=[\mu^{1},\cdots,\mu^{t-1}]
,
\alpha\in\real
,
\beta\in[\beta^{1},\cdots,\beta^{t-1}]
and
\tau,\gamma\in\real
,
and let us take

\displaystyle\Phi\coloneqq\sqrt{n}(\phi_{1},\ldots,\phi_{t-1}),\qquad\theta\coloneqq\big[\mu,\alpha,\beta,\tau,\gamma\big]\in\mathcal{S}^{t-2}\times\real\times\mathcal{S}^{t-2}\times\real\times\real,

(301a)

\displaystyle v\coloneqq\alpha v^{\star}+

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{t-1}\beta^{j}\phi_{j}\in\real^{n}\quad\Theta\coloneqq\left\{\theta=\big[\mu,\alpha,\beta,\tau\big]\,\Big|\,\alpha\asymp\gamma^{-1}\asymp\lambda,\|\mu\|_{2}=\|\beta\|_{2}=1,\tau\asymp\sqrt{\frac{\log n}{n}}\right\}.

(301b)

Recall that
A_{t}
consists of two parts:

\big\langle\sum_{j=1}^{t-1}\mu^{j}\phi_{j},\eta_{t}(v_{t})\big\rangle
and
\big\langle\eta_{t}^{\prime}\big\rangle\sum_{j=1}^{t-1}\mu^{j}\beta_{t-1}^{j}
.
In order to bound the first part of
A_{t}
, we intend to first derive a uniform control of the following function:

\displaystyle f_{\theta}(\Phi)\coloneqq\Big\langle\sum_{j=1}^{t-1}\mu^{j}\phi_{j},\eta(v)\Big\rangle

over all
\theta\in\Theta
,
where we define (with its dependency on
\theta
suppressed in the notation)

\displaystyle\eta(x)\coloneqq\gamma\,\mathrm{sign}(x)\circ(|x|-\tau 1)_{+}.

(302)

Towards this end, we first repeat the analysis in Section
F.2
(in particular, (
293
) and (
298
)) to derive

\big\|(|v|-\tau 1)_{+}\big\|_{2}\asymp\lambda\qquad\text{and}\qquad\|\eta(v)\|_{2}=\gamma\,\big\|(|v|-\tau 1)_{+}\big\|_{2}\asymp 1,

(303)

for any
\theta\in\Theta
.
We can then invoke the derivative calculation in (
283
) to arrive at

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla_{\Phi}f_{\theta}(\Phi)\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\|\mu\|_{2}}{\sqrt{n}}\left\|\eta(v)\right\|_{2}+\frac{\|\beta\|_{2}}{\sqrt{n}}\bigg\|\sum_{j=1}^{t-1}\mu^{j}\phi_{j}\circ\eta^{\prime}(v)\bigg\|_{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}+\frac{1}{\lambda\sqrt{n}}\Bigg(\sum_{l=1}^{k+t}\bigg|\sum_{j=1}^{t-1}\mu^{j}\phi_{j}\bigg|_{(l)}^{2}\Bigg)^{1/2}\lesssim\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\bigg(1+\frac{1}{\lambda}\sqrt{\frac{(t+k)\log n}{n}}\bigg)\asymp\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}},

where the second inequality applies (
303
) and (
288
),
the third inequality invokes the property of
\mathcal{E}
in (
284
),
and the last relation is valid as long as
\lambda^{2}\gtrsim\frac{k\log n}{n}
and
t\lesssim\frac{\lambda^{2}n}{\log n}
.
Additionally, it is trivially seen that
f_{\theta}(\Phi)
as a function of
\theta
is
n^{100}
-Lipschitz for any given
\Phi\in\mathcal{E}

and
|f_{\theta}(\Phi)|\lesssim n^{100}\big(\max_{j}\|\phi_{j}\|_{2}\big)^{100}
.
As a result, invoke Corollary
2
in conjunction with (
202
) to arrive at

\displaystyle\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}\Big|f_{\theta}(\Phi)-\mathbb{E}\left[f_{\theta}(\Phi)\right]\Big|

\displaystyle\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{t\log n}{n}},

(304)

with probability at least
1-O(n^{-11})
.

Next, we move on to consider the second part of
A_{t}
, namely,

\displaystyle\big\langle\eta_{t}^{\prime}(v_{t})\big\rangle\cdot\sum_{j=1}^{t-1}\mu_{t}^{j}\beta_{t-1}^{j}\qquad\text{where }\left\langle\eta_{t}^{\prime}(v_{t})\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\frac{\gamma_{t}}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\ind\Big(\big|\alpha_{t}v_{i}^{\star}+\sum_{j}\beta_{t-1}^{j}\phi_{j,i}\big|>\tau_{t}\Big).

Given that the indicator function is not Lipschitz continuous,
we resort to Corollary
3
to control it.
For any given
\theta\in\Theta
, define

\displaystyle h_{i,\theta}(\Phi_{i,:})\coloneqq\bigg|\alpha v_{i}^{\star}+\sum_{j=1}^{t-1}\beta^{j}\phi_{j,i}\bigg|,\qquad 1\leq i\leq n,

(305)

where
\Phi_{i,:}
denotes the
i
-th row of
\Phi
.
Clearly, for any
\theta,\widetilde{\theta}\in\Theta,
one can easily verify that

\big|h_{i,\theta}(\Phi_{i,:})-h_{i,\widetilde{\theta}}(\Phi_{i,:})\big|\leq n^{100}\big\|\theta-\widetilde{\theta}\|_{2}

for any
\Phi\in\mathcal{E}
; and for any
\theta\in\Theta
and
\tau\leq n
, one has

\mathbb{P}\Big(\tau-400n^{-100}\leq h_{i,\theta}(\Phi_{i,:})\leq\tau+400n^{-100}\Big)\lesssim n^{-1}.

Therefore, Corollary
3
together with (
202
) reveals that with probability at least
1-O(n^{-11})
,

\displaystyle\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}\left|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\ind\left(h_{i,\theta}>\tau\right)-\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{P}\left(h_{i,\theta}>\tau\right)\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}\sqrt{\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{P}\left(h_{i,\theta}>\tau\right)t\log n}+t\log n

\displaystyle\lesssim\sqrt{(k+n\cdot O(n^{-11}))t\log n}+t\log n\lesssim\sqrt{t(t+k)}\log n

(306)

holds simultaneously for all
\theta\in\Theta
,
where the last inequality comes from (
287
) given that
\tau\asymp\sqrt{\frac{\log n}{n}}
.
Recognizing that
|\mu^{\top}\beta|\leq 1
, we further have

\displaystyle\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}\left|\mu^{\top}\beta\sum_{i=1}^{n}\ind\left(h_{i,\theta}>\tau\right)-\mu^{\top}\beta\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{P}\left(h_{i,\theta}>\tau\right)\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim|\mu^{\top}\beta|\sqrt{t(t+k)\log n}\lesssim\sqrt{t(t+k)}\log n.

(307)

To summarize, let us decompose the quantity of interest in (
14a
) as follows:

\displaystyle\Bigg|\left\langle\sum_{j=1}^{t-1}\mu^{j}\phi_{j},\eta_{t}(v_{t})\right\rangle-\big\langle\eta_{t}^{\prime}(v_{t})\big\rangle\sum_{j=1}^{t-1}\mu^{j}\beta_{t-1}^{j}\Bigg|

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\leq\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}\big|f_{\theta}(\Phi)-\mathbb{E}[f_{\theta}(\Phi)]\big|+\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}\Big|\mathbb{E}[f_{\theta}(\Phi)]-\big\langle\eta^{\prime}(v)\big\rangle\cdot\sum_{j=1}^{t-1}\mu^{j}\beta^{j}\Big|

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad=\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}\big|f_{\theta}(\Phi)-\mathbb{E}[f_{\theta}(\Phi)]\big|+\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}\Big|\frac{\gamma}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mu^{\top}\beta\cdot\mathbb{P}\left(|h_{i,\theta}|>\tau\right)-\big\langle\eta^{\prime}(v)\big\rangle\mu^{\top}\beta\Big|,

where the last equality follows from Stein’s lemma, that is,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[f_{\theta}(\Phi)]=\mathbb{E}\left[\left\langle\sum_{j=1}^{t-1}\mu^{j}\phi_{j},\eta(v)\right\rangle\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[\left\langle\eta^{\prime}(v)\right\rangle\sum_{j=1}^{t-1}\mu^{j}\beta^{j}\right]=\frac{\gamma}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mu^{\top}\beta\cdot\mathbb{P}\left(|h_{i,\theta}|>\tau\right).

Taking the decomposition above collectively with (
304
) and (
307
) yields

\displaystyle\Bigg|\left\langle\sum_{j=1}^{t-1}\mu^{j}\phi_{j},\eta_{t}(v_{t})\right\rangle-\left\langle\eta_{t}^{\prime}\right\rangle\sum_{j=1}^{t-1}\mu^{j}\beta_{t-1}^{j}\Bigg|

\displaystyle\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{t\log n}{n}}+\frac{\sqrt{t(t+k)}\log n}{n}\asymp\sqrt{\frac{t\log n}{n}}=:A_{t},

(308)

where the last relation is valid under Assumption (
42
).

F.3.2
Quantity
B_{t}
in (
14b
)

Recall that quantity
B_{t}
is concerned with bounding
v^{\star\top}\eta_{t}(v_{t})
.
To do so, let us again adopt the definitions of
\Phi,\theta,\Theta,v
as in (
301
),
and definte the following function parameterized by
\theta
:

\displaystyle f_{\theta}(\Phi)\coloneqq v^{\star\top}\eta(v),

with the function
\eta
defined in (
302
).
In order to bound
v^{\star\top}\eta_{t}(v_{t})
,
we first develop a valid bound on
f_{\theta}(\Phi)
that is valid simultaneously for all
\theta\in\Theta
.

Towards this end, consider any fixed parameter
\theta\in\Theta
, and apply (
283
) to reach

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla_{\Phi}f_{\theta}(\Phi)\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\|\beta\|_{2}}{\sqrt{n}}\left\|v^{\star}\circ\eta^{\prime}(v)\right\|_{2}\lesssim\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\cdot\frac{1}{\lambda}\|v^{\star}\|_{2}\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{1}{n\lambda^{2}}},

(309)

where we have used property (
288
) as well as the fact that
\|\beta\|_{2}=1
.
Additionally, it is easily seen that
\left\|\nabla_{\theta}f_{\theta}(\Phi)\right\|_{2}\lesssim n^{100}
for any
\Phi\in\mathcal{E}
and

|f_{\theta}(\Phi)|\lesssim n^{100}\max_{j}\|\phi_{j}\|_{2}^{100}
.
As a consequence, Corollary
3
taken together with (
202
) indicates that, with probability at least
1-O(n^{-11})
,

\displaystyle\left|v^{\star\top}\eta_{t}(v_{t})-v^{\star\top}\int\eta_{t}\left(\alpha_{t}v^{\star}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}x\right)\varphi_{n}(\mathrm{d}x)\right|

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{\theta}\left|v^{\star\top}\eta-v^{\star\top}\int\eta\left(\alpha v^{\star}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}x\right)\varphi_{n}(\mathrm{d}x)\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{t\log n}{n\lambda^{2}}}=:B_{t}.

(310)

F.3.3
Bounding quantity
\kappa_{t}

This subsection develops an upper bound on the quantity
\kappa_{t}^{2}

defined in (
2.2
), which is crucial in controlling
D_{t}
.
From the choices of the denoising functions,
\eta_{t}^{\prime\prime}
is well-defined and equal to
0
except at two non-differentiable points.
To bound
\kappa_{t}^{2}
, it is thus sufficient to control quantities

\langle\int[\eta_{t}^{\prime}(\alpha_{t}v^{\star}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}x\big)]^{2}\varphi_{n}(\mathrm{d}x)\rangle
and
\langle\int[x\eta_{t}^{\prime}\big(\alpha_{t}v^{\star}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}x\big)]^{2}\varphi_{n}(\mathrm{d}x)\rangle
separately, given that
\|\beta_{t-1}\|_{2}=1
.

Let us first consider the term
\langle\int[\eta_{t}^{\prime}(\alpha_{t}v^{\star}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}x\big)]^{2}\varphi(\mathrm{d}x)\rangle
.
Recall our induction hypothesis
\alpha_{t}\asymp\lambda
as well as our assumptions

\lambda\gtrsim\sqrt{\frac{k\log n}{n}}
and
\tau_{t}\asymp\sqrt{\frac{\log n}{n}}
.
We shall divide the index set
[n]
into two parts and look at each part separately.
For those indices
i
obeying
v_{i}^{\star}\neq 0
, one has the trivial upper bound

\displaystyle\int\ind\Big(\Big|\alpha_{t}v_{i}^{\star}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}x\Big|>\tau_{t}\Big)\varphi(\mathrm{d}x)\leq 1.

(311)

Otherwise, for those entries with
v_{i}^{\star}=0
, we find that

\displaystyle\int\ind\Big(\Big|\alpha_{t}v_{i}^{\star}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}x\Big|>\tau_{t}\Big)\varphi(\mathrm{d}x)=\int\ind\Big(\Big|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}x\Big|>\tau_{t}\Big)\varphi(\mathrm{d}x)\leq 2\int_{\sqrt{n}\tau_{t}}^{\infty}\varphi(\mathrm{d}x)\lesssim\frac{1}{n},

provided that
\tau_{t}\geq 2\sqrt{\frac{\log n}{n}}
.
Putting these two cases together gives

\displaystyle\left\langle\int\Big[\eta_{t}^{\prime}\Big(\alpha_{t}v+\frac{\|\beta_{t-1}\|_{2}}{\sqrt{n}}x\Big)\Big]^{2}\varphi_{n}(\mathrm{d}x)\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\gamma_{t}^{2}\left\langle\int\ind\Big(\Big|\alpha_{t}v^{\star}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}x\Big|>\tau_{t}1\Big)\varphi_{n}(\mathrm{d}x)\right\rangle

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{n\lambda^{2}}\left(k\cdot 1+(n-k)\cdot\frac{1}{n}\right)\asymp\frac{k}{n\lambda^{2}}.

(312)

Similarly, it can also be established that

\displaystyle\left\langle\int\left[x\eta_{t}^{\prime}\left(\alpha_{t}v^{\star}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}x\right)\right]^{2}\varphi_{n}(\mathrm{d}x)\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\gamma_{t}^{2}\left\langle\int x^{2}\ind\left(\left|\alpha_{t}v^{\star}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}x\right|>\tau_{t}1\right)\varphi_{n}(\mathrm{d}x)\right\rangle\lesssim\frac{k}{n\lambda^{2}}.

(313)

Consequently, putting the above two cases together with the definition (
2.2
) yields

\displaystyle\kappa_{t}^{2}\lesssim\frac{k}{n\lambda^{2}}.

(314)

F.3.4
Quantity
D_{t}
in (
14d
)

We now turn to the analysis of
D_{t}
.
Note that
\eta_{t}^{\prime\prime}
is well-defined and equal to
0
except at two non-differentiable points.
Hence, to control
D_{t}
, it is sufficient to consider the following function:

\displaystyle\Big\|\sum_{j=1}^{t-1}\mu^{j}_{t}\phi_{j}\circ\eta_{t}^{\prime}(v_{t})\Big\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\gamma_{t}^{2}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Big(\sum_{j=1}^{t-1}\mu^{j}_{t}\phi_{j,i}\Big)^{2}\ind\Big(\Big|\alpha_{t}v_{i}^{\star}+\sum_{j}\beta_{t-1}^{j}\phi_{j,i}\Big|>\tau_{t}\Big).

(315)

Setting the stage, let us define
\Phi,\theta,\Theta,v,\eta
as in (
301
) and (
302
),
and introduce the following functions:

\displaystyle f_{i,\theta}(\Phi_{i,:})\coloneqq\bigg(\sum_{j=1}^{t-1}\mu^{j}\phi_{j,i}\bigg)^{2},\qquad\text{ and }\qquad h_{i,\theta}(\Phi_{i,:})\coloneqq\bigg|\alpha v_{i}^{\star}+\sum_{j=1}^{t-1}\beta^{j}\phi_{j,i}\bigg|.

For every fixed
\mu\in\mathcal{S}^{t-2}
,
\sum_{j=1}^{t-1}\mu^{j}\phi_{j,i}

is Gaussian with mean zero and variance
1/n
;
therefore,
f_{i,\theta}\geq 0
is
\frac{1}{n}
-subexponential with
\mathbb{E}[f_{i,\theta}]=1/n
(see

Vershynin, 2018
).
In addition, it can be straightforwardly checked that
(i)
\|\nabla_{\theta}f_{i,\theta}(\Phi_{i,:})\|_{2}\lesssim n^{100}
for any
\Phi\in\mathcal{E}
;
(ii)
|f_{i,\theta}(\Phi_{i,:})|\lesssim n^{100}\|\Phi\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{100}
;
and (iii)
\mathbb{P}\big(\tau-400n^{-100}\leq h_{i,\theta}(\Phi_{i,:})\leq\tau+400n^{-100}\big)\lesssim n^{-1}
for any
\tau\in\real
and any
\theta\in\Theta
.
By virtue of Corollary
3
and (
202
),
we can readily see that, with probability at least
1-O(n^{-11})
,

\displaystyle\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}\left|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Big(\sum_{j=1}^{t-1}\mu^{j}\phi_{j,i}\Big)^{2}\ind\Big(\Big|\alpha v_{i}^{\star}+\sum_{j=1}^{t-1}\beta^{j}\phi_{j,i}\Big|>\tau\Big)-\mathbb{E}\bigg[\Big\|\sum_{j=1}^{t-1}\mu^{j}\phi_{j}\circ\ind\Big(\Big|\alpha v^{\star}+\sum_{j=1}^{t-1}\beta^{j}\phi_{j}\Big|>\tau 1\Big)\Big\|_{2}^{2}\bigg]\right|

\displaystyle\qquad\lesssim\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}\frac{1}{n}\sqrt{\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{P}\left(\Big|\alpha v_{i}^{\star}+\sum_{j=1}^{t-1}\beta^{j}\phi_{j,i}\Big|>\tau\right)t\log^{3}n}+\frac{t\log^{2}n}{n}\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{t(t+k)\log^{4}n}{n^{2}}}

holds simultaneously for all
\theta\in\Theta
,
where the last inequality follows from the same argument as in (
306
).
Additionally, recalling that for general denoising functions, we have established relation (
232
).
When specialized to the current setting, it asserts that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\Big\|\sum_{j=1}^{t-1}\mu^{j}\phi_{j}\circ\eta^{\prime}(v)\Big\|_{2}^{2}\Bigg]-\max\left\{\Big\langle\int\Big[x\eta^{\prime}\Big(\alpha v^{\star}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}x\Big)\Big]^{2}\varphi_{n}(\mathrm{d}x)\Big\rangle,\Big\langle\int\Big[\eta^{\prime}\Big(\alpha v^{\star}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}x\Big)\Big]^{2}\varphi_{n}(\mathrm{d}x)\Big\rangle\right\}\leq 0.

Putting the above bounds together, using the definition (
2.2
) of
\kappa_{t}
,
and recognizing that
(\mu_{t},\alpha_{t},\beta_{t-1},\tau_{t},\gamma_{t})\in\Theta
,
we can obtain

\displaystyle\bigg\|\sum_{j=1}^{t-1}\mu^{j}\phi_{j}\circ\eta_{t}^{\prime}(v_{t})\bigg\|_{2}^{2}-\kappa_{t}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\gamma_{t}^{2}\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}\Bigg|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Big(\sum_{j=1}^{t-1}\mu^{j}\phi_{j,i}\Big)^{2}\ind\Big(\Big|\alpha v_{i}^{\star}+\sum_{j=1}^{t-1}\beta^{j}\phi_{j,i}\Big|>\tau\Big)-\mathbb{E}\bigg[\Big\|\sum_{j=1}^{t-1}\mu^{j}\phi_{j}\circ\ind\Big(\Big|\alpha v^{\star}+\sum_{j=1}^{t-1}\beta^{j}\phi_{j}\Big|>\tau\Big)\Big\|_{2}^{2}\bigg]\Bigg|

\displaystyle\quad+\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}\left\{\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\Big\|\sum_{j=1}^{t-1}\mu^{j}\phi_{j}\circ\eta^{\prime}(v)\Big\|_{2}^{2}\Bigg]-\max\left\{\Big\langle\int\Big[x\eta^{\prime}\Big(\alpha v^{\star}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}x\Big)\Big]^{2}\varphi_{n}(\mathrm{d}x)\Big\rangle,\Big\langle\int\Big[\eta^{\prime}\Big(\alpha v^{\star}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}x\Big)\Big]^{2}\varphi_{n}(\mathrm{d}x)\Big\rangle\right\}\right\}

\displaystyle\lesssim\gamma_{t}^{2}\sqrt{\frac{t(t+k)\log^{4}n}{n^{2}}}\asymp\frac{1}{\lambda^{2}}\sqrt{\frac{t(t+k)\log^{4}n}{n^{2}}}\eqqcolon D_{t},

(316)

where we remind the reader that
\gamma_{t}\asymp\lambda^{-1}
(see (
289
)).

F.3.5
Quantity
E_{t}
in (
14h
)

We now turn attention to quantity
E_{t}
,
which requires us to work with non-differentiable points.
Note that the denosing function
\eta^{\prime}_{t}
is only non-differentiable at two points:
-\tau_{t}
and
\tau_{t}
.
The goal of this subsection to prove that: with probability at least
1-O(n^{-11})
,

\displaystyle\sum_{m\in\{\tau_{t},-\tau_{t}\}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\ind\bigg(\bigg|\alpha_{t}v_{i}^{\star}+\sum_{j=1}^{t-1}\beta_{t-1}^{j}\phi_{j,i}-m\bigg|\leq\theta(m)\bigg)\lesssim k+t\log^{3}n+n\|\xi_{t-1}\|_{2}^{2}\eqqcolon E_{t}

(317)

holds for any choice
\theta(m)
satisfying

\displaystyle\sum_{m\in\{\tau_{t},-\tau_{t}\}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bigg|\alpha_{t}v_{i}^{\star}+\sum_{j=1}^{t-1}\beta_{t-1}^{j}\phi_{j,i}-m\bigg|^{2}\ind\bigg(\bigg|\alpha_{t}v_{i}^{\star}+\sum_{j=1}^{t-1}\beta_{t-1}^{j}\phi_{j,i}-m\bigg|\leq\theta(m)\bigg)\leq\|\xi_{t-1}\|_{2}^{2}.

Towards this, let us adopt the definitions of
\Phi,\theta,\Theta,v
in (
301
) as before,
and generate a Gaussian random variable
z\sim\mathcal{N}(0,1/n)
.
As shall be seen momentarily, the following two relations hold true uniformly over all
\theta\in\Theta
and all
\omega\in\real
obeying
\omega\lesssim n
:

\displaystyle\Bigg\|\bigg|\alpha v^{\star}+\sum_{j=1}^{t-1}\beta^{j}\phi_{j}-\tau 1\bigg|\circ\ind\bigg(\Big|\alpha v^{\star}+\sum_{j=1}^{t-1}\beta^{j}\phi_{j}-\tau 1\Big|\leq\omega 1\bigg)\Bigg\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad\geq(n-k)\mathbb{E}\left[\left|z-\tau\right|^{2}\ind\left(\left|z-\tau 1\right|\leq\omega\right)\right]-\frac{\log n}{n}\cdot O\left(\sqrt{(n-k)\mathbb{P}\left(\left|z-\tau\right|\leq\omega\right)t\log n}+t\log n\right),

(318a)

\displaystyle\bigg\|\ind\Big(\Big|\alpha v^{\star}+\sum_{j=1}^{t-1}\beta^{j}\phi_{j}-\tau 1\Big|\leq\omega 1\Big)\bigg\|_{0}\lesssim k+(n-k)\mathbb{P}\left(\left|z-\tau\right|\leq\omega\right)+t\log n.

(318b)

Taking these two inequalities (
318
) and (
318b
) as given for the moment (which we shall return to prove shortly),
we proceed to justify the following claim:
for any point
\omega\in\real
obeying
\omega\lesssim n
and

\displaystyle\Bigg\|\bigg|\alpha_{t}v^{\star}+\sum_{j=1}^{t-1}\beta^{j}_{t-1}\phi_{j}-\tau_{t}1\bigg|\circ\ind\bigg(\Big|\alpha_{t}v^{\star}+\sum_{j=1}^{t-1}\beta^{j}_{t-1}\phi_{j}-\tau_{t}1\Big|\leq\omega 1\bigg)\Bigg\|_{2}^{2}\leq\|\xi_{t-1}\|_{2}^{2},

(319)

one necessarily satisfies

\displaystyle\sum_{m\in\{\tau_{t},-\tau_{t}\}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\ind\bigg(\bigg|\alpha_{t}v_{i}^{\star}+\sum_{j=1}^{t-1}\beta_{t-1}^{j}\phi_{j,i}-m\bigg|\leq\omega\bigg)\lesssim k+t\log^{3}n+n\|\xi_{t-1}\|_{2}^{2}.

(320)

If this were valid, then one could immediately establish (
317
), thus completing the control of
E_{t}
.

In what follows, let us prove this claim (
320
).

•

Suppose the point
\omega
satisfies
\mathbb{P}\left(\left|z-\tau\right|\leq\omega\right)\lesssim\frac{k+t\log^{3}n}{n}
.
Then in view of (
318b
), one has

\displaystyle\bigg\|\ind\Big(\Big|\alpha v^{\star}+\sum_{j=1}^{t-1}\beta^{j}\phi_{j}-\tau\Big|\leq\omega\Big)\bigg\|_{0}\lesssim k+t\log^{3}n,

which holds uniformly over all
\theta\in\Theta
.
If this is the case for our choice
(\alpha,\beta,\tau)=(\alpha_{t},\beta_{t-1},\pm\tau_{t})
,
then we have established inequality (
317
).

•

Consider now the complement case where
\omega
satisfies

\mathbb{P}\left(\left|z-\tau\right|\leq\omega\right)\gg\frac{k+t\log^{3}n}{n}.

(321)

We first make note of the fact that
\omega
needs to satisfy
\omega\geq\sqrt{8/n}
in this case;
otherwise one must have

\mathbb{P}\left(\left|z-\tau\right|\leq\omega\right)=\mathbb{P}\left(\tau-\omega\leq z\leq\tau+\omega\right)\leq\mathbb{P}\left(z\geq\tau-\omega\right)\leq\mathbb{P}\left(z\geq\sqrt{\frac{2\log n}{n}}\right)\leq\frac{k+t\log^{3}n}{n},

which belongs to the previous case.
Based on this simple observation, direct calculations lead to

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left|z-\tau\right|^{2}\ind\left(\left|z-\tau\right|\leq\omega\right)\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[\left|z-\tau\right|^{2}\,\big|\,\left|z-\tau\right|\leq\omega\right]\mathbb{P}\left(\left|z-\tau\right|\leq\omega\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\geq\mathbb{E}\left[\left|z-\tau\right|^{2}\ind\{z\in[\tau-\omega,\tau-\omega/2]\}\,\big|\,\left|z-\tau\right|\leq\omega\right]\mathbb{P}\left(\left|z-\tau\right|\leq\omega\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\geq\bigg(\frac{\omega}{2}\bigg)^{2}\mathbb{P}\Big(z\in[\tau-\omega,\tau-\omega/2]\,\big|\,\left|z-\tau\right|\leq\omega\Big)\mathbb{P}\left(\left|z-\tau\right|\leq\omega\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\geq\frac{\omega^{2}}{8}\mathbb{P}\left(\left|z-\tau\right|\leq\omega\right)\geq\frac{1}{n}\mathbb{P}\left(\left|z-\tau\right|\leq\omega\right),

which combined with (
318
) and (
321
) gives

\displaystyle\bigg\|\Big|\alpha v^{\star}+\sum_{j=1}^{t-1}\beta^{j}\phi_{j}-\tau 1\Big|\circ\ind\Big(\Big|\alpha v^{\star}+\sum_{j=1}^{t-1}\beta^{j}\phi_{j}-\tau 1\Big|\leq\omega 1\Big)\bigg\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad\geq(n-k)\mathbb{E}\left[\left|z-\tau\right|^{2}\ind\left(\left|z-\tau 1\right|\leq\omega\right)\right]-\frac{\log n}{n}\cdot O\left(\sqrt{(n-k)\mathbb{P}\left(\left|z-\tau\right|\leq\omega\right)t\log n}+t\log n\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\geq\mathbb{P}\left(\left|z-\tau\right|\leq\omega\right)-\frac{\log n}{n}\cdot O\left(\sqrt{(n-k)\mathbb{P}\left(\left|z-\tau\right|\leq\omega\right)t\log n}+t\log n\right)\gtrsim\mathbb{P}\left(\left|z-\tau\right|\leq\omega\right).

Setting
\alpha=\alpha_{t}
,
\beta=\beta_{t-1}
and
\tau=\pm\tau_{t}
and utilizing (
319
),
we arrive at

\big\|\xi_{t-1}\big\|_{2}^{2}\gtrsim\mathbb{P}\left(\left|z-\tau_{t}\right|\leq\omega\right).

Taking this and expression (
318b
) collectively yields our advertised bound (
317
), given that
\theta(m)
is trivially below
n
with high probability.

With the above arguments in mind,
everything comes down to establishing the inequalities (
318
) and (
318b
), which shall be done in the following.

Proof of inequality (
318
).

First, given any
\theta\in\Theta
and any
\omega\lesssim n
, we find it useful to develop the following lower bound:

\displaystyle\Bigg\|\Big|\alpha_{t}v^{\star}+\sum_{j=1}^{t-1}\beta^{j}\phi_{j}-\tau 1\Big|\ind\bigg(\Big|\alpha_{t}v^{\star}+\sum_{j=1}^{t-1}\beta^{j}\phi_{j}-\tau 1\Big|\leq\omega 1\bigg)\Bigg\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Big|\alpha v_{i}^{\star}+\sum_{j=1}^{t-1}\beta^{j}\phi_{j,i}-\tau\Big|^{2}\ind\Big(\Big|\alpha v_{i}^{\star}+\sum_{j=1}^{t-1}\beta^{j}\phi_{j,i}-\tau\Big|\leq\omega\Big)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\geq\sum_{i:\,v_{i}^{\star}=0}\Big|\sum_{j=1}^{t-1}\beta^{j}\phi_{j,i}-\tau\Big|^{2}\ind\Big(\Big|\sum_{j=1}^{t-1}\beta^{j}\phi_{j,i}-\tau\Big|\leq\omega\Big).

(322)

Next, we aim to further bound the right-hand side of (
F.3.5
) by means of Corollary
3
.

Towards this, let us define the following functions:

\displaystyle f_{i,\theta}(\Phi_{i,:})

\displaystyle\coloneqq\Big(\sum_{j=1}^{t-1}\beta^{j}\phi_{j,i}-\tau\Big)^{2},\qquad\text{ and }\qquad h_{i,\theta}(\Phi_{i,:})\coloneqq\bigg|\sum_{j=1}^{t-1}\beta^{j}\phi_{j,i}-\tau\bigg|.

Note that for any fixed
\beta\in\mathcal{S}^{t-2}
,
\sum_{j=1}^{t-1}\beta^{j}\phi_{j,i}
follows a Gaussian distribution with variance
1/n
;
therefore,
f_{i,\theta}(\Phi_{i,:})
is a
\big(\tau^{2}+\frac{1}{n}\big)
-subexponential random variable with mean
\mathbb{E}[f_{i,\theta}]=\tau^{2}+\frac{1}{n}
.
We make the observation that
(i)
|f_{i,\theta}(\Phi_{i,:})|\lesssim n^{100}\max_{j}\|\phi_{j}\|_{2}^{100}
;
(ii)
\|\nabla_{\theta}f_{i,\theta}(\Phi_{i,:})\|_{2}\lesssim n^{100}
for any
\Phi\in\mathcal{E}
;
(iii)
\mathbb{P}\big(\tau-400n^{-100}\leq h_{i,\theta}(\Phi_{i,:})\leq\tau+400n^{-100}\big)\lesssim 1/n
for any
\tau\in\real
.
Therefore, applying Corollary
3
together with (
202
) yields: with probability exceeding
1-O(n^{-11})
,

\displaystyle\sum_{i:v_{i}^{\star}=0}\Big|\sum_{j=1}^{t-1}\beta^{j}\phi_{j,i}-\tau\Big|^{2}\ind\Big(\Big|\sum_{j=1}^{t-1}\beta^{j}\phi_{j,i}-\tau\Big|\leq\omega\Big)-(n-k)\mathbb{E}\left[\left|z-\tau\right|^{2}\ind\left(\left|z-\tau\right|\leq\omega\right)\right]

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\geq-\frac{\log n}{n}\cdot O\left(\sqrt{(n-k)\mathbb{P}\left(\left|z-\tau\right|\leq\omega\right)t\log n}+t\log n\right),

(323)

holds simultaneously for all
\theta\in\Theta
and all
\omega\lesssim n
,
where we denote
z\sim\mathcal{N}(0,1/n)
.
Careful readers might already notice that: when applying Corollary
3
, instead of considering the indicator function
\ind\left(h_{i,\theta}(X_{i})>\tau\right)
as in the original form, our result above is concerned with a different kind of indicator function
\ind\left(h_{i,\theta}(X_{i})<\tau\right)
;
fortunately, the proof of this version of indicator functions follows verbatim as that of Lemma
7
and Corollary
3
,
and hence we omit the details here.
Putting (
F.3.5
) and (
F.3.5
) together, we have validated relation (
318
).

Proof of inequality (
318b
).

Using exactly the same analysis as above and taking
f_{i,\theta}(\Phi_{i,:})=1
and
h_{i,\theta}(\Phi_{i,:})=\big|\sum_{j=1}^{t-1}\beta^{j}\phi_{j}-\tau\big|
, Corollary
3
ensures that

\displaystyle\bigg\|\ind\bigg(\Big|\alpha v^{\star}+\sum_{j=1}^{t-1}\beta^{j}\phi_{j}-\tau\Big|\leq\omega\bigg)\bigg\|_{0}

\displaystyle\leq k+\sum_{i:\,v_{i}^{\star}=0}\ind\Big(\Big|\sum_{j=1}^{t-1}\beta^{j}\phi_{j,i}-\tau\Big|\leq\omega\Big)

\displaystyle\lesssim k+\sqrt{(n-k)\mathbb{P}\left(\left|z-\tau\right|\leq\omega\right)t\log n}+t\log n

\displaystyle\lesssim k+(n-k)\mathbb{P}\left(\left|z-\tau\right|\leq\omega\right)+t\log n

holds simultaneously for all
\theta\in\Theta
and all
\omega\lesssim n
.
This completes the proof of (
318b
).

F.4
Establishing the induction hypotheses via recursion

The goal of this subsection is to finish the induction-based proof of (
278
).
We shall start by establishing (
278
) for the
(t+1)
-th iteration, assuming that it holds for the
t
-th iteration.
We will then return to verify the base case for the two types of initialization methods.
Before proceeding, we remind the readers of our assumptions:

\displaystyle\frac{t\log^{3}n}{n\lambda^{2}}\ll 1,\qquad\frac{k\log n}{n\lambda^{2}}\ll 1,

(324)

and we shall always take
\tau_{t}
to be on the order of
\sqrt{\frac{\log n}{n}}
with some sufficiently large preconstant.
In addition,

|\eta_{t}^{\prime}(w)|\lesssim\frac{1}{\lambda}\eqqcolon\rho,\quad|\eta_{t}^{\prime\prime}(w)|=0\eqqcolon\rho_{1},\quad|\eta_{t}^{\prime\prime\prime}(w)|=0\eqqcolon\rho_{2}\qquad\text{for any differentiable point }w\in\real,

(325)

where the calculation of
\rho
has made use of (
289
).

F.4.1
Inductive step for (
278
) regarding
\xi_{t}
,
\Delta_{\alpha,t}
and
\alpha_{t}

Assuming the induction hypotheses (
278
) hold at
t
,
we intend to prove their validity for
t+1.

Bounding
\xi_{t}
.

In terms of
\|\xi_{t}\|_{2}
, the result (
16
) of Theorem
2
together with (
325
) and
\|\beta_{t}\|_{2}=1
gives

\displaystyle\|\xi_{t}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\kappa_{t}^{2}+D_{t}}\,\|\xi_{t-1}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\qquad+O\left(\sqrt{\frac{t\log n}{n}}\|\beta_{t}\|_{2}+A_{t}+\left(\sqrt{\frac{t}{n}}\rho_{1}+\frac{\rho_{2}\|\beta_{t-1}\|_{2}}{n}\right)\|\xi_{t-1}\|_{2}^{2}+\rho\sqrt{\frac{E_{t}+t\log n}{n}}\|\xi_{t-1}\|_{2}+\frac{\rho E_{t}\|\beta_{t-1}\|_{2}}{n}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\kappa_{t}^{2}+D_{t}}\,\|\xi_{t-1}\|_{2}+O\left(\sqrt{\frac{t\log n}{n}}+A_{t}+\sqrt{\frac{E_{t}+t\log n}{\lambda^{2}n}}\|\xi_{t-1}\|_{2}+\frac{E_{t}}{\lambda n}\right).

Making use of the bounds (
314
), (
308
), (
316
) and (
317
), we can further derive

\displaystyle\|\xi_{t}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq c\sqrt{\frac{k}{n\lambda^{2}}+\frac{\sqrt{t(t+k)}\log^{2}n}{n\lambda^{2}}}\|\xi_{t-1}\|_{2}+O\left(\sqrt{\frac{t\log n}{n}}+\frac{1}{\lambda}\sqrt{\frac{E_{t}+t\log n}{n}}\|\xi_{t-1}\|_{2}+\frac{E_{t}}{n\lambda}\right)

\displaystyle\leq c^{\prime}\|\xi_{t-1}\|_{2}+C_{7}\bigg(\sqrt{\frac{t\log n}{n}}+\sqrt{\frac{k+t\log^{3}n}{n\lambda^{2}}}\|\xi_{t-1}\|_{2}+\frac{1}{\lambda}\|\xi_{t-1}\|_{2}^{2}+\frac{k+t\log^{3}n}{n\lambda}\bigg)

(326)

for some large enough constant
C_{7}>0
, where we take

c^{\prime}\coloneqq c\sqrt{\frac{k}{n\lambda^{2}}+\frac{\sqrt{t(t+k)}\log^{2}n}{n\lambda^{2}}}\ll 1

under the assumptions (
324
).
Supposing that

\|\xi_{t-1}\|_{2}\leq C_{8}\sqrt{\frac{(t-1)\log^{3}n+k}{n}}

for some constant
C_{8}>0
large enough, we can invoke (
326
) to reach

\displaystyle\|\xi_{t}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq c^{\prime}C_{8}\sqrt{\frac{(t-1)\log^{3}n+k}{n}}

(327)

\displaystyle\quad+C_{7}\bigg(\sqrt{\frac{t\log n}{n}}+C_{8}\sqrt{\frac{k+t\log^{3}n}{n\lambda^{2}}}\sqrt{\frac{(t-1)\log^{3}n+k}{n}}+\frac{C_{8}^{2}\big((t-1)\log^{3}n+k\big)}{\lambda n}+\frac{k+t\log^{3}n}{n\lambda}\bigg)

(328)

\displaystyle\leq C_{8}\sqrt{\frac{t\log^{3}n+k}{n}}

(329)

where we have made use of the relation (
324
) and the condition
c^{\prime}\ll 1
.
This in turn finishes the inductive step for bounding
\|\xi_{t}\|_{2}
.

Bounding
\Delta_{\alpha,t}
.

A direct application of inequality (
16a
) in Theorem
2
together with (
325
) gives

\displaystyle|\Delta_{\alpha,t}|\,\lesssim\,B_{t}+\rho\|\xi_{t-1}\|_{2}.

Replacing
B_{t}
and
\rho
with their corresponding bounds in (
310
) and (
290
),
and invoking (
278
), we arrive at

\displaystyle|\Delta_{t,\alpha}|

\displaystyle\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{t\log n}{n\lambda^{2}}}+\frac{1}{\lambda}\|\xi_{t-1}\|_{2}\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{k+t\log^{3}n}{n\lambda^{2}}}.

(330)

Controlling
\alpha_{t}
.

Equipped with the control of
|\Delta_{t,\alpha}|
in (
330
), we can now prove that
\alpha_{t+1}\asymp\lambda
for all
t\geq 1
;
in fact, we intend to prove a stronger result, namely, if
\alpha_{t}\asymp\lambda
,

\displaystyle\alpha_{t+1}=\lambda+O\bigg(\sqrt{\frac{k\log n+(t+1)\log^{3}n}{n}}\bigg).

(331)

Taking the definition (
15a
) of
\alpha_{t+1}
collectively with expressions (
300
) and (
41a
)
as well as the property
\lambda\asymp\alpha_{t}\gtrsim\sqrt{\frac{(k+t)\log n}{n}}
gives

\displaystyle\alpha_{t+1}

\displaystyle=\lambda v^{\star\top}\int\gamma_{t}\mathsf{ST}_{\tau_{t}}\left(\alpha_{t}v^{\star}+\frac{x}{\sqrt{n}}\right)\varphi_{n}(\mathrm{d}x)+\lambda\Delta_{\alpha,t}

\displaystyle=\frac{\lambda v^{\star\top}\int\mathsf{ST}_{\tau_{t}}\left(\alpha_{t}v^{\star}+\frac{x}{\sqrt{n}}\right)\varphi_{n}(\mathrm{d}x)}{\sqrt{\int\Big\|\mathsf{ST}_{\tau_{t}}\left(\alpha_{t}v^{\star}+\frac{x}{\sqrt{n}}\right)\Big\|_{2}^{2}\varphi_{n}(\mathrm{d}x)}+O\Big(\sqrt{\frac{t\log n}{n\lambda^{2}}}+\|\xi_{t-1}\|_{2}\Big)}+\lambda\Delta_{\alpha,t}

\displaystyle=\left(1+O\Bigg(\frac{1}{\lambda}\sqrt{\frac{t\log n}{n}}+\frac{\|\xi_{t-1}\|_{2}}{\lambda}\Bigg)\right)\frac{\lambda v^{\star\top}\int\mathsf{ST}_{\tau_{t}}\left(\alpha_{t}v^{\star}+\frac{x}{\sqrt{n}}\right)\varphi_{n}(\mathrm{d}x)}{\sqrt{\int\Big\|\mathsf{ST}_{\tau_{t}}\left(\alpha_{t}v^{\star}+\frac{x}{\sqrt{n}}\right)\Big\|_{2}^{2}\varphi_{n}(\mathrm{d}x)}}+O\Big(\sqrt{\frac{k+t\log^{3}n}{n}}\Big).

(332)

In order to bound (
332
),
we first observe that for every
\alpha
obeying
\sqrt{\frac{(k+t)\log n}{n}}\lesssim\alpha\lesssim 1
and
\tau_{t}\asymp\sqrt{\frac{\log n}{n}}
, it holds that

\displaystyle\left\|\mathsf{ST}_{\tau_{t}}\left(\alpha v^{\star}+\frac{x}{\sqrt{n}}\right)-\alpha v^{\star}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\mathsf{ST}_{\tau_{t}}\left(\alpha v^{\star}\right)-\alpha v^{\star}\right\|_{2}+\left\|\mathsf{ST}_{\tau_{t}}\left(\alpha v^{\star}+\frac{x}{\sqrt{n}}\right)-\mathsf{ST}_{\tau_{t}}\left(\alpha v^{\star}\right)\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\tau_{t}1\circ\ind\left(\left|v^{\star}\right|>0\right)\right\|_{2}+\left\|\frac{x}{\sqrt{n}}\circ\left(\ind\left(\left|\alpha v^{\star}\right|>\tau_{t}1\right)+\ind\left(\left|\alpha v^{\star}+\frac{x}{\sqrt{n}}\right|>\tau_{t}1\right)\right)\right\|_{2},

where the last step invokes relation
\big||\alpha v^{\star}+\frac{x}{\sqrt{n}}|-|\alpha v^{\star}|\big|\leq\frac{|x|}{\sqrt{n}}.

In addition, note that

\displaystyle\ind\left(\left|\alpha v^{\star}\right|>\tau_{t}1\right)+\ind\left(\left|\alpha v^{\star}+\frac{x}{\sqrt{n}}\right|>\tau_{t}1\right)

\displaystyle\leq\ind\left(\left|\alpha v^{\star}\right|>\tau_{t}1\right)+\ind\left(\left|\alpha v^{\star}\right|>\frac{\tau_{t}}{2}1\right)+\ind\left(\left|\frac{x}{\sqrt{n}}\right|>\frac{\tau_{t}}{2}1\right)

\displaystyle\leq 2\ind(|v^{\star}|>0)+\ind\left(\frac{|x|}{\sqrt{n}}>\frac{\tau_{t}}{2}1\right).

Taking the above two relations together yields

\displaystyle\left\|\mathsf{ST}_{\tau_{t}}\left(\alpha v^{\star}+\frac{x}{\sqrt{n}}\right)-\alpha v^{\star}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|\left(\tau_{t}+\frac{|x|}{\sqrt{n}}\right)\circ\ind\left(\left|v^{\star}\right|>0\right)\right\|_{2}+\left\|\frac{x}{\sqrt{n}}\circ\ind\left(\frac{|x|}{\sqrt{n}}>\frac{\tau_{t}}{2}1\right)\right\|_{2},

which further implies

\displaystyle\displaystyle\int\left\|\mathsf{ST}_{\tau_{t}}\left(\alpha v^{\star}+\frac{x}{\sqrt{n}}\right)-\alpha v^{\star}\right\|_{2}^{2}\varphi_{n}(\mathrm{d}x)

\displaystyle\lesssim\displaystyle\int\sum_{i\in\{v^{\star}_{i}\neq 0\}}\left(\tau^{2}_{t}+\frac{x_{i}^{2}}{n}\right)\varphi(\mathrm{d}x)+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}2\displaystyle\int^{\infty}_{2\sqrt{\log n}}x_{i}^{2}\varphi(\mathrm{d}x)

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{k\log n}{n}.

(333)

In words, relation (
F.4.1
) ensures that
\mathsf{ST}_{\tau_{t}}\left(\alpha v^{\star}+\frac{x}{\sqrt{n}}\right)
lies close to
\alpha v^{\star}
.

Next, we would like to employ the above relation to show that

\displaystyle\frac{v^{\star\top}\displaystyle\int\mathsf{ST}_{\tau_{t}}\left(\alpha v^{\star}+\frac{x}{\sqrt{n}}\right)\varphi_{n}(\mathrm{d}x)}{\sqrt{\displaystyle\int\Big\|\mathsf{ST}_{\tau_{t}}\left(\alpha v^{\star}+\frac{x}{\sqrt{n}}\right)\Big\|_{2}^{2}\varphi_{n}(\mathrm{d}x)}}=1+O\Big(\frac{1}{\alpha}\sqrt{\frac{k\log n}{n}}\Big);

(334)

if this were true, then combining it with (
332
), (
41a
) and
\alpha_{t}\asymp\lambda
would justify the bound stated in (
331
).
To prove (
334
), we find it helpful to consider the inner product between

\mathsf{ST}_{\tau_{t}}\left(\alpha v^{\star}+\frac{x}{\sqrt{n}}\right)
and
\alpha v^{\star}
as follows

\displaystyle\alpha v^{\star\top}\int\mathsf{ST}_{\tau_{t}}\left(\alpha v^{\star}+\frac{x}{\sqrt{n}}\right)\varphi_{n}(\mathrm{d}x)

\displaystyle\quad=\displaystyle\int\Big\|\mathsf{ST}_{\tau_{t}}\left(\alpha v^{\star}+\frac{x}{\sqrt{n}}\right)\Big\|^{2}\varphi_{n}(\mathrm{d}x)+\displaystyle\int\Big(\alpha v^{\star}-\mathsf{ST}_{\tau_{t}}\Big(\alpha v^{\star}+\frac{x}{\sqrt{n}}\Big)\Big)^{\top}\mathsf{ST}_{\tau_{t}}\left(\alpha v^{\star}+\frac{x}{\sqrt{n}}\right)\varphi_{n}(\mathrm{d}x)

\displaystyle\quad=\displaystyle\int\Big\|\mathsf{ST}_{\tau_{t}}\left(\alpha v^{\star}+\frac{x}{\sqrt{n}}\right)\Big\|_{2}^{2}\varphi_{n}(\mathrm{d}x)+O\left(\sqrt{\displaystyle\int\Big\|\alpha v^{\star}-\mathsf{ST}_{\tau_{t}}\Big(\alpha v^{\star}+\frac{x}{\sqrt{n}}\Big)\Big\|_{2}^{2}\varphi_{n}(x)}\sqrt{\displaystyle\int\Big\|\mathsf{ST}_{\tau_{t}}\left(\alpha v^{\star}+\frac{x}{\sqrt{n}}\right)\Big\|_{2}^{2}\varphi_{n}(\mathrm{d}x)}\right)

\displaystyle\quad=\displaystyle\int\Big\|\mathsf{ST}_{\tau_{t}}\left(\alpha v^{\star}+\frac{x}{\sqrt{n}}\right)\Big\|_{2}^{2}\varphi_{n}(\mathrm{d}x)+O\Big(\alpha\sqrt{\frac{k\log n}{n}}+\frac{k\log n}{n}\Big)

\displaystyle\quad=\displaystyle\int\Big\|\mathsf{ST}_{\tau_{t}}\left(\alpha v^{\star}+\frac{x}{\sqrt{n}}\right)\Big\|_{2}^{2}\varphi_{n}(\mathrm{d}x)+O\Big(\alpha\sqrt{\frac{k\log n}{n}}\Big),

(335)

where the last line is valid since
\alpha\gtrsim\sqrt{\frac{k\log n}{n}}
, and the penultimate step uses inequality (
F.4.1
) and the following crude bound:

\displaystyle\displaystyle\int\Big\|\mathsf{ST}_{\tau_{t}}\Big(\alpha v^{\star}+\frac{x}{\sqrt{n}}\Big)\Big\|_{2}^{2}\varphi_{n}(x)\lesssim\displaystyle\int\Big\|\alpha v^{\star}-\mathsf{ST}_{\tau_{t}}\Big(\alpha v^{\star}+\frac{x}{\sqrt{n}}\Big)\Big\|_{2}^{2}\varphi_{n}(x)+\alpha^{2}\leq\frac{k\log n}{n}+\alpha^{2}.

Similarly, we can also write

\displaystyle\alpha v^{\star\top}\int\mathsf{ST}_{\tau_{t}}\left(\alpha v^{\star}+\frac{x}{\sqrt{n}}\right)\varphi_{n}(\mathrm{d}x)

\displaystyle\quad=\displaystyle\int\Big\|\mathsf{ST}_{\tau_{t}}\left(\alpha v^{\star}+\frac{x}{\sqrt{n}}\right)\Big\|^{2}\varphi_{n}(\mathrm{d}x)+\displaystyle\int\Big(\alpha v^{\star}-\mathsf{ST}_{\tau_{t}}\Big(\alpha v^{\star}+\frac{x}{\sqrt{n}}\Big)\Big)^{\top}\mathsf{ST}_{\tau_{t}}\left(\alpha v^{\star}+\frac{x}{\sqrt{n}}\right)\varphi_{n}(\mathrm{d}x)

\displaystyle\quad=\displaystyle\int\|\alpha v^{\star}\|_{2}^{2}\varphi_{n}(\mathrm{d}x)+O\left(\sqrt{\displaystyle\int\Big\|\alpha v^{\star}-\mathsf{ST}_{\tau_{t}}\Big(\alpha v^{\star}+\frac{x}{\sqrt{n}}\Big)\Big\|_{2}^{2}\varphi_{n}(x)}\sqrt{\displaystyle\int\|\alpha v^{\star}\|_{2}^{2}\varphi_{n}(\mathrm{d}x)}\right)

\displaystyle\quad=\alpha^{2}+O\Big(\alpha\sqrt{\frac{k\log n}{n}}\Big).

(336)

Putting the above two relations together and recalling that
\alpha\gtrsim\sqrt{\frac{k\log n}{n}}
yields

\displaystyle\displaystyle\int\Big\|\mathsf{ST}_{\tau_{t}}\left(\alpha v^{\star}+\frac{x}{\sqrt{n}}\right)\Big\|_{2}^{2}\varphi_{n}(\mathrm{d}x)=\alpha^{2}+O\Big(\alpha\sqrt{\frac{k\log n}{n}}\Big)\asymp\alpha^{2},

(337)

and hence

\displaystyle\displaystyle\int\Big\|\mathsf{ST}_{\tau_{t}}\left(\alpha v^{\star}+\frac{x}{\sqrt{n}}\right)\Big\|_{2}^{2}\varphi_{n}(\mathrm{d}x)

\displaystyle=\alpha^{2}\bigg(1+O\bigg(\frac{1}{\alpha}\sqrt{\frac{k\log n}{n}}\bigg)\bigg);

\displaystyle\alpha v^{\star\top}\int\mathsf{ST}_{\tau_{t}}\left(\alpha v^{\star}+\frac{x}{\sqrt{n}}\right)\varphi_{n}(\mathrm{d}x)

\displaystyle=\alpha^{2}\bigg(1+O\bigg(\frac{1}{\alpha}\sqrt{\frac{k\log n}{n}}\bigg)\bigg).

This implies (
334
), thus completing the proof of (
331
).
Consequently, we complete the inductive step.

It remains to verify the base case for (
278
),
which is postponed to Section
F.4.3
.

F.4.2
Bounding
\alpha_{t+1}-\alpha_{t+1}^{\star}

Another condition claimed in Theorem
5
is the bound (
41b
) on the difference between
\alpha_{t+1}
and
\alpha_{t+1}^{\star}
,
which we study in this subsection.
In order to understand the dynamics of
\alpha_{t}
, it remains to understand the property of the function
f(\cdot)
defined in (
37
).
A little algebra yields

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}f(\alpha)}{\partial{\alpha}}=\frac{\lambda v^{\star\top}\displaystyle\int v^{\star}\circ\ind\left(\Big|\alpha v^{\star}+\frac{x}{\sqrt{n}}\Big|>\tau_{t}1\right)\varphi_{n}(\mathrm{d}x)}{\left(\displaystyle\int\Big\|\mathsf{ST}_{\tau_{t}}\left(\alpha v^{\star}+\frac{x}{\sqrt{n}}\right)\Big\|_{2}^{2}\varphi_{n}(\mathrm{d}x)\right)^{1/2}}-\frac{\lambda\left(v^{\star\top}\displaystyle\int\mathsf{ST}_{\tau_{t}}\left(\alpha v^{\star}+\frac{x}{\sqrt{n}}\right)\varphi_{n}(\mathrm{d}x)\right)^{2}}{\left(\displaystyle\int\Big\|\mathsf{ST}_{\tau_{t}}\left(\alpha v^{\star}+\frac{x}{\sqrt{n}}\right)\Big\|_{2}^{2}\varphi_{n}(\mathrm{d}x)\right)^{3/2}}\geq 0,

where the last inequality follows from the elementary relation
\mathbb{E}[X^{2}]\cdot\mathbb{E}[Y^{2}]\geq\big(\mathbb{E}[XY]\big)^{2}
.

In addition, we make the observation that every
\alpha=\lambda+O\Big(\sqrt{\frac{t\log^{3}n+k\log n}{n}}\Big)
obeys

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}f(\alpha)}{\partial{\alpha}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\lambda\left(\displaystyle\int\Big\|\mathsf{ST}_{\tau_{t}}\left(\alpha v^{\star}+\frac{x}{\sqrt{n}}\right)\Big\|_{2}^{2}\varphi_{n}(\mathrm{d}x)\right)-\lambda\left(v^{\star\top}\displaystyle\int\mathsf{ST}_{\tau_{t}}\left(\alpha v^{\star}+\frac{x}{\sqrt{n}}\right)\varphi_{n}(\mathrm{d}x)\right)^{2}}{\left(\displaystyle\int\Big\|\mathsf{ST}_{\tau_{t}}\left(\alpha v^{\star}+\frac{x}{\sqrt{n}}\right)\Big\|_{2}^{2}\varphi_{n}(\mathrm{d}x)\right)^{3/2}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\lambda\left(\lambda^{2}+O\Big(\lambda\sqrt{\frac{k\log n+t\log^{3}n}{n}}\Big)\right)-\lambda\left(\lambda-O\Big(\lambda\sqrt{\frac{k\log n+t\log^{3}n}{n}}\Big)\right)^{2}}{\left(\lambda^{2}-O\Big(\lambda\sqrt{\frac{k\log n+t\log^{3}n}{n}}\Big)\right)^{3/2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{k\log n+t\log^{3}n}{n\lambda^{2}}}\leq\frac{1}{2},

where the second line follows from (
F.4.1
) and (
F.4.1
),
and the last line is valid under the assumptions (
324
).

Based on the above properties, we further claim that
f(\alpha)=\alpha
has one solution — denoted by
\alpha^{\star}
— within the range
[\lambda/10,\lambda]
.
In order to see this, recall that in (
334
), we have shown that for any given
\alpha\asymp\lambda
,

\displaystyle f(\alpha)=\frac{\lambda v^{\star\top}\displaystyle\int\mathsf{ST}_{\tau_{t}}\left(\alpha v^{\star}+\frac{x}{\sqrt{n}}\right)\varphi_{n}(\mathrm{d}x)}{\sqrt{\displaystyle\int\Big\|\mathsf{ST}_{\tau_{t}}\left(\alpha v^{\star}+\frac{x}{\sqrt{n}}\right)\Big\|_{2}^{2}\varphi_{n}(\mathrm{d}x)}}=\bigg(1+O\bigg(\sqrt{\frac{k\log n+t\log^{3}n}{n\lambda^{2}}}\bigg)\bigg)\lambda,

(338)

where we invoke the assumption that
\frac{k\log n}{n\lambda^{2}}\ll 1
. In particular, by taking
\alpha=\frac{1}{10}\lambda
, we can deduce that

\displaystyle f\Big(\frac{1}{10}\lambda\Big)=\big(1+o(1)\big)\lambda>\frac{1}{10}\lambda.

In addition, it is easily seen that

\displaystyle f(\lambda)\leq\frac{\lambda\|v^{\star}\|_{2}\sqrt{\displaystyle\int\Big\|\mathsf{ST}_{\tau_{t}}\left(\alpha v^{\star}+\frac{x}{\sqrt{n}}\right)\Big\|_{2}^{2}\varphi_{n}(\mathrm{d}x)}}{\sqrt{\displaystyle\int\Big\|\mathsf{ST}_{\tau_{t}}\left(\alpha v^{\star}+\frac{x}{\sqrt{n}}\right)\Big\|_{2}^{2}\varphi_{n}(\mathrm{d}x)}}=\lambda.

Given that
\frac{\mathrm{d}f(\alpha)}{\partial{\alpha}}\in[0,1/2]
for any
\alpha\in[\lambda/10,\lambda]
,
we conclude that there exists a unique point within
[\lambda/10,\lambda]
obeying
f({\alpha})=\alpha
.

Hence, for any
t
such that
\alpha_{t}=\lambda+O\Big(\sqrt{\frac{t\log^{3}n+k\log n}{n}}\Big)
obeying

\displaystyle\big|\alpha_{t}-\alpha_{t}^{\star}\big|\leq C_{6}\sqrt{\frac{k\log n+t\log^{3}n}{n}}

for some large enough constant
C_{6}>0
, one can invoke
\alpha^{\star}_{t+1}=f(\alpha^{\star}_{t})
to deduce that

\displaystyle\big|\alpha_{t+1}-\alpha_{t+1}^{\star}\big|

\displaystyle\leq\big|f(\alpha_{t})-f(\alpha_{t}^{\star})\big|+C_{7}\sqrt{\frac{k\log n+t\log^{3}n}{n}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}\big|\alpha_{t}-\alpha_{t}^{\star}\big|+C_{7}\sqrt{\frac{k\log n+t\log^{3}n}{n}}\leq C_{6}\sqrt{\frac{k\log n+t\log^{3}n}{n}}

provided that
C_{6}>2C_{7}
,
where we recall that each
\alpha_{t}
satisfies (
331
) shortly after initialization.
Invoking the above relation recursively leads to

\displaystyle\big|\alpha_{t+1}-\alpha_{t+1}^{\star}\big|\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{k\log n+t\log^{3}n}{n}},

thus establishing the advertised bound (
41b
).

F.4.3
Initial condition for (
278
) with two initialization paradigms

In order to conclude the proof,
we still need to verify whether the induction hypotheses (
278
) hold at the initial stage of the algorithm.
In what follows, we shall look at two types of initialization schemes separately.

•

Suppose now that we have access to an initialization point
x_{1}
independent of
W
such that
\langle v^{\star},\,\eta_{1}(x_{1})\rangle\asymp 1
, as assumed in Theorem
5
.
In this case, one can see that

\displaystyle x_{2}=M\eta_{1}(x_{1})

\displaystyle=\big(\lambda v^{\star}v^{\star\top}+W\big)\eta_{1}(x_{1})=\lambda\langle v^{\star},\,\eta_{1}(x_{1})\rangle\cdot v^{\star}+W\eta_{1}(x_{1})

\displaystyle=\alpha_{2}v^{\star}+\phi_{1}+\xi_{1},

where

\displaystyle\alpha_{2}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\lambda\langle v^{\star},\,\eta_{1}(x_{1})\rangle\asymp\lambda,

\displaystyle\phi_{1}

\displaystyle\coloneqq W\eta_{1}(x_{1})+\zeta_{1},

\displaystyle-\xi_{1}=\zeta_{1}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\Big(\frac{\sqrt{2}}{2}-1\Big)\Big(\eta_{1}(x_{1})^{\top}W\eta_{1}(x_{1})\Big)\eta_{1}(x_{1}).

As discussed in Lemma
2
and in (
114
), we have
\phi_{1}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\frac{1}{n}I_{n})
and
\eta_{1}(x_{1})^{\top}W\eta_{1}(x_{1})\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\frac{2}{n})
, where we have used the fact that
\|\eta_{1}(x_{1})\|_{2}=1
. Therefore,
it holds that
\|\xi_{1}\|_{2}\leq\sqrt{\frac{\log n}{n}}
with probability at least
1-O(n^{-11})
.
As a result, we have established the induction hypotheses (
278
)
for
t=2
, as required by Theorem
5
.

•

Another type of initialization schemes considered in this paper is (
267
), which concerns Corollary
4
.
By definition, index
\hat{s}
is selected by maximizing the diagonal entries of
M
, resulting in statistical dependence between
x_{1}
and
W
.
As a consequence, Theorem
2
is not directly applicable.
To cope with the statistical dependency, let us generate an AMP sequence starting from
e_{s}
for each given
s\in\mathcal{S}_{0}
.
For each of these AMP sequences, it turns out that the initial condition for (
278
) when
t=3
is satisfied,
which is formulated in the result below. The proof of this lemma can be found in Section
F.7
.

Lemma 15
.

Consider the AMP procedure initialized with
\eta_{0}(x_{0})=0
and
x_{1}=e_{s}
for any given
s\in\mathcal{S}_{0}
(cf. (
268
)).
With probability at least
1-O(n^{-11})
, the iterate
x_{3}
admits the following decomposition:

\displaystyle x_{3}=\alpha_{3}v^{\star}+\beta_{2}^{1}\phi_{1}+\beta_{2}^{2}\phi_{2}+\xi_{2},

where
\phi_{1}
and
\phi_{2}
are i.i.d. drawn from
\mathcal{N}\big(0,\frac{1}{n}I_{n}\big)
, and

\displaystyle\alpha_{3}=\lambda v^{\star\top}\eta_{2}(x_{2})\asymp\lambda~~\text{ and }~~\|\xi_{2}\|_{2}\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{\log n}{n}}.

(339)

Taking a simple union bound over all
s\in\mathcal{S}_{0}
, we conclude that with probability at least
1-O(n^{-10})
, the initial condition (
278
)
is satisfied for
t=3
if AMP is initialized at
e_{s}
for any
s\in\mathcal{S}_{0}
, thus making Theorem
5
) applicable.
Further, Proposition
1
guarantees that
\mathbb{P}(\hat{s}\in\mathcal{S}_{0})\geq 1-O(n^{-11}).

Putting these together, we can guarantee that the AMP initialized at
e_{\hat{s}}
yields the required decomposition with probability at least
1-O(n^{-10})
,
as claimed in Corollary
4
.

In summary,
putting the above two initial conditions together with the previous inductive steps finishes the proof of
Theorem
5
and Corollary
4
.

F.4.4
Implications for
\ell_{2}
estimation accuracy

Our theory reveals the
\ell_{2}
estimation accuracy of AMP, which we briefly discuss in this subsection.
Consider the estimator
\frac{1}{\lambda}\mathsf{ST}_{\tau_{t}}\left(x_{t}\right)
and look at its
\ell_{2}
estimation error.
The triangle inequality directly implies

\displaystyle\left\|\mathsf{ST}_{\tau_{t}}\left(x_{t}\right)-\lambda v^{\star}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\mathsf{ST}_{\tau_{t}}\left(x_{t}\right)-\mathsf{ST}_{\tau_{t}}\left(v_{t}\right)\right\|_{2}+\left\|\mathsf{ST}_{\tau_{t}}\left(v_{t}\right)-\alpha_{t}v^{\star}\right\|_{2}+\left\|\alpha_{t}v^{\star}-\lambda v^{\star}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\|\xi_{t-1}\|_{2}+\left\|\mathsf{ST}_{\tau_{t}}\left(v_{t}\right)-\alpha_{t}v^{\star}\right\|_{2}+|\alpha_{t}-\lambda|

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\mathsf{ST}_{\tau_{t}}\left(v_{t}\right)-\alpha_{t}v^{\star}\right\|_{2}+O\left(\sqrt{\frac{k\log n+t\log^{3}n}{n}}\right)

with probability at least
1-O(n^{-11})
, where the last step follows from Theorem
5
and inequality (
331
).

Now consider the Lipschitz function
f(x)\coloneqq\|\mathsf{ST}_{\tau_{t}}(\alpha_{t}v^{\star}+x)-\alpha_{t}v^{\star}\|_{2},~x\in\real^{n}
.
Inequality (
105
) yields

\displaystyle\Big\|\mathsf{ST}_{\tau_{t}}\Big(\alpha_{t}v^{\star}+\sum_{j=1}^{t-1}\beta_{t-1}^{j}\phi_{j}\Big)-\alpha_{t}v^{\star}\Big\|_{2}-\mathbb{E}_{g\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\frac{1}{n}I_{n})}\Big[\left\|\mathsf{ST}_{\tau_{t}}\big(\alpha_{t}v^{\star}+g\big)-\alpha_{t}v^{\star}\right\|_{2}\Big]\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{t\log n}{n}}

holds with probability at least
1-O(n^{-11})
.
In addition, in view of (
F.4.1
) (taking
\alpha=\alpha_{t}
), we can deduce

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{g\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\frac{1}{n}I_{n})}\Big[\left\|\mathsf{ST}_{\tau_{t}}\big(\alpha_{t}v^{\star}+g\big)-\alpha_{t}v^{\star}\right\|_{2}\Big]\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{k\log n}{n}}.

Putting these pieces together ensures that, with probability at least
1-O(n^{-11})
,

\displaystyle\Big\|\frac{1}{\lambda}\mathsf{ST}_{\tau_{t}}\left(x_{t}\right)-v^{\star}\Big\|_{2}\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{k\log n+t\log^{3}n}{n\lambda^{2}}}.

(340)

F.5
Proof of Proposition
1

Let us begin by considering the magnitude of
\langle v^{\star},\eta_{2}(x_{2})\rangle
,
which is the focus of the claim (
269
).
From the AMP iteration (
3
), it is seen that

\displaystyle x_{2}=M\eta_{1}(x_{1})=\Big(\lambda v^{\star}\big(v^{\star}\big)^{\top}+W\Big)e_{s}=\lambda v^{\star}_{s}v^{\star}+We_{s},

(341)

where we note that
\eta_{1}(x_{1})=e_{s}
as a consequence of the denoising function (
36b
).
Recognizing that each
W_{ii}
(resp.
W_{ij}
with
i\neq j
) is an independent Gaussian random variable with variance
2/n
(resp.
1/n
),
one can invoke standard Gaussian concentration results to obtain
(
Vershynin, 2018
, Chapter 2.6)

\displaystyle\max_{1\leq i,j\leq n}\left|\big(We_{j}\big)_{i}\right|\leq 6\sqrt{\frac{\log n}{n}}

(342)

with probability at least
1-O(n^{-11})
.
Therefore, if
\lambda|v^{\star}_{s}|\geq 12\sqrt{\frac{k\log n}{n}}
,
then it follows from (
342
) that

\displaystyle|x_{2,i}|\geq|\lambda v^{\star}_{s}|\cdot|v_{i}^{\star}|-\big|\big(We_{j}\big)_{i}\big|

\displaystyle>6\sqrt{\frac{\log n}{n}},\qquad\text{if }|v^{\star}_{i}|\geq\frac{1}{2\sqrt{k}};

\displaystyle|x_{2,i}|\leq\big|\big(We_{j}\big)_{i}\big|

\displaystyle\leq 6\sqrt{\frac{\log n}{n}},\qquad\text{if }v^{\star}_{i}=0.

In the meantime, the above argument also reveals that

\displaystyle\mathrm{sign}(x_{2,i})

\displaystyle=\mathrm{sign}(v^{\star}_{i}),\qquad

\displaystyle\text{if }|x_{2,i}|>6\sqrt{\frac{\log n}{n}};

\displaystyle\big(|x_{2,i}|-\tau_{2}\big)_{+}

\displaystyle=0,\qquad

\displaystyle\text{if }|x_{2,i}|\leq 6\sqrt{\frac{\log n}{n}}.

With the preceding observations in place, we can demonstrate that

\displaystyle\Big\|\mathrm{sign}(x_{2})\circ(|x_{2}|-\tau_{2}1)_{+}\Big\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i=1}^{n}x_{2,i}^{2}\ind\Big(|x_{2,i}|\gtrsim\sqrt{\frac{\log n}{n}}\Big)\lesssim\sum_{i:v^{\star}_{i}\neq 0}\Big(|\lambda v^{\star}_{s}v^{\star}_{i}|+\sqrt{\frac{\log n}{n}}\Big)^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\lambda v^{\star}_{s}\right)^{2}+\frac{k\log n}{n}\asymp\left(\lambda v^{\star}_{s}\right)^{2}

under the assumption that
\lambda|v^{\star}_{s}|\geq 12\sqrt{\frac{k\log n}{n}}
.
In addition, the above properties also reveal that:

\displaystyle\big|\langle v^{\star},\mathrm{sign}(x_{2})\circ(|x_{2}|-\tau_{2}1)_{+}\rangle\big|

\displaystyle=\sum_{i:|x_{2,i}|>6\sqrt{\frac{\log n}{n}}}\left(|x_{2,i}|-\tau_{2}\right)|v^{\star}_{i}|\geq\sum_{i:|v^{\star}_{i}|\geq\frac{1}{2\sqrt{k}}}\left(|x_{2,i}|-\tau_{2}\right)|v^{\star}_{i}|

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{2}\sum_{i:|v^{\star}_{i}|\geq\frac{1}{2\sqrt{k}}}|x_{2,i}|\cdot|v^{\star}_{i}|\gtrsim\sum_{i:|v^{\star}_{i}|\geq\frac{1}{2\sqrt{k}}}|\lambda v^{\star}_{s}|\,v_{i}^{\star 2}\gtrsim\lambda|v^{\star}_{s}|,

(343)

where the last line follows from the assumption
\lambda|v^{\star}_{s}|\geq C_{5}\sqrt{\frac{k\log n}{n}}
for some large enough constant
C_{5}>0
(so that
|x_{2,i}|\geq 2\tau_{2}
), as well as the following condition (using the
k
-sparse property of
v^{\star}
)

\displaystyle\sum_{i:|v^{\star}_{i}|\geq\frac{1}{2\sqrt{k}}}v_{i}^{\star 2}=1-\sum_{i:|v^{\star}_{i}|<\frac{1}{2\sqrt{k}}}v_{i}^{\star 2}\geq 1-k\cdot\bigg(\frac{1}{2\sqrt{k}}\bigg)^{2}=\frac{3}{4}.

(344)

Putting the above pieces together and recalling that
\|\eta_{(}x_{2})\|_{2}=1
, we conclude that

\displaystyle\big|\langle v^{\star},\,\eta_{2}(x_{2})\rangle\big|=\frac{\big|\langle v^{\star},\mathrm{sign}(x_{2})\circ(|x_{2}|-\tau_{2}1)_{+}\rangle\big|}{\left\|\mathrm{sign}(x_{2})\circ(|x_{2}|-\tau_{2}1)_{+}\right\|_{2}}\asymp 1.

Next, we turn to proving the second claim of Proposition
1
,
towards which we would like to show that with probability at least
1-O(n^{-11})
,

\displaystyle|\langle x_{1},v^{\star}\rangle|=|v^{\star}_{\hat{s}}|\geq\frac{1}{2}\|v^{\star}\|_{\infty},

(345)

where
\hat{s}\coloneqq\arg\max_{i}\left|M_{ii}\right|
and
x_{1}=e_{\hat{s}}
.
Indeed, it follows from (
342
) that

\displaystyle\lambda\big(v^{\star}_{i}\big)^{2}-6\sqrt{\frac{\log n}{n}}\leq\Big|\big(\lambda v^{\star}v^{\star\top}+W\big)_{ii}\Big|\leq\lambda\big(v^{\star}_{i}\big)^{2}+6\sqrt{\frac{\log n}{n}}

with probability at least
1-O(n^{-11})
.
Given that
\hat{s}
maximizes
|(\lambda v^{\star}v^{\star\top}+W)_{ii}|
over all
i\in[n]
, we have

\displaystyle\lambda\big(v^{\star}_{\hat{s}}\big)^{2}+6\sqrt{\frac{\log n}{n}}\geq\Big|\left(\lambda v^{\star}v^{\star\top}+W\right)_{\hat{s}\hat{s}}\Big|\geq\Big|\big(\lambda v^{\star}v^{\star\top}+W\big)_{jj}\Big|\geq\lambda\big(v^{\star}_{\max}\big)^{2}-6\sqrt{\frac{\log n}{n}},\quad\text{with }j\coloneqq\arg\!\max_{i}|v^{\star}_{i}|.

In the case when
\lambda\|v^{\star}\|_{\infty}\gtrsim\sqrt{\frac{k\log n}{n}}
,
the above inequality immediately establishes (
345
) by observing that

\|v^{\star}\|_{\infty}\geq\sqrt{\frac{\|v^{\star}\|_{2}^{2}}{k}}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{k}}.

We have thus completed the proof of Proposition
1
.

F.6
Proof of Proposition
2

For notational convenience,
we omit the index of
j
in
\mathcal{I}_{j}
and write
\mathcal{I}
instead throughout this proof,
as long as it is clear from the context.

Step 1: analysis for a single round.

Let us first state some concentration properties regarding
v^{\star}_{\mathcal{I}}
.
By construction,
\|v^{\star}_{\mathcal{I}}\|_{0}
can be viewed as the sum of
k
independent Bernoulli random variables, each of which has mean
p
.
The Bernstein inequality tells us that

\displaystyle\Big|\big\|v^{\star}_{\mathcal{I}}\big\|_{0}-kp\Big|\leq\sqrt{2kp(1-p)\log\frac{2}{\delta}}+2\log\frac{2}{\delta}

holds with probability at least
1-\delta
.
It thus implies that

\big\|v^{\star}_{\mathcal{I}}\big\|_{0}-pk=o(pk)

under the assumption that
kp\gtrsim\log n\gg\log\frac{2}{\delta}

and
\delta\asymp 1
(e.g.,
\delta=\frac{1}{10}
).
In addition,
\|v^{\star}_{\mathcal{I}}\big\|_{2}^{2}=\sum_{i\in\text{supp}(v^{\star})}v^{\star 2}_{i}\ind(i\in\mathcal{I})
is the sum of
k
independent bounded random variables, with total variance bounded above by

\mathsf{Var}\big(\|v^{\star}_{\mathcal{I}}\big\|_{2}^{2}\big)\leq p\big\|v^{\star}_{\mathcal{I}}\big\|_{\infty}^{2}\sum_{i\in\text{supp}(v^{\star})}v^{\star 2}_{i}=p\big\|v^{\star}_{\mathcal{I}}\big\|_{\infty}^{2}.

Invoking Bernstein’s inequality again gives

\displaystyle\left|\big\|v^{\star}_{\mathcal{I}}\big\|_{2}^{2}-p\right|\leq\big\|v^{\star}_{\mathcal{I}}\big\|_{\infty}\sqrt{2p\log\frac{2}{\delta}}+2\big\|v^{\star}_{\mathcal{I}}\big\|_{\infty}^{2}\log\frac{2}{\delta},

with probability at least
1-\delta
.
Consequently, it guarantees that with probability
1-\delta
,

\displaystyle\|v^{\star}_{\mathcal{I}}\big\|_{2}^{2}-p=o(p)~\text{ and }~\|v^{\star}_{\mathcal{I}^{c}}\big\|_{2}^{2}=1-p+o(p),

(346)

under the assumption that
\|v^{\star}\|_{\infty}=o\big(\sqrt{\frac{\log n}{k}}\big)
and
p\gtrsim\frac{\log n}{k}
.
Combining the above two relations gives

\displaystyle\lambda\big\|v^{\star}_{\mathcal{I}}\big\|_{2}^{2}~\gtrsim~\frac{\big\|v^{\star}_{\mathcal{I}}\big\|_{0}}{\sqrt{n}},

(347)

with the proviso that
\lambda\gtrsim k/\sqrt{n}.

Suppose now that there exists an oracle algorithm (as in (
271
)) whose returned solution
v_{\mathcal{I}}=\mathsf{Oracle}(M_{\mathcal{I},\mathcal{I}})
(computed solely based on
M_{\mathcal{I},\mathcal{I}}
) satisfies

\displaystyle\langle v^{\star}_{\mathcal{I}},v_{\mathcal{I}}\rangle\asymp\big\|v^{\star}_{\mathcal{I}}\big\|_{2}\asymp\sqrt{p}

with probability at least
1-\delta
for some small constant
\delta
(note that the randomness comes from the sampling process).
Taking
v\coloneqq M_{\mathcal{I}^{c},\mathcal{I}}v_{\mathcal{I}}
yields

\displaystyle v=M_{\mathcal{I}^{c},\mathcal{I}}v_{\mathcal{I}}=(\lambda v^{\star}_{\mathcal{I}^{c}}v_{\mathcal{I}}^{\star\top}+W_{\mathcal{I}^{c},\mathcal{I}})v_{\mathcal{I}}=\alpha_{1}v_{\mathcal{I}^{c}}^{\star}+\phi_{0},

(348)

where
\alpha_{1}=\lambda\langle v^{\star}_{\mathcal{I}},v_{\mathcal{I}}\rangle\asymp\lambda\sqrt{p}
and
\phi_{0}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\frac{\|v_{\mathcal{I}}\|_{2}^{2}}{n}I)
.
Importantly, both
\alpha_{1}
and
\phi_{0}
are independent of
W_{\mathcal{I}^{c},\mathcal{I}^{c}}
.
Based on the construction (
277
) of
x_{1}
, it suffices to verify

\displaystyle\frac{\big\langle v_{\mathcal{I}^{c}}^{\star},\mathsf{ST}_{\tau_{1}}(v)\big\rangle}{\big\|v_{\mathcal{I}^{c}}^{\star}\big\|_{2}\big\|\mathsf{ST}_{\tau_{1}}(v)\big\|_{2}}\asymp 1.

(349)

In order to validate (
349
), we look at the distributional of (
348
).
First, given the fact that
\|\phi_{0}\|_{\infty}\leq 6\sqrt{\frac{\log n}{n}}
with probability at least
1-O(n^{-11})
and

\alpha_{1}\asymp\lambda\sqrt{p}\gtrsim\sqrt{\frac{k\log n\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{n}},

we can use (
348
) to get

\displaystyle\mathrm{sign}\big(\mathsf{ST}_{\tau_{1}}(v_{i})\big)=\mathrm{sign}\big(v^{\star}_{i}\big)\qquad\text{and}\qquad\begin{cases}\Big|\mathsf{ST}_{\tau_{1}}(v_{i})\Big|\geq\frac{1}{2}\alpha_{1}|v_{i}^{\star}|\mathds{1}\Big(\lambda\sqrt{p}|v_{i}^{\star}|\geq C_{8}\sqrt{\frac{\log n}{n}}1\Big)\\
\Big|\mathsf{ST}_{\tau_{1}}(v_{i})\Big|\leq 2\alpha_{1}|v_{i}^{\star}|\mathds{1}\Big(\lambda\sqrt{p}|v_{i}^{\star}|\geq C_{7}\sqrt{\frac{\log n}{n}}1\Big)\end{cases}\qquad i\in\mathcal{I}^{c}

for some suitable constants
C_{7},C_{8}>0
.
As a result, we can see that

\displaystyle\Big\langle v_{\mathcal{I}^{c}}^{\star},\frac{\mathsf{ST}_{\tau_{1}}(v)}{\|\mathsf{ST}_{\tau_{1}}(v)\|_{2}}\Big\rangle\gtrsim\frac{\alpha_{1}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{B}}v_{i}^{\star 2}}{\alpha_{1}\|v^{\star}\|_{2}}\qquad\text{with }~\mathcal{B}\coloneqq\Big\{i\mid i\in\mathcal{I}^{c};|v_{i}^{\star}|\geq C_{8}\frac{\sqrt{\log n/n}}{\lambda\sqrt{p}}\Big\}.

(350)

Moreover, recalling the concentration results
\|v^{\star}_{\mathcal{I}}\big\|_{2}^{2}-p=o(p)
and
\big\|v^{\star}_{\mathcal{I}}\big\|_{0}-pk=o(pk)
, we find that

\displaystyle\sum_{i\in\mathcal{B}^{c}\cap\mathcal{I}^{c}}v_{i}^{\star 2}

\displaystyle=\Big\|v_{\mathcal{I}^{c}}\circ\ind\Big(v_{\mathcal{I}^{c}}<\frac{C_{8}\sqrt{\log n/n}}{\lambda\sqrt{p}}1\Big)\Big\|_{2}^{2}\leq\big(k-kp+o(kp)\big)\cdot\frac{C_{8}^{2}\log n}{\lambda^{2}pn}

\displaystyle\leq\big(k-kp+o(kp)\big)\cdot\frac{C_{8}^{2}\log n}{10C_{8}^{2}k\log n\log\frac{1}{\delta}}\leq\frac{1-p}{10\log(1/\delta)}(1+o(1))\leq\frac{1}{10}\|v_{\mathcal{I}^{c}}^{\star}\|_{2}^{2},

provided that
\lambda^{2}p\geq 10C_{8}^{2}\frac{k\log n\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{n}
.
Substitution into (
350
) gives

\Big\langle v_{\mathcal{I}^{c}}^{\star},\frac{\mathsf{ST}_{\tau_{1}}(v)}{\|\mathsf{ST}_{\tau_{1}}(v)\|_{2}}\Big\rangle\gtrsim 1,

which together with
\|v_{\mathcal{I}^{c}}\|_{2}\asymp 1
establishes the required inequality (
349
).

Step 2: repeating the procedure for
N
times.

Thus far, we have proved that inequalities (
346
) and (
349
) are satisfied with probability
1-\delta
, for
\delta
being some small constant (e.g.,
\delta=1/10
);
here, the uncertainty comes from the random sampling process.
In order to boost the success probability, we — as detailed in Step 1 — repeat the sampling procedure for
N\coloneqq 10\log n/\log(1/\delta)
times,
outputing
N
independent subsets
\mathcal{I}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{I}_{N}\subseteq[n]

and
N
corresponding estimators (denoted by
v^{j}
for
1\leq j\leq N
).
Then there exists at least one subset
\mathcal{I}_{j}
such that, with probability at least
1-\delta^{N}=1-O(n^{-10})
,

\displaystyle\|v^{\star}_{\mathcal{I}^{c}}\big\|_{2}^{2}=1-p+o(p)\qquad\text{and}\qquad\frac{\big\langle v_{\mathcal{I}^{c}}^{\star},\mathsf{ST}_{\tau_{1}}(v^{j})\big\rangle}{\big\|v_{\mathcal{I}^{c}}^{\star}\big\|_{2}\big\|\mathsf{ST}_{\tau_{1}}(v^{j})\big\|_{2}}\asymp 1

(351)

for
v^{j}\coloneqq M_{\mathcal{I}_{j}^{c},\mathcal{I}_{j}}v_{\mathcal{I}_{j}}
.
Based on these two relations, we arrive at

\displaystyle\frac{\mathsf{ST}_{\tau_{1}}(v^{j})^{\top}M_{\mathcal{I}_{j}^{c},\mathcal{I}_{j}^{c}}\mathsf{ST}_{\tau_{1}}(v^{j})}{\|\mathsf{ST}_{\tau_{1}}(v^{j})\|_{2}^{2}}

\displaystyle=\lambda\frac{\big\langle v_{\mathcal{I}_{j}^{c}}^{\star},\mathsf{ST}_{\tau_{1}}(v^{j})\big\rangle^{2}}{\big\|\mathsf{ST}_{\tau_{1}}(v^{j})\big\|^{2}_{2}}+\underbrace{\frac{\mathsf{ST}_{\tau_{1}}(v^{j})^{\top}W_{\mathcal{I}_{j}^{c}\mathcal{I}_{j}^{c}}\mathsf{ST}_{\tau_{1}}(v^{j})}{\big\|\mathsf{ST}_{\tau_{1}}(v^{j})\big\|^{2}_{2}}}_{=:\varepsilon_{j}}

\displaystyle=\lambda\frac{\big\langle v_{\mathcal{I}_{j}^{c}}^{\star},\mathsf{ST}_{\tau_{1}}(v^{j})\big\rangle^{2}}{\|v_{\mathcal{I}_{j}^{c}}^{\star}\|_{2}^{2}\big\|\mathsf{ST}_{\tau_{1}}(v^{j})\big\|^{2}_{2}}\cdot\|v_{\mathcal{I}_{j}^{c}}^{\star}\|_{2}^{2}+\varepsilon_{j}

\displaystyle\gtrsim\lambda(1-p)+\varepsilon_{j},\qquad\text{where }\varepsilon_{j}\sim\mathcal{N}\Big(0,\frac{1}{n}\Big)

\displaystyle\asymp\lambda

(352)

Here, we remind the readers that since
v^{j}
is independent of

W_{\mathcal{I}_{j}^{c}\mathcal{I}_{j}^{c}}
, and

\varepsilon_{j}
follows a Gaussian distribution
\mathcal{N}(0,\frac{1}{n}).

We also make note of the relation that

\lambda(1-p)\gtrsim\frac{k(1-p)}{\sqrt{n}}\gtrsim\frac{\log n}{\sqrt{n}}
and

\max_{1\leq j\leq N}|\varepsilon_{j}|\leq 5\sqrt{\frac{\log N}{n}}
with probability
1-O(n^{-11})
.

Therefore, if we select
\widehat{j}
according to (
277
) as follows:

\displaystyle\widehat{j}\coloneqq\arg\max_{j}~\Bigg\{\frac{\mathsf{ST}_{\tau_{1}}(v^{j})^{\top}M_{\mathcal{I}_{j}^{c},\mathcal{I}_{j}^{c}}\mathsf{ST}_{\tau_{1}}(v^{j})}{\|\mathsf{ST}_{\tau_{1}}(v^{j})\|_{2}^{2}}\Bigg\},

then we necessarily have

\displaystyle\frac{\big\langle v_{\mathcal{I}_{\widehat{j}}^{c}}^{\star},\mathsf{ST}_{\tau_{1}}(v^{\widehat{j}})\big\rangle}{\big\|v_{\mathcal{I}_{\widehat{j}}^{c}}^{\star}\big\|_{2}\big\|\mathsf{ST}_{\tau_{1}}(v^{\widehat{j}})\big\|_{2}}\asymp 1.

(353)

Otherwise, we would end up with

\displaystyle\frac{\mathsf{ST}_{\tau_{1}}(v^{\widehat{j}})^{\top}M_{\mathcal{I}^{c},\mathcal{I}^{c}}\mathsf{ST}_{\tau_{1}}(v^{\widehat{j}})}{\|\mathsf{ST}_{\tau_{1}}(v^{\widehat{j}})\|_{2}^{2}}

\displaystyle=\lambda\frac{\big\langle v_{\mathcal{I}_{\widehat{j}}^{c}}^{\star},\mathsf{ST}_{\tau_{1}}(v^{\widehat{j}})\big\rangle^{2}}{\big\|\mathsf{ST}_{\tau_{1}}(v^{\widehat{j}})\big\|^{2}_{2}}+\frac{\mathsf{ST}_{\tau_{1}}(v^{\widehat{j}})^{\top}W_{\mathcal{I}_{\widehat{j}}^{c}\mathcal{I}_{\widehat{j}}^{c}}\mathsf{ST}_{\tau_{1}}(v^{\widehat{j}})}{\big\|\mathsf{ST}_{\tau_{1}}(v^{\widehat{j}})\big\|^{2}_{2}}

\displaystyle\ll\lambda+\sqrt{\frac{\log n}{n}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\mathsf{ST}_{\tau_{1}}(v^{j})^{\top}M_{\mathcal{I}^{c},\mathcal{I}^{c}}\mathsf{ST}_{\tau_{1}}(x^{j}_{1})}{\|\mathsf{ST}_{\tau_{1}}(x^{j}_{1})\|_{2}^{2}},

where
j
corresponds to the one obeying (
351
).
This, however, contradicts the definition of
\widehat{j}.

We have therefore concluded the proof of Proposition
2
.

F.7
Proof of Lemma
15

In this subsection, we analyze the first three AMP iterates when initialized at
\eta_{0}(x_{0})=0
and
x_{1}=e_{s}

for any given
s\in\mathcal{S}_{0}
, where
\mathcal{S}_{0}\coloneqq\{s\in[n]\mid|v^{\star}_{s}|\geq\frac{1}{2}\|v^{\star}\|_{\infty}\}
(defined in expression (
268
)).
Without loss of generality, we shall assume
v_{s}^{\star}>0
throughout this subsection.

The 2nd iterate.

It follows from the AMP update rule that

\displaystyle x_{2}=M\eta_{1}(x_{1})=\Big(\lambda v^{\star}\big(v^{\star}\big)^{\top}+W\Big)e_{s}=\lambda v^{\star}_{s}v^{\star}+We_{s},

where we use
\eta_{1}(x_{1})=e_{s}
in view of the definition of the denoising function in (
36b
).
Recalling that in the proof of Theorem
2
, we establish the decomposition (
51
) with
\phi_{j}
and
\beta_{t}^{j}
defined in (
47
) and (
44
) respectively.
Instantiating this to the current case, we have

\beta_{1}^{1}=1\qquad\text{and}\qquad\phi_{1}=We_{s}+\Big(\frac{\sqrt{2}}{2}-1\Big)e_{s}^{\top}We_{s}\sim\mathcal{N}\Big(0,\frac{1}{n}I_{n}\Big).

Hence,
x_{2}
can be expressed as

\displaystyle x_{2}

\displaystyle=\alpha_{2}v^{\star}+\phi_{1}+\xi_{1},

(354)

where
\alpha_{2}=\lambda v^{\star}_{s}\gtrsim\lambda
obeying
|\alpha_{2}|\geq\|v^{\star}\|_{\infty}
, and
\xi_{1}=(1-\frac{\sqrt{2}}{2})(e_{s}^{\top}We_{s})e_{s}
.

To proceed, we find it helpful to make note of the following two properties.
First, from expression (
F.5
), we know that

\displaystyle\gamma_{2}^{-1}=\big\|\mathrm{sign}(x_{2})\circ(|x_{2}|-\tau_{2}1)_{+}\big\|_{2}\geq\big|\langle v^{\star},\mathrm{sign}(x_{2})\circ(|x_{2}|-\tau_{2}1)_{+}\rangle\big|\gtrsim\lambda|v^{\star}_{s}|.

(355)

In addition, with probability at least
1-O(n^{-11})
, one can derive from (
342
) that

\displaystyle|\xi_{1,s}|=\Big|\Big(1-\frac{\sqrt{2}}{2}\Big)e_{s}^{\top}We_{s}\Big|\leq\max_{1\leq i\leq n}|W_{ii}|\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{\log n}{n}}.

(356)

The 3rd iterate.

In view of decomposition (
51
), we write

\displaystyle x_{3}

\displaystyle=M\eta_{2}(x_{2})-\langle\eta_{2}^{\prime}(x_{2})\rangle\eta_{1}(x_{1})=\alpha_{3}v^{\star}+\beta_{2}^{1}\phi_{1}+\beta_{2}^{2}\phi_{2}+\xi_{2},

where (see(
55
))

\alpha_{3}=\lambda v^{\star\top}\eta_{2}(x_{2})\qquad\text{and}\qquad\xi_{2}\in\mathsf{span}\{e_{s},\eta_{2}(x_{2})\}.

Proposition
1
tells us that
\alpha_{3}\asymp\lambda.

Next, we look at the size of
\|\xi_{2}\|_{2}.

To begin with, by definition, we have
\mu_{2}^{1}=\frac{\xi_{1}^{\top}e_{s}}{\|\xi_{1}\|_{2}}=1
for
\xi_{1}=(1-\frac{\sqrt{2}}{2})(e_{s}^{\top}We_{s})e_{s}
.
Combining this with relation (
56
) for
t=2
, we arrive at

\displaystyle\|\xi_{2}\|_{2}=\langle\phi_{1},\delta_{2}\rangle-\langle\delta_{2}^{\prime}\rangle+\Delta_{2}+O\Big(\sqrt{\frac{\log n}{n}}\Big),

(357)

where
\delta_{2}
takes the following form

\displaystyle\delta_{2}=\eta_{2}(\alpha_{2}v^{\star}+\phi_{1}+\xi_{1})-\eta_{2}(\alpha_{2}v^{\star}+\phi_{1}).

To control
\|\xi_{2}\|_{2}
, it then suffices to upper bound
\Delta_{2}
as well as

\langle\phi_{1},\delta_{2}\rangle-\langle\delta_{2}^{\prime}\rangle
.
Let us start with the quantity
\Delta_{2}
.
Recall that
|\Delta_{2}|\leq A_{2}\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{2\log n}{n}}
(in view of (
308
)), where
we remind the readers that the proof of (
308
) is built upon the assumptions
\alpha_{2}\lesssim\lambda
and
\tau_{2}\asymp\sqrt{\frac{\log n}{n}}.

We then move on to consider the quantity
\langle\phi_{1},\delta_{2}\rangle-\langle\delta_{2}^{\prime}\rangle
.
First, given that
\xi_{1}
is along the direction of
e_{s}
, the entries of
\delta_{2}

are all zero except for
\delta_{2,s}.

With this observation in mind, the term of interest can be written as

\displaystyle\big|\langle\phi_{1},\delta_{2}\rangle-\langle\delta_{2}^{\prime}\rangle\big|

\displaystyle=\Big|\phi_{1,s}\delta_{2,s}-\frac{1}{n}\delta_{2,s}^{\prime}\Big|\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{\log n}{n}}\cdot\frac{|\xi_{1,s}|}{\lambda v_{s}^{\star}}+\frac{1}{n\lambda v_{s}^{\star}}.

(358)

To see why the last inequality is valid, we use inequality (
355
) to obtain

\displaystyle|\delta_{2,s}|=\big|\eta_{2}(\alpha_{2}v^{\star}_{s}+\phi_{1,s}+\xi_{1,s})-\eta_{2}(\alpha_{2}v^{\star}_{s}+\phi_{1,s})|\leq\gamma_{2}|\xi_{1,s}\big|\lesssim\frac{|\xi_{1,s}|}{\lambda v^{\star}_{s}},

as a result of the Lipschitz property of
\eta_{t}
, and in addition,

\displaystyle|\delta_{2,s}^{\prime}|\leq 2\gamma_{2}\lesssim\frac{1}{\lambda v^{\star}_{s}}.

Taking inequality (
358
) together with (
356
) and recalling
v^{\star}_{s}\gtrsim\frac{1}{\sqrt{k}}
(cf. (
269
)) lead to

\displaystyle\big|\langle\phi_{1},\delta_{2}\rangle-\langle\delta_{2}^{\prime}\rangle\big|

\displaystyle\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{\log n}{n}}\sqrt{\frac{k\log n}{n\lambda^{2}}}+\frac{\sqrt{k}}{n\lambda}\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{\log n}{n}}.

Substitution back into (
357
) gives

\displaystyle\|\xi_{2}\|_{2}\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{\log n}{n}}.

We have thus established Lemma
15
.
